# Changelog

Release history for the `issue-viewer` web app and the `issue-cli` CLI.
GitHub releases on `kacperwerema/dark-factory` are the source
of truth for release notes; `issue-cli process changes` fetches them live
and falls back to this embedded file when the API is unreachable. Keep
entries here mirrored with the GitHub release descriptions.

Lives at `cmd/issue-cli/CHANGELOG.md` (co-located with the CLI) because Go's
`//go:embed` cannot reference files outside the embedding package's directory.

Entries are newest-first. Each entry has the form:

    ## <version> — <YYYY-MM-DD>

    - user-visible change
    - another user-visible change

Shipped changes collect under `## Unreleased` at the top. A release is cut
per milestone (the `version:` on issues, see docs/roadmap.md): the lead
renames `## Unreleased` to `## vX.Y.Z — YYYY-MM-DD` and pushes, and the
release-marker Action tags and builds it. Pushes with `## Unreleased` on top
release nothing.

## v0.65.0 — 2026-11-21

- **A regression test pins ship's install guard to the 2026-11-19 incident's own command** (cli/issue-cli-ship-runs-npm-ci-in-a-worktree-whose-node-modules-root-and). ship with `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund` in a worktree whose root and packages/* node_modules link the main checkout's skips the install while the lock file is main's, or makes the tree's own copies first once it changed, and says which; a stand-in npm that empties every node_modules it reaches leaves the main checkout's untouched. The guard itself shipped with the file-churn fix (no install runs through a node_modules link at any depth).
- **`worktree add` no longer hands over a tree without `node_modules` as ready** (cli/worktree-add-for-planodkrywanie-w-solverze-printed-success-but-made-no). Under `worktree_deps: own` with no `worktree_setup`, a new tree got no `node_modules` at all and `worktree add` printed success, so `npm run` failed with `spawnSync node_modules/.bin/tsx ENOENT`. Now `own` gives the tree its own copy of each `node_modules` the main checkout has (root and workspace packages; a clone on APFS, no download, never a link into the main checkout) where the lock file is the same; with a `worktree_setup` it leaves the install to that. A link or copy that fails is named in the step instead of skipped, and `worktree add` exits non-zero with `✗ no node_modules … in the worktree` and the install command (`npm ci`) when a folder the main checkout has is still missing.
- **`release cut` no longer asks to pick a fix already picked with a rewritten changelog, and a soak report the owner moved out of the release no longer holds the cut** (factory/release-cut-a-pick-with-a-rewritten-changelog-counts-as-picked-and-a-soak-report-the-owner-moved-out-of-the-release-no-longer-blocks-the-cut). A commit counts as picked when the release branch has a commit whose `(cherry picked from commit <sha>)` line names it, even if the patch differs (`release pick` rewrites the changelog hunk), on the **Every done fix is on release/v0.N** check and in `release pick`. A report from a candidate whose `version:` is past the milestone, with the owner's ok recorded (a decision with the owner's words, or a lead comment naming the new version and quoting the owner), drops out of **No open reports against v0.N.0 candidates** and shows under **Moved out on the owner's ok** (`moved out: <slug> → 0.N+1`); one moved without the ok still holds the cut, and the check says so. See [Cutting a release](../../docs/roadmap.md#cutting-a-release-candidate-soak-publish).
- **`video/elevenlabs` gets a check, a free voice browse and capped music** (factory/videoelevenlabs-connector-a-subscription-check-a-free-shared-voice-browse-and-capped-music-generation). `connector check video/elevenlabs` now has a built-in check (no `check:` needed): it reads `/v1/user/subscription` with the Keychain key and prints the plan and the credits used and left this period, never the key, and meters nothing. `connector exec video/elevenlabs -- voices search [--language pl] [--gender female] [--use-case …] [--commercial] [--json]` lists library voices with their preview URLs, free, so the owner listens before a `voice_id` goes into `voices:`. `-- music --prompt "…" --length-ms N --out x.mp3` makes a music bed through `/v1/music`, capped like `tts` in the same monthly pool (ElevenLabs credits, which its API calls characters; about 900 a minute): refused with no cap, at the cap, after an unknown cost, on a free plan, or when its estimate passes what is left; the cost is `character-cost`, else the subscription's count before and after. It writes `<x>.licence.json` beside the MP3 and prints the storyboard `music` block and the reel's legal lines. docs/departments.md#spend-caps, docs/reels.md#voice-and-music-from-elevenlabs.
- **A key or an `.env` file can't be committed or pushed: leak-check, the commit-msg hook and the pre-push guard refuse it, in every project with the guard** (factory/leak-check-and-pre-push-refuse-secrets-key-patterns-and-tracked-env-files-for-df-and-every-project-with-env-ignored-in-the-reel-starter-template). The secret scan (`internal/privacy/secrets.go`) knows ElevenLabs (`sk_…`), Stripe live (`sk_live_`/`rk_live_`), GitHub (`ghp_`/`github_pat_`), AWS (`AKIA…`), Slack (`xox…`), OpenAI/Anthropic (`sk-proj-`/`sk-ant-`), a PEM private key block and a JWT, and refuses a tracked `.env` or `.env.*` (`.env.example`, `.sample`, `.template` pass). Each hit names the file, the line and the kind, never the value; placeholders (`sk_…`, `AKIA…EXAMPLE`) pass. `make leak-check` runs it over the tree; `issue-cli privacy pre-push`, which the guard calls, runs it over the commits a push sends, also in projects with no `privacy:` block (add the guard with `issue-cli --project <slug> privacy hook`; `privacy check` shows a `secret` line, amber with that fix when the guard is missing); the commit-msg hook refuses one staged. This repo's `.gitignore` and the reel template (`reel init` now writes a `.gitignore`) ignore `.env` and `.env.*` but `.env.example`. docs/departments.md#secrets says where each kind of key lives.
- **A reel kit: `issue-cli reel` turns a storyboard JSON into a checked 9:16 MP4 on the shelf** (factory/reel-kit-storyboard-json-schema-render-and-qa-command-template-shapes-a-project-fills-hook-plus-list-beforeafter-app-demo). `reel init` copies a starter Remotion template with three shapes (hook-list, before-after, app-demo) into the project, which then owns its copy; Remotion stays its npm dependency, under Remotion's licence (free for companies of up to 3 people). `reel validate` lists every storyboard problem at its JSON path (`reel schema` prints the JSON Schema); `reel render` fills the template and encodes with FFmpeg (narration at its scene starts, music under it, -14 LUFS, H.264/AAC 1080x1920), after `mem-guard check`; `reel qa` fails a reel on duration, 9:16, loudness, black frames, captions outside the Instagram/TikTok safe zones and captions out of sync; `reel shelve` refuses a red reel and puts a green one on the shelf with its QA report, shown under the player (`output add --report`). The kit reads files only: TTS audio, word timings and AI clips come from the connectors. docs/reels.md.
- **Paid connectors have a monthly spend cap the connector enforces; the Video template ships `kling` and `elevenlabs` for reels** (factory/spend-capped-connectors-for-reels-elevenlabs-and-one-video-generation-api-keychain-keys-a-monthly-cap-the-connector-enforces): a connector's `spend:` block meters each billing call into `<issues>/.connectors/spend.jsonl` and refuses billing calls once the calendar month reaches `cap:`, with no cap set, or after a call whose cost is unknown (`connector spend add` records it). `meter: json` reads the cost from the command's JSON (Kling's `creditsConsumed`); `meter: elevenlabs` is issue-cli's own text-to-speech call (`connector exec video/elevenlabs -- tts --placement <p> …`) with `voices:` by placement, a real person's voice refused without `consent:`. `issue-cli connectors`, the company room and Needs you show spend against the cap. The template ships no cap: the owner writes it (docs/departments.md#spend-caps).
- **Video makes reels: a `reel` type with a legal check on the script, one owner review on the shelf and the publish stamp** (factory/reel-type-in-the-video-department-brief-script-with-a-legal-check-render-one-owner-review-on-the-shelf-publish-by-the-owner; reels epic D-2). The video workflow gets `default_type: studio` (the partner flow, its 6 owner_only gates unchanged) and `reel`: brief → script → render → review → publish → done, `render` a new base status only reels walk. Brief, script and render pass without the owner. script → render and render → review run the new `reel_legal_check` validator (plain Go, no shell): a nutrition or health claim needs a `claim: <phrase> = <authorisation>` line (EU 1924/2006), music a licence, AI content a label (EU AI Act art. 50), a face a presenter persona and a consent file, and no testimonial (Omnibus) or mark with endorsement words passes. The phrases are the project's own `departments/video/reel-legal.yaml`, which `department add video` installs; a missing file holds every reel. review → publish needs the reel approved on the shelf and the owner_only publish stamp; publish → done asks for nothing more. `issue-cli reel-legal [<slug>]` prints the findings without moving. A live Video gets the type from `workflow upgrade --department video` (catalog `reel-type`). Docs: docs/departments.md#reels.
- **The payments kit has a consent flow: the 14-day waiver record, the order confirmation, a two-step withdrawal button and a two-step subscription cancel** (factory/reusable-checkout-consent-flow-14-day-withdrawal-waiver-checkbox-confirmation-email-withdrawal-button-and-subscription-cancel-flow-on-the-payments-kits-events; payments report o-20261120-193a, §6 item 9). A `consent:` block in `payments.yaml` turns it on: `GET /v1/consent/texts` gives the checkbox text and its version, a checkout carries `consent: {waiver, text_version}` (required when `required: true`) and answers signed `withdraw_url` / `cancel_subscription_url` links; `payment.paid` carries the record and an order-confirmation message for the app to send; the kit's `/withdraw/<payment>` page is two steps ("Odstąp od umowy tutaj", then "Potwierdź odstąpienie od umowy", Directive 2023/2673 art. 11a) and sends the new `withdrawal.requested` event once with a receipt; `/cancel/<subscription>` is two steps too and `subscription.cancelled` carries a receipt. The kit never mails or refunds by itself. The wording is a placeholder pending the owner's lawyer's review, and `mode: live` refuses it; docs/payments.md names the Polish law status and the re-check date, 2027-04-01.
- **Sales can turn payments on: two payments-kit connectors, a read token, live rules and a `payments` partner slot** (factory/payments-connector-preset-and-catalogue-entry-a-project-enables-payments-with-a-provider-sandboxlive-field-and-keychain-keys-wired-into-the-sales-template; payments report o-20261120-193a, §6 item 3). `department add sales-b2c` writes two connectors commented out beside Umami and SendPortal: `payments`, which uses the kit's new `read_token` and can only look up a payment or the provider (the kit answers 403 to anything else), and `payments-refund`, which uses the api token, sends, and is held until the owner approves the output, on top of the owner-only gate before publish. Both use `keychain:` references only. `payments-kit` gains `payment <id>`, `provider` and `refund <id> --idempotency-key <key> [--amount N]`, which call a running kit with `$PAYMENTS_TOKEN`. `payments-kit check` prints the mode on its first line. In `mode: live` an `env:` key is refused (Keychain items only), and in any mode a `read_token` must not be the same key as `api_token`. The charging block's refund reads the payment through `sales/payments` and refunds through `sales/payments-refund`, with the issue's slug as the key. The partners catalog has a `payments` slot with the kit as its default. Still the stand-in provider only; checkout texts wait for Legal. Docs: docs/payments.md#from-a-department.
- **`spend --context` splits the floor by part, for leads and workers** (cli/spend-context-splits-a-sessions-first-turn-floor-by-part-system-and-tools-claude-md-files-brief-skills-mcp-for-leads-and-workers; the lead floor report o-20261120-07ad, follow-up 5). Under `Workers:` and `Leads:`, under each top session and in one box's view, a line splits the first turn into system prompt, tools (the three biggest named, such as Artifact), MCP, CLAUDE.md files (global, project, memory), skills, brief and other, from the request Claude Code writes before the first answer, scaled to the floor's tokens (an estimate, about ±10%). So the floor before and after a launch or brief change shows which part moved. `--json` gives `floor_parts` with each tool's size. Lengths and names only: no text.
- **The nightly clean holds per area: only the Go cache waits on a go build or test, and an over-cap cache is trimmed in the day** (factory/clean-holds-per-area-not-all-at-once-only-the-go-cache-waits-on-a-running-go-test-and-an-over-cap-cache-is-trimmed-by-day). On 2026-11-21 `issue-cli clean` refused everything with `clean waits: go test is running` while one worker tested, and the Go cache grew from 34 GB to 59.7 GB in a day over its 40 GB cap. Now a go build or test, or a live local CI run, holds only the `gocache` area (a CI run also its project's `worktrees`); transcripts, tmp, snapshots and playtest run beside it, the cache's line says `waits: <why>` and a `waited` line lists the areas. Mem-guard still holds the whole clean. The viewer's tick tries a waiting cache alone until it runs, and in the day trims the cache alone once the hourly disk reading has it over its cap by more than 25%. A clean that has waited more than 3 h is said once on the lead tick: `clean: waits since <time> (<how long>): <hold>`. Docs: docs/workbench-files.md#the-nightly-clean.
- **Leads launch lean like workers: no skill list, no Artifact, Workflow, SendFeedback or ReportFindings tools** (factory/leads-launch-lean-like-workers-artifact-skills-and-workflowsendfeedbackreportfindings-off-a-per-project-leads-tools-list-keeps-one-and-the-floor-shown-before-and-after-df-lead-under-35k). A Claude Code lead (the CEO, department and group leads) starts with a per-session `--settings` turning every skill off (plugins stay on), `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ARTIFACT=1` and the three tools in its one `--disallowedTools` list; its MCP servers and connectors are unchanged. That is about 29k tokens of a 56k-67k first turn that every lead turn re-read (about 119M a week). `leads: tools: [Artifact]` in workflow.yaml keeps one for a project's leads, `leads: setup: full` gives them the whole setup back; lint warns on other values. The spawn's steps print `Lead setup (leads:): …`, and `issue-cli spend --context` has a `Leads:` line. See docs/agent-dispatch.md#lead-setup.
- **A lead's brief carries pins as titles with their ids, and one copy of its state after a flush** (factory/lead-brief-carries-one-state-source-state-file-or-handoff-note-not-both-and-pinned-memories-as-titles-with-their-ids; the lead floor report's levers 5 and 6). A lead brief now shows each pinned memory, lead-scope or project, as `<id> (<kind>) <title>`: its first sentence without the parentheses that hold the owner's quote and source, at most 120 characters, then each later sentence that starts with "Never" whole, so the hard limits stay; `issue-cli memory show <id>` prints the rest. DF's memory block went from 5.8k to 3.2k characters. A worker's briefing keeps its pins whole. After the nightly flush the fresh lead's `## Your state file` leaves out the state's Workers, Queue and Owner asks sections, which the note above it holds (about 2.1k characters on DF's state); a rotate's prompt and a plain spawn already carried one of the two.
- **No disk or memory leaks over a 24/7 run** (factory/no-disk-or-memory-leaks-over-a-247-run-tests-clean-their-temp-dirs-clean-gets-a-tmp-area-health-flags-unrotated-sessions-and-growing-processes-every-leak-source-has-a-bound). On 2026-11-21 `$TMPDIR` held 6.8 GB that no clean covered (4.5 GB of `pt-bins-*` from cmd/playtest's tests, 1.2 GB of make's cover profiles, 53k Chrome entries), and three leads had run 6 to 19 days. Every test binary now runs on a private `TMPDIR` that is removed at exit and fails the binary when a test leaves anything in it. `issue-cli clean` has a `tmp` area: the factory's temp names older than `clean.tmp_hours` (24) and open by no live process (lsof) go; another project's only under `clean.tmp_prefixes`. The nightly flush rotates every department and group lead that ran 20 h, not only the CEO. `issue-cli health`, the Needs you list and the lead tick name a lead past 26 h, a `lead-*` session no project owns, an idle worker holding a slot and a long-lived process (viewer, lead, CI run) whose RSS more than doubled in 24 h, each with the command that rotates, closes or restarts it; nothing is stopped by itself. docs/fs-churn.md lists every place the factory writes or holds memory over time and what bounds it.
- **The factory runs 24/7: the lead tick wakes the lead when a project started nothing for an hour with room to start, in every project** (factory/the-factory-never-idles-overnight-the-tick-wakes-the-lead-when-nothing-started-for-an-hour-with-free-capacity-a-reports-follow-ups-are-news-idle-owner-waiting-sessions-free-their-slot-an-empty-queue-sweeps-itself-in-every-project; owner: "the AI and the factory work 24/7"). On 2026-11-20 two finished reports held both worker slots, the queue was empty and nothing started from 23:00 to 07:49. `lead tick` now says `idle: nothing started for 1h05m (since 23:00) with room to start: <why>: <what to do>` after `leads.idle_minutes` (workflow.yaml, default 60, negative turns it off) and again each such stretch, when the queue is empty, every item is held, auto-start is off or idle sessions fill the cap (not under a machine hold or busy workers). Any worker's `report:` comment is news (`report: <slug>'s report has N follow-ups to file`), and so is a report output on the shelf with no `report:` after it. A finished report before its gate with an open owner box holds no worker slot and the tick closes its idle session; under the worker cap, as under a memory hold, the tick closes idle sessions that wait on the owner and asks the queue again. With the queue empty under YOLO and auto-start, the tick queues another retro sweep once the last one closed, up to 4 a day. Under YOLO the tick also moves a worked issue whose worker has gone through the next gate YOLO passes when `transition --dry-run` says Ready (`moved: <slug> human-testing → done`; never an experiment's, owner_only or design-first gate, never into the work status): on 2026-11-21 eleven finished reports waited at human-testing for up to 4 days. `issue-cli yolo --help` no longer says YOLO never passes the Human Testing approval. Docs: docs/agent-dispatch.md#the-factory-runs-247.
- **Every Marketing department keeps the page rules: `<title>` at most 60 characters and a `charset=utf-8` header, checked by `issue-cli page-check`** (marketing-departments-title-at-most-60-characters-and-charsetutf-8-header-as-a-rule-and-a-check-in-every-project; owner: the title rule belongs in Dark Factory, in every marketing department). A site's six articles had 66–78-character titles with the brand suffix (Bing: "Title too long") and served `text/html` with no charset, so Bing read their OpenGraph as Latin-1. The new `issue-cli page-check <url|sitemap.xml>` GETs each page (a sitemap expanded to its URLs), names each one whose `<title>` is over 60 characters (counted in characters, brand suffix included; none or two also fail) or whose text response lacks `charset=utf-8` in its Content-Type, with the fix, and exits 1; read-only, no project or connector needed, `--json`. The marketing template gets the rule in role.md (`## Page rules`), a page-check box in the piece and seo-pass `## Draft` (so draft → owner review waits for it) and in the seo-baseline's Technical basics, and the weekly SEO pass runs page-check on every sitemap and opens one fix per site. A live Marketing gets it through `department update` and the new catalog entry `page-rules` (`workflow upgrade --department marketing`).
- **A payments kit every project can plug in, working end to end on a stand-in provider** (factory/payments-module-abstraction-one-provider-neutral-interface-checkout-subscription-webhook-events-refund-invoice-hook-that-every-project-can-plug-in-working-end-to-end-on-a-stand-in-provider; owner: "we want the implementation of the abstraction, and of how it can work, to happen now. We'll plug some provider in later"). `payments-kit serve` runs beside a project's app, whatever its stack. The app opens hosted checkouts (one-off or subscription), looks up payments, refunds and cancels over local HTTP. It gets each provider event once, as `payment.paid`, `payment.failed`, `payment.refunded`, `subscription.renewed` or `subscription.cancelled`, signed by the kit. Amounts are in minor units, methods are named the same for every provider (`card`, `blik`, `pay_by_link`, `bank_transfer`, `wallet`), and an invoice hook (`none`, `record`) is where a merchant-of-record or KSeF invoice attaches. Webhooks are checked for signature and age. A repeated one is sent to the app once, also after a restart, and an app that is down gets the event on the provider's retry. No card data passes through the kit (hosted checkout only), and `payments.yaml` takes keys only as `env:`/`keychain:` references. The stand-in provider has a hosted page, signed webhooks, refunds and decline modes. A real provider is one adapter that passes `paymentstest.Contract`. Build it with `go build ./cmd/payments-kit`. Docs: docs/payments.md.

- Public CI is green again on Linux: the crew tests' stand-in panes run `exec sleep`, since Ubuntu's `/bin/sh` (dash) kept `#{pane_current_command}` at `sh` and five crew tests timed out on v0.64.0.

## v0.64.0 — 2026-11-20
- **`create` warns when a done issue may already hold the new one** (cli/create-warns-when-a-new-issue-shares-its-sweep-source-and-item-number-or-most-of-its-title-with-a-done-issue, from retro sweep o-20261120-3387 item N4): when the brief names the same sweep source and item (an output id, `report <hex>` or the report issue's slug; `item N` or `follow-up N`), or the title shares at least 5 words and half of the shorter title's, `create` lists the done issues and the `close <new> --fixed-by <done>` line. It never refuses. Queue items 444 and 445 re-filed shipped follow-ups this way.
- **A criterion only the installed build can prove no longer holds testing** (workflow/a-box-that-can-only-be-proven-after-the-install-is-listed-by-criteria-proven-instead-of-holding-testing-and-the-leads-check-runs-after-the-install-briefing-text-first). Such a criterion ("the installed issue-cli works in every project", a number measured after the change reached agents) could not be proven before landing: a worker never installs and the lead installs only main. It held testing → human-testing, cost a lead round, and once an unlanded branch build was installed to prove it. The in design prompt now has the criterion name its check, `(after install: <command>)`, with T0 and T1 for a measure-after window; the testing prompt and the `criteria_proven` hint record it with `prove <slug> <AC> --value "after install: <command>"` and a provisional decision, so the gate lists it as reported; and the lead addendum has the lead, after installing main, find them with `issue-cli search "after install:"`, run each and post a `tests:` comment. Text only: an engine-read `(after landing)` marker is the owner's experiment. docs/Workflow/proof.md#proof-after-the-install.
- **A bare `append --section X --replace` names the command that replaces a section** (cli/a-refused-append-section-x-replace-names-the-command-that-works-replace-section-name-body-file-f, from retro sweep o-20261120-3387, item N2: 7 post-mortems and retros). `append --replace` swaps one line and takes that line's text, so an agent replacing a whole section with `append --section "Test Plan" --replace --body-file plan.md` got `flag needs an argument: --replace` and the help pointer. That call, `--replace` last, and an empty `--replace ""` now refuse (exit 2, nothing written) with `issue-cli replace <slug> --section 'Test Plan' --body-file <file>` for the whole section and the one-line `append --replace '<old line>' --body '<new line>'` form, with the call's slug and section filled in.
- **`issue-cli affected` and `attach` take a worker's own slug** (cli/issue-cli-affected-and-attach-take-the-slug-from-the-workers-session-like-the-other-worker-commands-and-the-not-both-message-says-none-given-when-none-was-given). In a dispatched worker's session (`ISSUE_VIEWER_ISSUE_SLUG`), `affected` with neither a slug nor `--files` reads the worker's own issue, and `attach <file> --section X` attaches to it (a first word that names no issue is a file, as with `evidence add`). Before, `affected` refused and `attach` took the file for the slug. `affected` with nothing given now says `none given`; `not both` only when both a slug and `--files` were given.
- **The header's agents box counts the agents waiting on you** (ui/headers-agents-box-shows-0-working-0-idle-0-waiting-on-an-agent-while-every-live-worker-waits-on-the-owner). With every live worker at an owner gate or with a question, the box read 0 working, 0 idle, 0 waiting on an agent while the floor drew them as live robots. It now also says `N waiting on you` with an orange dot (`map.agents.you`), on the map and every project page, so its numbers add up to the live agents.
- **A lead's background tick that a signal ends says so, and loses none of the lines it held** (cli/lead-tick-watch-observe-background-bash-dies-with-exit-144-and-no-output). A department lead's `lead tick --watch --exit-on-news --observe` ended twice with `exit code 144` and an empty output; the starts and status moves it held for the next news went with it, and nothing said them again. Reinstalling issue-cli was not the cause (a running tick outlives `make install`; neither stop came near an install): a signal from outside issue-cli ended it. A `--watch` tick now catches SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP, prints what it held and `lead tick: stopped by SIGTERM (pid N); start it again with Bash run_in_background:` with its command, and exits 128+n. Under `--exit-on-news` it keeps what it holds in the lead's store on each tick, so a tick killed without a word (SIGKILL) hands those lines to the lead's next tick (`lead tick: the tick before this one (pid N) ended without a word …; it held:`). The lead's next call says a stopped tick at once (`the last one (pid N) was stopped by SIGTERM at 00:14`), and a tick gone without a word as `killed, or the session that ran it ended` instead of blaming the session alone. A new tick that stops an older one waits for it to go before it records itself (docs/patterns/lead-session.md#waiting).
- **Less file churn on the Mac: local CI skips an unchanged install, worktrees share node_modules, landed worktrees go at once** (factory/less-file-churn-on-the-mac-ci-skips-npm-ci-when-the-lock-file-is-unchanged-worktrees-share-node-modules-worktrees-go-right-after-merge-fseventsd-at-80-cpu-3-1-gb-journal; fseventsd at 80% CPU with a 3.1 GB journal). Local CI skips a `ci.run` entry that is one install command (`npm ci`, `pnpm install`, `yarn`, `bun install`) while the lock file, package.json, the command and `node --version` match the stamp of the last install (`ci run --reinstall` forces it): in an npm workspace project 29k rewritten files per main move become 0. Every worktree DF makes (dispatch, `worktree add`, `dispatch --fresh`, the critic) links each `node_modules` of the main checkout while the lock file is the same; `worktree deps [--own|--shared]` shows, copies (APFS clone) or relinks them, and `worktree_deps: own` turns it off. No install writes through a link at any depth: ship, `ci.run`, `prove --run` and `worktree_setup` skip it or clone the tree first. A worker-made `.worktrees/<slug>` goes at close and at the lead's tick once its branch is on main, also without `worktree: true`. A test issue-viewer exits with its parent (`ISSUE_VIEWER_EXIT_WITH_PID`), and `doctor leaks` names an orphan one. `scripts/fs-churn.sh` measures it; docs/fs-churn.md writes up a journal-free APFS volume for the owner to decide on.
- **`lead tick --exit-on-news` no longer wakes the lead again for the same hold** (factory/lead-tick-exit-on-news-wakes-the-lead-on-every-tick-with-the-same-hold-nothing-ready-to-start-line). With load near its line, ticks switched between a quiet load hold and `hold: nothing ready to start (first: …)`, and each switch said the same line again as news, waking the lead's whole context. The tick state now keeps the hold said last (`last_hold`): back with the same reason and first item, it is held and printed once above the next news. A different reason or first item is news once; a start forgets it.
- **One version is supported: no patch for a release older than the newest published one** (factory/one-supported-version-status-and-release-stop-offering-patches-on-release-branches-older-than-the-newest-published-release; owner: "We support 1 version"). Waiting for release still offered `Patch v0.62.1 on release/v0.62` after v0.63.0 was out. Now status, `status --json` and the card show no candidate older than the newest published release tag, and `release cut`, `release pick` and `release publish` refuse an older version (pick: an older release branch, and a leftover older release worktree no longer makes pick ask for `--release`), naming the rule. docs/roadmap.md, Releases, says so.
- **A smaller floor in every worker turn: CLAUDE.md from 19.4k to 4.7k characters, the briefing trimmed** (factory/shrink-the-worker-floor-text-project-claude-md-halved-and-the-briefing-trimmed-the-floor-measured-before-and-after, from the token cost sweep). The Validation section and the UI checks moved word for word to the new docs/testing.md, and the rest of CLAUDE.md points at the docs that hold it; `TestClaudeMdStaysSmall` now caps it at 8 KB. The worker briefing says the same rules in fewer words (15.6k to 14.3k characters on the same issue). About 4.7k tokens off a fresh worker's 43k floor, an estimate until the next worker on main measures it.
- **Fewer turns per worker issue: a measured pilot** (factory/fewer-turns-per-worker-issue-a-measured-pilot-batched-reads-one-line-refusals-turns-per-issue-before-and-after, from the token cost sweep o-20261118-1f67, lever #2). Tweak and bugfix workers took 54.9 turns per issue over 2026-11-11 → 11-18, each re-reading the whole context; a read-only look-up straight after another was the most common repeat turn (2.78 per issue) and a `transition --dry-run` the next one we could cut (0.86). The briefing's `## Reading code and docs` now asks for a step's reads in one turn (one command over several files, or several tool calls in one message), and a dry run's text ends with `Skip --dry-run next time: …` (not in `--json`, not on a real call or a refusal). `spend --turns` shows `DRY RUNS` and `FOLLOW-ON READS` (`dry_runs`, `follow_on_reads`: a read turn straight after another of the same session) per issue and in the total, so the after window is the same command.
- **`append --section` puts a body's sub-headings under the ones the section already has** (cli/append-section-onto-an-existing-sub-heading-appends-under-it-or-warns-instead-of-writing-a-duplicate-heading, from report 3f16, cause 8: ~37 retros). `append --section "Test Plan"` with `### Automated` and `### Manual` blocks, on a Test Plan that has both, wrote both headings a second time at the section's end. Now a block whose sub-heading the section holds at that level goes at the end of that sub-heading's text, a new sub-heading still goes at the section's end, text before the first sub-heading goes before the section's first one, and a block already there word for word is skipped; the auto-routed form (`--body "## Test Plan\n### Manual\n…"`) does the same. The success line names the sub-headings it merged into, and an append that still writes a heading twice in one place (a sibling section's `## Notes` in a `--section Design` body) says so on stderr.
- **A new issue-cli command learns every list it must join from one failing test** (cli/a-new-issue-cli-commands-checklist-one-test-names-every-registry-it-must-join-commandaccess-readsamples-unknown-flag-test-at-once, from report 3f16, cause 13: ~15 retros). `commandAccess`, `readSamples` and the Commands-table row each had their own test, and the readSamples one stayed quiet until commandAccess had the entry, so a worker found them one red run at a time. `TestEveryCommandJoinsEveryRegistry` (guard-check) now names every missing entry in one error per command, with the file, what to add, the `guardVerbs` rule for a guarded verb and a `-run` line for the tests every command must pass (`commandWideTests`; `TestCommandWideTestsListsEveryTestOverAllCommands` keeps it complete). The old tests keep their stale-entry checks as `TestCommandAccessNamesOnlyCommands` and `TestCommandsTableNamesOnlyCommands`.
- **Pitfalls for in progress and shipping fit the step's memory budget again, so leak-check and guard-test pitfalls show when they matter** (factory/pitfall-memories-carry-an-at-status-where-it-matters-leak-check-at-shipping-so-the-step-that-needs-them-shows-them): the two shipping leak-check pitfalls are one now and the guard-test pitfall is tied to in progress; docs/memory.md says to sum a status's memories before tying one more there.
- **A refused transition names every unmet requirement, not just the first** (workflow/transition-says-in-one-call-which-boxes-with-ids-are-missing-and-the-briefing-stops-suggesting-transition-to-see-whats-missing, from report 3f16, cause 5: ~43 retros). The engine stopped at the first failing rule, so a move gated on two box sections refused on I2, then again on TP1: one round per rule. The refusal now ends with `Also unmet for this move (n more):` and the rest, each with its box ids and fix, as the dry run lists them, and the `--check` one-call line ticks the boxes of all of them. The stage briefing stops saying `--dry-run lists what is still unmet`: it says to run the move with `--check`, since its refusal lists everything.
- **The nightly lead flush starts the lead again after it closes it, so its tick and the queue don't stop overnight** (factory/lead-flush-starts-a-fresh-lead-after-it-closes-one-so-the-tick-and-the-queue-dont-stop-overnight). A 23:30 flush closed a project's lead and nothing started a new one: the tick died with the session and the queue stood still until the morning. `issue-cli lead flush close` now records a handover in the lead's state file, closes the session, stops the old tick if it outlived it and starts the same lead again; the fresh lead's first prompt carries the note and the summary, `## Your state file` and `## Restart these watches` with the lead's tick. A start that fails after the close says so with the owner's `lead spawn`. `lead spawn` still refuses every worker; the flush starts only the lead it closed. A new `lead tick --watch` stops any older tick of the same lead that still runs, so a lead never ticks twice. Moving the flush back to its night slot is the owner's call.
- **Workers start without the Workflow, SendFeedback, ReportFindings and ListAgents tools** (factory/workers-start-without-the-built-in-tools-they-never-use-artifact-workflow-sendfeedback-reportfindings-listagents-with-the-floor-measured-before-and-after, from report 1f67). Their schemas sit in every turn of a worker (about 10k tokens of a 55k first turn) and workers made no call to them in 3 days. A lean worker now starts with `--disallowedTools=Workflow,SendFeedback,ReportFindings,ListAgents`, in the one list beside its connectors' denied tools; Artifact stays off by env as before. `workers: tools:` keeps any of the five by name, and lint names any other. Leads, codex and `setup: full` keep everything; Agent and ScheduleWakeup stay for workers, which call them.
- **The lead tick says a queue-low sweep's `report:` and a worker's `question:` as news** (factory/the-lead-tick-wakes-the-lead-when-a-queue-low-sweeps-report-lands-so-its-proposals-are-filed-before-the-queue-runs-dry). On 2026-11-18 a sweep's report landed at 02:01 and a worker asked the lead at 00:31; `lead tick --watch --exit-on-news` said neither, the queue ran dry at 02:35 and nothing started until 07:30. The tick now says, once per comment, `report: <sweep>: …: file its proposals …` for a `report:` on an open run of the queue-sweep job, and `question: <slug>: …: answer it …` for an open issue whose newest comment is a worker's `question:` (not the owner's or a lead's); both wake the watch (docs/patterns/lead-session.md#what-counts-as-news).
- **A timing test no longer turns main red on a saturated machine alone** (factory/timing-check-fails-a-page-test-when-another-project-saturates-the-cpu-teststatuspagefastonabigproject-red-at-9dead35-at-load-100-on-14-cores-green-on-a-rerun). At load 65.8 on 14 cores a warm /status measured 3.76× its /board baseline, best of 8, against Ratio 3: a heavier request loses more to a saturated CPU than its light baseline. `timing.Check` now reads the 1-minute load once the operation misses its limit and doubles Ratio above the core count (`SaturatedSlack`), naming the load in its result; a /status 5× slower (11.9× in that run) still fails. 4 of the last 10 reds on main were timing tests.
- **`issue-cli spend --kinds` and `--turns` measure where the tokens go: by project and kind of session, and each issue's worker turns by cause** (factory/issue-cli-spend-measures-every-place-tokens-go-per-project-and-kind-of-session-critics-sweeps-flush-and-bench-named-turns-split-by-cause-prices-and-a-weekly-limit-share). `--kinds [--all-projects] [--days N | --since/--until]` prints sessions, turns, tokens, est. USD, share and % of the weekly limit per project × kind (workers by type and stage, leads, subagents, post-mortems, critics, sweeps, lead-flush and other jobs, benchmark, reviews, yours); prices show as assumed or confirmed (`confirmed: true` in prices.yaml) and `weekly_limit: {usd|tokens: N}` fills % WEEK. `--turns [<slug>]` splits worker turns into transition (and refusals), validate, single-file read, red round and rest. Both `--json` for a before/after measurement (docs/workflow-stats.md). Critic and benchmark sessions now start named (`critic-<issue>`, `bench-<task>`).
- **Docs and report workers start on Sonnet on every start path, held by a test** (factory/docs-and-report-workers-still-run-on-opus-although-workflow-yaml-routes-them-to-sonnet). Report 1f67 counted Opus tokens on docs and report workers. Measured on the transcripts, every one started since the routing change (746bf783) ran on claude-sonnet-5-5; the Opus ones started before it, or were lead sessions that quote a report issue's `Type:` line. `TestDocsAndReportWorkersStartOnSonnetOnEveryPath` now holds dispatch, queue start, auto-start, jobs (`DispatchSpec`) and takeover (`TakeoverSpec`) to the repo's own `workflow.yaml` routing.
- **`issue-cli cms up` stands a project's Ghost up in one owner command, and "Stand up the CMS" walks the go-live** (factory/stand-up-a-projects-cms-ghost-in-one-owner-command-and-the-go-live-walk-through, from the CMS spike). Marketing's `cms` connector had a helper but no Ghost: the owner would write a compose, click through Ghost's setup, make an integration and copy its key into Keychain. Now `cms up`, under `mem-guard check`, writes a loopback compose outside any repo (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/ai-workflow-engine/cms/<project>/`: `restart: unless-stopped`, the update check, Gravatar and RPC ping off, mail pointed where nothing listens, device verification off), starts it, sets Ghost up with no browser, puts a random owner password and the integration's Admin API key straight into Keychain (`ghost-owner`, `ghost-admin-key`, account the project; on stdin, never printed, in argv or a file) and turns outbound link tagging off. A second run changes nothing that is right (an edited compose is kept, the integration reused). It writes no config: the owner uncomments the connector. `department add marketing` prints it, from the Ghost row's new `setup:` in the partners catalog. A worker is refused. docs/stand-up-the-cms.md has the sign-in and the go-live steps, all the owner's: upstream's MySQL 8 compose, the site `url:`, TLS, DNS, real mail, moving the key.
- **Marketing's `piece` and `seo-pass` publish to the site through the `cms` connector and record its URL** (factory/marketing-piece-and-seo-pass-publish-write-through-the-cms-connector-and-record-ghosts-url, from the CMS spike). Publish said "where the brief says" and named no tool. Now `piece` has its own brief (it names `site (cms)` when the department has the connector), draft (the markdown that goes out, on the shelf, with no `cms` call) and publish (`connector exec <department>/cms -- sh departments/<department>/cms-ghost.sh publish --file <the approved file> …`, the URL it prints to `output publish --url` and `## Published`); `seo-pass` publish reads an existing page with `get --slug` before `update --id` and publishes new posts, one `output publish` per output. With no `cms` connector both name the approved file and ask the owner for the link in an `answer:` comment. A live Marketing gets it by a splice (docs/departments.md, "Bringing a template change into a live department"); `workflow upgrade` proposes the new catalog entry `cms-publish`.
- **Marketing can publish to a Ghost site: a commented `cms` connector, its helper `cms-ghost.sh` and a `cms` partner slot** (factory/marketing-a-cms-connector-for-ghost-its-publish-helper-and-a-cms-partner-slot, from the CMS spike). `department add marketing` now installs `departments/<key>/cms-ghost.sh` beside `role.md`, our own POSIX sh over Ghost's Admin API (curl, openssl, jq). Its `publish`, `update` and `get` calls each print one JSON line `{id, status, url}`, the url for `output publish --url`. publish puts a piece out at once, or scheduled with `--at`, never as a draft, and never with `?newsletter=`. update reads `updated_at` first. The file goes out byte for byte as one markdown card: the helper reads the card back and sends nothing when it differs. The 5-minute HS256 token's HMAC is computed over stdin, so the admin key is on no command line. Marketing's connector examples gain `cms` (`kind: tool`, `sh` on the helper, `GHOST_ADMIN_KEY: keychain:ghost-admin-key`, `sends`, `needs_approved_output`, no `per_run:`). The partners catalog gains a `cms` slot: Ghost by default, with Payload, Strapi, Decap CMS and WordPress listed. Marketing's `partner_slots:` gains `cms`. The CI images install jq.
- **A gate's `hint:` now shows on `criteria_*`, `approved_for`, the decision rules and `critique`** (legacy-validate-rules-criteria-and-decisions-show-an-actions-hint-on-failure). Their failures kept their own fix text, so a `hint:` set on them was never read. Now each keeps what is wrong (the unproven criteria, the uncited refs, the critic's blocks findings) and puts the hint in place of its fix, as the checkbox and comment rules already did; with no hint the text is unchanged, and `approved_for` is still an approval error to the code that checks for one. The repo's `criteria_proven` gate gets a hint saying a criterion box ticks with `issue-cli prove`, never `check`. `commits_reference_issue` and `handoff_answered` still keep their own text (lint's `hints` check names a hint set on them).
- **A vitest title with spaces proves without quotes, and `prove --run` finds it beside a shorter name** (cli/prove-test-with-a-test-name-containing-spaces-polish-vitest-names-e-g). `Proves: AC1 kuchnia z daniami prawie tylko na obiad …` without quotes became one test link per word. The words after the criterion ids are now one test when a test file on the branch has the whole phrase (else one per word, as before), and the commit-msg note names the quoted form. `prove --run` in a project without go.mod looked names up with `git grep -o`, which prints only the longest match on a line, so a name sitting inside another linked title's line read `not found`; it now reads whole lines, as the proof check does.
- **A string flag with no value fails with `flag needs an argument`** (cli/issue-cli-check-script-new-with-no-name-fails-with-script-name-use). A value flag written last, before `--`, or before another flag of the command took the `--` that ends the flags, or that next flag, as its value: `check-script --new` failed with `script name "--"`, and `check-script --new --yes` with `script name "--yes"`. Every command now refuses it with `flag needs an argument: --new` (exit 2). A word that only looks like a flag and names none of the command's (`-5`) is still a value.
- **`issue-cli prompt-cell <slug>` says which prompt cells a proposal reaches, and `experiment run --baseline <run>` pins a baseline when main moves** (factory/issue-cli-prompt-cell-proposal-shows-which-workflow-yaml-prompt-cell-an-experiment-reaches-with-a-baseline-pin-that-survives-a-main-move, from the 7da6 sweep). A type's own status prompt replaces the base one, so an edit to the base `in progress` prompt never reaches a tweak task: the 0b05 plan found by hand that the guard-tests proposal reaches 2 of its 6 tasks. `prompt-cell` reads workflow.yaml at main and at the proposal's branch (or takes `--cell <status>` / `--cell <type>:<status>`), lists each edited (type · status) cell with whose text it is, checks the benchmark's tasks (or the proposal's `## Bench tasks`) and ends `Reach: N of M tasks`, each missed task with why. Read-only; `--json`. `experiment run --baseline <run>` uses a finished run's commit in place of main for the guard and the diff and never runs it again, so one baseline serves every candidate cut from it; a branch that merged main's later commits is refused. The plan prints the Reach line, and the "does not contain main" refusal names a run to pin.
- **The page speed tests ride out load, and the guard catches a hand-rolled retry loop** (factory/testsystemspagefastonabigproject-flakes-on-main-under-load-move-it-and-its-siblings-onto-timing-check). `TestSystemsPageFastOnABigProject` failed on main under load with its best warm time (282 ms) under its 300 ms limit: each of its 4 rounds failed when 3 tries in a row missed. It and the Stats, Health, Company and Retros page tests, plus the prove-link and cold-show CLI tests, now go through `timing.Check`. `TestEveryTimeLimitGoesThroughTheHelper` now counts a limit with a fixed floor (`max(300ms, k×base)`) as fixed and catches any sample a loop takes this round and holds to such a limit; polls and pure ratios still pass.
- **`memory show`, the consolidate Unhelpful list and the memory page's red chip show the status mix behind a score** (factory/memory-show-and-the-unhelpful-list-show-the-status-mix-so-an-empty-score-is-visible-before-anyone-forgets-a-memory, from the f9bc sweep). A "no help" score was one number: nothing said whether its stages were at a status where a lift could show. `memory show` now names where the unscored stages were (`· 20 not scored (statuses that nearly always pass first try: idea 18, testing 2)`), each memory on `consolidate`'s Unhelpful list carries a `stages: scored: in progress 15 · not scored: idea 20` line, and the memory page's red usefulness chip says the same in its tooltip. `memory list --json` carries the counts as `usefulness.no_room_at`.
- **Memory usefulness is scored only at statuses where a lift can show** (factory/memory-usefulness-is-scored-only-at-statuses-where-a-lift-is-possible-not-20-no-help-stages-at-idea, from the f9bc sweep). 80% of injections land at `idea`, where stages pass first try about 100% of the time, so every memory's lift there is 0 by construction: all 87 measured memories read "no help" and had their strength halved. Now a status is scored only when its baseline is below 0.95 and at least 3 of its visits failed first try; injections elsewhere are counted, not scored (the window, the 8 and 15 thresholds and the per-status scores skip them), and `memory show` says `· 20 not scored (statuses that nearly always pass first try)`. `memory list --json` carries them as `usefulness.no_room`.
- **The `Catalog: n/a` line can be recorded before the commit, and `Catalog: <id>` names a bumped entry** (cli/the-commit-hook-accepts-catalog-id-for-a-bump-and-catalog-check-isnt-red-before-the-first-commit, from the 7da6 sweep). `make validate` on an uncommitted change was red at catalog-check until a commit carried its n/a line, so workers ran it twice. `issue-cli workflow catalog-na <id>[,<id>] <why>` records the line in the checkout's own git dir: catalog-check counts it while the change is uncommitted, and the commit-msg hook writes it into the next commit and drops the record (with everything committed and the record left, it counts for nothing and catalog-check says to paste it). A bare `Catalog: <id>` after a `catalog-bump` is accepted by the hook and catalog-check when the entry is bumped past the last release, and refused with the `catalog-bump` to run when it isn't.
- **Every command run by a stale `./issue-cli` says so** (cli/every-issue-cli-command-warns-once-when-a-worktrees-issue-cli-is-older-than-cmd-or-internal, from the 7da6 sweep). Workers' checks ran old code without a word: only `check-script --yes` compared the branch build with its code. Now a branch build (`issue-cli` at the root of a checkout holding `cmd/issue-cli`) older than the newest commit or Go source under `cmd/` and `internal/` ends every call with one stderr line, `issue-cli: warning: this ./issue-cli is older than its code (built …, <what> changed …): this call ran the old code; rebuild: …`. The call, its stdout and exit code are unchanged; the check runs beside the command, and the installed build never prints it. The briefing's *Your issue-cli build* section says so.
- **`make test` runs cmd/issue-cli in four shards and internal/bench in three, with one bench smoke run fewer** (factory/rebalance-the-cmdissue-cli-shards-and-internalbench-so-the-unit-wall-goes-under-100-s, from the 7da6 sweep). The three cmd/issue-cli shards were already even (97 to 106 s each). The 37 s smoke test takes 7 to 13 s on a quiet machine. The suite uses about 7 of the 14 cores and spends more system time than user time, so spawning processes, not the split, holds the wall. `TEST_SHARDS` defaults to 4 and internal/bench takes 3 shards. The `--keep` smoke test is folded into the broken-harness one, which drops one whole fixture run. Fresh `make test` on this Mac: 100 to 107 s against 102 to 105 s before. Under 100 s holds only on a quiet machine.
- **Tests with a time limit share one timing helper** (factory/one-shared-timing-helper-for-absolute-limit-tests-best-of-n-rounds-against-a-cheaper-baseline-used-by-the-bench-memory-ceo-switcher-ci-connectors-and-playtest-tests, from the 7da6 sweep). Nine tests timed one sample against a fixed limit, so a burst of load on that sample could fail them. `internal/timing`'s `Check.Run` times the operation, and when it misses its quiet-machine limit times a cheaper baseline right after, over up to 8 rounds (3 for timeouts), passing on the best of each: under the limit, or under Ratio× the baseline once three baselines are in (`Max` caps that for a timeout check, under the time the uncut command takes). The bench wall cap, the three memory 5,000-item checks, the CEO switcher's hung capture, ci's command timeout, the connector check timeout, playtest's `waitHTTP` budget, the commit-msg leak scan and the status page's `statusTooSlow` use it. None of them had failed in the 51 recorded local CI runs: this keeps them from starting.
- **A guard test stops a new single-sample timing test** (factory/a-guard-test-fails-a-test-that-times-a-single-sample-against-a-fixed-limit-after-the-shared-timing-helper-lands, from the 7da6 sweep). `TestEveryTimeLimitGoesThroughTheHelper` (internal/timing, run by `make guard-check`) reads every `_test.go` and fails one that holds a single timed sample (`time.Since`, `timing.Of` or a timer helper's result) to a fixed limit, outside a loop or in a loop whose round fails on its own sample, naming the file:line and the fix: `timing.Check`, or a `// one sample: <why>` comment. Poll deadlines and rounds that pass on their best are left alone. It found three on main: the merge-check cost test and the cold stats request now go through `timing.Check`, and a lower bound in the helper's own test is waived.
- **The storage card's volume is the data volume, and says so** (factory/health-reads-the-data-volume-not-the-storage-card-says-15-while-the-data-volume-is-at-93). Report 7da6 read `df /` (macOS's sealed system volume, a few percent) as the card's figure; the card already reads the volume holding Go's build cache, the Data volume. `storage.volume_used_pct`'s help line now names it, and a test pins that no disk reading statfs's `/`.
- **Builds use go1.27.4, so `make security-check` passes govulncheck again** (release/make-security-check-fails-on-govulncheck-go-2026-6859-nethttp-in-go1-27-3-fixed-in-go1-27-4). govulncheck reported GO-2026-6859 in go1.27.3's net/http, reached from the web push sender, and `make security-check` exited 1 after its barriers held. go.mod now names `toolchain go1.27.4` (the `go 1.25` line is unchanged), so `go` with the default `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` and public CI build with the fixed net/http, and released binaries carry it. docs/API/security.md says how to raise the line for the next standard-library advisory.
- **`TestCrewAllListsTwoProjects` no longer goes red under load** (factory/testcrewallliststwoprojects-fails-main-under-load-the-stand-in-pane-isnt-seen-as-working-before-crew-is-read). Its stand-in `sleep 600` session was read before the pane's shell had exec'd `sleep`, so the Crew page showed the worker as exited ("only a shell is left"). The crew tests now wait until each stand-in pane runs its command.
- **The suite no longer goes red from 23:20 to midnight on `TestLeadTickNamesAGreenBranchWaitingToMerge`** (factory/testleadticknamesagreenbranchwaitingtomerge-fails-from-2320-to-midnight-its-fixtures-head-is-the-real-clock). Its fixture committed at the real clock and the tick 40 minutes later crossed midnight; the fixture now pins its commit dates at midday.
- **A nightly clean keeps the disk down** (factory/a-nightly-clean-job-keeps-the-disk-down-go-cache-cap-transcripts-over-14-days-health-snapshots-merged-worktrees-and-playtest-runs). The data volume reached 93% on 2026-11-17 with a 263 GB Go build cache that nothing trimmed. `issue-cli clean` now runs once for the machine: the Go cache's oldest entries go until it is under 40 GB, agent transcripts (`*.jsonl`, never `memory/`) older than 14 days go, `.health/snapshots` keep 7 days in full and one a day before that, and merged worktrees and playtest runs are pruned. The viewer starts it every night at 04:00 and early while the volume is over 85% used; it waits while a local CI run or a go build or test is live, or mem-guard says no (live workers don't hold it: only the oldest entries go, as in Go's own trim). One report line per area with the MB freed and the volume before and after; the Needs you disk row and `storage.clean_freed_mb` show the last clean. `clean:` in projects.yaml sets it (`off`, `at`, `gocache_cap_gb`, `transcript_days`, `snapshot_days`), `clean_off: true` leaves a project out; `--dry-run` shows what would go.

## v0.63.0 — 2026-11-17
- **A report waiting on the owner's review holds no worker slot** (factory/a-finished-report-waiting-at-human-testing-doesnt-hold-a-worker-slot-idle-report-sessions-stalled-the-queue-twice-on-11-17). Idle report sessions at human-testing held 3 of 4 slots twice on 2026-11-17, about 2 hours each. Now the worker cap doesn't count a session whose issue is past its `has_output` gate with a good output on the shelf; `lead tick` and `agents sweep` close that idle session, the tick prints `closed: agent-<slug> (… its output <id> "<title>" is on the shelf …)` and posts a `session: closed` comment, the Crew page says why on the stopped worker, and a re-dispatch's briefing gets *Resuming after a closed session*.
- **The in-progress prompt says first that a criterion box ticks with `prove`, not `check`** (workflow/the-in-progress-guidance-says-at-the-top-that-a-criterion-box-ticks-with-prove-not-check, from the 376 sweep). Workers ran `check` on an acceptance criterion, were refused, and met the proof link only later. The first lines of the `in progress` prompt (workflow.yaml and the development template) now name `issue-cli prove` and `prove --list`.
- **`experiment run` and an experiment's page show what the run will cost** (factory/experiment-run-dry-run-and-the-experiment-page-show-the-cost-preview-tokens-hours-usd-or-prices-unset-and-a-reused-baseline, from the 390 sweep). The owner decides to spend on every run, and the only estimate (≈ 210M tokens, 8–10 h for baseline and candidate) was hand-written in one proposal's comment. `experiment run` (and `--dry-run`) now ends its plan with `Cost: ≈ 80M tokens, 4.0 h, prices.yaml unset`, counting both runs, or only the candidate when the baseline on main's sha is reused (the line under it says which). The numbers are the benchmark's newest finished run's mean per task × its task count, the same as `bench run`'s estimate (`bench.ForecastRun`), priced with today's `prices.yaml`; a missing price or unread usage says so, never $0. With no benchmark or no finished run it says there is no estimate. An experiment issue with no verdict yet shows the same as a **Cost preview** row in its Fields card.
- **`issue-cli bench smoke` proves the benchmark harness before a real run** (factory/a-bench-smoke-runs-one-fixture-task-end-to-end-with-a-stand-in-agent-so-the-harness-is-proved-before-a-real-run, from the 390 sweep). The fixture's three tasks run through `bench run`'s code with the issue-cli under test (or `--setup`), a stand-in worker that applies each known solution, and a stand-in judge and scorer: no model, no tokens, about ten seconds. Nine checks (fixture, run, stages, trace, usage, calls, isolation, report, control) end in `bench smoke: PASS` or `FAIL (<check>)`, exit 1; the control makes sure a worker that changes nothing still fails. A PASS writes `smoke.json` in the bench folder, and `bench run` and `bench` print `smoke: PASS <date> on <version>` or say none was run for this issue-cli.
- **`issue-cli stats --spec-flow` and a Spec flow section on the Stats page** (factory/issue-cli-stats-spec-flow-and-a-stats-page-section-critic-findings-held-or-overridden-clarify-answered-or-assumed-red-rounds-post-mortem-actions). Counts what the spec-flow pieces leave on disk: critique runs (findings by severity, and for each run whose blocks held a gate how it was cleared: fixed, overridden, held again, still holds), clarify rounds (owner-answered, assumed, open), red-round questions and post-mortem actions (applied, refused, overtaken, waiting). `--days N` (default 30) or `--all`, `--json`. New critique runs record how long the critic took (`took_ms`), and their comment says `took …`; older runs show "—". Plain `issue-cli stats` ends with a `Spec flow:` line.
- **The chore prompt no longer says "question and stop" under YOLO, and a guard test holds every status prompt to it** (workflow/chore-guidance-no-longer-says-question-and-stop-under-yolo-with-a-guard-that-no-status-guidance-says-stop-without-the-yolo-exception, from the 376 sweep). A chore that needs code or the owner's review now names it in a `question:` comment and closes with the steps done; `TestNoStatusGuidanceStopsWithoutTheYOLOException` fails on any status prompt that still says stop once the YOLO filter has run, unless it is listed with a reason.
- **Playtest scenarios generate retros and build a release branch with rc tags** (factory/playtest-scenarios-can-generate-retros-and-a-release-branch-with-rc-tags, from the 376 sweep). A pager over 30+ retros needed 30+ committed fixtures, and no scenario could tag a commit only a release branch has, so the release card's ux-test box went N/A. Now a `generate:` entry with `kind: retro` writes that many retros into the project's `retros/` (an hour apart, Date line and mtime alike, so the order holds on every run), and a branch in `branches:` takes its own `tags:`, made after its commits on the branch tip by default. New scenarios `retro-pager` (67 to sort, 33 processed) and `release-branch` (release/v1.5 in its worktree, rc.1 on a picked fix, rc.2 on the tip) come with sessions of the same names; docs/patterns/playtest/web-app.md documents both keys.
- **`check-script --yes` on a report says no scripts are needed, and `check-edit` takes its text after the id** (cli/check-script-yes-on-a-report-says-no-scripts-are-needed-and-check-edit-takes-its-text-positionally, from the 376 sweep). Workers on a report ran `check-script --yes` because the YOLO line told them to, got the `--new` how-to, and read it as a duty. Now an issue whose type's path runs no guided checks gets `✓ No check scripts needed for <slug>: its type report (…) has no status that runs guided checks. Nothing to run.` (exit 0; `--list` says `none needed`), and the YOLO line names `check-script --yes` only on a path that runs them. `check-edit <slug> D3 "new text"` works like `--text`; giving both is a usage error.
- **`create` keeps a private project's name out of a new issue's slug** (cli/create-warns-when-a-sweep-or-report-title-names-a-private-project-so-its-slug-and-branch-dont-carry-the-name, from the 376 sweep). A sweep named after another project put that name in its branch and every `Refs:` line, and `make leak-check` caught it only at validate. Now, in a dark-factory checkout, a name from the private-names list in the title or slug leaves the slug (`issue` when nothing is left), the slug is pinned with `slug:`, and create warns naming the list's line, never the name. The title keeps it. `handoff` gets the same, and nothing changes on other projects' boards.
- **`issue-cli attach` and `issue-cli evidence add` take any file, not only images** (cli/attach-and-evidence-add-take-any-file-text-pprof-not-only-images, from the 376 sweep). A perf issue's criterion asked for an attached CPU profile and `attach` refused it. Now a file that is no image keeps its cleaned name (`attachments/<hash>/cpu.pprof`, `_evidence/<slug>/AC1-top.txt`) and gets a plain `[name](url)` link; the viewer shows a text file as text and serves anything else as a download, never as a page (nosniff, sandbox CSP), and the Evidence tab lists such files in a Files card. A non-image named like an image is refused; the browser upload stays images only.
- **`issue-cli ship` lands an issue's branch on local main in one command** (cli/issue-cli-ship-merge-main-validate-fast-forward-and-on-a-moved-main-re-merge-and-re-check-with-a-refused-ff-never-hidden-by-a-pipe). In the issue's worktree it merges main (`chore(merge): main into <slug>`), runs the full check (`--check`, else `ci.run`'s default tier), and fast-forwards the main checkout; when main moved meanwhile it merges again and, on a clean merge, runs the re-check (go build, go vet, the touched packages' tests; `--recheck`), fast-forwards again (up to `--rounds`, 3) and then runs the full check on the landed tree. A PASS is remembered per HEAD, so a re-run after a refusal skips it. The last stdout line is the verdict with a matching exit code: `ship: LANDED <sha>` (0), `ship: NOT LANDED (<reason>)` (1: conflict, red check, ff refused, main moved N times), `ship: LANDED <sha>, check after landing FAIL (<steps>)` (3), so a refused fast-forward can't hide behind a pipe's exit code. A landing posts a `progress:` comment naming the sha each check ran on; ship never pushes, forces or ticks a box. `--dry-run` prints the plan. The shipping prompt and the `local-merge-shipping` catalog entry name it.
- **Dispatch, the briefing and `start` say when main may already hold the brief's named test** (factory/dispatch-says-already-holds-on-main-when-a-briefs-named-test-already-passes-there, from the 376 sweep). A worker was handed TestCommandSucceeds_StubBinary a day after 2c4c60e9 fixed it and spent a session learning so. Now, when the title or brief names a Go test main defines and main has commits since the issue's created day that change that test function or name it in their message (merges and the issue's own `Refs:` commits aside), the briefing gets `## Already on main?` with each commit and the `go test -run` that checks it, `issue-cli dispatch` prints `main may already hold it: <sha> <subject> (<Test>)` and still starts the worker, and `start` prints the block when the briefing lacks it. Git only, ~0.1 s per test; nothing once the work branch has commits of its own.
- **`replace --section` warns when it drops checkboxes, not only a `###` subheading** (cli/replace-section-warns-when-it-drops-template-checkboxes-not-only-a-sub-heading, from the 376 sweep). Three workers rewrote `## Idea` or `## Design` as prose, lost the template boxes without a word, and then failed `transition --check I1 I2` on unknown ids. Now a whole-section replace whose new body lacks a box the old section had (matched by text, ticks and order aside) prints `⚠ the new body drops N boxes the old section had; I1 I2 no longer name them:`, each box with its old id, tick and text, and the `issue-cli check-add` line that puts one back.
- **The /status speed test rides out bursts of load instead of turning main red** (factory/teststatuspagefastonabigproject-fails-main-under-bursty-load-its-slow-machine-ratio-compares-single-samples-taken-at-different-moments): it times /board and /status in turns over up to eight rounds and compares the best of each, and checks itself that a /status 5× slower still fails.
- **The lead tick queues a retro sweep itself at the refill line under YOLO with auto-start on** (factory/the-lead-tick-at-5-ready-items-left-queues-a-sweep-itself-under-yolo-with-auto-start-on-so-the-queue-never-runs-dry, from the 376 sweep). On 11-16 the queue sat empty about 10 hours after the tick's `queue:` line went unanswered. Now, at 5 open items or fewer with YOLO and auto-start both on (not paused), `issue-cli lead tick` files a run of `builtin:retro-sweep` (the project's own job on that brief when it has one, else the implicit `product/queue-sweep`, typed report when the workflow has it) and queues it with no quote, so auto-start's words start it; the line says `queued sweep <slug> as item N`. At most one a day, none while one is open; no job's standing start is turned on, and the lead still files the sweep's proposals. Lint's open-run check leaves the implicit job's runs alone.
- **A lead whose background tick is gone hears so on its next issue-cli call** (factory/a-resumed-lead-re-arms-its-background-tick-lead-start-checks-for-a-live-tick-and-starts-one, from the 376 sweep). After a crash a watchdog resumed the lead, but the `lead tick --watch` shell had died with the session, and nothing said so until a later check. `lead tick --watch` now records in the lead's store when it exits on its own; after any issue-cli call a lead makes, stderr says `lead tick: no background tick is running (…)` with the command to start again with Bash `run_in_background` when the recorded tick's process is gone (at once), when the last tick exited on news over 10 minutes ago and none started since, or when none ever ran. The tick's own runs, `rotate`, `flush`, `spawn` and `--json` stay quiet (docs/patterns/lead-session.md#waiting).
- **`make validate` prints each step's seconds, and local CI keeps them per step** (factory/make-validate-prints-each-steps-seconds-and-local-ci-records-them-per-step). A sweep had to rebuild per-step times by hand: the run record held one command, make validate, 400 s. Each step now prints `validate: <step> <seconds>s` when it ends (a cheap step's after its `[<step>]` output) and `validate: total <seconds>s` just before the verdict, which stays last. Local CI reads those lines into the command's `steps: [{name, duration_ms}]` in `.ci/runs/<sha>.json`, and `issue-cli ci status` prints `Slowest step at <sha>: <step> <N>s (<cmd> <N>s)` for the newest run (`slowest_step` in `--json`).
- **Stand-in tests use the machine's warmed stand-in cache instead of a private one each** (factory/stand-in-tests-share-one-warmed-cache-per-package-instead-of-each-paying-macoss-first-exec-wait-about-90-s-idle-a-run). Tests that only need a stand-in to exist (internal/standin, internal/checks' `stand_in`, internal/tmuxguard's guarded runs, which wrote three fresh executables per run) link the cache `standin.Write` and lib.sh keep per machine, so they no longer pay macOS's first-exec check every run; one test per implementation keeps the cold path in a private cache. In a full `go test ./...` the four slowest took 21.4 s on main and 4.4 s after (8.3 s with lib.sh's new cold-path test).
- **`make validate` runs its cheap steps beside the suite, and the guard tests once** (factory/make-validates-cheap-steps-run-beside-the-suite-and-the-guard-tests-arent-run-twice): the steps before `test` run one by one in the background at lower priority while the suite runs; each logs to a file and is printed whole, its lines led by `[<step>]`, when it ends, and a red one still prints `validate: red so far` at once. The suite skips the tests `guard-check` runs (`GUARD_SKIP`), and `guard-check` writes their cmd/issue-cli coverage for `cover-cli` to add. The verdict line names red steps in `VALIDATE_STEPS` order.
- **`make test` also runs internal/viewer, internal/bench and internal/agentrun in shards** (factory/internalviewer-bench-and-agentrun-tests-run-in-shards-too-so-the-test-step-ends-near-120-s). The Makefile's `TEST_SHARD_PKGS` names each sharded package with its count (cmd/issue-cli at `TEST_SHARDS`=3, viewer 3, bench 2, agentrun 2); `TestShardedPackagesRunEveryTestOnce` checks each splits into shards that run every test once. `tools/test-shards` builds the sharded packages' test lists in parallel and keeps a package's last plan (user cache folder, per checkout) while it lists the same tests, so a cached shard's `-run` pattern, part of go test's cache key, no longer moves with every local CI run's times. A fresh `make test TEST_CACHE=0` took 90 s and 112 s against 178 s and 170 s, measured back to back (the second pair at load 60-79 on 14 cores); the step now ends with cmd/issue-cli's shards.
- **A second `make validate` on an unchanged tree takes its packages from go test's cache.** `make test` no longer passes -count=1: `scripts/test-cache.txt` lists every test package cached or fresh (fresh ones, which ask git about the checkout or read this machine, keep -count=1 and say why), and `fresh-test` lines run single tests (the live-board ones, the git ls-files ones) fresh beside the cached run. The coverage profile moved to TMPDIR and a viewer fixture stopped writing `.agent-logs` into the package folder: both changed folders the repo-walking tests read, so they reran every time. `make test TEST_CACHE=0` runs it all fresh; local CI's slow tier does. A second `make test` dropped from about 170 s to 45 s (factory/make-test-drops-count1-for-hermetic-packages-an-unchanged-tree-re-validates-in-25-s-not-266-s).
- **A playtest scenario's board no longer reads the host's disk or fseventsd** (factory/scenario-boards-read-the-hosts-disk-at-86-used-every-playtest-waiting-count-is-one-too-many-17-slow-tier-steps-red). The scenario viewer is a real binary, so the disk reading was on: with this Mac's volume at 86% used, every session's Needs you counted a Disk row and 17 slow-tier steps went red. The scenario env sets `ISSUE_VIEWER_NO_MACHINE=1`, which `internal/diskuse` and `internal/fseventsd` honour, and drops the caller's `ISSUE_VIEWER_PROJECT` (run from a worker's shell, it renamed radio-comments' lead).
- **`make test` runs cmd/issue-cli's tests in 3 parallel shards** (factory/cmdissue-clis-tests-run-in-parallel-shards-so-the-test-step-stops-waiting-on-one-244-s-package). `tools/test-shards` lists the package's tests, splits them by the newest local CI run's times (`internal/testshard`) into `TEST_SHARDS` go test processes beside one go test for the rest, checks every test is in exactly one shard, keeps output whole lines for `ci gotest`, and merges the shards' coverage profiles into `cover.out`. The step now ends with internal/viewer: 118-126 s against 140 s at the same load.
- **`make validate` runs cmd/issue-cli's tests once** (factory/make-validate-counts-coverage-in-the-test-run-cover-cli-no-longer-re-runs-cmdissue-clis-tests-133-s). The test step now writes cmd/issue-cli's coverage profile (`-coverpkg=./cmd/issue-cli/... -coverprofile=cover.out`) and cover-cli reads it to check `CLI_COVERAGE_FLOOR`, with the same `cmd/issue-cli coverage: N% (floor: N%)` line, instead of running those tests a second time (about 135 s, a third of the run). A missing profile is red; `make cover-cli` alone still runs the tests itself.
- **Tests build each command of this module once per test binary** (factory/tests-build-issue-cli-once-per-test-binary-not-once-per-test-one-takes-55-8-s). `internal/testbin.Path(t, "./cmd/issue-cli")` runs one `go build` per package per test binary and hands every test the same file (`Link` puts it under a test's own name); it builds with go's settings from the binary's start, so a test that moved HOME or TMPDIR still builds from the warm cache, and tmuxguard's cleanup removes the folder. cmd/issue-cli's three building tests and the viewer's newcomer walkthrough use it; `TestEveryGoBuildInATestGoesThroughTestbin` fails on a new `exec.Command("go", "build", …)` in a `_test.go`. Under `scripts/under-load.sh` the three tests took 12.5 s → 9.9 s; in local CI, where each in-test build competed with the suite's compiles, one took 55.8 s.
- **Post-mortem copies are one row with one answer, and fixed actions need none** (workflow/post-mortem-review-keeps-up-identical-actions-across-post-mortems-are-one-row-with-one-owner-answer-and-actions-already-fixed-on-main-need-none). 641 post-mortem actions waited for the owner in 227 post-mortems, many the same lesson told again. On `/p/<project>/postmortems` a group of copies is now one row with its count and every post-mortem it came from, every copy ticked; **Agree** joins Refuse and Overtaken there and applies the group once (one memory, commit or experiment), ticking the other copies `Applied: as <file>#<id>: …` with the same owner words. A `file` or `skill` action is a copy only of one editing the same file. On this board one answer per group cuts 641 answers to 497. `issue-cli postmortem fixed <file>#A<n> --by <slug|sha> [--copies]` links an action to its fix: once the issue is done (or at once for a commit on main), or when a memory already has its exact words, it shows under **Fixed already** and leaves the queue, its count and Needs you. `issue-cli postmortem list --grouped [--json]` prints the page's rows for the lead.
- **`health lint --workflow <file> [--projects <file>]` lints a candidate workflow file and its jobs before the merge that makes them live** (workflow/issue-cli-lint-workflow-file-checks-a-candidate-workflow-file-and-its-jobs-before-the-merge-that-makes-them-live, from the cross-project sweep). projects.yaml lives outside the repo, so a project's new jobs went live at once while their brief waited for the merge, and `health lint` read only the live files: a worker found its mistake after the merge. The new flags run every lint check on the candidate files (Product's workflow file, the project's projects.yaml entry) against the same issues and print only what they change: `+` each warning added, `-` each removed, matched on check and message so a moved line is no change. A file in a worktree lints with that checkout as the project root, so a brief the branch adds counts as there. `process workflow --file` names the command.
- **Lint warns when the roadmap falls behind.** `issue-cli health lint` check `roadmap-behind`: when docs/roadmap.md's version table stops before the highest `version:` an open issue carries, one warning names each milestone it lacks (`lacks 0.61, 0.62`) and points at a docs issue to add them. No roadmap, or no table, is quiet (docs/workbench-files.md#the-lint-family).
- **A project's deploy is a list in its workflow.yaml.** `deploy:` names the steps in order (`name`, `run`, optional `needs:` main-green, no-live-workers or owner-ok, `probe:`, `timeout:`). `issue-cli deploy --dry-run` prints the plan and what would refuse each step now; `issue-cli deploy` runs them, stops at the first red or refused step, and records each step under the issues dir's `.deploys/`; `--from <step>` goes on after a fix, `--last` prints the newest run. An owner-ok step needs the owner's words in `--quote`; workers may only dry-run. The Report shows a Last deploy card, and `status.md` a Last deploy line (docs/workflow.md#deploy).
- **Owner steps: a hand step only the owner can do waits on Waiting on you with its due date and exact text** (factory/a-dated-owner-step-an-agent-records-a-hand-step-with-a-due-date-and-the-exact-text-and-waiting-on-you-shows-it-until-the-owner-ticks-it, from the cross-project sweep). Steps like reinstalling a phone build whose free signing lasts 7 days, or requesting indexing of six URLs in Search Console, lived in handoff notes and report text, so the owner's list never had the URLs or the date. `issue-cli owner-step add --title … --due <day> --body "<exact text>" [--repeat 7d]` records one on the project (a worker's on its issue); Needs you's new **Your steps** section and the Status page show it with its due line, red once overdue, and the body as written. **Done** is the owner's act: the owner's signed-in browser (`POST …/owner-steps/<id>/done`), a lead relaying `--quote`, never a worker. A repeating step opens again on the tick's day plus the interval. `owner-step list`, `drop`; the lead's state file, the rotate's handoff note and `lead flush pack` list the open steps. A body that looks like it holds a secret is refused.
- **`issue-cli evidence add` puts a still where the Evidence tab shows it, from a worktree** (cli/issue-cli-evidence-add-puts-a-still-where-the-evidence-tab-reads-it-from-a-worktree, from the cross-project sweep). A worktree has no issues/ tree, so another project's workers guessed where stills go from its CLAUDE.md, and one run's captures (~290 MB) stayed in the worktrees until the owner deleted them. `issue-cli evidence add <file>… [--step <id>] [--force]` copies images (as `attach` reads them: sniffed, 10 MB each) into `<issues>/_evidence/<slug>/` of the primary checkout from any folder, keeping each name (`--step TP3` saves `TP3-<name>`), refuses a name already there unless `--force`, and prints each path and its markdown link; `evidence list` prints what is there. The issue's Evidence tab gains a Stills card for that folder, and the worker briefing's `## Worktree` section names the command.
