- **The nightly slow tier is green again: three playtest sessions follow the pages they check** (factory/slow-tier-red-at-ab66f30-department-goalstp6-stats-dashboardtp4-and-workflow-timetp4-sessions-went-stale-when-the-stats-page-and-department-lint-changed). department-goals' scenario gives Marketing its own mission (lint now flags a template's sample), and the Stats sessions count charts and table cards as lower bounds, so a new chart or table no longer turns the slow tier red.
- **`comment --amend` and `append --replace` correct a wrong comment or line where agents look for it** (cli/issue-cli-comment-and-append-can-correct-the-agents-own-last-entry, from the cross-project sweep). Another project's workers wrote correction comments and correction paragraphs because `comment` and `append` had no way to fix one: the commands that do (`comment edit`, `replace --match`) went unfound. `issue-cli comment <slug> --amend --text "..."` is `comment edit <slug> last`: your own latest comment, its number and date kept, the old text in its edits; another author's, the owner's and approval:/dispatch:/stage:/yolo-* comments are refused. `issue-cli append <slug> --section S --replace "<old line>" --body "<new line>"` replaces the one line of S that reads the old line (spaces at either end aside, regexp characters literal); none or several are refused with the matching lines numbered. Each `comment` and `append` now ends with the line that corrects it.
- **`goal write --merge` sets a job file's named metrics and keeps the others** (factory/goal-write-can-set-one-metric-of-a-jobs-file-without-dropping-the-others, from the cross-project sweep). `goal write` replaced `<issues>/.goals/<dept>/<job>.json` whole, so two scripts of one run couldn't each write their own ids: another project routed half its metrics through plan-watch. `--merge` reads the file under a lock, sets the named metrics and keeps the rest with their own dates; a merge into a file it can't read refuses instead of dropping it. Each metric now carries its `date`, and the goals date a value by it, so a kept metric from a missed run still shows "no value since". A whole write (still the default) names the ids it dropped and points at `--merge`. Every write stamps `"written": "<today>"`, the day it ran (not `--date`), for a gate that checks the file is from this run.
- **A report's briefing no longer asks for HANDOFF.md at a worktree root it doesn't have** (workflow/the-briefing-drops-its-handoff-md-worktree-line-for-a-type-of-work-that-has-no-worktree, from the cross-project sweep). Three retros on report issues in another project named the clash: the report type says nothing is built and there is no branch, while the briefing's `## Handoff` said "Keep HANDOFF.md current at the root of your worktree". A type of work that builds nothing (its path ships in its work status or the owner's test: report, chore, epic) now gets a `## Handoff` that asks for a `progress:` comment at each step instead (what's done, the next step, open questions; the next agent reads the issue trail), and `transition`'s reminder says the same. Feature, tweak, docs and bugfix briefings are unchanged.
- **`issue-cli worktree add` makes the issue's worktree and runs the project's setup** (cli/issue-cli-worktree-add-makes-the-issues-worktree-and-branch-and-runs-the-projects-setup-for-projects-whose-workers-make-their-own, from the cross-project sweep). In a project without `worktree: true` the briefing told workers to run the raw `git worktree add`, which runs no `worktree_setup`: seven retros from another project since 11-14 lost 3–10 minutes each linking node_modules by hand. `issue-cli worktree add [<slug>]` (an alias of `worktrees`) makes `.worktrees/<slug>` on `work/<slug>` from the main branch with dispatch's own step (sparse excludes, checkout, `worktree_setup` once) and prints the path last; a worktree that exists is left as it is and says so. The briefing's `## Branch` section names it.
- **The briefing's zsh note names the shell traps workers keep hitting** (factory/the-worker-briefings-shell-notes-add-the-zsh-traps-workers-keep-hitting, from the cross-project sweep). Another project's retros named the same zsh traps eight times, and the `## Shell on this machine` section covered none of them. Its zsh bullet, and the Shell notes in docs/getting-started.md, now also say: print separators with `printf` (`echo =====` fails, since a word starting with `=` is a command lookup), loop over a command list with a shell function, quote globs in a test command's arguments, put a heredoc in a file instead of nesting one, use `git -C <dir>` or absolute paths instead of `cd` in a compound command, and write an `rm` target held in a variable as `"${VAR:?}"`.
- **Lint and the out-of-work line name the goals and epics nothing works on** (factory/lint-and-the-lead-tick-name-a-project-goal-with-no-open-issue-queued-item-or-job-behind-it, from the cross-project sweep). A lead whose queue ran dry saw only "think up more" and listed candidates by hand, with no check that any work stood behind the goals the owner set. An issue now names its goal in `goal:` (`create --goal hq/clients`; `queue add --idea "…" --goal <key>` for an idea), and the new `goal-without-work` lint check warns on each goal and open epic with no open issue, active queue item or job behind it, with the command that adds work; a project with no goals gets one note, not a warning. `queue list`, `queue next`, the lead's `Queue:` line and the tick's `queue: nothing open left` line add `look first at: hq/clients (Paying clients), epic <slug>`.
- **The lead tick names a branch that waits at shipping unmerged** (factory/the-lead-tick-names-a-green-worker-branch-that-has-waited-unmerged-for-30-minutes, from another project's lead). A finished issue's branch sat at shipping for 13 hours off main with nothing in the tick to say so. `issue-cli lead tick` now says `merge: <slug> is shipping and <branch> has waited 40m unmerged (since 09:10): git -C <main checkout> merge --ff-only <branch>` (the conventional merge commit when main moved on) once the branch has waited `leads.green_branch_minutes` (default 30) since the later of its head commit and the move into the merge status, and again each hour while it waits. It is news; the tick merges nothing.
- **A CI or worktree setup command that would empty node_modules through a symlink is refused, with the fix** (cli/a-ci-or-setup-command-that-would-delete-node-modules-through-a-symlink-is-refused-and-says-the-fix, from another project's retros). A Node worktree whose `node_modules` links to the main checkout's lost it all to `npm ci`: npm empties node_modules first, through the link, so the main checkout, every other worktree and the running app were left with an empty folder (measured: the target empty, the link gone). Local CI (`issue-cli ci run`), `prove --run` and `worktree_setup:` now read the command first: `npm ci` and its aliases, `npm install --force`, `pnpm prune`, `pnpm install --force` or `rm -r node_modules/` while `node_modules` there is a symlink stop before running, with one line naming the link, its target and the fix (`rm <link>`, then run it again). The check follows `cd`, `npm --prefix` and an `ln -s … node_modules` earlier in the same command; a real node_modules folder runs as before.
- **Needs you and Status list a human-testing issue whose only open box is the owner's** (cli/needs-you-omits-a-human-testing-issue-whose-human-testing-box-is-unticked-and, from another project's lead). An issue in human-testing with its Human Testing box (HT1) unticked and a `yolo-owner-check:` waiting was on no Needs you list and not in Status's Waiting on you: the boxes of the section the move into a status appends counted as the worker's work left, the owner's included. A box only the owner may tick (the Human Testing section, or marked "(owner)") no longer holds the gate back, so its approval shows under To approve; dispatch and auto-start read the same rule.

- `make linux-check`'s image has curl, so the Connect socials tests pass there (factory/three-connect-socials-tests-time-out-with-503-under-make-linux-check). The socials connector's `ready:` step curls the tool, and the ubuntu:24.04 image had no curl: each held call waited out 120 s for a 503 and `internal/viewer` hit its 10-minute timeout. The test rig now fails at once naming curl when it is missing.
- **Workers start without the skills and the Artifact tool their type of work never uses** (factory/workers-start-without-the-skills-their-type-of-work-never-uses-and-the-issue-first-records-the-floor-by-part). A lean worker's first turn was 54.5k tokens, 12k of them the Artifact tool's schema and 7k the 31 skills bundled with Claude Code or synced from claude.ai, which the lean setup could not name since they have no folder. The launch now learns their names from the project's transcripts and earlier workers' settings and turns them off with `disableBundledSkills`, and runs the worker with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ARTIFACT=1`. `workers: skills:` items can be kept per system, type or label (`{skill: "anthropic-skills:pdf", types: [report]}`), `workers: tools: [Artifact]` keeps the tool, and lint warns on a kept skill nothing lists.
- **The proof check and `prove --run` find Rust tests that live inline in `src/*.rs`** (cli/prove-test-does-not-find-rust-test-functions-that-live-in-src-rs-inline). A test link's name had to be in a file whose path looks like a test file (`_test.`, `.test.`, `tests/` …), so a `#[test]` fn in a `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `src/main.rs` came back `not found` and workers fell back to a commit link. A `.rs` file that holds a test attribute (`#[test]`, `#[cfg(test)]`, `#[tokio::test]` …) now counts as a test file; one without still doesn't.
- **A quoted `it()` title in a `Proves:` line is one test link** (cli/prove-a-test-link-must-be-one-whole-word-so-vitest-it-titles-polish, from another project's worker). A `Proves:` value was split on spaces, so a vitest title with spaces, colons or parentheses became one test link per word, most of them `not found`. A double- or single-quoted string is now read whole (`Proves: AC3 "lista: id (ryba)"`) and matched as one phrase, and the commit-msg hook's hint for a word that isn't a test name says to quote the title. Each test name is now found on its own: the lookup printed only the longest match on a line, so a short name on the same line as a longer linked one read `not found`.
- **`prove --run` runs the project's `ci:` commands where there is no `go.mod`** (cli/prove-run-only-runs-go-test-in-a-nodetypescript-project-…). It refused every project without a `go.mod`, so a Node or TypeScript project could only hand-write its `tests:` comment. Now it runs the default-tier `ci: run:` commands in the issue's worktree as local CI runs them; each linked name has to be in a tracked test file, a green run posts the `tests:` comment naming the command, a red one shows the output's tail and posts nothing.
- **YOLO waits for an open (owner) box due before the build** (from another project's lead). A criterion "A design sketch shown to the owner before building … (owner)" was open while YOLO passed in design → backlog → in progress, and the feature was built without the owner's look. Now each gate that ends at or before the work status refuses under YOLO while an unticked (owner) box is due before the build (its text says before the build, building, implementation or any code, or it sits under an early status's section such as `## Design`), naming the box; the issue waits in Needs you, and once the owner ticks it YOLO passes the gate. Other (owner) boxes still wait in the after-the-fact queue. `yolo status`, the briefing's YOLO rule and docs/Workflow/yolo.md list it.
- **`retro list` says a sweep's brief decides whether it files** (cli/retro-lists-closing-line-says-what-a-sweeps-brief-decides-about-filing-not-that-a-sweep-files-nothing). Its closing line said a sweep files nothing, against sweep briefs that ask the worker to file issues. It now reads "A sweep files issues when its brief asks for them; otherwise it lists its proposals in its report and the lead files them.", and docs/CLI/overview.md and docs/patterns/lead-session.md quote it word for word. The `builtin:retro-sweep` brief still asks for none.
- **`memory add --updates` refuses a fact that shares under a third of the words with the one it would replace** (cli/memory-add-updates-refuses-a-fact-that-shares-almost-no-words-with-the-one-it-replaces). `--updates` replaces the whole text, and a worker once followed it onto an unrelated memory (19% of the words in common) and had to restore it by hand. Add now prints the old text beside yours and names `--new` (a different fact) and `--supersedes` (no longer true); `--updates <id> --yes` replaces it anyway. The near-duplicate refusal and the done/retrospective memory hint now say which flag fits a different fact: `--new`, not `--updates`.
- **`doctor leaks` names worktrees nothing uses outside `.worktrees/`, and `worktrees prune` removes old release trees** (factory/doctor-leaks-names-a-scratchpad-worktree-and-a-subagents-merged-worktree-and-worktrees-prune-removes-a-published-releases-worktree). A worker's tree in its scratchpad, a subagent's under `.claude/worktrees/` on a merged branch and six release trees stayed registered with no command naming them. `doctor leaks` now lists a registered worktree in a temp or scratchpad folder (gone, or untouched for an hour with nothing live inside) and a subagent's tree whose branch is on main (untouched for a day), with `git worktree remove` (after `unlock` when locked; `--stop` never forces). `worktrees prune` removes `.worktrees/release-vX.Y` once its line is published (or was skipped for a newer one), a newer line is out and its branch has nothing past its tag; the newest published line, a line in soak and a branch with a pick waiting are kept with why. The storage family measures local CI's `.worktrees/.ci` on its own (`storage.ci_worktree_mb`), out of `storage.worktrees_mb`.
- **The storage family and Needs you count the Go build cache and the agent transcripts** (factory/the-storage-health-family-and-needs-you-count-the-go-build-cache-and-the-agent-transcripts-and-name-the-trim-command). The cache held 180 GB on 2026-11-16, 138 GB of it created that day, while the family's checkout total said 4.4 GB: every worktree's builds and tests write their own entries, and Go trims only what went unused for 5 days. The storage family now writes `storage.gocache_mb`, `storage.gocache_new_mb` (created in the last 24 h, macOS), `storage.transcripts_mb` (Claude Code's projects, Codex's sessions) and `storage.volume_used_pct`, from one walk an hour kept in the state dir (`internal/diskuse`; a snapshot walks before its families, outside their timeout, and the viewer refreshes it hourly). Needs you lists one row, once across projects, while the cache is over 100 GB or the volume over 85% used, naming `go clean -cache` and that no worker may be running; nothing runs it. /health's Where to look lists **Disk outside the checkout**.
- **`health lint` keeps a removed department's decisions and outputs as history, not warnings** (workflow/lint-reads-decisions-and-outputs-of-a-removed-department-as-history-not-as-warnings). A decision scoped to a department `projects.yaml` no longer has, when the board still holds that department's work (an output, an issue's `department:`), and the outputs of such a department, are now printed after the warnings as `N info lines, kept as history (no warning, no score)` and left out of the lint count; `--json` lists them under `history`. A department the board never saw is a typo and still warns.
- **Health's `e2e_wait` reads the nightly slow run again** (factory/healths-e2e-wait-reads-the-slow-tiers-green-run-instead-of-saying-no-e2e-run): local CI kept the newest 50 run records across all tiers, so a day of merges pruned the slow tier's run and its `e2e.json`, and the score said "no e2e run in local CI" while `ci status --tier slow` said green. Each tier now keeps its own 50 runs, logs and results. `unit_cost_trend` without data says when it will have some: `needs unit results from 30 days ago: the oldest snapshot with them is from <date>, so it has data from <date> (N days)` (row `score.component.waits_days`), in `issue-cli health`, the Health view and the review pack.
- **Health's `unit_wait` scores the best unit wall of the last 5 CI runs, and each run records its load** (factory/healths-unit-wait-is-scored-on-the-best-recent-run-not-on-a-wall-time-taken-under-a-loaded-machine). One run under a busy machine dropped the score 25 points with the same tests. Local CI now reads the load average around each command with `results:` and keeps it with the core count (`tests.load`, `tests.cores` in the snapshot; the `results:` log line says `load 16.4 on 14 cores`). `unit_wait` reads the new `tests.wall_seconds_best{kind=unit,window=5runs}`, and an older snapshot falls back to its one wall. When the load was above the cores, the slowest-test rows, the Health view's Slowest tests subtitle and the review pack say so (docs/workbench-files.md, "Scores").
- `issue-cli health lint --check memory` names a cluster of 3 or more memories that retell one fact in other words (each linked pair shares 45% or more of the shorter one's words; `memory add`'s and `memory consolidate`'s half-the-words line misses a paraphrase), by id with the merge as its fix, and a project pitfall or howto whose text names a step (in design, in progress, "at shipping") with no `--at`, with the `memory edit <id> --at` that makes transition print it there (factory/lint-flags-a-cluster-of-near-duplicate-memories-and-a-pitfall-that-names-a-step-but-has-no-at-and-the-three-known-clusters-are-merged).
- **A design edit shows the backlog gate's answer as it saves** (workflow/a-design-edit-prints-the-backlog-gates-refusals-as-it-saves-so-sub-bullets-the-must-not-link-and-checkbox-ids-show-before-the-move): `issue-cli replace` and `append` on an issue in design that change Acceptance Criteria or `## Must not` print `⚠ the backlog gate would refuse: criteria_checkboxes: …` for a plain bullet or sub-bullet and a template box, and `⚠ the backlog gate would warn: …` for a criterion no test could fail or a Must not line with no `→ ACn`, each with its fix; other edits print nothing new. The Must not placeholder shows the link (`… → AC<n>, or → none: <why>`; catalog `must-not-section` v0.63.0; the old placeholder still reads as one). `transition --check` names the section of an id outside the sections the move waits on: `⚠ I2, I3 ticked in ## Idea; this move waits on ## Implementation (open: Im1 Im2)`.
- **The lead tick says once a day how far the installed issue-cli is behind main** (cli/the-lead-tick-says-once-a-day-how-far-the-installed-issue-cli-is-behind-main-and-which-shipped-fixes-wait-on-an-install). A fix to what the lead or a worker sees did nothing until someone ran `make install`, and nothing told the lead it was pending (main was 68 commits ahead of the installed build). On the engine's own repository, when the running build is more than 10 commits behind main, `lead tick` prints one `note:` line a local day with the count, up to 5 shipped `fix:` subjects touching issue-cli's code, and the install command. It is held, never news: `--exit-on-news` prints it with the next news. Nothing runs the install.
- `decisions_cited` (and `decisions_respected`, which reads `## Design` the same way) counts every ref in a comma list after one full ref, with or without each `#`: `<slug>#D-5,D-6,D-7` and `<slug>#D-5, #D-6` cite all of them, on a cited line or an n/a one. Several `n/a <ref>: <why>.` sentences on one line each set their own refs aside with their own reason (a `.` or `;` ends one). The refusal lists the forms that count (workflow/decisions-cited-counts-every-ref-in-a-comma-list-after-one-full-ref-and-every-na-sentence-on-one-line).
- `issue-cli decisions --in-force` and `decisions --touched` with no slug (or an empty one, which the briefing's `--in-force "$ISSUE_VIEWER_ISSUE_SLUG"` gives outside a session) take the session's issue, as `show` does, instead of failing with `issue not found: --`; with no session they ask for the slug and name the flag. `--in-force --status S` no longer takes `--status` as the slug (cli/issue-cli-decisions-in-force-and-touched-with-no-value-take-the-issue-from-the-session-instead-of-failing-with-issue-not-found).
- The Status page (the Report, status.md, the classic page) renders warm in about 0.15 s on the engine board instead of 0.7 s: its git readings (the release card's tags, branch tips, the branch's changelog, the commits since a candidate, the release config) are kept until a ref, a tag or the config changes, and each queue item and backlog candidate is checked against the one kept tmux session list instead of a `tmux has-session` each; 36 child processes a warm request become 1. Start, dispatch and the other actions still ask tmux afresh
- The Retro page keeps each retro's lowercased text with its parse, so the "pattern that comes back" no longer re-splits every retro once per term: 3.1 s → 89 ms on a 500-retro fixture, 105 ms → 10 ms of build on this repo's 510. Its show-all view (`?all=1`) pages each list 30 at a time (`to_sort_page=`, `processed_page=`, Newer / Older), and the classic page 50 (`page=`) (ui/the-retro-page-renders-in-under-500-ms-with-hundreds-of-unarchived-retros).
- The Stats page's first visit after a viewer start no longer waits for the whole aggregate (21–27 s on a big board): it waits half a second at most, then shows the page with "Building these numbers for the first time since the viewer started" under the title and reloads every 3 s until they are in.
- "Is this issue's work on main" is one rule, `gitmain.WorkAt`, for the queue's `--merged` blockers, `queue done`'s SHA, `worktrees prune` and the lead tick: the branch's head is past its dispatch base and main contains it. A branch cut with no commits of its own no longer gets a done SHA (or a false `not on main` note) and a sweep keeps its tree (`no commits past its dispatch base`); at done its tree still goes. The main branch is named once: `ci.branch`, else origin's HEAD, `main`, `master`, the main checkout's branch, for local CI, the queue, prune, health, the board and the agent page alike (before, the queue and CI took the checked-out branch first and the agent page knew only main and master) (factory/one-branch-is-on-main-check-and-one-main-branch-resolver-for-the-queue-prune-and-lead-tick).
- `TestDocNumbersCopiesAgree` also holds the auto-start worker default, gap, start-weight floor, turn drop-out and share window, the lead tick's refill line and long hold, and the worktree prune's idle time to the sentences that quote them (docs/numbers.md lists them); the share line reads `autostart.ShareWindow` instead of a literal 24h.
- `issue-cli lead tick` removes the worktree of a parked issue or a side exit after done the way it removes a done one, once its branch is on main or has no commits of its own (a tree with unlanded commits, changes or a live session stays), so a tree reclaim freed no longer waits for the daily sweep. Its `land:` line finds a done issue's branch through `checks.IssueBranch`, so a branch named in the `branch:` field is named for landing too (factory/a-parked-or-obsolete-issues-worktree-is-removed-the-way-a-done-one-is).
- `issue-cli health lint --check shas --fix` swaps each stale `## Implementation` SHA for the commit that landed, when that is mechanical: of the commits on main naming the issue's slug, exactly one has the stale commit's subject. One write per issue under its lock, each swap printed, each ref it kept listed with why (and, when one main commit has the subject but doesn't name the slug, a hint to check it by hand). The shas finding's fix line names it first (workflow/health-lint-fix-shas-replaces-a-stale-implementation-sha-with-the-one-commit-that-landed).
- Lint's `criteria` check no longer asks for a test on each acceptance box of a type whose path skips testing (report, docs, chore): those warnings could never clear. Feature, tweak and bugfix boxes still warn, and the Must not findings stay on for every type (workflow/lints-criteria-check-doesnt-ask-a-report-or-spike-for-a-test-on-each-criterion).
- `issue-cli doctor leaks` (and `--stop`, and the playtest runner's reap at start) counts a Chrome on an old `chromedp-runner*` profile as left behind only once it has been detached to pid 1 for 10 minutes, the age the agent-home rule already used, so a live test's Chrome that just detached is no longer listed or stopped (factory/doctor-leaks-stop-only-stops-a-chromedp-chrome-that-has-been-detached-long-enough-as-the-agent-home-rule-does).
- The lead tick and `queue next` tell a memory hold by its kind, not by the hold text's first words: a start's verdict, a job's status and a hold refusal carry `autostart.HoldKind` (`hold_kind` in `queue next --json`), so rewording a hold line no longer stops the tick from closing finished workers' sessions to free memory. `autostart.MemoryHolds` is the one memory-line rule; the share's turn and jobs call it (`TestOneMemoryLineRule`) (factory/the-lead-tick-tells-a-memory-hold-by-its-kind-not-by-the-hold-texts-first-words).
- A job's standing start runs under the window `queue next` decides with (one function, `agentrun.StartLimits`): with auto-start off and no queue limits it has no worker cap instead of 2 (`no worker cap (<proj> queue default)`), and it waits auto-start's `--gap` while auto-start is on instead of a fixed 5 minutes (factory/a-jobs-standing-start-uses-the-queues-no-cap-default-when-no-limits-are-set-as-queue-next-does).
- The commit-msg hook (`make hooks`) warns when a staged line or the commit message holds what `make leak-check` refuses (a name from the private-names list, a real home or scratchpad path): `warning: <file>:<line>: …` with the stand-in to use, never the name itself. It only warns, the commit goes through, and it runs in a dark-factory checkout only (factory/the-commit-hook-warns-when-a-staged-file-names-another-project-on-this-machine).
- The shipping status guidance in this repo's workflow.yaml no longer says nobody removes an issue's worktree: it says, like the briefing, that the engine removes it once the branch is on main and the session has closed (workflow/the-shipping-guidance-stops-telling-workers-that-nobody-removes-their-worktree).
- **`make validate` runs the repo-wide guard tests before the suite** (factory/make-validate-runs-the-repo-wide-guard-tests-as-a-cheap-step-before-the-full-suite): a new cheap step after codemap-check, `make guard-check`, runs the tests listed in `scripts/guard-tests.txt` (the command table, lint checks in the docs, doc numbers, every worker guard consulting the sandbox pass, …) in seconds, so a missing docs line shows as `validate: red so far: guard-check` instead of after the full suite. `TestGuardCheckListsEveryGuardTest` fails when a `TestEvery*`, `Test*ListedInDocs` or `TestDocNumbers*` test is missing from the list, or a listed one doesn't exist.
- In a session dispatched for the branch's issue (`ISSUE_VIEWER_ISSUE_SLUG`), the commit-msg hook writes the `Refs: <slug>` line into the first commit's message itself and says so, instead of a note asking for an amend that could leave a `tests:` comment's sha stale; a merge, another issue's session or none keeps the note (workflow/the-commit-hook-writes-the-refs-line-itself-on-a-dispatched-workers-first-commit).
- The briefing's `--project` injection leaves an `issue-cli` glued to a path or name as written (`cmd/issue-cli`, `./cmd/issue-cli`, `my-issue-cli`); a command-position `issue-cli` and the worker's own `./issue-cli` build still get it (cli/the-project-injection-in-the-briefing-leaves-paths-like-cmdissue-cli-alone).
- Run without `--project` inside a git worktree (or subfolder) of a project in projects.yaml, issue-cli picks that project: the one whose root has the cwd's git common dir. When it can't pick one, the `ambiguous project` error lists every configured project and no longer marks the first one `(default)` (cli/issue-cli-run-inside-a-projects-worktree-picks-that-project-without-project).
- **A lead keeps a compact state file, rotates instead of growing, and looks old context up; workers read docs in short** (factory/lead-context-is-kept-as-a-compact-state-file-the-lead-rotates-instead-of-growing-old-context-is-compressed-and-searchable-and-workers-read-compact-references)
  - `issue-cli lead state` renders `$XDG_STATE_HOME/ai-workflow-engine/lead/<lead>.md` (16 KB at most): live workers, the queue head with the owner's words, open owner asks, today's decision refs and lead memory ids, the background shells it waits on, tomorrow's items and notes (`lead state note|ask|done`). The tick rewrites it; earlier days' notes fold into that day's summary.
  - The 300k context line now says `rotate`: `issue-cli lead rotate` writes the handoff note from the state file and a digest of the old chat (owner words, decisions, the commands that changed something, no tool output) into `sessions/`, never over the day's note, then closes the session after its turn and starts the same lead, whose first prompt carries the state file and the watches to restart.
  - `issue-cli lead history <words>` searches the digests and the lead's transcripts on disk and prints only the matching lines.
  - `issue-cli ref <page> [<heading words>]` prints a docs page's outline (`ref cli`: 9 KB for the 181 KB CLI page) or one section; `ref --measure` counts the pages workers read and their first-hour tokens. The worker briefing's `## Reading code and docs` names `codemap`, `affected` and `ref`.
- `issue-cli check-add <slug> --section "<name>" --text "…"` adds one open box after the section's last box and prints its id (`✓ Added AC5 [Acceptance criteria #5]: …`); every other box keeps its id. An unknown section is refused with the issue's sections, an add that would move another section's short ids is refused, and an agent can't add a box the owner ticks (`(owner)` text, Human Testing). `add-check`, `checklist-add`, `add-box` and `box-add` name it in the unknown-command hint (cli/check-add-adds-a-box-to-a-section-golf-workers-tried-check-add-add-check-checklist-add).
- `issue-cli lead tick --watch --exit-on-news` exits only on news the lead must act on (red CI, a branch to land, the queue at its refill point, a stalled or failed worker, a hold over 1 h, a `doctor leaks` finding, the context nudge, the owner's comment). A plain start and a session closed under a memory hold are held and printed above the next news, one line each; `--verbose` exits on them as before. Under `--observe` a status move is news only when it waits on a gate, on the owner's testing or was parked. Held starts now say so after 1 h, not 2 h. See docs/patterns/lead-session.md#what-counts-as-news.

- `issue-cli output list` lists the shelf as `issue-cli outputs` does (same rows; `--department`, `--issue`, `--state`, `--json`), and `outputs` takes `--department` too. `output add --path` from a worker finds a file that is only on its issue's branch (the project's checkout doesn't have it yet) in the issue's worktree and records the branch; the shelf row says `(branch <name>)`, and after the merge the file is read from the checkout again. A path on the checkout works as before (cli/output-list-lists-the-shelf-and-output-add-path-reads-the-issues-worktree).

- **Docs and reports start on Sonnet; a routing rule can name the model per type of work, and stats and the bench compare models** (factory/a-routing-rule-can-name-the-model-per-type-of-work-the-sidecar-records-it-and-the-benchmark-compares-models-before-the-owner-turns-one-on). Every worker on this Mac ran on Opus, and `agent_models` pins one model for a whole project. Now, with no rule naming one, Claude workers on `docs` and `report` issues start with `--model claude-sonnet-5-5`; every other type and every lead keeps the project's model (a job run counts as its job's type). **Upgrading:** this applies to every project as soon as it runs this issue-cli, with no file change; to keep the project's model for docs or reports, add `model: default` to their rule (`routing: rules: - {type: docs, agent: claude, model: default}`); `workflow upgrade` proposes `agent-routing` v0.63.0 with the new key. A rule's `model:` (`{type: tweak, agent: claude, model: claude-sonnet-5-5}`) runs what it matches on that model, over `agent_models`; a fallback to the other CLI drops a model it can't take. The launch report's Agent line names the model, the stats sidecar records it on each dispatch, and lint names a model that isn't a plain name (it is never passed). `issue-cli stats --by-model` and the Stats page's **By model** table compare done issues by type and model: reopened, red rounds and tokens p50 from the worker transcripts (`unknown`, never 0, when none were read). `issue-cli bench run <b> --model <name>` runs the golden tasks on a model, and `bench compare` of that run and one on the project's model puts them side by side by type (`claude · claude-sonnet-5-5` rows). See docs/agent-dispatch.md#the-model-per-type-of-work.

- A lead waits in one way: a background `issue-cli lead tick --watch --exit-on-news`, started again after each news, never a /loop cron, a ScheduleWakeup poll or a Monitor (each wakes the whole context; timed lead loops measured at 476k–960k tokens per wake). Every lead brief, the CEO's and each department lead's, says so (docs/patterns/lead-session.md#waiting).
- `issue-cli lead watch --run '<cmd>' [--every 10m] [--until-change | --until '<regex>']` runs a probe in the shell and exits only when its output (with its exit code) changes or matches; quiet runs print nothing.
- `issue-cli lead tick --observe [--department <d>]`: a read-only tick for a lead that starts no work. It never starts or closes anything and says its scope's due jobs, status moves, comments, new shelf items and comments, and ended workers.
- `lead tick` says once a day `lead context <n>k: flush and hand over` when the lead's own last turn read over 300k tokens of context.
- **`prove --list` on an issue with no Acceptance Criteria says there is nothing to prove, and `Requires:` stops listing a proof gate that would pass** (cli/prove-list-on-an-issue-with-no-acceptance-criteria-says-none-are-needed-and-the-dry-run-stops-listing-it). On a bugfix whose path never asks for criteria, `prove --list` failed with "has no checkboxes under ## Acceptance Criteria", and the `Requires:` list under `start` and `transition` named "every acceptance criterion has a proof link" for a gate that passes it anyway: 5 retros on one project found that confusing. Now `prove --list` prints `no acceptance criteria: nothing to prove` and exits 0. Where the path does ask for criteria, it names the transition and the `issue-cli append` that adds one. The gate drops out of `Requires:` while it would pass (no criteria, or created before its date). Entering the first work status, an issue without criteria hears where its path asks for them, or that they are optional and each one needs a proof link at the gate.
- **The briefing's Shell and Local CI sections name the project's own CI command, not `make validate` and `go test`** (cli/the-briefings-local-ci-and-shell-lines-name-the-projects-own-ci-command-not-make-validate-and-go-test). A project whose CI is `npm test` and that has no Makefile or go.mod got this repo's `make validate`, `go test -v` and "When `make validate` or a test fails" in every briefing. The sections now read the default-tier `ci:` command from workflow.yaml and whether the work dir has a `go.mod`: rtk and pipe examples use that command (`npm test | tail`), the `validate:` verdict lines appear only where CI is make validate, and `go test -v` advice only in a Go project. Without a `ci:` command the Local CI line says "your project's tests". This repo's briefing reads as before. See docs/getting-started.md#shell-notes.
- **No test or script waits under 30 s for an executable it just wrote or built, and the stand-in guard covers every package** (factory/no-test-or-script-waits-under-30-s-for-an-executable-it-just-wrote-or-built-and-the-stand-in-guard-covers-every-package). `TestAdoptedTestsWriteStandInsThroughTheHelper` now reads every `_test.go` in the module instead of a list, so a new package's test that writes an executable without `standin.Write` (or a `not a stand-in:` note) fails at once; the connectors, depttemplate, memory, outputs and wfedit stand-ins moved onto the helper. Waits on a binary built in the same run are 60 s, in a retry loop: the newcomer walkthrough's viewer, `scripts/readme-shots.sh` (which now stops when the viewer never answers), guided checks' `wait_for_url` (default 10 s → 60 s) and two ported checks. The playtest runner's reap of a killed runner's Chrome stops its helper processes too, not only the main one: under load they outlived the reap by seconds.
- **In a repo with no git remote, shipping's push and PR boxes come ticked `N/A: no git remote`** (workflow/in-a-repo-with-no-git-remote-shippings-push-and-pr-boxes-take-na-no-git-remote-by-themselves). A project without a remote got "Commit pushed to the remote" and "PR opened if applicable" at every shipping, and about 10 retros since 11-11 say the worker reworded or ticked them by hand: Shipping boxes take no N/A. The move into shipping now asks git whether the project's repo has a remote; when it has none, each open Shipping box that names a push or a PR (not one that forbids it, "no push, no pull request", and not an owner box) is ticked with `(N/A: no git remote)`, and the transition says which. `check <slug> <id> --na "no git remote"` takes such a box by hand on that repo, for an issue already in shipping. A repo with a remote, or a folder git can't read, sees no change. "no git remote" joins the named N/A forms, and the bundled template's shipping prompt says to push "if the repo has a remote".
- **A recurring job's standing start uses the project's own limits, not auto-start's 50% and 2 workers** (factory/a-jobs-standing-start-uses-the-projects-queue-limits-not-auto-starts-50-and-2-worker-defaults). On 15.11 a project's SEO job was held at "memory 51% ≥ 50%" and "2/2 workers live" after the owner had raised that project's queue to 75% and 5 workers: `job due` and `job run --scheduled` always took the defaults. A job now runs under auto-start's window while it is on and not paused, else the queue's limits when the lead set any (`queue limits --below/--max-workers`), else the defaults, as `queue next` does. mem-guard's block and the machine-wide start gap apply as before. A hold names the limit and its source (`memory 80% ≥ 75% (acme queue limit)`, `2/2 workers live (auto-start default)`), and `job auto on` prints the limits in force now. See docs/departments.md#recurring-jobs.
- **A queue item can wait for its blocker's code on main instead of its done: `queue block N --on <slug> --merged`** (factory/a-queue-item-can-wait-for-its-blockers-branch-on-main-instead-of-its-done-queue-block-merged). On 11-14 a project's first localization issue was merged and deployed while its done waited on the owner's approval, and four queue items waited with it: a blocker cleared only at its last status. `queue block … --merged` and `queue add … --blocked-on <slug> --merged` clear the blocker once its work is on main: its recorded merge sha, its `work/<slug>` tip or an earlier recorded branch head that the main branch contains, never the dispatch's base (a branch with no commits of its own is on main trivially). `queue list` shows `waits for <slug> on main`, then `cleared: <slug> on main at <sha>`; a plain blocker whose work is already on main says so in its hold with the `--merged` command. Without the flag nothing changes. See docs/agent-dispatch.md, The lead's queue.
- **Box listings mark the owner's boxes, and a check that names one among the agent's ticks the agent's** (cli/owner-boxes-are-marked-in-checklist-output-and-a-multi-id-check-ticks-the-agents-boxes-and-names-the-refused-owner-box). One project's workers made 77 refused `check <slug> HT1` calls in a week: nothing in `checklist` said HT1 was the owner's, and `check <slug> HT1 Do1 Do2` refused the whole call, so Do1 and Do2 stayed open too. `checklist`, `show`, `start`, a transition's checklist and check's own lists now print an owner box as `HT1 [ ] (owner's) …`, with one line naming the Owner checks page (`--json` items: `owner: <why>`); the marker reads the same report the tick guard does. `check` by several ids from an agent ticks the agent's boxes and prints `✗ Not ticked, the owner's: HT1 … (why)` for each owner box; it fails only when it ticked nothing. `transition --check` (and `--dry-run --check`) follow the same rule and go on with the move. Unknown ids still tick nothing. See docs/CLI/overview.md.
- **Projects without `worktree: true` get their issue's branch found, and the briefing names it** (cli/projects-without-worktree-true-prove-check-script-and-the-commit-hint-find-the-issues-own-branch-and-the-briefing-names-it). prove, `prove --run`, `commits_reference_issue`, check-script and the commit-msg `Refs:` hint looked only for `work/<slug>` in `.worktrees/<slug>`, while workers in such projects make their own worktrees under other names: "not checked: no work branch", "no worktree", no script found. One lookup now tries the issue's `branch:` field (`issue-cli set-meta <slug> --key branch --value <name>`), `work/<slug>`, the recorded branch head, then the one worktree whose branch ends in the slug or whose HANDOFF.md names it. The worktree is git's entry for the branch, whatever the folder is called. A miss names what it tried and the fix (`git branch -m … work/<slug>`, or the `branch:` field). The briefing of such a project gets a `## Branch` section (`git worktree add .worktrees/<slug> -b work/<slug>`), and `issue-cli health lint`'s new `worktrees` check suggests `worktree: true` when open issues have worktrees anyway (a warning only). Projects with `worktree: true` see no change. See docs/Workflow/proof.md#finding-the-issues-branch.
- **A dimmed suggestion in a worker's input box is no longer read as an owner answer left unsent**. On 2026-11-13 a lead read three idle workers' input boxes with a plain `tmux capture-pane -p` and reported the owner's answers stuck there. They were Claude Code's greyed-out prompt suggestions, and no delivery path had lost anything. The Crew page's Tail now reads the pane with escapes and shows such text as `(greyed suggestion, not typed: …)`. The lead guide's Screen row says to use `-e`. A worker's briefing gets `## The owner's words on this issue`: the owner's newest comments, so an answer written while no session was live reaches the next session's briefing, not only `issue-cli show`. A test now lists every caller that types into an agent's pane with its delivery check, so a new one can't skip the check.
- **No leaked children: the playtest browser and real-CLI daemons are stopped on every exit, and `issue-cli doctor leaks` lists what is left** (factory/no-leaked-children-the-playtest-browser-and-real-cli-daemons-are-stopped-on-every-exit-with-debug-only-resource-tracing-and-doctor-leaks). fseventsd grew again while nothing in the Go code watched files: the suspects were three playtest Chromes parented by launchd (12 days, 6 days, 10 hours old) and a `codex app-server-daemon` 10 days old, each holding watchers. The playtest runner now stops its browsers and scenario boards on SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP, gives Chrome its own profile (`$TMPDIR/dark-factory-playtest-<runner pid>-*`, removed at exit) and reaps, at its first browser, the Chromes a killed runner left. tmuxguard (every test binary) and a check script's sandbox (lib.sh) stop, at exit, every process whose `CODEX_HOME` or `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is their temp home, found by its environment, which macOS's `ps` no longer shows. `issue-cli doctor leaks` lists what the factory left running, with pid, age and the command that stops it (`--stop` stops them); `lead tick` runs it once a day. `DF_TRACE=resources`, or `--debug` on issue-cli and the viewer, traces every start and stop of an outside resource to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/ai-workflow-engine/trace/resources.jsonl` (rotated at 10 MB), with fseventsd's footprint every 10 minutes beside the count of live resources; off by default, it opens no file.
- **A plain `prove` link takes about 0.3 s, not 0.4–0.9 s** (cli/proves-slow-path-found-and-fixed-p95-14-s-max-37-s). Every prove call YAML-parsed every playtest results.yaml in the checkout and every session file in the worktree and the checkout to keep the few naming the issue; files that don't mention the slug are now skipped unparsed (checklist and show read the sessions the same way and gain too). `prove --run`'s seconds (p95 17 s, max 37 s since 11-10) are the `go test` it starts: a fresh commit's compile under load and slow tests; prove caches nothing of its own.
- **A release publish checks the candidate it ships, not whether local CI has tested main's newest head** (factory/a-release-publish-checks-the-candidate-it-ships-not-whether-local-ci-has-tested-mains-newest-head). `release publish v0.62.1` refused with `head 96abff0 not tested yet (green at 6788873)` while four workers kept landing on main, though a patch ships release/v0.62, not main. Once a release branch exists (a patch, or a minor after its first cut), the Waiting for release card reads **Local CI not red on main** (red still refuses, with the reason) and a new **Candidate rc.K validated in the release worktree** check. The new `issue-cli release check <version>` runs local CI's commands (`make validate` here) in the release worktree and records the commit that passed; `release cut` names it as the next step, and `release publish --dry-run` lists the checks it reads. See docs/roadmap.md#cutting-a-release-candidate-soak-publish.
- **`show` stays under a second when its branch scan is cold** (cli/issue-cli-show-takes-under-a-second-on-this-board). After main moved or a branch committed, show's Shared files section re-ran merge-base, `rev-list --count` and diff for every open branch and pair one after another: 70 git calls on the live board, 3.0–3.6 s quiet and 8–16 s under load (the issue's body or trail was not the cause). Those calls now run 8 at a time, and the main checkout and the git cache's folder are read from the `.git` files instead of `git worktree list` (three times per show) and `rev-parse`: cold show 3.0 s → 0.95 s, warm 0.49 s → 0.41 s, 8 git processes → 3. The output is unchanged; the viewer's branch views share the scan.
- **`queue next` reads the machine once per run** (cli/queue-next-takes-under-a-second-so-the-leads-5-minute-tick-stays-cheap). It spawned 25 processes a run (tmux's session list four times, every pane twice, has-session per started item, `git worktree list` four times), and on a loaded machine each spawn is what costs (p95 6 s). Now one run, and each `queue next --start` the lead tick makes, reads each once: 9 processes, 0.27 s → 0.14 s on a quiet machine, 0.48 s → 0.26 s at load 28. The start decision under the lock still reads afresh, and a dispatch watches its pane afresh.
- **A red `workflow catalog-check` ends with one line to paste for every red entry** (cli/a-red-catalog-check-prints-one-catalog-line-to-paste-that-covers-every-red-entry). It failed 17 of 50 runs since 11-10, often 2–3 times per issue, because each red entry printed its own `Catalog: n/a <id>` hint and a worker pasted one, re-ran and found the next. Each red entry now names the `sources:` pattern each file matched (`changes internal/health/score.go (matches internal/health/**)`), and the run ends with `issue-cli workflow catalog-bump <id>,…` for entries whose own symbol (`file.go#Symbol`) or `detect:` region changed, and one `Catalog: n/a <id>,<id>,… <why>` line naming every other red entry. The clilog record of a red run lists the red entry ids under `red`. See docs/CLI/overview.md.
- **The worker briefing's seven how-to sections are one short `## How to work`** (factory/the-worker-briefings-seven-how-to-sections-become-one-short-how-to-work-section). Before you start, Your goal, How to work on this issue, the free-commands list, the bug report, Workflow review and the approval list said start, show, check and transition three times over in ≈8.1k chars. One section now walks the loop (start, work the boxes, transition), where to ask, what needs approval, how to report a bug and the retrospective, each command once, in ≈3k chars on real issues; a test keeps it under 3,000 for an 80-char slug.
- **A memory hold names the idle sessions it could free, and `lead tick` closes them and asks again** (factory/a-memory-hold-names-and-lead-tick-closes-worker-sessions-whose-issue-is-done-or-waiting-on-the-owner). The memory hold was the costliest one (23 runs and ≥142 min over 11-12 → 11-15), while worker sessions of done issues whose branch wasn't landed, and of issues waiting on the owner (a ready gate, an open `question:`), stayed open and held memory. Under a memory hold `queue next` (and `--start`) now adds `could free: agent-x (done, idle since 14:05)` per idle worker session whose issue is done or waits on the owner (no issue-cli call for 15 minutes, not in a turn, not paused by mem-guard, nobody attached; `could_free` in `--json`), and `close them: issue-cli lead tick`. `lead tick` closes those sessions, prints `freed under the memory hold: closed agent-x (done), …` and asks the queue once more. An issue in progress with no owner wait is never closed; the worktree and branch stay. The 50% limit is unchanged.
- **The load holds a start only when both the 1- and the 5-minute average are at or over the cores** (factory/auto-starts-load-hold-needs-both-the-1-and-the-5-minute-load-average-over-the-core-count). One `make validate` pushes the 1-minute average over the cores for a minute or two; auto-start read only that one, so the lead held ≥125 minutes over 11-12 → 11-15 at 15.5–16.5 on 14 cores while the machine was fine. Auto-start, the queue, jobs, `bench nightly` and the critic now read both averages from `sysctl vm.loadavg` / `/proc/loadavg` and hold only while both are at or over the limit (the cores or `max_load`). Hold and start lines print both: `hold: load 16.5/14.2 on 14 cores (max 14)`, `memory 31% < 50%, load 4.3/5.0 on 14 cores (max 14), 1/2 workers`. See docs/agent-dispatch.md, Auto-start.
- **A dispatched worker's issue-cli takes the slug from its session, and the briefing names it once** (cli/a-dispatched-workers-issue-cli-takes-the-slug-from-its-session-and-the-briefing-names-the-slug-once). With `ISSUE_VIEWER_ISSUE_SLUG` set (every dispatched worker has it), `issue-cli check D1`, `transition --to testing`, `comment "progress: …"`, `show`, `decide`, `prove`, `clarify`, `retrospective` and the other commands on the worker's own issue leave the slug out (`output add` too, as `--issue`); the first default says so once on stderr. A slug on the command line always wins, a mistyped `<system>/<name>` stays not found, and without the env nothing changes. The worker briefing writes those commands without the slug: a 140-character slug went from 29–33 repeats to at most 3, and the sweep issue's briefing from 24,409 to 20,636 chars. See docs/CLI/overview.md#a-workers-own-slug.
- **The worker briefing's Decisions in force lists the hard ones in full and the other soft ones as a count** (factory/the-worker-briefings-decisions-in-force-lists-the-hard-ones-in-full-and-the-soft-ones-as-a-count). Every hard decision and the soft ones that are the issue's own (recorded on it, its parent, or an issue it cites) are printed, grouped under the issue that records them, without scope or quote; every other soft one is one line, `N soft ones: issue-cli decisions --in-force <slug>`. Measured on the sweep's issue: 3,182 → 1,759 chars. `start`, `show` and the lead briefs are unchanged. See docs/workflow.md#decision-ledger.
- **`transition --check <ids>` ticks the boxes and moves the issue in one call; a refusal for open boxes ends with that call** (cli/transition-check-ids-ticks-the-boxes-and-moves-the-issue-in-one-call-and-a-refused-transition-prints-that-one-line). Workers spent 3–4 turns per move on checklist → check → `transition --dry-run` → transition. `issue-cli transition <slug> --to <s> --check D1 D2` ticks the ids with check's rules (an unknown id, an owner box or a criterion its proof ticks refuses the call: nothing ticked, nothing moved), then transitions; `--dry-run --check` previews. A transition refused only for boxes the agent may tick ends with `Tick them and move in one call:` and that command, ids filled in; nothing is added when an owner box, an approval or another rule is open, and no gate command is re-run to decide. The worker briefing's steps name the one call. See docs/CLI/overview.md, "One call: tick and move".
- **A dispatched worker's `start` prints only what its briefing lacks, and the briefing drops its run-show step** (cli/a-dispatched-workers-start-prints-only-what-the-briefing-lacks-and-the-briefing-drops-its-run-show-step). In the issue's dispatched session `start` prints the claim, checklist and Next, plus the comments, decisions and memories that came since the briefing; `--full` prints everything, as outside a dispatch. Step 2 of the briefing no longer runs `show`. Measured: start 12.3k → 4.5k chars, and the 8.9k of `show` leave the first two turns (≈4.2k tokens per dispatch, in every later turn's context). See docs/CLI/overview.md#in-a-dispatched-session.
- **`release pick` keeps a released changelog section as it was tagged** (factory/release-pick-never-writes-a-second-unreleased-heading-under-a-released-changelog-section). The changelog merges with `merge=union`, so a pick never conflicted on it and git kept both sides: picking v0.62.1's fixes left `## v0.62.0 — 2026-11-15` followed by a second `## Unreleased` with picked lines, and v0.62.0's notes read as empty. A picked commit that touches the changelog now leaves the branch's changelog as it was plus that commit's new lines at the top of `## Unreleased` (opened on top if missing), skipping lines already there, and every pick is followed by a check (one `## Unreleased` at most and first, the branch's own releases as at their tags, older sections unchanged): a red check puts the branch back and names the lines. Replaying the 38 v0.62.1 picks gives one `## Unreleased` on top and a v0.62.0 section identical to its tag.
- **`spend --context` shows where the tokens come from, and Stats tracks the context floor** (cli/spend-context-shows-each-sessions-context-floor-and-per-turn-size-and-stats-tracks-the-floor). Nearly all input is cache reads: every turn re-reads its whole context, so a session costs context × turns. `issue-cli spend --context [--days N] [<box>]` prints per box and for the top 5 sessions: turns, the floor (the first turn's context: CLAUDE.md, briefing, tools), median and max context per turn, compactions and the floor's share of what was read (52% for this repo's workers over 7 days, floor 82k). A new `context` health family records the worker sessions' floor per day and backfills from the transcripts; the Stats page draws it as the Context floor trend, so a lighter briefing or launch config shows up the next day. Numbers only, as spend.
- **A lead acting on another project signs with its own name** (cli/autostart-weight-records-the-lead-that-ran-it-not-the-target-projects-lead). `lead-dark-factory` running `issue-cli --project atlas autostart weight 3 --quote …` recorded `(set by lead-atlas)`, the target project's lead. The CLI took a spawned lead's name from `--project`. It now takes it from the lead's own `ISSUE_VIEWER_PROJECT` (or its `lead-…` tmux session name when that is missing). Autostart on, pause, off and weight, `approve`'s "recorded by", critique overrides and relayed decisions now name the session that ran the command. The lead's role and what it may do are unchanged.
- **Marketing's `competitor-scan` type settles who competes into `departments/<key>/competitors.md`** (workflow/the-marketing-templates-competitor-scan-type-writes-departmentskeycompetitors-md-after-owner-review). A new type in the marketing template (system SEO; research → competitors → owner review → publish → done) reads brand.md and icp.md, runs the ICP's queries through web search only (no paid data, nothing published), and puts the competitors (Direct, Adjacent), what ranks per query, the topic gap and a proposed file on the shelf. After the owner approves (owner_only), publish writes competitors.md with Direct, Adjacent, Queries and Baseline sections. The seo-pass brief and the weekly SEO job read its queries and competitors where it exists and say so where it doesn't. Lint names a type's missing file (competitors.md, keywords.md, market.md) once an issue of that type has run to owner review. Catalog entry `competitor-scan`; see docs/departments.md, "Competitor scan".
- **`department add` and `department update` set a project's own mission and goals; lint flags a department still on the template's sample** (cli/department-add-sets-a-projects-own-mission-and-goals-and-lint-flags-a-department-still-on-the-templates-sample). `department add <template> --mission "…" --goal "<id>=<name>/<target>/<period>"` writes the project's own; without them the entry's mission and goal lines end `# sample: replace` and the add's last line is the command to set them. `department update <name> --mission/--goal/--drop-goal` changes only those lines of projects.yaml, on any department. `health lint --check departments` warns while a mission or goal still reads a bundled template's sample (today's or an earlier release's, so older entries too), naming the update command; set it to the same value to keep it (`# sample: kept`).
- **Workers start with only the MCP servers, plugins and skills their issue needs** (factory/workers-start-with-only-the-mcp-servers-and-plugins-their-issue-needs). A worker's first turn was 82k tokens (median of 103 sessions), carrying every MCP server, plugin and skill of the owner's Claude Code. An issue's Claude Code worker now starts with `--strict-mcp-config`: its department's connectors plus the servers the new `workers: mcp:` block names for its system, type or label, copied from the owner's config; claude.ai connectors are off, and `--settings` turns off every plugin and personal or project skill `workers: plugins:` / `skills:` does not keep. Leads and codex workers keep the whole setup, as does `workers: setup: full`. The dispatch report names what loaded; lint check `workers` names a server the owner's config lacks. See docs/agent-dispatch.md, "Worker setup".
- **`make linux-check PKG=` takes several packages** (factory/make-linux-check-pkg-takes-several-packages). `PKG="./internal/gitdir ./internal/shell"` reached `go test` as one argument, so the run failed on a package that doesn't exist. The script now splits PKG on blanks, globbing off, and passes each package as its own argument, with or without `RUN=`.
- **An issue's worktree is removed once its branch is on main, and `issue-cli worktrees prune` sweeps the rest** (factory/an-issues-worktree-is-removed-once-its-branch-is-on-main-and-a-daily-sweep-prunes-merged-idle-worktrees). Nothing removed a worktree after its issue shipped: this machine had 293 here (16 GB) and 117 in another project (30 GB), almost all merged. The transition to done now removes the issue's worktree after closing its session (`git worktree remove`, then `prune`; the branch stays), and keeps it, saying why, when it is dirty (untracked files count) or a pane or process is inside it. `issue-cli lead tick` removes a done issue's tree once the worker's own session has closed, and once a day runs the new `issue-cli worktrees prune [--dry-run]`: merged, clean trees idle for 3 hours go, and each kept one is listed as dirty, unmerged, live, recent or special (release-*, .ci, detached). `dispatch`, `dispatch --fresh` and the critic make a removed worktree again; the Disk use card shows the worktrees' size beside their count.
- **New department on the company floor opens adding a department** (ui/new-department-on-the-company-floor-opens-adding-a-department). Clicking the floor's + New department did nothing: its template form opened below the plot, which clips what overflows it. The plot, and a new last row of the phone list, now link to `/p/<project>/departments/new`, which with no template asked lets you pick one; Preview and Add work as before. The classic page keeps its tile.
- **The repo's CLAUDE.md is under 20 KB, and a test keeps it there** (factory/the-repos-claude-md-under-20-kb-the-per-file-structure-map-moves-to-a-doc-a-guard-keeps-it-small). It went into every session's first turn at 75 KB, about 19k of a worker's 82k-token start. The per-file map moved to docs/code-structure.md and the long doc list to docs/doc-map.md, both linked; CLAUDE.md is 16 KB, and `TestClaudeMdStaysSmall` fails over 20 KB, naming the size and where the text goes.
- **The factory notices a runaway fseventsd and tells the owner how to restart it** (factory/the-factory-notices-a-runaway-fseventsd-and-tells-the-owner-how-to-restart-it). macOS's file-event daemon ran at ~100% CPU with a 13.2 GB footprint for 16 days, counted toward mem-guard and held worker starts, and nobody saw it. The viewer now reads it every 10 minutes (`ps` and `top`, no root); over 2 GB, or over 50% CPU on two readings 10 minutes apart, Needs you gets one row (once across projects) and `issue-cli status` a `Machine:` line, each naming its share of memory and the owner's command `sudo killall fseventsd`, which nothing runs for them. The new `machine` health family records its CPU and footprint daily. See docs/workbench-files.md, "The machine family".
- **`lead tick --watch` stays quiet on holds that clear by themselves** (cli/lead-tick-watch-stays-quiet-on-holds-that-clear-by-themselves). In its first 10 minutes it woke the lead twice for nothing to do (`hold: load 15.5 on 14 cores (max 14)`, then another project's turn). Under `--watch` a hold for memory, the load, the worker cap, the gap, another project's turn, a resting subscription or another lead's start in progress now prints nothing; after an hour of held starts it says `hold: nothing started for 1h (since 09:00), now: <the hold>` once. CI red, an empty or low queue, auto-start off or paused and nothing ready still print at once, and a tick without `--watch` still says every new hold. See docs/patterns/lead-session.md, The tick.
- **`issue-cli lead tick` is the lead's 5-minute check-in, and it speaks only when the lead has to act** (cli/issue-cli-lead-tick-does-the-leads-5-minute-tick-itself-and-speaks-only-when-something-needs-the-lead). One tick runs `queue next --start` (a due job first, every hold as there), looks for done issues whose branch is not on main, reads local CI, what waits on the lead, stalled agents and takeovers, counts the queue and reads the owner's new comments, and closes finished worker sessions whose branch is on main. It prints a prefixed line (`start:`, `land:`, `ci:`, `hold:`, `queue:`, `owner:`) only for news, each thing once; a quiet tick prints nothing. `--watch [--every 5m]` loops it; `--exit-on-news` exits at the first news, so a background shell wakes the lead once per piece of news instead of a Monitor line every tick (125 wakes in one night before, 105 of them saying nothing new). The lead brief's new Tick line and `lead whoami` name it. See docs/patterns/lead-session.md, The tick.
- **Auto-start shares the machine's start gap by owner-set weights** (factory/auto-start-gives-a-higher-start-priority-project-first-claim-on-the-shared-start-gap). The one start per 5 minutes went to whichever lead ticked first, so atlas waited over an hour while dark-factory took each gap. Now `start_weight:` on a project in projects.yaml (1 when unset; `issue-cli autostart weight <n> --quote`, never a worker) shares it: a weighted round robin over the projects waiting right now, so weights 3 and 1 give 3 of every 4 starts and 1, and no waiting project waits more than the sum of the weights in starts. A project that stops asking for 2 gaps, or that its own limits hold, holds no one up and saves no turns. The hold says `hold: atlas' turn (weight 3; dark-factory next in 2 starts)`; `autostart status`, the Report's Auto-start and Queue cards and status.md show each project's weight and starts over the last day. See docs/agent-dispatch.md, "The turn between projects".
- **An unquoted flag value with a space gets a quote-it hint** (cli/an-unquoted-flag-value-with-a-space-gets-a-quote-it-hint). `append <slug> --section Test Plan` said only `unexpected argument "Plan"`; when the stray word follows a string flag's value the error now adds `A value with a space needs quotes: --section "Test Plan"`. Other unexpected arguments keep the plain message. `TestUnquotedFlagValueGetsAQuoteItHint` guards it.

- **Lint warns on a `hint:` its rule never shows** (workflow/lint-warns-on-a-hint-set-on-a-validate-rule-that-never-shows-it). `issue-cli health lint`'s new `hints` check names the edge and the rule when a validate action sets `hint:` on a rule that keeps its own fix text (`criteria_*`, `approved_for`, `decisions_*`, `critique`, `commits_reference`, `handoff_answered`). `has_output` and `outputs_approved` now show their hint, as the bundled Marketing, Sales (B2C) and Legal templates already expected.
- `issue-cli telemetry report` counts `dispatch --autostart` and `job run` holds as held, not as failures: a hold, a usage-limit rest, the machine's start gap, a job not due or waiting for the owner still exit 1, now logged as `hold` like `queue next --start`'s (cli/telemetry-counts-holds-from-dispatch-autostart-and-job-run-as-holds).
- **A Ctrl-C test passes under `scripts/under-load.sh`** (factory/scriptsunder-load-sh-passes-sigint-to-the-command-it-runs). The script started its command as a background job of a non-interactive sh, so the command and every child had SIGINT ignored and could not trap it: `TestLibCleanupRunsOnInterrupt` failed under it when run as a test binary (`go test` hid it, since cmd/go resets SIGINT). The command now starts with SIGINT and SIGQUIT at their default, an INT or TERM sent to the script goes on to the command as the same signal, and Ctrl-C still stops the hogs and the command. `TestUnderLoadCommandSeesSigintAtTheDefault` guards it.
- **The CEO chat shows the YOLO band while YOLO is on** (ui/the-ceo-chat-page-shows-the-yolo-band-while-yolo-is-on). `/p/<project>/lead` was the one project page without it: talking to the CEO, the owner could not see that gates were passing without them. It now draws the same hazard band under the header (one line on a phone, opening Extend and Turn off), and the "N to review after the fact" count while passes wait.
- **The CEO chat shows a command waiting on a permission prompt, or refused as risky, as it happens** (ui/the-ceo-chats-conversation-shows-a-command-waiting-on-a-permission-prompt-or-blocked-as-risky-as-it-happens). Before, the Conversation said only "working" while a lead waited on a permission prompt or the auto mode classifier stopped a command. Claude Code's permission prompt ("Do you want to …?") is now drawn at the end of the Conversation, with the tool, the command and one button per choice, and the working line says *Waiting on a permission prompt: Bash · <command>*. A tool call that the auto mode classifier or a built-in safety check refused shows under the CEO's turn as a red *blocked* item with the command and the reason. It is read from the transcript, so it stays after the pane scrolls. A menu option the TUI wraps onto two lines now counts as one option.
- **A command that names `--project` after another global flag gets no second one** (cli/issue-cli-lines-that-already-name-project-after-another-flag-get-no-second-project). Briefings and hints added `--project <slug>` unless it came straight after `issue-cli `, so `issue-cli --config X --project Y show s` read `issue-cli --project p --config X --project Y show s`. `tracker.InjectProject` now reads the global flags before the command (`--config`, `--project`, `--json`, `--debug`) the way issue-cli parses them.
- **`prove --step` says when the step belongs to another issue's session** (cli/prove-step-says-when-the-step-belongs-to-another-issues-session). Proof reads the runs of the issue's own playtest sessions only, so a step link to another issue's session step was saved but stayed "not run" for good, and its hint sent the worker to run a session whose runs never count. `prove --step` now refuses a step id that only another issue's session has, naming each such session and its issue, and suggests `proves:` on a step of the issue's own session or `--value` with the other session's result. An id no session has yet is still saved.

- **A release whose changelog section is over GitHub's limit is still published** (factory/the-release-marker-action-shortens-release-notes-over-githubs-125000-character-limit). GitHub refuses a release body over 125,000 characters, and v0.62.0's section was 136,306, so the Release Marker action failed with HTTP 422. Its notes now come from `scripts/release-notes.sh`: a section over 120,000 bytes goes out as each entry's bold title (an entry without one keeps its first sentence) and a link to the changelog at the tag; if even that is over, the list stops at the limit and says how many entries are left. A smaller section goes out in full, as before.

## v0.62.0 — 2026-11-15
- **No test file is over 1000 lines** (factory/test-files-over-1000-lines-are-split-per-topic). The six largest `_test.go` files (tracker's `workflow_test.go` and `issue_test.go`, issue-cli's `cmd_subcommands_test.go` and `main_test.go`, the viewer's `handlers_dispatch_test.go`, checks' `lib_test.go`) move their tests unchanged into files by topic; every package lists the same tests as before.
- **The nine production Go files over 1000 lines are split along their seams** (factory/the-biggest-production-go-files-are-split-along-their-seams-with-no-behaviour-change). `tracker/issue.go`, `cmd_bench.go`, `cmd_memory.go`, the viewer's `stats_dashboard.go`, `status.go`, `timeline.go` and `report.go`, `playtest/scenario.go` and `bench/run.go` gave whole declarations to 13 new files beside them (body edits, frontmatter writes and checkboxes; bench run and report; status markdown; …). Code only moved: no behaviour changes, and the largest production file is now 997 lines.
- **`make test` starts the viewer, issue-cli and bench packages first** (factory/make-test-starts-the-viewer-and-issue-cli-packages-first). go test runs the packages it is given in the order they are named, and `internal/viewer`, named only through `./...`, started 26–55 s after the first package. The Makefile now names `TEST_FIRST` (those three) before `./...`, each still run once: the viewer starts with the first package, and the unit wall time fell by 87, 2 and 51 s over three paired runs (little when `cmd/issue-cli` is the last to finish).
- **The remaining packages' test stand-ins come from the shared warmed helper, and guided checks' `stand_in` keeps its own** (factory/the-remaining-packages-test-stand-ins-come-from-the-shared-warmed-helper). checks, bench, tracker, proof, playtest, the viewer and issue-cli write their stand-ins through `standin.Write` (a body with its own `#!` line keeps it), with per-test paths read from `$0` or the environment so each body is the same run to run; `TestAdoptedTestsWriteStandInsThroughTheHelper` covers them, and a mode that is no stand-in says `not a stand-in:` beside it. lib.sh's `stand_in` now links each distinct script from a warmed copy under `$TMPDIR/dark-factory-standins` too (records found from `$0`), so a guided check's stand-in starts at once. `TestFindBashPrefersAVersionFourOrNewer` 0.88 s → 0.02 s; the checks package 28 s → 11 s quiet, 59 s → 16 s under `scripts/under-load.sh`.
- **The viewer parses its templates once per process** (ui/newserver-parses-the-templates-once-per-process-and-clones-them). `NewServer` parsed all 102 page templates every time, and the viewer's tests build hundreds of servers. The parse now happens once, and each server gets its own clone with its `sidebar` func bound to that server only. A test subset's parse CPU went from 0.27 s to 0.01 s. `TestTemplatesParsedOncePerProcess` counts the parses and `TestServerTemplateFuncsBindToTheirServer` checks the binding.
- **The Updates page lists tmux sessions once per request** (ui/the-updates-page-asks-tmux-once-per-request-not-once-per-proposal). Each proposal's card asked `tmux has-session` for its install agent (~35 calls a page, about 4 s across a test subset). One session listing now answers every card, so a request makes at most 2 tmux calls (list-sessions and list-windows, shared with the rest of the page through the viewer's 500 ms reading); `TestUpdatesAsksTmuxOncePerRequest` counts them.
- **tmuxguard's real-CLI guard tests run in parallel** (factory/tmuxguards-real-cli-subtests-run-in-parallel-and-the-package-takes-under-12s). `TestNoTestReachesARealAgentCLI`'s eight child runs, and the two sibling child-run tests, each have their own folders and tmux server, so they run at once: the test goes from 8.5 s (22.5 s under load) to about 1.3 s, and `internal/tmuxguard` from 14.3 s to 2.4 s.
- **A folder outside any git repository is known as one without running git** (factory/a-folder-outside-any-git-repository-is-known-as-one-without-running-git). In a folder with no repository (a fixture board in a temp folder) every git call failed after ~18 ms. `gitdir.Outside` now checks the folder and its parents for a `.git` or a bare repository with stats only, and the shared git runners (tracker, checks, privacy, gitmain, gitfile, the viewer's tag and branch reads, the milestone) answer "not a git repository" without starting git. Nothing is kept, so a `.git` made later is seen on the next call; a set `GIT_DIR` still asks git. The viewer's Status/Report/Needs/Station/... test subset went from 969 failing git calls to 26 and from 38.9 s to 17.0 s.
- **A pass over many issues derives each workflow scope once** (workflow/workflowmemo-derives-each-department-type-and-system-workflow-once-per-pass). `WorkflowMemo.ForIssue` cloned the config twice per issue (ForType, then ForSystem); each department's workflow now keeps a `ScopeMemo` that derives a (type, system) scope once and hands each issue a light copy with its own decision holds, and an issue off its path its own rescued one. `ForIssue` clones once, and lint's reports check scopes through the memo too. The health page spends 0.7% of its time cloning instead of 4%; its time is lint and the header (D-1).
- **The Health page's Lint section reuses a recent lint run** (ui/the-health-pages-live-lint-section-reuses-a-recent-lint-run). Every `/health` load ran `issue-cli health lint`'s checks again (~0.5 s on the dark-factory board). The viewer now keeps the last run per project for 30 seconds: a reload inside that window reuses it, and the first load after it gets the last run while a new one is built in the background, so an edit shows by the next load. On that board a repeat `/health` went to 117–170 ms. A scenario viewer still runs lint on every load.
- **Test stand-ins pay macOS's first-exec check once per distinct script** (factory/test-stand-ins-pay-macoss-first-exec-check-once-per-distinct-script-not-once-per-test). `internal/standin.Write` keeps each distinct stand-in body once in a cache folder named by its sha256, runs it once there and hard-links it in; memguard's, the socials rig's and agentrun's stand-ins go through it, with what differs between tests read from the environment. The socials rig also puts a no-op `sleep` on PATH for the Marketing template's ready step. `TestRead` 1.5 s → 0.3 s, the socials setup test 13.4 s → 1.3 s. `TestAdoptedTestsWriteStandInsThroughTheHelper` keeps those packages on the helper.
- **An issue's decisions are parsed once per file version** (ui/decisions-are-parsed-once-per-issue-file-version-not-once-per-index-build). Every build of the decision index (Needs you's expired decisions, the challenges, connections, lint) parsed every body's D-n lines again; the parse is now kept with the cached issue file, so a warm page parses none (a warm `/p/dark-factory/company` went from 159ms to 93ms). `LoadCounts.DecisionParses` counts them and `TestPagesLoadTheBoardOnce` wants none on a warm page.
- **Pages that count owner checks read each guided check script once** (ui/pages-that-count-owner-checks-read-each-guided-check-script-once-per-request-not-once-per-issue-per-worktree). Status, the board, Needs you and Crew asked every issue in owner-test which worktree holds its `scripts/checks/*.sh`, and each ask read every script in every worktree (on a board of 93 such issues and 33 worktrees, ~30k reads per page). A script's header is now kept by its size and mtime until it changes, and in the viewer each folder is listed once per page. On that board the second request went from 1.1 s to 95 ms (Status), 1.15 s to 72 ms (board), 0.62 s to 125 ms (Needs you) and 0.62 s to 138 ms (Crew).
- **The shape family measures shell functions without reading glob stars as comments** (factory/the-shape-family-measures-shell-functions-without-reading-glob-stars-as-comments). The brace scanner read `//` and `/*…*/` as comments in shell too, so the glob `*/.worktrees/*` in lib.sh opened a "comment" to the next `*/` and `_ck_tmp_base` (5 lines) measured 345, the longest function in the snapshot. In shell only `#` starts a comment, at a word start (`${#x}` stays a length); `${x//a/b}`, `*/` globs and URLs are text.
- **docs/CLI/overview.md's Commands table lists every command** (cli/docsclioverview-mds-commands-table-lists-every-command). `affected`, `agents`, `claim`, `codemap`, `dispatch-prompt`, `done`, `help`, `next`, `search`, `stats`, `unclaim` and `version` had no row, and `init` / `upgrade` were only named in prose; each now has one, worded from its `--help` short line. `TestCommandsTableListsEveryCommand` fails for a registered command the table has no row for, and for a row naming no command or alias.
- **The short-SHA guard catches a `%.7s` format verb** (cli/the-short-sha-guard-catches-a-7s-format-verb). `check-script`'s "removed in" line cut its commit with `%.7s` instead of `gitdir.ShortSHA`; it now goes through `ShortSHA`, and `TestOneShortSHA` flags any `%.Ns` verb in a string literal (`%%` escapes aside).
- **The API overview names only functions that exist** (ui/docsapioverview-md-names-only-functions-that-exist-on-main): docs/API/overview.md said `tmuxHasSession`, `RecordLead` and `EpicBase`, gone from the code; it now names `agentrun.HasSession`, `LeadSpec` / `DepartmentLeadSpec`, and says `IssueWorktree` starts `work/<slug>` from HEAD with no special branch for epics.
- **Recently changed on the memory page and `telemetry report --since` count calendar days** (factory/recently-changed-on-the-memory-page-and-telemetry-report-since-count-calendar-days). Both cut at now minus n×24h and now start at `period.DaysBack`, midnight of the date n days back, so an entry dated exactly n days back stays in on the day after a DST change. `--since 30d` can now reach up to a day further back. `TestOneDayWindow` also catches `time.Duration(n) * 24 * time.Hour`, with each span that isn't a date window named by file and text.
