- **Every test binary runs git with `maintenance.auto` and `gc.auto` off** (factory/every-test-binary-runs-git-with-maintenance-auto-and-gc-auto-off). `internal/tmuxguard`, which every `TestMain` calls, now adds both as `GIT_CONFIG_*` entries after any the caller set, so no test's git leaves a detached maintenance run writing `.git/objects` while `t.TempDir` cleans up ("directory not empty"). About 50 test files that ran git without the flags are covered without a change.
- **Printed hints paste into a shell unchanged** (cli/printed-issue-cli-tmux-and-git-commands-quote-their-values-with-shell-quote): fix, undo and next-step lines (`issue-cli check <slug> <box text>`, `transition --to`, `dispatch --force --quote`, the validations' `append --section`, lint fixes, the post-mortem brief's `git log --grep`, the takeover's `tmux kill-session -t`) wrote values in Go's double quotes or a hand-written `'%s'`, so a box text with a backticked command or `$HOME` ran or expanded when pasted. Every value now goes through `shell.Quote`: bare when plain (`--to testing`), else single-quoted (`--to 'in progress'`). `--project` is no longer written inside a single-quoted value, and `TestOneShellQuote` fails on `%q`, `'%s'` or `"%s` in a printed `issue-cli`, `tmux` or `git` command.
- **Whole-file writes no longer share a fixed temp name** (factory/whole-file-writes-go-through-gitfile-writeatomic-with-a-unique-temp-name). The benchmark file, traces, the critic's raw outputs, the socials compose override and the lead flush note were written to `<path>.tmp` and renamed, so two writers of one file could clobber each other's temp file; they, the clilog scrub and the memory log rewrite now go through `gitfile.WriteAtomic` (a unique `os.CreateTemp` name) with the modes they had. `TestOneTempName` keeps a fixed `path + ".tmp"` name out of non-test code.
- **The one-primary-action guard holds six more pages** (ui/the-one-primary-action-guard-holds-the-pages-already-within-the-rule). YOLO review, Health, Decisions, the Report, Company and Postmortems each get a `buttons-*.yaml` playtest session holding the page at today's count of visible actions with `one_primary`. docs/UI/workshop-board.md now lists all eleven guarded pages with their counts, all measured by the playtest `buttons` check at 1440x900 on each session's scenario board.
- **Every Workshop Board page marks its title and is checked on a phone** (ui/every-workshop-board-page-carries-wb-title-and-is-checked-by-phone-fold). The docs reader, the retro sorting room, the output reader, Crew across projects (`/crew`), Stats across projects (`/stats`), Telemetry and the CEO chat now carry `data-testid="wb.title"` on their h1 (the docs and output readers' `docs.title` / `odoc.title` became `wb.title`). A new playtest session, `phone-fold-titles`, checks at 390x844 that each title starts in the top half of the screen.
- **The Sales SendPortal example says its API paths are unchecked** (factory/the-sendportal-example-says-its-api-paths-are-unchecked): the commented `sendportal` connector `department add sales-b2c` writes now says its paths (`/api/v1/ping`, `/api/v1/campaigns/<id>/send`) and port 8000 come from SendPortal's docs, unchecked against a running install, and that `issue-cli connector check sales/sendportal` confirms them before the first send.
- **A changed department template file fails a test until its old hash is kept** (factory/a-test-fails-when-a-bundled-department-file-changes-without-its-old-hash-in-earliertexts). Changing a bundled `role.md` or job brief without its old sha256 in `earlierTexts` made `department update` call every install of the old text edited. Today's hash of each such file in every template is now pinned in `internal/depttemplate/testdata/current.sha256`, and `TestBundledFilesChangeOnlyWithTheirEarlierHash` names the three steps when one changes.
- **Telemetry counts a `queue next --start` hold as a hold, not a failed call** (cli/telemetry-counts-queue-next-starts-hold-as-a-hold-not-a-failed-call). "Nothing started from the queue" and "Not started: hold:" still exit 1 for the lead's tick, but are recorded with a new error class, `hold`, instead of `validation`. `telemetry report` leaves holds out of the failure count, the error % and the retry sequences and shows them as `N held`; the shared daily batch counts them under `errors.hold` but not in `fails`, and `hold` is in the batch's vocabulary. Before, holds gave `queue next` a 75% error rate and buried its real validation failures.
- **`replace --section` warns when it drops a `###` subheading** (cli/replace-section-warns-when-it-drops-a-subheading-the-old-section-had). A whole-section replace whose new body lacks a `###` heading the old section had used to print only `✓ Replaced section`; a checkbox-only Test Plan lost `### Automated` / `### Manual` and `has_test_plan` said so only at the next transition. Now it names each dropped heading (`⚠ the new body drops ...`), and on Test Plan adds that `has_test_plan` needs both.
- **Lint flags a done issue's Implementation SHA that is not on main** (workflow/lint-flags-a-done-issues-implementation-sha-that-is-not-on-main): the new `shas` check reads the commit refs in each done issue's `## Implementation` and warns on one that resolves to a commit the main branch does not reach. That is usually the SHA a commit had before an amend or a rebase. The fix names the landed commit's lookup (`git log --oneline main --grep '<slug>'`). One `git cat-file --batch-check` and one `git rev-list` call cover the whole board. Refs git doesn't know (another repository's commits, attachment hashes) are skipped.
- **Lint's `testids` check reads the ids from the repo, not from the build** (factory/lint-testids-reads-the-ids-from-the-repos-internaltestids-not-from-the-ids-built-into-the-binary). It asked the list compiled into issue-cli, so a build older than the tree called new ids unknown: 17 `socials.*` warnings with v0.61.0-rc.2. It now parses the project's `internal/testids/*.go` on disk, and its "did you mean" reads the same list.
- **A done queue item with no SHA gets it once its branch lands on main** (cli/a-done-queue-item-with-no-sha-gets-its-merge-sha-once-the-branch-lands-on-main). An item marked done while `work/<slug>` was ahead of main (`not on main`), or done by reconcile, kept no SHA forever. Now `queue`, `queue next` and every queue change record the work tip once main contains it and save it; `queue done N` does the same and says so (`✓ Item 220 done at 23d4e8f: work/<slug> is on main`), and while the tip is still ahead prints `isn't on main yet` instead of ✓.
- **The Umami examples in Marketing and Sales are read-only rest connectors** (factory/the-marketing-and-sales-b2c-umami-examples-are-read-only-rest-connectors): the commented `umami` connector `department add marketing` and `department add sales-b2c` write was a `curl` tool, so a call could send any request despite its "read-only" note. It is now `kind: rest` on `url: http://localhost:3000/api` with the key as an `Authorization: "Bearer keychain:umami-api-key"` header and only `get: [/websites/*/stats]`, the path the visits goals read: issue-cli makes the GETs itself and no write call is reachable. The weekly SEO pass and weekly funnel briefs, the examples' comments and docs/departments.md give the one call, `issue-cli connector exec <dept>/umami -- '/websites/<id>/stats?startAt=<ms>&endAt=<ms>'`. An installed entry you already uncommented keeps its `curl` tool until you edit it.
- **`process workflow --file` shows a candidate workflow file before it replaces the live one** (cli/issue-cli-process-workflow-file-shows-a-candidate-workflow-file-before-it-replaces-the-live-one): `process workflow --file <path>` and `process transitions --file <path>` read any workflow file without writing anything: its lifecycle, every type's path and gates, a load error naming the file (exit 1), and the file checks `health lint` runs on a workflow file (an unknown key with its nearest name, the template stamp, placeholders). `--workflow` (an alias for `--system`) with a path in it is refused and points to `--file` and `--department`, instead of naming a system overlay that doesn't exist. docs/departments.md's splice steps now check a `marketing.next.yaml` copy with `--file` before moving it over the live file.
- **Lint warns on a rest connector's `get:` that opens a whole level of the API** (factory/lint-warns-on-a-rest-connectors-get-pattern-that-opens-a-whole-level-of-the-api): a `get:` entry whose first segment is a wildcard (`/*`, `/*/status`, `/[a-z]*`) matches every route at the API's first level, including any that changes state on GET. The `departments` check now warns on it, with a fix naming the first segment (`get: /<resource>/status`). Postiz's `social-analytics` entry, wildcards only at the end, stays clean; `connector exec` still allows the pattern.
- **`make validate` checks the code map before the suite** (workflow/make-validate-checks-the-code-map-in-a-cheap-step-before-the-full-suite): a new cheap step, `make codemap-check`, runs `TestEngineMapHasNoNewUpwardImport` before `test`, so a new `internal/` package missing from `workbench.contexts` shows in seconds. Its message names each unmapped package and says to add it to a context's `packages:` under `workbench.contexts` in workflow.yaml.
- **Department template tests no longer break when a template grows** (factory/department-template-tests-find-entries-by-name-and-count-files-written-from-the-template): the Marketing and Video connector checks find each connector by name instead of by position and count, and the viewer's Add tests take "N files written" from the plan Add makes instead of a literal. A connector example or a `files:` entry added to a bundled template turns none of them red.
- **An unknown command names the command that does the job** (cli/an-unknown-command-names-the-command-that-does-the-job): `unknown command: X` now adds `Did you mean: issue-cli <command> — <its short help>` when one is near: a short hint table for words that name a command called otherwise (`body`, `view`, `read` → `show`; `list-comments`, `thread` → `comments`; `move` → `transition`; `tick` → `check`; …), the one command a name starts (`trans`), a known command in another case (`Comments`), or a typo within two edits (one under five letters). With nothing near it prints no guess. `Run: issue-cli help` still ends it.
- **`create` and `update` take `--parent`, and `update` takes `--body-file`** (cli/issue-cli-create-and-update-take-parent-and-update-takes-body-file): `--parent <epic>` writes `parent:` and refuses a slug that isn't an epic on the board (`type: epic`, or already another issue's parent), or the issue itself, writing nothing. Before this the only way was `set-meta --key parent`, and the usage log had 27 `unknown_flag --parent` refusals. `update --body-file <path|->` reads the new body raw from a file or stdin, as `create` and `append` do.
- **The live-pane routing tests no longer miss their wait for a fresh stand-in** (factory/flaky-under-load-the-live-pane-limit-and-stop-point-routing-tests-miss-their-10-s-wait-for-the-stand-in): macOS checks a freshly written executable on its first exec, now and then for over 10 s, and the pane stayed blank past the tests' 10 s wait (local CI red at 723407c). Each stand-in in internal/agentrun is now run once before a pane starts it (`writeStandIn`), and a guard test refuses an executable written any other way there.
- **A hint on a legacy validate rule is shown when the rule fails** (workflow/a-hint-on-a-legacy-validate-rule-is-shown-when-the-rule-fails): a `hint:` on `has_comment_prefix`, `section_checkboxes_checked` and the other original legacy rules (`body_not_empty`, `has_checkboxes`, `section_has_checkboxes`, `has_assignee`, `all_checkboxes_checked`, `has_test_plan`) was never printed. It now replaces the built-in fix, templated like the structured rules' hints, in the transition error and in `transition --dry-run`, so the `docs:` and `report:` hints in workflow.yaml reach the worker. What is wrong (the missing comment, the open boxes) still shows first.
- **Docs: the places a new lint check touches** (factory/docs-the-places-a-new-lint-check-touches): docs/CLI/overview.md has an "Adding a lint check" list (the registry, `boardChecks`, the guard tests, the doc lists, Settled rules, the lint scenario); the lint family list in docs/workbench-files.md now names every check, and `TestLintChecksListedInDocs` keeps it so.
- **`output show` prints where the file is, and `--text` prints a markdown output** (cli/output-show-prints-where-the-file-is-and-can-print-a-markdown-outputs-text): `issue-cli output show <id>` adds a `File:` line with the absolute path of the newest version (marked `(missing)` when it is gone), so a worker in any folder can open it. `output show <id> --text` prints a markdown output's newest version and nothing else; any other output refuses, naming its path or link.
- **The storage health family measures a big checkout in a few seconds** (factory/the-storage-health-family-measures-a-12-gb-checkout-in-a-few-seconds): the family used to walk the main checkout in one serial walk. On a 13.7 GB checkout that took 23 s, past the 20 s family timeout, so snapshots recorded no disk use. It now reads folders with a pool of 8 readers and takes about 4 s, and a cancelled or timed-out snapshot stops the walk. The sizes and metric names are the same.
- `bench fixture init --config <file>` prints its two next commands with `--config <file>` (the absolute path, shell-quoted), so `issue-cli --config <file> --project bench-fixture bench candidates` runs as printed instead of failing with project_resolution against the default projects.yaml. Without `--config` the lines are unchanged (cli/bench-fixture-inits-next-line-carries-the-config-it-was-given).
- **`memory candidates` reads `report:` comments and shelf reports' Known items** (cli/memory-candidates-reads-report-comments-too): a `report:` comment is a candidate like `progress:`, and so is each list item under a `Known` or `Corrections` heading of a markdown output on the shelf linked to the issue (`output <id> · known`), so a report-only run's facts can be remembered. The empty-state line names `report:`.
- **`make linux-check` can run one package or test** (factory/make-linux-check-can-run-one-package-or-test): `make linux-check PKG=./internal/x RUN=TestY` runs only `go test <pkg> -run <test> -count=1` in the ubuntu:24.04 container (either variable alone works), says the run was a subset, and still ends on `linux-check: PASS` or `linux-check: FAIL (go test)`. Without them it runs CI's full steps as before.
- **The health score's flaky component is measured** (factory/the-health-scores-flaky-component-is-measured-from-local-ci-and-playtest-runs): `tests.e2e.flaky_share{window=14d}` now comes from the ux-test runs already on disk (the main checkout's and local CI's `.playtest/runs`): a step that failed and passed on one build is flaky, and the share is those steps over the steps run in 14 days. `issue-cli health --family tests` names each flaky step. With no run in the window the component still says "no data" and names `make ux-test`, never 0%. The score, /health and the Stats page stop pointing at issues that are no longer on the board.
- **Lint warns on acceptance boxes kept outside ## Acceptance Criteria** (workflow/lint-warns-on-acceptance-boxes-kept-outside-acceptance-criteria): on an open issue whose path runs `criteria_checkboxes` or `criteria_proven`, `health lint`'s `criteria` check flags boxes under an `**Acceptance:**` label or an "Extra criteria" line in another section (usually `## Brief`), once per issue. `prove` refuses those boxes, a `Proves:` trailer naming them is refused, and `criteria_proven` passed without them. The fix line is the `issue-cli append` that moves them into the criteria section, then the `replace` that drops them from where they were. A tweak's path has no criteria gate, so tweaks are not flagged.
- **One JSON write for the sidecars** (factory/the-one-json-write-guard-covers-the-stats-and-data-sidecars-and-the-goal-job-file): the stats and data sidecars and a goal job's metrics file are written through jsonfile.Write, byte for byte as before, and TestOneJSONWrite now also catches a function that marshals JSON and hands it to an atomic byte writer.
- **The post-mortem brief names only branches that exist and reads a branch's own commits** (workflow/the-post-mortem-brief-names-only-branches-that-exist-and-its-commit-range-leaves-out-mains-merged-commits): `postmortem brief` names `work/<slug>` for the issue and for each epic child only while that branch exists; a merged and deleted one gets `git log --grep <slug> main` instead. The commit range is `git log --first-parent`, so a `chore(merge)` of main no longer lists other issues' commits.
- **A criterion a test proves but a judgement step also lists is flagged before close** (workflow/a-criterion-a-test-proves-but-a-judgement-step-also-lists-is-flagged-before-close): `issue-cli prove <slug> --list` and the transition into the `criteria_proven` gate print a `Warning:` line for each unticked criterion that is the owner's only because a playtest judgement step lists it in `proves:`, while a `Proves:` trailer or a prove test/commit link proves it too, naming the step to drop `proves:` from. `prove --json` lists them under `overlaps`. A warning, not a gate.
- **A post-mortem's memory action can name the status it matters at** (factory/a-post-mortems-memory-action-can-name-the-status-it-matters-at): a `Place: memory` action may carry `At: <status>`. When the owner agrees, the memory is tied to that status as `memory add --at` ties it, so `transition` shows it to whoever enters that status (a near-duplicate that gets updated is tied too). `postmortem write` refuses a status the workflow doesn't have, and `At:` on any other place. The round checks the status again, so one dropped since leaves the action open. The brief lists `At:` with the statuses, and the post-mortem page shows it beside the place.
- **Report follow-ups that are the owner's calls stop warning in lint** (factory/report-follow-ups-that-are-the-owners-calls-stop-warning-in-lint-forever): `health lint`'s `reports` check leaves out a follow-up ending `(owner)` or under a sub-heading or lead-in line naming the owner ("Owner actions:"), counts only the rest, and warns once per report instead of once per shelf output. The report type's prompt says to mark owner items `(owner)` (catalog `report-type` v0.61.0).
- **An n/a line can name several decisions** (workflow/an-na-line-naming-several-decisions-is-read-as-na-for-each-not-as-citing-them): `n/a <ref>, <ref>…: <why>` in `## Design` sets each ref aside with the one reason (after the first, a bare `#D-n` takes the slug before it). Before, such a line didn't parse and every ref on it counted as cited, so decisions_cited passed without a reason and the diff audit read an n/a as a citation. A line that starts with `n/a` but doesn't parse now cites nothing, and decisions_cited refuses it, quoting the line and the form to write.
- A queued issue reaching done while `work/<slug>` isn't on the main branch gets no SHA instead of the main branch's head, which had nothing to do with the issue: the item is noted `not on main`, `transition` prints `✓ Queue item N marked done (not on main)`, and `queue` and the Report's Queue card show `done (not on main)`. The main branch is the project's (`ci.branch`, else the main checkout's branch), not main or master (cli/a-queue-item-marked-done-on-an-unmerged-branch-gets-no-sha-not-mains-head).
- **The clarify nudge fires only where a round belongs** (cli/the-clarify-nudge-fires-only-on-a-path-with-a-design-status-and-says-how-to-write-the-line): `transition` no longer prints "left idea with no ## Clarify" for an issue whose path has no design status, such as a tweak. Where it still prints, it names the command that writes the one-line round: `issue-cli append <slug> --section Clarify --body "Nothing to ask: <why>."` (`issue-cli clarify` only reads a round).
- **The commit-msg refusal gives one answer on an issue with no acceptance criteria** (cli/the-commit-msg-refusal-stops-contradicting-itself-on-an-issue-with-no-acceptance-criteria): a refused `Proves:` line there (`Proves: B1`) now gets only the note that no Proves: line is needed, with `issue-cli check <slug> <id>` for the box, instead of a playtest-step hint about `proves: [AC1]` followed by the note contradicting it. Issues with criteria keep the step hint. docs/Workflow/proof.md says a brief's boxes take `check`, not `Proves:`.
- **`make validate` refuses an unfinished merge** (workflow/make-validate-refuses-an-unfinished-merge-or-conflict-markers): its new first step, `merge-check`, goes red while the checkout's own git dir has a MERGE_HEAD or a tracked file has a line starting with a conflict marker, and names each such line as file:line. It takes about a tenth of a second. A fixture that needs marker lines goes on `MERGE_CHECK_ALLOW`. CLAUDE.md's Validation paragraph lists every step, and a test keeps that list in step with `VALIDATE_STEPS`.
- **A red slow tier shows in the Local CI line and plain `ci status`** (workflow/the-workers-local-ci-line-and-plain-ci-status-say-when-the-slow-tier-is-red): the Local CI line in `start` and the dispatch briefing adds `Local CI tier slow: red at <sha> (<age> ago): <failed> — inherited from main, not caused by your branch` while that tier's newest run is red, so a worker whose `make ux-test` fails no longer reads green and debugs main's red as its own. Green tiers keep the line as it was. Plain `ci status` prints a `tier <t>:` line for every tier with a run, without `--tier`.
- **`transition` shows the lead's comments posted since your last move** (cli/transition-shows-the-leads-comments-posted-since-the-worker-last-moved-the-issue): `transition` and `--dry-run` print every `lead:` comment newer than the issue's last move first, under `== From the lead since your last move ==` (every one when no move is recorded, never the caller's own); `--json` carries them as `lead_comments`, and a real move warns on each with no reply after it. Nothing refuses over one. Scope a lead adds after dispatch now reaches the worker at its next step.
- Connect socials: a network's 15-minute sign-in hold is ended only by its own timer: a timer firing as a new Connect replaces the hold no longer stops Postiz under the new sign-in. Its test no longer races a 300 ms hold on a loaded machine (ui/flaky-under-load-testsocialsconnectholdspostizforthesigninthenstopsit-races-its-300-ms-postiz-hold).
- docs/departments.md has the steps for bringing a template change into a live department workflow by hand (a dated backup, the bundled file beside yours, splice, then `process workflow --department`, `process transitions --department --type` and `health lint --check department,departments,schema,owner-only`), and says why there is no dry run of a candidate file.
- **Postiz analytics for Marketing's social measure step, through a read-only REST connector** (factory/postiz-analytics-for-marketings-measure-step-a-read-only-rest-connector-on-the-same-per-run-lease). There is a new connector kind, `rest`. `issue-cli connector exec <dept>/<name> -- <path>…` makes one GET per path under the connector's `url:`, in issue-cli itself, with its `headers:` secrets, and prints one JSON line each (`{path, status, body}`). GET is the only method, and a path outside its `get:` allow-list, or one that leaves the base (`..`, `//`, a host, an encoded `/`), is refused before anything starts. Redirects aren't followed, and a key the answer repeats prints as `[secret]`. `department add marketing` writes another commented connector, `social-analytics`: Postiz's public analytics API (per post and per channel) with the same `keychain:postiz` item, `sends: false`, on the `social` connector's `per_run:` lease (`tool: postiz`), so Postiz starts for the call and stops after unless a social run still holds it. The social type's measure prompt now reads through it. `issue-cli connectors` lists a rest connector's paths, and lint's `departments` check covers `kind: rest` and `get:`.
- `check-script` finds an issue's scripts in a worktree on any branch: when there is no `work/<slug>` worktree and the project folder holds no script for the slug, the one linked worktree whose `scripts/checks/*.sh` names the issue is its checkout (a project whose workers branch as `<system>/<short>` in `.worktrees/<short>` had to wrap check-script to get there). Two such worktrees: it runs from the one you are in, else names both and exits 1. The viewer's ▶ Run checks and Needs you's checks-due row see the same worktree (cli/check-script-cant-find-a-script-that-lives-on-the-issue-branch-in).
- **A connector's `deny_args:` reaches nested arguments** (factory/nested-deny-args-a-connector-refuses-an-argument-inside-an-array-or-object-e-g-postizs-socialpost-typenow). An argument key may be a path, `.` into objects and `[]` over every item of an array, e.g. `socialPost[].type`: the bridge refuses a call when any value there matches, naming where (`socialPost[1].type: now`), before the server sees it. Top-level keys work as before; a malformed path refuses that tool until fixed and `health lint` names it. Marketing's commented Postiz `social` connector now refuses `socialPost[].type: now` at publish, so only scheduled posts go out
- The nightly lead flush reads the lead's standing rules it is asked to look over: its worker sees the lead scope in `memory list`, `search`, `show` and `consolidate`, which no other worker does, and changes none of it. Editing, forgetting, updating or superseding a lead-scope memory is now refused to anyone but a lead or the owner; the flush names the stale ones in its progress comment for the next lead (factory/the-lead-flush-run-can-read-the-leads-pinned-rules-it-is-asked-to-tidy-or-its-brief-says-it-cant).
- **Reddit in Marketing's social channel** (factory/reddit-as-a-social-source-and-channel-read-chosen-subreddits-for-research-post-and-comment-on-them-through-owner-review). `department add marketing` writes `departments/<key>/subreddits.md`, the owner's list of subreddits with each one's self-promotion rule (`health lint`, check `departments`, warns on a row without one), and a commented read-only `reddit` connector (reddit-mcp-buddy, MIT, app-only keys from the same Keychain items Postiz uses, write tools off). Social research reads only the listed subreddits, and nothing from Reddit without the connector; the brief plans only in a listed subreddit with its rule quoted; posts go through `social` (Postiz) after the owner_only gate, and replies in others' threads are drafted for the owner to post by hand. Reddit closed new API access on 2026-10-31, so a Reddit app is the owner's step. Department templates gain `files:`, starter files written to `departments/<key>/`, never over an edited copy.
- **Connect socials from the factory** (factory/connect-socials-from-the-factory-an-owner-only-page-to-set-up-postiz-and-connect-each-network-with-the-fields-it-needs-keys-into-the-keychain). The owner's page `/p/<project>/socials/<dept>`, linked from the room's Connectors block: step 1 writes Marketing's `social` connector into projects.yaml after showing the diff and the Postiz key into Keychain; step 2 lists each network with the developer-app values it needs (into Keychain as `postiz-env/<VAR>`, passed to Postiz through a compose override that holds `${VAR}` references only), opens the network's own sign-in, connects Bluesky with an app password kept nowhere, reads connected / expired / disabled back (`<issues>/.connectors/socials.jsonl`, an expired network is a Needs you row) and disconnects. Every Postiz call runs inside the per_run lease and Postiz is stopped after; a sign-in holds it at most 15 minutes. Values go to `security -i` on stdin, never argv, a file, a log or an answer. `issue-cli connectors <dept>` prints the page's address.
- A box that starts with a status or gate name, such as `owner review → publish stays owner_only for social`, is no longer read as the owner's box: words after `Owner` that are followed by an arrow (`→`, `->`, `=>`) or by status, gate, stage, step, column or transition name the status, so `prove` and `check` tick it like any criterion. `Owner review of the page` is still the owner's (cli/an-acceptance-criterion-starting-with-the-status-name-owner-review-e-g).
- **Postiz as Marketing's social connector, stopped after every run** (factory/postiz-as-marketings-social-connector-started-per-run-and-stopped-after-every-action-proved-for-every-way-out). `department add marketing` now writes a third commented connector, `social`: Postiz over MCP on `127.0.0.1:4007` with its key as `Bearer keychain:postiz`, `sends: true`, its AI tools off, tools per status (reads at brief, none at draft or owner review, scheduling only at publish) and `per_run:` on `tool: postiz`, whose `stop:` stops every container labelled with the compose project, not only the file's services. Tests run the template's own start, ready and stop against a stand-in docker for each way a run ends (EOF, SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP, a failed start, SIGKILL then the sweep), and a guided check does one real call and finds no Postiz container left running. Postiz is the social slot's default in the partners catalog (Mixpost Lite stays listed), and lint's AGPL exception warning also finds a connector by its `per_run: tool:`, so a slot-named connector isn't missed.
- Marketing's `social` channel is live in the bundled marketing template, with a research step before the brief: research → brief → draft → owner review → publish → measure → done (system `Social`). Research reads what works on similar profiles and competitors (posts, formats, cadence, hooks) and which trends are rising, each finding with its link, read-only, through the department's `trends` and `competitors` connectors when they are on; the report goes on the shelf and the brief cites it. Catalog entry `social-channel`; docs/departments.md, "Social: research before the brief".
- **A finished worker's session closes with its issue.** The owner's Mac ran out of memory with 117 Claude Code sessions in tmux, 49 of them workers of `done` issues, some 11 days old: nothing closed a worker's session when its issue closed, only a rule in the lead's prompt. Now `transition` into a closed status (done, parked, a side exit), `done` and `close` end the issue's `agent-<slug>` and the `limited-<slug>` a takeover left (their windows in a shared `tmux_session`) and print one line: `Worker session: closed agent-cli-x (cli/x is done).` The worker moving its own issue, or one in a turn, closes when that turn ends: a detached shell waits for the pane's working line to go (at most 30 minutes), so the agent still reads the output and can add a memory. A session mem-guard paused is left. Leads and the post-mortem agent are never closed (docs/agent-dispatch.md#closing-finished-workers).
- **`issue-cli agents sweep [--dry-run]` and a viewer sweep close what is left.** The sweep closes worker sessions whose issue is closed and any `agent-*` / `limited-*` pane that is only a shell, but never one mem-guard paused, one in a turn, one a person is attached to or the caller's own; workers are refused. The viewer holding `viewer.json` runs it every 10 minutes and logs each close; a closed issue's session whose name another project's open issue holds is kept.
- **A due recurring job on its standing start starts from the lead's tick.** The lead-flush job had a standing start since 2026-11-05 and never ran: the lead's tick runs `queue next` and `autostart next`, never `job due`. Both now print `⚠ job <dept>/<job> is due (<period>) on its standing start: issue-cli job run <dept>/<job> --scheduled`, and `queue next --start` starts that run first, through the same checks as `job run --scheduled` (mem-guard, the worker cap, the machine-wide gap, one lock), before any queue item. Nothing runs on a timer.
- Test only: a test binary removes the private tmux folders a killed run left (`tmg-*` and `tmx-*` older than 2 hours, the check-script library's marked `ckt-*` older than a day), killing each one's server by its own `-S` socket. Seven such servers had lived 8 to 14 days.
- **On a phone every Workshop Board page shows its title in the first screen** (ui/phone-view-looks-right-one-line-yolo-band-one-row-header-the-company-floor-as-a-readable-list-on-a-phone). At 640px and below the header is one row: ‹ one level up, the page's name, the waiting count as "! N" and ☰; YOU, the counts and Board | Report open from the ☰, and the hall's tabs stay one row under the header (no tap to switch hall views). The company page has the same header. The YOLO band is one line ("YOLO on · N passed · until …") that opens Extend and Turn off on a tap. The company floor shows its rooms as a list (open, to review, Chat; Product: Hall) instead of the plan scaled to a third. Above 640px nothing changes. The playtest `size` check takes `max_top`, and `playtest/sessions/phone-fold.yaml` checks each page's title starts in the top half of a 390x844 screen.
- **An SEO baseline type in Marketing, and a walk-through for the owner** (factory/seo-baseline-in-the-marketing-workflow-technical-basics-waitlist-pre-rendered-articles-measurement). The marketing template gains `seo-baseline` (system SEO, once per site: brief → draft → owner review → publish → measure → done). Its brief writes the owner's answers to five questions under `## Owner answers` and doesn't wait for brand.md (technical, not content); brief → draft appends `## Technical basics` (sitemap with lastmod, robots.txt naming the allowed bots, JSON-LD, an Open Graph image, llms.txt, noindex by header on the app subdomain), `## Waitlist` (a no-JS form, consent and privacy policy, unsubscribe, spam limits, N/A when the traffic goes elsewhere), `## Pre-rendered pages` (a static site generator, an article template the build enforces, IndexNow on deploy) and `## Measurement`, each box naming what checks it, plus `## Owner steps`: `(owner)` boxes for Search Console, the service account, Bing Webmaster Tools, the CDN's AI-bot blocking and the deploy, which no agent can tick. draft → owner review needs the four sections and the change set on the shelf (`has_output: site`); publish → measure needs the owner's steps. An eval scores its runs, role.md tells Marketing to file it first, installed Marketings get it from `workflow upgrade --department marketing` (catalog `seo-baseline`), and docs/seo-baseline.md walks the owner through it.
- **Fewer buttons: one primary action per card, the rest under ⋯ More** (ui/fewer-buttons-one-primary-action-per-page-and-card-the-rest-under-more-the-rule-written-down-and-guarded). Each card or row on the Workshop Board now shows one primary action and at most one other. The Station's title keeps Rename, Classic view, Edit in nvim and Delete… under ⋯ More, and its Agent card shows Open in tmux (live) or Dispatch, with the other agent, Send the prompt, the custom actions and the dispatch prompt in its menu (11 → 7 buttons). Needs you rows show their primary and ⋯ with Review and Chat (16 → 11). The CEO chat shows Attach, Send, Interrupt and More at every width, with Send now, the mode switch, the other controls and the key pad under More (17 → 6). Memory rows show Save and ⋯ with Pin and Delete. An Updates card's Decline sits inside Details. Nothing was removed. The menu works without script, and on a phone it opens as a sheet at the bottom of the screen. The rule is in docs/UI/workshop-board.md. The playtest runner gains a `buttons: {max, within?}` check and a `one_primary` layout rule, and `buttons-*` sessions hold each page at its count.
- **Under YOLO, a design-first issue waits for the owner at the design gate** (workflow/under-yolo-an-issue-labelled-design-first-waits-for-the-owner-at-the-design-gate). Label an issue `design-first` and, with YOLO on, its gate out of the design status (`in design → backlog`) stays the owner's: `transition` refuses it and says why, `--dry-run` keeps the approval among what's missing, nothing is written on the issue, and it waits in Needs you like any gate. Its later gates pass as usual, and on a path with no design status the label holds nothing. `yolo status`, the turn-on dialog and the briefing's YOLO rule list it among what YOLO never passes (docs/Workflow/yolo.md).
- **Keyword research for positioning in Marketing** (factory/keyword-researcher-for-positioning-in-marketing-free-data-first-clusters-by-intent-a-positioning-line-on-the-shelf). The marketing template gains a `keyword-research` type in research mode: research (free data first: Search Console through the `openseo` connector, autocomplete, competitors' pages, forums and reviews; paid volume only through `openseo` with its Keychain-held key) → shortlist (`## Keywords`: clusters in the ICP's words with intent, a difficulty guess and our fit, own, contest or leave; `## Positioning`: one statement and the clusters it rests on; the draft on the shelf, `has_output: report`) → owner review (`owner_only`, the newest shelf version approved) → publish (`departments/<key>/keywords.md`, brand.md and icp.md untouched) → done. It gates nothing: the `piece` and `seo-pass` briefs read keywords.md where it exists. An eval scores its runs, `role.md` lists keywords.md, and installed Marketings get it from `workflow upgrade --department marketing` (catalog `keyword-research`). A worked example on a placeholder plumber is the test fixture and is in docs/departments.md.
- **The hall shows and edits a department's own workflow** (ui/the-hall-shows-and-edits-a-department-workflow-like-the-software-one-marketing-first-every-types-path-and-gates). `/p/<project>/hall?department=marketing` draws Marketing's `workflows/marketing.yaml` like Product's line: its stations, gates and issues, every station page and Edit form keeping the department. A switcher under the title moves between Product's and each department's workflow, and **Paths by type** shows every type's path (foundation, market-size, piece, seo-pass, report…) with its gates and an "only you, even in YOLO" mark on owner_only ones, on Product's hall too. Editing a department file git does not track writes it in place, checked and read back, and commits nothing; the form says so. The Edit form no longer drops `owner_only:` or `hint:` from a gate it keeps. `department show` prints the hall's link, and the floor's department room links to it.
- **Every Workshop Board page works on a phone** (ui/phone-view-works-on-every-workshop-board-page-no-sideways-scroll-16px-gutters-big-tap-targets-tables-as-rows). At 390px no page scrolls sideways any more: the hall and the sidebar Board stack their stations in path order with each gate as a 44px bar (they were 794px wide), /crew no longer widens to its longest agent name (760px), and headers with goal chips fit (417px). The header is one row (logo, crumbs, Waiting on you, YOU, ☰), and the ☰ opens the counts, goal chips and YOLO switch under it, with no script. Value tables (Stats, Health's Departments, the Report's benchmark) become one card per row with their column names, the Report, Stats and Health keep 16px gutters, the company floor's map no longer leaves an empty band under it, and pills, tabs and row links are 44px tall. Above 640px nothing changes. Playtest sessions `phone-pages-*` check every page at 390x844.
- **The nightly slow tier is green again** (ui/the-slow-tier-is-green-again-crew-all-tp5-tail-and-nudge-sheets-and-department-templates-tp8-marketing-on-updates). Two playtest steps were red: department-templates/TP8 still expected Marketing's 2 goals after the third (visits) came in, and crew-all/TP5 started its stand-in agent pane as `cat`, which tmux runs through `$SHELL -c`, so under load the Crew page could catch the pane as a bare shell and show the agent as exited with no Nudge. The sessions start their panes as `cat -` (exec'd directly), and the playtest guide says so.
- **`issue-cli create` takes the body in the same call** (cli/gtm-weekly-plan-brief-and-workflow-guidance-tell-the-agent-to-run-issue-cli). `create --body "..."` (or `--body-file <path>`, `-` for stdin, raw as `append` reads it) writes the issue's first text above its status template. GTM's week-plan guidance installed before the bundled text was fixed still says `create … --body`, and `department update` keeps a `workflows/gtm.yaml` that `workflow upgrade` changed, so those agents hit an unknown flag and had to create, then append.
- **A partner's licence is linted, and no partner is vendored** (factory/a-partners-licence-is-linted-and-no-partner-is-vendored). Check `departments` warns on a connector, or a `partner:` block, that the partners catalog knows as AGPL until its entry names `exception: <slug>#D-<n>`: the owner's decision on that project's board, in force and with their words. The warning names the tool, its licence and the rule, and points at the permissive rows (`department partners --slot`); a tool the catalog doesn't know raises nothing. Catalog rows gain `marks:` (paths only that tool's checkout has, required on AGPL rows), and `TestTrackedFilesHoldNoPartnerFile` reads them for every tool instead of one hard-coded list; `PARTNER_CHECKOUTS` names checkouts for the byte-for-byte comparison.
- **`issue-cli department partners` lists the options with licence, cost and needs** (factory/issue-cli-department-partners-lists-the-options-with-licence-cost-and-needs). A public partners catalog bundled as `cmd/issue-cli/templates/partners.yaml`: one row per outside tool by slot (analytics, email, seo, social, video, support), with its shape, licence, the LICENSE file read and the date, upstream, what it needs, whether it runs always or per run, and whether it costs money in words, never a price. AGPL rows name the decision recording their exception or say none; social lists Postiz and Mixpost Lite with no default. `department partners [--slot <slot>] [--json]` lists it; templates name their slots in `partner_slots:`, and `department add` ends with a Partner options block (`partner_options` in `--json`) and writes nothing more. Listing turns nothing on.
- **SendPortal as a commented email connector in Sales** (factory/sendportal-as-a-commented-email-connector-in-sales). `department add sales-b2c` also writes a `sendportal` connector into the entry commented out, below `umami`: a `kind: tool` running `curl` against SendPortal's REST API (MIT, self-hosted), `SENDPORTAL_API_TOKEN: keychain:sendportal-api-token`, a `check:` on `/api/v1/ping`, `sends: true` and `needs_approved_output: true`, so `connector exec` refuses it until the owner approves the issue's newest output. Its comment warns that double opt-in is unverified in SendPortal: check a list collected under a new consent version before a campaign goes to it. The `launch` type's publish prompt names the connector and stops when the department has none. The mail provider stays the owner's choice.
- **The social channel slot gets its prompts, and every commented slot is tested** (factory/the-social-channel-slot-gets-its-prompts-and-every-commented-slot-is-tested). The marketing workflow's commented channel slots (`ppc`, `social`, `cold-outreach`) each sit between `# BEGIN channel <name>` / `# END channel <name>` markers, once under `evals:` and once under `types:`, so a channel is uncommented alone and comes with its eval. `social` carries commented brief, draft, publish and measure prompts that name only the department's `social` connector: the networks' rules read at brief, no connector call at draft, only the approved posts scheduled at the approved dates (never "now" unless the owner says so) with each post listed under `## Published`, per-post numbers or "no value yet" at measure. PPC and cold outreach stay a description and a path. Tests load each slot uncommented, lint it on an installed Marketing and check `owner review → publish` stays `owner_only`.
- **Umami as a commented analytics connector, and visits as a real goal** (factory/umami-as-a-commented-analytics-connector-and-visits-as-a-real-goal). `department add marketing` and `department add sales-b2c` write an `umami` connector into the entry commented out: a `curl` tool against Umami's REST API, `UMAMI_API_KEY: keychain:umami-api-key`, `sends: false`, no `per_run:`. Marketing gains a `visits` goal read from `<issues>/.goals/marketing/weekly-seo-pass.json`, which the weekly SEO pass writes with `issue-cli goal write marketing/weekly-seo-pass visits=<n>`; Sales' weekly funnel review reads visits from the same connector. Where Umami runs stays yours.
- **A per-run tool starts at its first call, not at session start** (factory/a-per-run-tool-starts-at-its-first-call-not-at-session-start). A `url:` department connector with `per_run:` no longer starts its heavy tool when an agent session loads it. The first time the tool is up, `issue-cli connector exec` saves what the server answered to `initialize` and to its lists (state dir, 0600, never a header). Later sessions answer those from the copy, and `ping` locally, while the tool stays down. The first `tools/call` (after `at:` and `needs_approved_output:`) takes the hold under mem-guard, opens the server's session and goes on. A held start is that call's JSON-RPC error with mem-guard's line, and the session goes on. With no saved copy the session holds at launch as before. codex gets `tool_timeout_sec` for such a server, and `issue-cli connectors` shows `starts per run at the first call, stopped`.
- **Connectors that start one tool can share its runs** (factory/two-connectors-naming-one-tool-share-one-per-run-lease). `per_run: tool: <id>` on two connectors, in two departments or two projects, gives them one set of leases in the state dir's `connector-tools/<id>/`: a run joins the tool another connector's run started, and only the last run out stops it, with its own `stop:` and secrets. Before, the first run out stopped the tool under the other's run. Lint `departments` warns about an id that isn't `[a-z0-9._-]` up to 64, and `connector exec` refuses it. Without `tool:` nothing changes.
- **One trace per issue and benchmark task: a span per stage** (factory/one-trace-per-issue-and-benchmark-run-a-span-per-stage-with-agent-briefing-size-tokens-time-cli-calls-and-gate-result). Each status visit gets a span: agent and model, sessions, the briefing sent (bytes, estimated tokens, the memory ids and decision refs it carried), tokens in / out / cached from the worktree's transcripts, wall time, issue-cli calls and refused ones, whether the gate out passed first try, and back moves. `<issues>/.traces/<slug>.jsonl` is rewritten after every transition; `issue-cli trace <slug> [--json] [--write]` prints it; the Timeline tab has a By stage table; `run.json` has a `trace` per task and `bench compare` a By stage section. Dispatches, reprompts and `start` now record their briefing's size and ids in the stats sidecar. Unknown shows as —, never 0; nothing leaves the machine and no text is stored (docs/workflow-stats.md#traces).
- **The stalled-worker tests hold under load** (factory/testapromptwinsovernofirstcall-reads-the-pane-before-the-dialog-shows-and-turns-main-red-under-load). Under load, the test pane's interactive shell could print `sh: child setpgid (…): Operation not permitted` after the approval-prompt fixture. That line ended the pane, so `TestAPromptWinsOverNoFirstCall` saw no prompt and turned main red. The fixtures are now drawn by a plain `sh -c` that forks nothing after them, and each test waits for the fixture's full last line. The stall scan itself is unchanged.
- **A connector allows tools and argument values per status** (factory/a-connector-allows-tools-and-argument-values-per-status). `at: {<status>: {allow: [...], deny_args: {<tool>: {<argument>: [<value>]}}}}` on a `url:` department connector limits each `tools/call` by the worker's issue status, read again at each call, so a transition in the same session changes what passes: a status `at:` doesn't name allows no tool, `allow:` names every tool callable there, and `deny_args:` refuses one argument value (Postiz's `create_post` with `type: now` while drafting) while other values pass. A refused call is a JSON-RPC error naming the status and what it allows, and never reaches the server. A lead has no issue status and is refused; `--as-owner` passes. `issue-cli connectors` and the worker's briefing name the statuses; `health lint` (`departments`) warns on a status the department's workflow doesn't have, on `at:` without `url:`, and on names that aren't tools'.
- **A connector can require an approved output before it runs** (factory/a-connector-can-require-an-approved-output-before-it-runs). `needs_approved_output: true` on a department connector makes `issue-cli connector exec` hold it until the worker's issue has an approved output on the shelf, the newest version approved and none still in review (the `outputs_approved` rule). A `tool` or a `command:` MCP server doesn't start; a `url:` server's `tools/call` gets a JSON-RPC error and never reaches the server, except the tools named in `read_tools:`, and the shelf is read at each call so an approval opens the next one in the same session. `--as-owner` passes; a lead or the CEO is refused. `issue-cli connectors` and the worker briefing name the hold, and lint `departments` warns on the field on a `command:` server and on `read_tools:` that does nothing. Opt-in: `sends: true` alone changes nothing, so Video's `openmontage` still runs on the owner's word before any output.
- **Health is a registry families plug into, and the engine's contexts no longer form a cycle** (factory/health-becomes-a-registry-families-plug-into-untying-the-measurement-and-quality-knot). `internal/health` imports no package that registers into it: local CI's test results reach the tests family through `health.SetTestResults`, set by `internal/ci`, and the main checkout, branch and head come from the new `internal/gitmain`. `internal/leadchat` takes a small `Transcripts` interface instead of importing bench and spend. In the engine's code map, health is its own Health registry context on the core layer, `internal/testresults` is in the kernel and `internal/timeline` is in Quality gates & CI. With the three known tracker leaks set aside, no contexts form a cycle any more (before, six domain contexts formed one). Snapshots, the health review and the lead flush behave as before.
- **Briefings are built above the core, so tracker imports no context above it** (factory/briefing-assembly-moves-out-of-tracker-so-the-core-imports-no-context-above-it). The worker's dispatch prompt, the stage briefing, the CEO's and the department leads' briefs and their ## Rules and ## Memory sections moved from `internal/tracker` to the new `internal/briefing`, in the Agent runtime context. `tracker` now imports only kernel packages: the engine's code map has no upward import left, and its three known tracker leaks are gone from `workbench.contexts`. A one-file change to `internal/rules` reaches 8 packages instead of 42, `internal/memory` 15 instead of 44, `internal/autostart` 20 instead of 43. Every briefing is the same, byte for byte.
- **Nothing below lint imports it any more** (factory/lint-registers-its-health-family-itself-and-wfedit-takes-its-check-as-a-function-so-nothing-below-lint-imports-it). The lint health family now lives in `internal/lint` and registers itself, so `internal/health` no longer imports lint. The station editor (`wfedit.Apply`) gets its lint check as a function, which the viewer passes in. Both upward imports are gone from the engine's code map and from its known-leak list. Health, Stats and the hall's Edit form behave as before.
- **A status can start its work in a fresh session** (factory/stage-contract-session-fresh-or-continue-per-status-and-a-briefing-trimmed-to-the-stage). `session: fresh` on a status in workflow.yaml (unset: `continue`, today's single session): entering it with a live worker, `transition` posts a `stage: fresh session at <status>` mark and tells the worker to write HANDOFF.md and end its turn; the lead's tick (`queue next`, `autostart next`, `agents`) prints `issue-cli dispatch <slug> --fresh`, which closes the old session and starts `agent-<slug>` in the same worktree on the issue's own dispatch words. Any dispatch at a fresh status gets a stage briefing of paths only (the issue file, the sections the stage before wrote, HANDOFF.md), the stage's guidance, output and gate out, and the decisions and memories for that status, under `briefing_budget:` (3000 estimated tokens by default; parts fold to their command). `dispatch-prompt --json` carries its size as `stage:`. HANDOFF.md is now asked for on every issue, not only under `routing:`. No bundled workflow sets it yet: the fresh-vs-continue experiment decides.
- **Memory works as a cache: usefulness from the traces, unhelpful memories decay, durable facts only during work** (factory/memory-as-a-cache-usefulness-from-stage-traces-unhelpful-memories-decay-durable-facts-only-during-work-and-the-rest-through-distillation). Each memory a stage is shown is now recorded with that stage's issue and status, and joined to the issue's trace. A memory's usefulness is how often the stages it reached passed their gate first try compared with the usual rate at that status, over its last 20 known stages, with a score from 8. `memory show`, `memory list --json`, the memory page (a chip and a Usefulness filter) and the consolidate pack show it. A memory measured no help ranks lower, and stops reaching the statuses where it showed no help. After 15 stages it goes on the consolidate pack's new Unhelpful list, proposed to the owner for forgetting, never forgotten by the run. Pinned memories are never touched. A worker's `memory add` refuses notes on its own issue ("this issue", its branch, its slug) and says they belong in the issue, HANDOFF.md or the post-mortem. Lint `memory` flags such memories. docs/memory.md has "Memory as a cache" with the layers and what lives where.
- **Bench runs score design, code and docs separately** (factory/evals-per-stage-score-design-code-and-docs-separately-in-run-json). A golden task with stage references now gets `stages:` in run.json: the run's `## Design` graded against design.md by the judge and the rubric, the code by its hidden tests and check, the docs diff by the docs rubric, each with its status, score, pass and a one-line reason. A stage that can't be graded reads unknown with the reason, never 0, and the task's outcome is unchanged. `bench report` prints a stages line per task and `bench compare` a By stage, scores table (`stage_scores` in `--json`). An eval's `stages:` names each stage's status and adds rubric lines and a pass bar; lint check `evals` warns on a stage status the workflow lacks. docs/benchmark.md "Scores per stage"; catalog entry `workflow-evals`.
- **`make playtest-check` reads the board from every checkout** (factory/make-playtest-check-reads-the-board-from-the-primary-checkout-so-it-runs-in-a-worktree). Run from the primary checkout, or anywhere with no issue-cli, it read no board: it checked step ids for shape only and printed every closed issue's `proves:` warning. It now reads `LIVE_BOARD` first, then the named or matched project's issues dir, then the board beside the git common dir, so a stale step id fails and closed issues stay quiet from the primary checkout and a worktree alike. A project named but not listed still gets one note and never reads another board.
- **Guided checks and `make security-check` keep their sandbox calls out of the worker's clilog** (cli/guided-checks-and-security-check-keep-their-sandbox-calls-out-of-the-workers-clilog). Sourcing `lib.sh` now unsets `ISSUE_CLI_LOG`, and `scripts/security-check.sh` starts its sandbox viewer without it, so sandbox calls no longer count against the worker in Timeline idle time or CLI error telemetry. The library's tick and comment on the real issue still log to the caller's clilog.
- **Golden tasks carry a reference per stage, a fixture project with known tasks, and a sandbox as of the base commit** (factory/golden-set-for-stage-work-dark-factory-tasks-plus-a-small-fixture-project-each-with-a-reference-per-stage). `bench task add` writes `design.md` (the issue's `## Design` as it left design), `docs.diff` and a docs rubric (`--docs-rubric`) beside the snapshot, with a `stages:` block on the task; `bench show` and `bench candidates` print them. `bench fixture init <dir> [--config <file>]` writes `example.com/tally`, three done tasks with known solutions and the same SHAs on every machine; it adds no task, the owner picks. A bench sandbox now holds the project's and machine's memory cut at the base commit's time (none of the task's own issue or agent), sets `XDG_DATA_HOME` in the sandbox (the owner's machine memory was readable before), and keeps no branch, tag or reflog past base (the clone's `main` used to point at today's tip, merged commit included). docs/benchmark.md.
- **YOLO passes the Human Testing approval too** (cli/under-yolo-transition-human-testing-documentation-dry-run-says-ready-the). Older builds' briefings said the Human Testing approval stayed the owner's even in YOLO, while `transition --dry-run` passed the gate out of human-testing, so a worker couldn't tell whether to stop. The owner chose to pass it: a gate that asks for that approval now passes in YOLO like any other and waits in the after-the-fact queue. The briefing, the YOLO rule, `yolo on` / `yolo status` and the never-passes list no longer name it. Experiments, `owner_only` gates, release publish and gates into optional statuses still wait for the owner, and the CEO's `approve --to human-testing` still refuses.
- **A code map: contexts on layers, and what a branch reaches** (workflow/code-map-modules-grouped-into-contexts-with-the-boundaries-and-each-branchs-review-footprint). `workbench.layers` and `workbench.contexts` group a repository's Go packages into named contexts. The imports are read from the source with go/parser, with no go list. `issue-cli codemap` prints the contexts, the imports between them and the ones pointing up a layer, and `issue-cli affected <slug> | --files` shows a branch's footprint: the packages it changes and reaches, the contexts it works in and reaches, and the binaries to rebuild. The Station shows the footprint at testing and human-testing, `/p/<project>/codemap` draws the map with click-to-highlight, and the critic's brief lists the footprint. Lint check `contexts` warns on an unmapped package and on an upward import that `leaks:` doesn't list. The engine's own map has 12 contexts over 5 layers. Catalog entry `code-map`.
- Test only: the new-department playtest session is green again (ui/the-new-department-playtest-session-picks-marketing-by-name-so-the-slow-tier-is-green-again). It now picks the Marketing template by name before Preview, so a template that sorts ahead of Marketing (Legal, since 2026-11-03) no longer turns TP4 and TP5 red.
- Test only: the slow tier's health-lint and postmortems sessions are green again (ui/the-slow-tiers-health-lint-and-postmortems-sessions-count-what-yesterdays-new-checks-and-fixtures-added). Their fixtures had drifted under the 2026-11-05 `roles` check and the post-mortem grouping fix. `TestLintScenarioCarriesTheSessionsThreeWarnings` and `TestPostmortemsScenarioGroupsAsItsSessionsCount` now pin what each fixture shows, in `make validate`.
- **Post-mortems start on their own only for the types you list** (workflow/post-mortems-start-on-their-own-only-for-features-and-epics-postmortem-auto-types-the-rest-print-the-ask-command). `postmortem: auto_types: [feature, epic]`: with `start: auto`, only the listed types start their post-mortem by themselves; the rest print `issue-cli postmortem start <slug>` so the lead or owner can still ask for one. An unknown type is a lint warning. An epic's brief lists its children's files, branches and retros. This repo sets `[feature, epic]`.
- **A wrong mark on the post-mortem queue can be undone.** `/p/<project>/postmortems` has a Marked lately card: the owner's refused and overtaken actions of the last 7 days, with Undo on the ticked rows, from the owner's browser only. The action is open again and the retro keeps a `Reopened:` line with the time and the old line; `postmortem show` and the post-mortem page show it.
- **`issue-cli bench candidates` answers in seconds** (factory/bench-candidates-answers-in-seconds-on-the-live-board). It ran about 17 git calls per done issue, 100 s on a board with 350 of them; it now runs five, however many issues are done, and prints the same rows. A recorded commit gone from the repository now reads `<sha> is not in this repository` in `--all`.
- The 7-day windows (the week report and HQ report, the Radio, the map, hall and room "this week" counts, Status's recently done) count calendar days from local midnight, so the day after a DST change holds the whole seventh day and nothing of the eighth (`period.DaysBack`, `TestOneDayWindow`).
- The briefing's shell note now tells a worker to watch the `make validate` log, stop at a `validate: red so far: <step>` line and take the verdict from the last `validate:` line; docs/getting-started.md says the same (factory/the-workers-shell-note-says-to-stop-at-validates-red-so-far-line).
- **`issue-cli health lint` names a section holding `show`'s own output** (factory/lint-warns-on-a-show-footer-pasted-into-an-issue-section). New check `show-footer`: a section rebuilt from `show` runs on past the last heading and pastes `== Checklist ==`, the tick hints and the comments into the issue, and no gate notices. The finding gives the issue and the section; a quote in a code span, blockquote or fence doesn't warn. Closed issues count.
- Test only: no test reaches a real agent CLI. Every test binary points `CODEX_HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` at empty folders, so a real `claude` or `codex` finds no login, and what it writes there fails the run whichever way PATH was set; `CHECK_REAL_CODEX=1` gives codex its home back. This caught two viewer tests that started a real Claude lead on every `make validate` and local CI run: they no longer do.
- `issue-cli append` with a block opening `## X` goes to the `## X` section or starts a new one, instead of landing under a `### X` inside another section with its heading lost. An issue with both is no longer refused. `--section` still matches any level.
- **A clilog no longer makes a branch build read as stale.** `check-script --yes` counted every file under `cmd/` and `internal/`, so a file a run left there refused an up-to-date `./issue-cli`. It now counts Go sources and the files `go:embed` lines name (factory/a-tests-clilog-no-longer-makes-the-build-read-as-stale).
- **A heavy tool partner starts for a run and stops after** (factory/a-tool-partner-starts-for-a-run-and-stops-after-under-mem-guard). A connector's optional `per_run:` block (`start:`, `ready:`, `stop:`, `timeout:`) makes `connector exec` start the tool under `mem-guard check` and stop it when the last run ends, however the run ends. Runs share one start, a tool the owner left up is never stopped, and the viewer's sweep (`issue-cli connector sweep`) stops what a killed run left. A stopped tool's check records `stopped`, not failing; a failed start or stop is a Needs you row. docs/departments.md#per-run.
- **A worker stopped at a prompt, or one that never started, is reported** (cli/a-worker-stopped-at-a-prompt-or-one-that-never-started-is-reported). `queue next`, `autostart next`, `issue-cli agents`, the Crew card, status.md and the phone's Worker stuck push now name a worker at Codex's or Claude's approval prompt or Claude's question, and a worker with no issue-cli call in the five minutes after its launch, whatever stopped it. Nothing types into the pane.
- **One shell quoter, one short SHA, one atomic JSON write** (factory/one-shellquote-one-short-sha-one-atomic-json-write): `shell.Quote`, `gitdir.ShortSHA` (7 characters everywhere) and `jsonfile.Write`, each with a guard against a new copy. A safe word prints bare in every command, a word with `=` is always quoted (zsh reads a leading `=word` as a path), and an empty value in a validation command stays one argument.
- `issue-cli critique` runs by hand when no gate leaves the issue's status, instead of refusing: same read-only critic, record and comment, but it holds nothing, counts no red round and says so. The Fresh Critic doc now says this project has the gate off, and a test keeps that line in step with workflow.yaml.
- A flag quoted with its value, such as `memory add "fact" "--supersedes m-1"`, is refused with exit 2 on every command. It used to exit 0 and go into the text with the flag not acted on. Text that names no flag of the command still goes in, and `--` lets through text that really starts with one.
- Test only: the changelog guard now fails on an entry above `## Unreleased` as well as one under a released version, and `TestChangelogPreambleHasNoEntries` reads the whole preamble. One entry that sat there moved under `## Unreleased`, so it reaches the next release notes.
- **YOLO's owner text names only what stops** (workflow/yolos-owner-text-names-only-what-stops). The worker briefing, the YOLO rule and `yolo status` said the Human Testing approval stays the owner's on any workflow with that status, though the gate out of it asks for `documentation` and YOLO passes it, so workers stopped for nothing. They now name it only when a gate on the reader's path asks for that approval.
- /systems is about twice as fast on a big board (453 to 197 ms, in process, on this repo's own): the search for file names in issues only looks at words with a dot in them, and the system pages and /health build the header's numbers without building the whole company page.
- The Health page loads in under half a second on a big board: its Lint section no longer re-reads every playtest session or walks the whole test id list.
- **Playtest scenarios no longer fail with the calendar** (ui/the-evidence-tab-scenario-stops-ageing-into-the-30-day-shot-shrink). The `evidence-tab` session went red once its fixture passed 30 days, because the scenario viewer shrank the committed shots and a `.png` link gave a 404. Every scenario holding `.playtest/runs` now sets `health: shrink_shots_after_days: 0`, and `TestScenarioShotsDontAgeOut` fails on one that doesn't.
- Playtest scenario viewers read tmux, mem-guard and the load afresh (`ISSUE_VIEWER_READ_AFRESH`), so a step sees the sessions it just started and live agents no longer read "stopped". A normal viewer keeps its 500 ms reading.
- **A worker stopped at its usage limit hands its branch to the other agent** (factory/when-a-worker-hits-its-usage-limit-mid-task-the-other-agent-takes-the-branch-over-from-the-workers-handoff). Under `routing:` a worker keeps `HANDOFF.md` at its worktree's root, never committed. `issue-cli queue next`, `autostart next` and `agents` print `dispatch <slug> --takeover` for a stopped worker: it quotes the issue's own `dispatch:` words, renames the limited session `limited-<slug>`, types nothing into it, and starts the other agent as `agent-<slug>` in the same worktree. Once per issue, within `mem-guard` and the machine's one start per 5 minutes.
- **Standing rules in two layers** (factory/factory-rules-in-two-layers-rules-that-apply-on-every-project-and-rules-for-only-this-project-for-briefs-and-memory-alike). `~/.config/ai-workflow-engine/rules.md`, the owner's, reaches every project's leads and workers, and `rules:` in workflow.yaml adds a project's own. Briefings show the two apart, and pinned memories the same way. A project rule can declare `narrows: <key>`, and the machine rule still prints beside it; `issue-cli health lint --check rules` names one that contradicts a machine rule without saying so. `issue-cli rules` lists both and writes nothing. A private project is now refused `--updates`, `--supersedes` and `memory edit --text` on a machine memory, as it already was `--scope machine`.
- `bench run --agents claude,codex` runs every golden task on both agents in one run. `bench compare <run>`, `bench report` and the Report's Benchmark card show pass rate, time, tokens and cost by agent and type, with a `Routing:` line naming each `routing:` rule the numbers support or contradict. A Codex worker's usage comes from its rollout files; a cost with no price reads `cost unknown`, never a guess. `bench candidates` shows 20 by default, the fewest risks first and across types (`--limit`, `--all`), and leaves out branches over 15 files.
- **Lead rules live in issue-cli memory.** `memory add --scope lead` keeps a standing rule for every lead and no worker, in the project's log. The CEO's and department leads' briefs carry the pinned ones in full on their own budget, and one clipped line for each unpinned one; `lead spawn` and `lead flush pack` name `memory list --scope lead`, and the Memory page filters by it (docs/memory.md#lead-rules).
- **The critic runs on the other agent than the worker** (factory/the-critic-runs-on-the-other-agent-than-the-worker). `issue-cli critique` now reads the worker's agent from the dispatch record instead of guessing it from routing, and picks the other one. When the other rests or isn't installed it runs on the same agent, and the run record (`worker`, `why`, `same_agent`), its comment and the station's Critique block say so. `--agent` and `critique: agent:` still win.
- **Statuses can be renamed.** `role:` on a status (`design`, `queue`, `work`, `verify`, `owner-test`) says what it means to the engine, so renaming `backlog` or `human-testing` keeps start's hand-off, auto-start, `next`, the guided-check hints and YOLO's refusal, which used to go quietly. Nothing changes without the key: the old names hold the roles. Lint check `roles` warns on a bad role and on a file filling some roles but not all, `process workflow` prints them, and `workflow upgrade` proposes the `status-roles` entry.
- **A memory can say which status it matters at** (factory/a-memory-can-say-which-status-it-matters-at): `memory add --at <status>` ties a fact to a step, not a subject. `transition` prints the memories of the status it enters, at most 3 and 600 characters with a `+N more` line and nothing at all when there are none; `start` prints them under `At <status>:`. `memory edit <id> --at <status>|none` sets or clears it, `memory list|search --at` filter, a status no workflow has is refused, and lint's `memory` check names a memory whose status has gone. The Memory page shows it on the row.
- `issue-cli uncheck` withdraws a box's N/A: an unticked box drops its closing `(N/A: …)`, so a later tick no longer claims it.
- `routing: codex:` says how a Codex start may run: `writable: [issues, git]` adds those folders to its sandbox and `approval: ask | auto-review | never` picks who answers its requests. Unset, a start's command line is unchanged. Nothing here sets a value; docs/agent-dispatch.md names each option's risk, and lint names an unknown value.
- `make link-check` reads a link's exact case: a link that only a Mac's case-insensitive disk finds (`workflow/x.md` for `docs/Workflow/`) now fails on the Mac too, not just on Linux, and the line says what the disk calls it.
- Lint check `reports` warns on a done report whose output has a Follow-ups list that no issue or queue item quotes (by slug, output id or `output <short id>`, or a child of the report). A warning only: it shows a follow-up with no home.

- The worker briefing says a brief's cause, cache key, fix or quoted form is a proposal: measure it on the unchanged code or read the parser first, record a D-n if it differs, and retitle the issue when the repro disproves the title.
- A Codex start trusts exactly its own folder for that run and skips the update prompt, so a worker reaches its prompt with nothing to press. A pane still at a startup dialog a minute after its launch shows as `stalled at <dialog>` in `issue-cli agents`, `queue next`, `autostart next`, the Crew card, status.md and the Worker stuck push.

- `playtest check` warns when an open issue's judgement step and machine step prove the same criterion, and the playtest guide says why: `proves:` on a judgement step makes the criterion the owner's, so the machine step's pass never ticks it.
- **A worker that dies after launch shows up.** Past the launch watch a dead agent left a session that looked live. Both Crew pages now show it as *agent exited · only a shell is left* with no Nudge, push sends one *Worker exited*, and `--reprompt` / Re-send prompt refuse instead of pasting the briefing into the shell (`issue-cli dispatch` replaces the session).
- **`make validate` reports its cheap checks first.** gofmt, vet, catalog-check, link-check, the new `leak-check` and playtest-check run before the test suite and the coverage gate, and a red step prints `validate: red so far: <step>` at once. Every step still runs, and the last line is still the verdict. catalog-check's refusal says a `Catalog:` line counts only once committed.
- **A nightly job ends the lead, and the next lead starts where it stopped.** A job with `brief: builtin:lead-flush` on the Product entry writes `sessions/<date>-lead-<project>.md` (80 lines at most, six set sections) and a `.summary.md` of the day's lead chat, tidies memory and closes the lead, through `issue-cli lead flush pack|note|summary|close --run <slug>`. `lead spawn` now puts the newest note and its summary into the CEO's first prompt, beside the pins. Off until the owner adds the job and turns on its standing start. The flush starts, sends and decides nothing. Runs use the new `chore` type (`idea → in progress → done`, no approval, no post-mortem), because a nightly run has nothing for the owner to review: add it with `issue-cli workflow upgrade` (docs/patterns/lead-session.md#the-nightly-flush).
- Routing has a usage stop point per subscription: `routing: stop_at:` (default 98, 100 is off). An agent at or over that percent of its weekly limit, read from the line its CLI prints in a pane, rests until the reset, so routed starts fall back to the other agent, and auto-start, the queue and jobs hold only when no agent to try is free, with the reason. `issue-cli agents`, the Crew header and status.md show each percent once seen. A Claude reset written `Nov 5 at 8am` now parses (it was a one-hour guess), and a stale date no longer rests a subscription until next year.
- **The owner's post-mortem round works across post-mortems.** `/p/<project>/postmortems` lists every waiting action by place, with copies together, and Refuse or Overtaken (a commit on main) act on the ticked rows of a group, from the owner's browser only; nothing is applied there. An `Overtaken:` action stops counting in `postmortem list`, `show`, the pages and Needs you. Needs you now also reads post-mortems under `retros/archive/`.

- `make validate` prints `validate: FAIL (no steps ran)` and exits non-zero when `VALIDATE_STEPS` is empty, instead of `validate: PASS` with nothing checked. The briefing's shell notes (and docs/getting-started.md#shell-notes) now say how to keep a verdict: `make validate > <log> 2>&1` and its `validate:` line in a later call, `rtk grep` is ripgrep so use `-g '*.go'` rather than `--include`, never pipe or chain a command whose exit code decides the next step, and a "no verdict" from auto mode means sending that call alone (factory/the-shell-note-and-validate-keep-the-verdict-readable).

- A worker's briefing now carries the red-on-main rule: a failure main already has gets filed with `issue-cli create` without asking, or named in the worker's `tests:` comment when it has an issue. The rule was only in the lead-session docs, which workers aren't pointed at.
- The viewer's map (`/`) and Report answer warm in about 130 and 165 ms on the Dark Factory board, down from 0.6 and 0.4 s: the map no longer builds the engine's whole Report for one release line, a page reuses tmux, mem-guard and load readings for 500 ms instead of spawning each several times, and filtered board loads and retros come from the file cache.
- Test only: `TestYOLOReviewRunsNoGitPerEntry` no longer goes red when a clock second ticks over while its board is written. No cache was stale: the last issue landed alone on the review's page 2. `newGitBoard` pins its dates.
- `issue-cli retro list` shows the retros and post-mortems written since the last sweep, and `issue-cli retro archive --until <time>` moves them to `retros/archive/` with a log line that is the next sweep's mark. A post-mortem still waiting for the owner stays where Needs you reads it. The `builtin:retro-sweep` job brief holds the steps and says the lead files the proposals.
- `make linux-check` runs the public CI's steps in an `ubuntu:24.04` container on the working tree (apt tmux and shellcheck, Go through GOTOOLCHAIN, a non-root user, `PLAYTEST_CHROME=none`), so a Linux-only failure shows before a release. It refuses when `mem-guard check` is blocked and says so when docker is missing or down. `make test` now says when shellcheck is missing, which go test hid.
- Guided check scripts ask the build under check who the shell is, so a worker keeps its identity on a machine with no installed `issue-cli`.

- **Lint warns on a docs issue that asks for code or a test.** New check `types`: an open `type: docs` issue whose `## Wanted` or open criterion names a test, a package or a `.go` path gets a warning, with `set-type <slug> tweak` as the fix, since the docs path has no Test Plan to check that work (factory/lint-warns-on-a-docs-type-issue-whose-body-asks-for-code-or-a-test).
- **`check-script --yes` refuses a stale `./issue-cli`.** A branch build older than the newest commit or file under `cmd/` and `internal/` stops the machine run before any script starts, with the change it found and the build command, so a pass can't be about old code.
- **`issue-cli start` and the briefing name the open branches on the same files.** An `Open branches` block lists each open `work/` branch whose diff touches files the issue's system maps to, or that its own branch already changes, with the `issue-cli ask` to agree who changes what before the merge. A branch behind main is told to merge it before editing anything.
- **Department work filed by system alone gets a warning.** An issue whose `system:` names a department that doesn't list it, with no `department:` field, is Product's: `create` now says so, and the `departments` lint check flags each open one. `output add` on an issue that already has a file output with the same title hints `output revise <id>`.
- **The briefing quotes an issue as written.** `--project` used to go in after every `issue-cli` in a dispatch prompt, even inside the issue's title and quoted lines, so a retro's words read differently in the briefing than in the file. The title, a body's blockquotes and fenced blocks, and a command inside a quoted string now stay as written; commands the worker is told to run still get it, and one that names a project keeps it. Hints follow the same rule, so `prove --lead "issue-cli check-script <slug> --yes"` prints its quoted command bare, and a lead link's command is refused if it names `--project`: the runner sets the lead's.
- **`make serve-stop` keeps to the scenario's own environment.** A scenario rebuilt from its folder alone had no environment of its own, so its commands inherited `$TMUX`, the real home and the caller's folder.
