They now get the same isolated environment as a staged scenario, and its tmux calls name the private socket.
- The commit-msg hook notes the `Refs: <slug>` line on a `work/<slug>` branch until one commit references the issue, so a fast-forward ship needs no empty commit.
- **System guidance reaches workers while they build, on every type.** The API overlay's route rules, once printed only on leaving `in progress`, are now in the `in progress` prompt; Factory's two Settled-rule pointers reach tweaks and bugfixes as well as designs; the CLI overlay points at "Adding a command".
- **Department templates share their lines, and the docs' numbers have one table.** The heavy-tools sentence (one trigger, one wording, mem-guard where installed), the publish line, the Published box, the shelf hint, the licence's changes clause and the stamp header each have one source under `templates/departments/_shared`, and `TestSharedTextsQuotedWordForWord` fails on a template that rewords one. `docs/numbers.md` is generated from the constants (`TestDocNumbersInSync`), and `TestDocNumbersCopiesAgree` fails on a sentence quoting another value. Token counts read one way everywhere (12.3k, where some reports said 12k), and week boundaries and keys come from `internal/period`.
- **`make validate` runs every step and names each red one.** A red step no longer hides the steps after it: the last line is `validate: FAIL (test, catalog-check)`, every red step named, and the exit stays non-zero. CLAUDE.md adds that date rules count calendar days and pin `now` in fixtures; the lead-session docs add that the first worker to see a red `ci status` shows on main files it or names its issue in the `tests:` comment.
- **"Provisional" means one thing.** The Stats card's Provisional count, its `decisions.provisional` metric, `decisions --state provisional` and the drift sweep now count the same decisions: active and marked YOLO or provisional. An unmarked decision with no quote is `decisions.no_quote`, and the tile's note says how many. The tile links to `?state=provisional`, which lists what it counts. Deferred and expired decisions no longer offer Confirm or read `needs owner's words`.
- **The Catalog box says what to write, and in progress runs catalog-check first.** The Design box ended in "or n/a", which agents copied into commits as a line catalog-check refuses. It now says: the entry added or bumped, a `Catalog: n/a <id>[,<id>...] <why>` line for an entry catalog-check names, or no `Catalog:` line when it names none. The in-progress guidance says to run `issue-cli workflow catalog-check` on the staged change before the first commit and put the ids in one line.
- A project with no `workflow.yaml` now runs on the bundled `development` template itself (types, `criteria_proven`, current prompts) instead of a shorter hand-kept copy.
- One closed-status rule everywhere (`tracker.IsClosedStatus`: the workflow's end, anything listed after it, and parked). `issue-cli list --status open|closed` counts parked as closed, `children_closed` asks by each child's own workflow, and a department file's end counts as done on every page. Statuses like `closed` or `archived` close only where a workflow lists them after its end.
- Parking a queued issue no longer marks its queue item done.
- The issues list and board keep a status's colour after an auto-refresh whatever its case: `/issues.json` now sends `status_color`, `status_text_color` and `priority_color`.
- **The project lead's brief is the built-in one plus an addendum.** `leads.project_addendum` in `workflow.yaml` goes after the built-in brief, so a project no longer loses lines added to it later. An old `leads.project` still replaces the brief, and lint warns. This repo's own override had dropped how to check on an agent and that workers message the lead only when the human says so; both are back. Every department lead's brief now states the partner licence rule in one sentence.
- **The briefing's red-round line names the critic, and docs that quote the code are checked.** A worker's `## Red checks` section and `process schema` now say what the docs say: the critic's held runs count, each its own round, only a failure that comes back counts, and an owner comment starts the count over. The YOLO rule, that phrase, the shell notes, the close rule, the messaging rule and the sandbox rules are quoted from one source, and `TestDocsQuoteTheCode` fails when a doc drifts. The stale line in docs/agent-dispatch.md about agents messaging each other is rewritten to match the brief.
- **One machine hold for every start, and nightly stops at the core count.** Auto-start, the queue, recurring jobs and `bench nightly` now ask one rule, `autostart.MachineHold`: memory under the line, the 1-minute load under the core count or the `max_load` the owner set on the project's auto-start. Nightly used to start at load == cores and ignore `max_load`; jobs had no load hold at all, and the queue dropped `max_load` while auto-start was paused. All mem-guard calls go through `internal/memguard`, which holds its lines and pins its output formats. The lead brief's holds sentence is generated from `Decide`'s rules, so it names the load and the queue's local CI red. Hold reasons read the same everywhere: `mem-guard not found`, `mem-guard says no: …`, `load 14.0 on 14 cores (max 14)` (factory/one-machine-hold-for-auto-start-queue-jobs-and-nightly-and-a-memguard-package).

- A bench run now prices a model exactly as the rooms' spend does: a key prices every model it is a prefix of, so a USD budget trips on such a model, and `prices.yaml` beats a benchmark file's price for the same key.
- **Every rule is on one list, read one way.** `children_closed` was missing from it, so `process schema` left it out and a station edit carrying it was refused; it's listed now, and it and `decision_unchallenged` print a sentence in `process transitions`. The issue timeline shows the description of a rule that takes an argument, `health lint` reports an unknown rule once, and `rule: "name:arg"` without the space now runs as it reads in lint and the station editor.
- **YOLO's list of gates matches what it passes.** The `yolo on` output and the turn-on dialog came from a second copy of the rule: with `human-testing` optional they listed testing → human-testing as passed while the transition refused it and the same dialog called it the owner's. One rule, `yolo.Eligible`, now decides a step for both, and the list walks every type's path as the never-passes list does, so steps only a type walks (`idea → in progress`) show too. A gate into an optional status is named on the never-passes list when a workflow has one. The briefing's "still the owner's" list now also names the Human Testing approval, release publish and gates into optional statuses, and `docs/Workflow/yolo.md` quotes the rule as workers get it, with guard tests holding the copies together.
- **One waiting-on-you number on every page.** The Report, the classic Status card, `status.md`, the CEO chat, `/crew`, `/updates` and `/leads` each counted their own subset; they now all say the length of the Needs you list (across projects the sum, with unsent bug reports once), and what they list adds up to it: connectors, due jobs, a nightly regression, post-mortems, decision challenges, expired decisions, clarify rounds and partner stages show under Other on the Status pages and in the chat. A done issue's open question no longer counts. The Board's chip counts something else, so it now says so: **Issues with a marker · N**. A guard test holds a row of every kind and reads each page. (ui/one-waiting-on-you-count-on-every-page)
- **The YOLO review and the issue page's tabs stop running git for each entry.** Branch heads and the main branch now come from one `git for-each-ref`, kept until a ref changes, instead of a `rev-parse` and a `symbolic-ref` per ask. The review's logs, greps and ancestor checks are kept by commit sha, and one request reads the shared CLI logs, playtest sessions and recorded heads once. dark-factory's review went from 193 git calls in 2.0 s to none in 0.55 s when warm, a smaller board's from 155 calls in 1.0 s to 1 in 0.13 s, and a Station tab from 15–19 calls to 2. The proof report itself is never kept, so a new commit, comment or playtest run shows on the next load.
- **The stats page no longer builds its aggregate in the request.** The warm-up already covered the default view; what cost 7–9 s was any done issue changing after it. Such a visit now shows the last aggregate at once, with an "Updating" line, and rebuilds in the background (7.8 s → 0.6 s on this board). A range wider than the last aggregate does the same and says how far back the numbers reach.
- **`/debug/pprof/` on the running viewer.** An audit can read goroutines and heap without stopping it: the owner's sign-in, on loopback only (docs/API/security.md).
- **`dispatched` means an agent is running.** A session whose agent exited on launch used to stay as a shell window, reported as started, and the next dispatch called it live. `agentrun.Start` now watches every launch for 3 seconds (dispatch, auto-start, the queue, lead spawn, job runs, the post-mortem, the health review) and looks again after the prompt delivery check. A launch that dies comes back as an error quoting the pane's last lines, with its session removed and nothing recorded as started. A session that is only a shell is replaced instead of re-attached.
- **`make playtest-check` checks step ids against the real board.** It picks its project the way `make playtest-report` does and reads the issue files once, in about a second, instead of one failing `issue-cli` call per session in the worker's clilog. A project it can't find is one `note:` line. Six stale step ids in the declared-connections and shared-memory sessions are fixed.
- Pages and CLI commands are several times faster on big boards: parsed issue files and workflow files are kept and only re-parsed when their size or mtime changes, each page shares one board load, issue bodies render only on the pages that show them, and comments come with the issue (Report 1.4 s → 0.35 s, `/` 1.8 s → 0.46 s, `list` 105 → 51 ms, pages allocate about 8× less).
- `decisions_cited` reads a shorthand `#D-n` in `## Design` when a full ref sits on the same line (`sim/rules#D-5 and #D-6`), so a design that cites that way no longer takes a hold or a `yolo-hold:` comment. `decisions_respected` reads it too. A bare `#D-n` alone on its line still names nothing.
- **`issue-cli show` in half a second on a busy board.** Its Shared files block scanned every open branch with git each call: about 450 `git merge-base` runs, 10.8 s on a board with 91 worktrees. Answers between commits (merge-base, commit counts, small diffs, and "no common ancestor") are now kept in `issue-cli-gitcache.jsonl` in the repo's git common dir, main's sha is read once per scan, and merged worktrees are no longer compared with every branch. `show` now takes 0.5 s, or 0.8 s right after another branch committed. The Station's first load uses the same cache.
- **The growth scout.** `brief: builtin:growth-scout` is a recurring job that reads what a department knows (README, brand.md, icp.md, competitors.md, market.md, its goals and earlier reports) and the web, and puts one report of 10-15 growth experiment cards on the shelf: hypothesis, channel, the first cheap test (under a day and $0-20), metric and threshold, risk (ask Legal), needs and ICE. Nothing is executed; the owner picks. Its runs are type `report` by themselves (a job's `type:` still wins); a workflow without one refuses the run and lint warns with the fix. The bundled `marketing` template gains the `report` type (research → owner review → done; catalog `report-type`): an installed marketing workflow takes it from `issue-cli workflow upgrade --department <dept>`, `department update` keeps the file.
- **Pages no longer list worktrees once per issue in human-testing.** The waiting-on-you count (sidebar, floor, Needs you, /, /crew, /leads) ran `git worktree list` once for each issue in human-testing on every page. The listing is now kept per repository until its worktrees change (one added, removed, moved or switched to another branch). On a board with 65 issues in human-testing the shelf page went from 806 to 130 ms and /needs-you from 935 to 458 ms.
- **Partners in the docs.** docs/departments.md#partners now sets studio partners (the `partner:` block, an adapter) beside tool partners (a connector), says how to swap one, the licence rule (MIT, Apache or BSD first, GPL-3.0 with care, AGPL only as an exception the owner records, never vendored) and how partners run here: keys as `keychain:` references, `mem-guard check` before heavy tools.
- **Marketing names a rankings source.** `department add marketing` writes a self-hosted OpenSEO connector (`openseo`, MCP at `http://localhost:3011/mcp`, its sending tools in `deny_tools:`) into Marketing's entry commented out; uncomment it to turn it on. It costs nothing until you add a DataForSEO key, which stays in Keychain and OpenSEO's own `.env`. The seo-pass measure step and the weekly SEO brief read ranks, Search Console and GA4 from it, within $1 of paid data per pass. Templates can carry such examples with `connector_examples:`.
- **Turn off single tools on a connector.** `deny_tools: [<tool>, …]` on a department's `mcp` connector switches those tools off in its agents' sessions: claude is launched with `--disallowedTools=mcp__<connector>__<tool>,…`, codex with `-c mcp_servers.<connector>.disabled_tools=[…]`, so a research server such as OpenSEO stays `sends: false` without its agents pinging IndexNow, sharing reports or starting paid schedules. The launch step says how many tools are off, and `issue-cli connectors` and the briefing list them. `issue-cli health lint` (check `departments`) warns when a server the engine knows has tools that send or spend left on without `sends: true`, and about a bad or duplicate name or `deny_tools:` on a `tool` connector. claude's `--mcp-config` is now written `--mcp-config=<path>`, so its list can no longer take the prompt as a second config.
- **The drift sweep counts a provisional decision's age in calendar days.** `issue-cli decisions drift` lists a provisional decision that reaches beyond its issue when it is more than 14 calendar days old: its date against today's local date, never hours, so a DST change or a late hour no longer lists a 13-day-old one (main's drift tests went red at 23:05 the week after the autumn change). One exactly 14 days old is not listed. With no mark the path window starts at local midnight 14 days back.
- **HTTP MCP connectors without a bridge.** A `kind: mcp` connector may name `url:` (a Streamable HTTP server: https, or http on this machine) instead of `command:`, with `headers:` whose values are secret references after an optional prefix (`Authorization: "Bearer keychain:openseo"`). `issue-cli connector exec` bridges the agent's stdin/stdout to it in process, adding the headers to each request, so mcp.json holds no secret and no npm bridge such as `mcp-remote` is needed. It keeps the server's session, reads JSON and SSE answers, never follows a redirect, and answers a failed request with a JSON-RPC error that never names a header. `issue-cli connectors` lists each header as set or missing; `health lint` (`departments`) checks the new keys. See docs/departments.md#connectors.
- **Goals a job fills from a connector.** A recurring job writes the numbers it read (from a connector or the owner) with `issue-cli goal write <dept>/<job> <id>=<value>…`: it replaces `<issues>/.goals/<dept>/<job>.json` atomically, in the metric run format, and names the goal reading each id. Goals read it with `source: file:<issues>/.goals/<dept>/<job>.json#<id>` (a `file:` path starting with `<issues>/` is read from the issues folder, so a run in a worktree reaches the goals and a private tracker keeps the numbers private). Once the job misses a whole run, the goal shows "no value since <date>" instead of the number, on `issue-cli goals`, the company page and the project map, and drops out of the roll-up. A missing file names the job and its next run; `goal set` on such a goal points at `goal write`; lint `departments` names a path whose job doesn't exist. `file_exists` gates take the `<issues>/` form too (`file-exists-gates` bumped). The sales-b2c template's goals, weekly funnel brief and gate use it (docs/departments.md#goals-a-job-fills-from-a-connector).
- **A post-mortem agent's session closes itself once its post-mortem is written.** `issue-cli postmortem write`, run inside its own session (`postmortem-<slug>`, or that window in a shared session), closes exactly that session a second after printing its result (never `kill-server`), so no post-mortem agent idles at its prompt holding memory. `postmortem start --again` treats a live session whose post-mortem is written as finished: it removes it and starts, instead of holding as "running already".
- **Legal department template and the handoff gate.** `issue-cli department add legal` installs **Legal**, a top-level department whose agents check a product against GDPR and consumer law, citing each finding by article from the official text (EUR-Lex, ISAP) with the date it was read, and never give a legal sign-off. Its `legal` workflow has four types: `compliance-check` (the default; a report on the shelf), `document` (privacy policy, terms, DPIA, art. 30 register; publish is owner_only), `legal-check` (the answer to a handoff) and `due-diligence` (a neutral engineering pack, owner_only to share). Jobs `monthly-baseline` and `law-watch`; goals `baseline-pass` and `open-findings`. A product's workflow asks Legal before a risky branch ships with the new rule `handoff_answered: legal` and `paths:` (globs): a branch whose changes match one waits until a handoff to Legal filed for it is done; lint's `departments` check warns on a rule naming no department. Catalog entry `handoff-gate`.
- **Owner sign-in survives a viewer restart, and a refused approval signs you in on the page.** The viewer keeps only a hash of each owner session (with its expiry and browser) in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/ai-workflow-engine/owner/sessions.json` (0600), so a reinstall or restart no longer signs your browser out; Sign out and the 30-day expiry still end it. When an owner decision is refused because the browser isn't signed in (Agree on Review after YOLO, Approve, Needs you, the Station), a sign-in dialog opens over the page: Send me a code, type it, Sign in, and the action goes through. Agents and issue-cli get the same refusal text as before; YOLO still never passes owner-only gates.
- **Sales (B2C) department template.** `issue-cli department add sales-b2c --parent gtm` installs a Sales department under GTM, beside Marketing, on the new `sales-b2c` workflow. Its types are `offer` (the default), `landing` (the landing page and waitlist), `launch` (to the waitlist), and the weekly `funnel-review` and `support-digest` reports. Briefs wait for Marketing's brand foundation, and owner review → publish is `owner_only` for everything a customer sees. Its goals (visits, sign-ups, active testers, retention) read `departments/sales/funnel.json`, which only the weekly funnel job writes, and only with numbers it actually read; both jobs stay off until `job auto on`. Checkout, price changes, refunds and the revenue goals ship as a commented "when you start charging" block behind the same owner_only gate. Templates may carry `connections:`: department add prints the handoffs to declare (Marketing → Sales, Sales → Product via HQ) and never writes them. A group template's `optional:` names departments that can join it later (GTM: Sales). A goal whose source file isn't written yet reads "no value yet: … is not written yet", and lint gives one finding per missing file.
- **Agree all on the review after YOLO.** In the owner's signed-in browser, the review page's summary has `Agree all N…`. A confirm names the counts (issues, gates, owner checks, assumed answers, decision items) and the oldest date, and takes optional words. It agrees every page of the queue as the page loaded it, through the same record as an issue's Agree; an item that came in after the page loaded is left waiting. Owner checks are agreed without a tick, so their boxes stay open. `POST …/yolo/review/all` is owner-only (403 for curl, agents and a signed-out browser), and `issue-cli yolo queue` points the owner to it (ui/agree-all-on-the-yolo-review-page-one-owner-only-button-agrees-every-gate-and-owner-check-in-the-queue).
- **Video studio department template.** `issue-cli department add video [--partner-path <dir>]` installs a Video department that drives an outside studio program, OpenMontage, as its partner. Its `video` workflow goes brief → proposal → script → scene plan → assets → compose → review → publish → done, and the owner stamps proposal, script, scene plan, assets, review and publish (`owner_only`: YOLO passes none). The add records only the partner's path and pinned commit, adds an `openmontage` connector whose Keychain keys are all optional, and prints the install steps (clone at the pinned commit, `make setup`, `HF_HOME`, the venv on PATH, HyperFrames telemetry off, licence notes) without running them. The viewer's preview shows the same steps. Templates may now carry `partner:`, `connectors:` and `install:`. Nothing of OpenMontage is in this repository, and a test guards that.
- **Guided checks: owner-only commands pass on a marked sandbox, and scripts keep the worker's identity.** `sandbox_project` writes `.issue-cli-sandbox` beside the sandbox's projects.yaml; on that config a worker's owner-only commands (department add/remove, job run, output review, transitions and claims of other issues, yolo on, owner-box ticks, approve, hard decisions) pass, while every project path and both XDG dirs resolve inside the temp folder and the config isn't the owner's. A pass says so on stderr and as `"sandbox": true` in the clilog. Agent starts (dispatch, lead spawn, lead message, queue next --start, health review --spawn, postmortem start, experiment queue, bench run/nightly) stay refused there, said and logged as `sandbox_refused`. `clean_shell` and `sic` now carry the caller's identity (`ISSUE_VIEWER_ISSUE_SLUG`, `ISSUE_VIEWER_LEAD_SCOPE`, the new `ISSUE_VIEWER_WORKER`, `AGENT_NAME`, `CLAUDECODE`) instead of dropping it; `lead whoami` reports the sandbox verdict; `CHECKS_LIB_VERSION` 4. Tests keep every guard asking the pass and every script keeping the identity (cli/sandbox-marker-owner-only-commands-pass-on-a-sandbox-config-only-owner-approves-the-design).
- **Answer a partner's gate in Dark Factory: the gate bridge.** A partner stage that awaits you has **Approve** and **Send back** on its Needs you row and on the Station's Partner block (your browser only; Send back needs your words). The answer is a D-n on the issue with your words, its ref naming the stage and the ask (`partner-approved:pitch@20261101T102000Z`), and the issue's worker is told how to write the partner's own approval quoting it (OpenMontage: `human_approved=True` plus a `decision_log` entry). An answered stage leaves Needs you; approving an engine gate that carries the partner answers both; a send-back keeps the issue's status. `issue-cli decide --ref partner-approved:…` is the owner's act (a worker is refused, a lead relays `--quote`), the worker briefing has a Partner bullet with the rule, `show` / `start` say the next step, and a gated stage the partner marks approved with no owner D-n is flagged by `health lint` (`departments`), on the Station and in `show` (docs/departments.md#the-owners-answer).
- **Partners: a department's outside studio, read only.** A department may name a `partner:` in projects.yaml (`name`, `adapter`, `path` to your own checkout, `commit` pin, optional `live_port`). An adapter reads the partner's files as data and says where each issue (one partner project: `partner_project:`, else the slug's last part) is: the Station's Partner block (`OpenMontage: pitch · awaiting you`, the stage's summary, spend against the budget, artifacts, the live view while it answers), the room's foot item, and a Needs you row under To approve when a stage awaits you (on the issue's gate row instead when it has a ready gate, so one decision is one row). `issue-cli show` / `start` print the partner line and `department show` the block; `health lint` (`departments`) warns about an unknown adapter, a missing path and a checkout off its pin. The first adapter, `openmontage`, reads `project.json`, the stage order from the checkout's `pipeline_defs`, and `checkpoint_*.json` at version 1.0; another version shows "partner format not understood", never a stage. Swapping the partner changes only the `partner:` block (docs/departments.md#partners).
- **The automatic post-mortem starts an agent again.** With `postmortem: agent:` unset it ran an empty command (zsh: permission denied), still posted `postmortem: started` and left an idle session that held the next start. It now runs `--agent`, else `agent:`, else routing's pick; a launch whose agent is gone right away is a failed start (session removed, no comment); a `postmortem-*` session whose pane is only a shell is treated as dead. `agentrun.Start` refuses an empty agent.
- **Video outputs on the shelf.** `issue-cli output add --kind video --file <x.mp4|x.webm>` copies a video onto the shelf (up to 200 MB; other files keep 25 MB, and the refusal names the size, the limit and an ffmpeg command for a smaller cut). With `ffmpeg` on PATH, add and `output revise` write a poster next to it (the frame at 1 s); without it the add still goes through and says so. The shelf row shows the poster with a ▶ corner and opens the reader, which plays the video with Approve / Reject and comments on the whole video; the Station's Outputs block plays it too. `…/shelf/file/` serves .mp4/.webm with their media type and answers Range requests (206) with the shelf's sandbox headers. Playtests gain a `media` check (`{target, state: loaded|playing}`).
- **Connector secrets can be optional.** A connector's `optional:` lists env vars from its `secrets:` it runs without: `connector exec`, the check and the launch pass only the keys that are set, and an unset optional one is not missing (no briefing warning, no Needs you row, its MCP server still loads). `connector check` prints and records each optional secret as set or not set (`"optional":[{"var":…,"set":…}]`, never a value); `issue-cli connectors` and the room read "not set (optional)". Secrets stay `env:` / `keychain:` references; `health lint` warns about an optional name that isn't one of the secrets (factory/connector-secrets-can-be-optional-so-a-department-runs-with-only-the-keys-that-are-set).
- **`workflow scan` from a worktree writes the worktree's workflow.yaml.** Run from another worktree of the project's repository (a worker's `.worktrees/<slug>`), scan and `workflow upgrade` used the primary checkout's file, so a worker's scan left an uncommitted change on main's working tree. They now use the same file in the cwd's worktree, and refuse when that worktree lacks it (cli/issue-cli-project-p-workflow-scan-run-from-a-workers-worktree-writes-the).
- **A criterion can wait on the lead's command.** When the proof needs something only the lead can run (a guided check script the worker guard refuses), the worker links it with `issue-cli prove <slug> <AC> --lead "issue-cli check-script <slug> --yes"` and stops, with no `question:` comment. The criterion reads "waits on the lead: <command>" on `prove --list`, in the criteria_proven gate, on the Station (a lead tag) and on the Evidence tab. The lead's tick (`queue next [--start]`) lists it under "Waits on you", and the Report's Queue card counts it. The lead's `prove <slug> --lead-run` runs the command (as argv, never through a shell; only `check-script` / `prove --run` on the issue's own slug), records the run in the sidecar and ticks the criterion once it passes. A run older than the branch's last code commit no longer counts (workflow/a-proof-that-waits-on-the-leads-act-shows-as-waits-on-the-lead-command-on-the-leads-tick-and-the-station).
- **A helper for tests that read the live board.** `liveboard.Dir(t)` (internal/liveboard) gives a test the primary checkout's `issues/` through the git common dir, so it works from a worktree, and skips on a fresh clone with no board; `LIVE_BOARD=<dir>` names another. The settled-rules tests use it, and CLAUDE.md's Validation section names it (factory/a-convention-and-helper-for-tests-that-read-the-live-board-git-common-dirs-issues-skip-on-a-fresh-clone).
- **Overlays can append to a status prompt.** `prompt_append:` on a status in a `systems:` or `types:` overlay adds its lines after the prompt in force instead of replacing it, so a system's guidance reaches tweaks and bugfixes whose type sets its own prompt. Start, transition and the dispatch briefing show both; the lint's type/system prompt clash names it; catalog entry `status-prompt-append`, `system-overlays` bumped (workflow/overlays-can-append-to-a-status-prompt-prompt-append-so-system-guidance-reaches-every-type).
- **decisions_respected names 10 touched files and "and N more".** Its refusal, its `yolo-hold:` entry and the transition dry run listed every file the branch changed under a hard decision's `paths:`, so a layout move printed about 1000 paths; they now name the first 10 and count the rest. `issue-cli decisions --touched <slug>` still lists them all (factory/decisions-respected-caps-its-touched-file-list-at-10-and-and-n-more).

- **Lint check `decisions`, and the Settled rules as scoped decisions.** `issue-cli health lint --check decisions` reports a D-n attribute group that names a decision key but doesn't parse or scopes to a system, department or type the project lacks, `hard` without the owner's words, a `paths:` glob matching no file and an `at:` status no workflow has. The Settled rules' source decisions get scopes (`system:Factory`, `department:marketing`, `project`) and `hard` where the owner's words are on the line, from `internal/tracker/testdata/settled-rules.tsv` through a guided check script the lead runs, so a Factory issue's Decisions in force block carries them.
- **A `Proves:` trailer naming a test points at `prove --run`, and an "or none" box takes `check --na`.** When the commit-msg hook accepts `Proves: AC1 TestX` it notes that AC1 stays unproven until `issue-cli prove <slug> --run` posts a tests: comment; on an issue with no `## Acceptance criteria` (box ids such as `I1` in the line, or any Proves: line) it says no Proves: line is needed there. `transition --dry-run`'s criteria_proven line names `prove <slug> --run` when a linked test has no tests: report yet. `check <slug> <id> --na "<reason>"` on a `## Shipping` box whose text ends in "or none" ticks it as it is and posts the reason as a `progress:` comment, instead of refusing (cli/a-proves-trailer-naming-a-test-points-at-prove-run-and-an-or-none-box-takes-check-na).
- **catalog-check passes a pure rename by itself.** A file the branch moves with its content unchanged (`git diff -M100%`) no longer asks for a bump or a `Catalog: n/a` line; a move that also edits the file still does, and so does a renamed bundled template. A bare `Catalog: n/a` is now told to leave the line out when the change touches no catalog entry, and the tweak and bugfix in-progress guidance says when a Catalog: line is needed (workflow/catalog-check-passes-a-pure-rename-by-itself-and-an-id-less-catalog-na-is-told-to-leave-the-line-out).

- **This repo's workflow.yaml is lint-clean on paths and its feature record.** The Workflow system's code map names the root `workflow.yaml` and `workflow.yaml.example` (the old `internal/viewer/` paths matched nothing), and the `features:` record now lists `postmortem` and `clarify-round`, which the file already had (factory/health-lint-to-zero-the-13-issue-cli-health-lint-warnings).
- **Docs say "a Mac", not "this Mac".** docs/memory.md's footprint measurements say "on a 36 GB M-series Mac", and docs/UI/workshop-board.md's browser note is about a Mac without Google Chrome, so the public docs no longer read like one laptop's notes. "This machine" stays: it means the reader's own machine (factory/docs-speak-for-any-machine-the-this-mac-sweep-121-mentions-in-72-files).

- **`transition --dry-run` lists a red-round hold.** The dry run now shows the cap the real transition would refuse on: an open `question: red rounds:` on a check this transition runs (or a local CI or `--cannot-fix` check), or a check at the cap that transition would post as a question first; it posts nothing. A question on a check the workflow no longer runs holds neither, so the two agree (cli/transition-refuses-on-a-red-round-hold-for-a-check-the-workflow-no-longer-has).

- **The shelf reader shows a markdown output's YAML frontmatter as a metadata table.** An output starting with a `---` block (an SEO article's `title:`, `slug:`, `faq:`) no longer renders as a rule, a paragraph and a list above the article: its keys sit in a small table on top (lists as lists, nested maps as key/value pairs, YAML that does not parse as it is), and the article's `# ` heading is the page title again, else the frontmatter's `title:` (cli/pka-shelf-read-wywietla-frontmatter-yaml-pliku-md-jako-zwyky-tekst).
- **The critic's raw output is kept beside each run; `critique --reparse <run id>`.** Each `issue-cli critique` run writes the critic's stdout to `<issues>/.critique/raw/<run id>.txt`, scrubbed by the same secret scan as memory (each secret becomes `[redacted: <kind>]`), and the run in `runs.jsonl` names the file. `issue-cli critique <slug> --reparse <run id>` parses that output again with the current parser and prints it beside the recorded report, recording nothing, so a parser fix can be tried on a real run (factory/the-critics-raw-output-is-kept-beside-each-run-so-a-parser-fix-can-be-tested-on-real-data).

- **Answer the critic by editing the design in place.** The `critique-gate` catalog entry (v0.61.0) gives the `in design` status prompt a line: a critic finding is answered by changing the design where it is wrong (`issue-cli replace <slug> --section "Design"`, or `--line` / `--match`), never by appending an "after critic run N" note, since the critic reads the design, not its history, and flags the old text against the new. The gate's refusal says the same. `TestCritiqueInDesignPromptSaysEditInPlace` checks the snippet and any bundled template that runs the rule (factory/answering-the-critic-the-design-prompt-says-edit-the-design-in-place-not-append-after-run-n-notes).
- **`clarify --assume` records each assumed answer as a provisional D-n.** Each open answer it marks assumed (and each one a YOLO gate marks) also adds `clarify round, provisional: Q1 <question> → <recommended option>` under `## Decisions`, and the answer line names it: `assumed — took the recommendation (D-3)`. The choice is recorded once, so the critic no longer asks for it again as `decide --provisional`. The owner's later answer to that question records a D-n that replaces the provisional one (cli/clarify-assume-records-each-assumed-answer-as-a-provisional-d-n-so-the-critic-doesnt-ask-for-it-twice).
- `check-script` after the merge brings back the files a script keeps beside it (`scripts/checks/<name>-*`, `<name>.*`, a `<name>/` folder) from the commit that removed it, and every run sets `CHECKS_DIR` to the folder the script sits in. A script that calls its probe no longer fails from history; the library's Gotchas and the `--new` skeleton say to use `$CHECKS_ROOT` and `$CHECKS_DIR`, never `BASH_SOURCE`.
- **`make install` and `install.sh` replace a binary by renaming, never in place.** Each binary is written to a temporary file beside the old one and `mv -f`d over it: a new file in one step, so macOS never kills it at start for the old file's code signature (exit 137, no output, as when a build was `cp`d over `~/.local/bin/issue-cli` on 2026-11-02), and an agent never runs a missing or half-written issue-cli. `make install BINDIR=<folder>` names the folder (default `$GOBIN`, else `$(go env GOPATH)/bin`, as before) (cli/issue-cli-w-localbin-nadpisany-w-miejscu-o-0945-2-11-kade-wywoanie).
- **`issue-cli decisions drift` and the `decision-drift` job.** The sweep lists what moved away from the decisions between gates: open issues citing replaced or expired refs; commits on main since the last sweep that changed a hard decision's `paths:` while their issue (a `Refs:` line, else the work branch that made them) doesn't cite it; "Settled rules" lines in the docs naming replaced refs, with file:line; provisional decisions older than 14 days that reach beyond their issue. It only reads; `--record` moves the mark in `<issues>/.decisions/drift.json`. `brief: builtin:decision-drift` runs it as a recurring job that files one issue with the report; its standing start is off until the owner turns it on. `decisions --state provisional` lists the provisional ones (factory/decision-drift-sweep-decisions-drift-and-the-decision-drift-job).
- **`issue-cli attach <slug> <file>...` puts screenshots on an issue.** It saves each image where the viewer's upload saves pasted ones (`<issues>/attachments/<hash>.<ext>`, the same code) and prints a markdown image link per file; `--section <name>` also appends the links. Only PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP sniffed from the bytes, at most 10 MB, and only from your worktree, the project or the temp folder; one bad file saves nothing. No more curl against `POST /p/<project>/upload` (cli/issue-cli-attach-slug-file-put-a-screenshot-on-an-issue-without-curl-against-the-viewer).
- **`list --parent <epic>` and `decisions <slug>`.** `issue-cli list --parent <epic>` lists the issues whose `parent:` (or legacy `umbrella:`) names that epic, whatever their version, and refuses a slug that is neither an issue nor anyone's parent; `show` on an epic prints a `Children (N):` line with each child's status. `issue-cli decisions <slug>` is `decisions --issue <slug>`, the way `decide <slug>` names one (cli/issue-cli-list-parent-epic-and-decisions-slug-read-an-epics-children-and-one-issues-decisions).
- **The YOLO rule prints once a session.** Under YOLO the rule's paragraph prints in the briefing and in `start`; `show` and `transition` print one line in its place, `YOLO on: the rule is in your briefing.` The briefing's ask step names the issue's slug instead of a literal `<slug>` (workflow/the-yolo-rule-prints-once-per-session-and-the-ask-rule-fills-in-the-slug).
- **`make validate` ends with one PASS or FAIL line.** The steps run one by one and the last line on stdout is `validate: PASS` or `validate: FAIL (<step>)`, so a backgrounded run whose wrapper exits 0 can no longer hide a red step; read that line (CLAUDE.md, Validation). `VALIDATE_STEPS=` runs a subset (cli/make-validate-ends-with-one-pass-or-fail-line-so-a-backgrounded-run-cant-hide-a-red-result).
- **The worker briefing names the folders its worktree leaves out.** The Worktree section gives the absolute path of the issues tree and, for a department's issue, of its `departments/<key>/` folder, where brand.md and icp.md are written for the gates to find. A path under `issues/` in the brief (`issues/Factory/designs/x.md`) is shown as its absolute path (factory/the-worker-briefing-names-where-issues-and-departments-files-live-since-the-worktree-leaves-them-out).
- **One copy of the playtest guide.** docs/patterns/playtest is the source; `cmd/issue-cli/guides/playtest` (what `issue-cli process playtest` prints) is generated from it by `go generate ./cmd/issue-cli/...` (tools/playtest-guides). `TestPlaytestGuidesInSync` names that command in each failure: a guide that differs, is missing, or has no source in docs/ (workflow/one-copy-of-the-playtest-guide-cmdissue-cliguidesplaytest-is-generated-from-docspatternsplaytest).
- **Marketing's publish gate waits for the newest shelf version; research counts its links.** Two new validation rules: `outputs_approved[: <kind>]` passes when the shelf holds an approved (or published) output for the issue and none still in review, so a revised output (`output revise`, back in review) holds `owner review → publish` until the owner approves that version; `section_min_links` counts distinct http(s) links in a section. The bundled marketing workflow (marketing@v0.61.0, catalog entry `brand-foundation`) runs `outputs_approved` on every `owner review → publish` and needs 5 links under `## Research` instead of 200 characters. The foundation and market-size prompts say brand.md, icp.md and market.md come from the settled sections with the owner's answers applied, in a stated format, and how to revise in place on an `answer:` comment; the owner_only gate says why beside it. `department update` no longer replaces an earlier marketing.yaml: `workflow upgrade` offers the new gates (cli/marketing-foundation-leftovers-the-publish-gate-checks-the-latest-shelf-version-research-checks-links-the-prompt-says-where-brand-md-comes-from).
- **Live sessions show without a UTF-8 locale.** tmux prints a tab in a `-F` format as `_` when the client has no UTF-8 locale (no LANG, as in a container, launchd or cron; tmux 3.5a and 3.8 alike), so the Crew page, worker matching, `issue-cli lead list` and lead tabs saw no sessions there. Every format now uses the printable separator `|:|` (`agentrun.Format`, `SplitFields`); `TestNoTmuxFormatUsesATab` keeps tabs out and `TestTmuxListsReadTheInstalledTmux` reads the installed tmux in the C locale. `TestNewcomerWalkthrough` no longer depends on the machine's login profile.
- **Amending a commit keeps its proof.** A `tests:` comment naming a commit (`prove --run at <sha>`) still holds after that commit is amended or rebased: the proof check matches it to the branch commit with the same `git patch-id`, so a message-only amend no longer reads stale; a code change in the amend still does. A `prove --commit` link to a sha no longer on the branch is found by patch too. The shipping prompt says to put `Refs: <slug>` in the last code commit before running the tests (workflow/amending-a-commit-keeps-proofs-and-tests-comments-valid-proof-matches-by-patch-and-the-shipping-prompt-adds-refs-before-the-last-commit).
- **release pick skips publish's changelog move.** A fix's work branch cut from main after a publish carries `docs(changelog): vX is published`, and `release pick <slug>` cherry-picked it onto the release branch: a second `## vX` heading and no `## Unreleased`, so the patch's publish refused (v0.60.1). Pick now leaves out any commit that changes only the changelog, adds no `## Unreleased` line and opens a version heading, and says so (`✓ Skipped publish's changelog move on main (<sha>)`), whether it came with a slug or by its sha (cli/release-pick-carries-mains-vx-is-published-changelog-commit-onto-the-release-branch-and-duplicates-the-version-heading).
- **Outsider polish from the v0.60 run-through.** Eleven broken relative doc links fixed (`docs/workflow.md` links without `.md`, `docs/UI/overview.md`, the playtest guide), and `make validate` now runs `make link-check`: every relative link in the tracked markdown points at a file (internal/doclinks; fragments and code are not checked). `issue-cli --version` and `-v` run `version`. `issue-cli next` on a fresh project with no `version:` lists every version's work instead of asking for a "project.yaml". `create` no longer prints "Thank you!". README and getting-started say that `make install` from a clone installs a commit-msg hook. `.gitignore` re-includes the tracked demo test issue and the playtest scenarios' `.agent-logs` fixtures. Every test binary's TestMain (internal/tmuxguard) drops `ISSUE_CLI_LOG` and `ISSUE_VIEWER_*` as it drops `$TMUX`, so a test run from a worker's shell never writes to its clilog. `make playtest-check` with no board prints one note for every unresolvable session instead of one per session (cli/outsider-polish-from-the-v0-60-run-through-broken-doc-links-and-a-link-check-issue-cli-version-next-on-a-fresh-project-creates-thank-you-the-make-install-hook-note-gitignore-exceptions-tests-drop-issue-cli-log).
- Each `## Must not` item ends with the criterion that fails when it is broken, `→ AC2`, or `→ none: <why>`: lint check `criteria` (and `transition` out of design) warns about an item with neither, or naming a box the issue doesn't have; the in-design prompt says so (catalog `must-not-section` v0.61.0).
- **Every route is in the security inventory, checked by a test.** `TestEveryRouteIsInTheSecurityInventory` reads `routes.go` (the mux patterns and each case of `handleProjectRoutes`) and fails for a route with no row in docs/API/security.md's Route inventory, a row with the wrong method, or a row whose route is gone; its message says to classify the route R/W/O/X and run `make security-check`. It found three routes with no row (`hall/station/<s>/edit`, `memory`, `memory.json`), now listed. The API overlay in `workflow.yaml` says the same in its in design prompt and on the way into testing, and that viewer tests' `newTestServer` signs in as the owner while `strangerHeader` opts out (api/every-route-in-the-router-is-in-docsapisecurity-mds-inventory-checked-by-a-test-and-the-api-briefing-says-so).
- **report-bug says what main fixed since an old build, and joins reports by their quoted error.** When the reporter's issue-cli build is behind the engine's main, report-bug searches main's commits since that build for each error the report quotes (`git log -S`) and lists the ones that touch it under "Maybe fixed since <version>:" in the issue or the `report:` comment, adds the `maybe-fixed` label and prints them with the hint to try a current build first (`--json`: `maybe_fixed`). An open cli-bug whose report quotes the same error (3+ words, not a command line) now takes a second report as a comment whatever its title (`--json`: `matched: "error"`).

## v0.60.1 — 2026-11-02

- **Public CI green on Linux.** v0.60.0's first public CI run failed on three tests that pass on a Mac. The guided-check library (`internal/checks/lib.sh`) passes shellcheck: the four variables it sets for the sourcing script carry a commented `SC2034` disable. `PLAYTEST_CHROME=none` now turns Chrome off in the viewer's no-JavaScript floor test too, so it skips instead of running a program called `none`. The system page marker test dates its fixture files apart: the page shows 8 rows ordered by the newest status change, which without a transition record is the file's modification time, and Linux stamps those from a coarse clock, so files written in one tick tied and the row with the `needs revision · D-5` marker was cut. Checked in an `ubuntu:24.04` container with ci.yml's setup (factory/public-ci-green-on-linux-shellcheck-warnings-in-lib-sh-playtest-chromenone-run-as-a-program-system-page-card-markers-missing).

## v0.60.0 — 2026-11-02

- **The slow tier is green: seven playtest steps fixed.** The first `make ux-test ALL=1` run went red on seven steps. Four were stale sessions: release-candidate-soak no longer expects scenario builds to say `dev` (they now report the checkout's version), stats-dashboard counts the new Decisions in force trend, red-rounds' fixture repeats the same failure three times (a new failure starts the count over, so the old one counted 1), and ceo-chat TP8 checks the desktop controls row only, since a phone folds them under More. Three were page bugs: on a phone the CEO chat's message box and keys no longer spill over the Waiting band when a permission card is up (the compose part scrolls inside the chat), the /leads card puts Talk to the CEO below its pills instead of over them, and the Crew page's Classic view · All projects links don't break mid-name. Playtest sessions gain a per-step `viewports:` (some of the session's sizes), and the `no_overlap` layout rule compares what shows, cut at scrolling panels (ui/slow-tier-green-before-v0-60-0-the-seven-playtest-steps-red-on-the-first-make-ux-test-all1-run).
- **README for the public release.** README.md says Version 0.60 (November 2026) and has a v0.60 line under What's new: one name, Dark Factory; the module `github.com/kacperwerema/dark-factory`; install from this repo; the viewer in `cmd/issue-viewer`; CI on every public snapshot; outside pull requests asked to open an issue first (factory/readme-for-the-v0-60-public-release-version-0-60-and-a-v0-60-line-under-whats-new).
- **Every playtest session runs every night.** `make ux-test ALL=1` runs every session on any branch: it drops `ISSUE=` and `S=`, so a worktree's own slug doesn't narrow it. Local CI gains `ci.nightly: {tier, at, timeout}`: the viewer's CI watcher (and `issue-cli ci watch`) starts that tier once a night, after `at` (default 03:00), when no run is live and `mem-guard check` allows it, with its own `timeout` (this repo: the slow tier, `make ux-test ALL=1`, 3h). Each failed step prints `FAIL <session>/<step> <title>`, and the slow tier's `failures:` regex reads those lines, so `ci status --tier slow` names what went red and the red log is kept like any red run's. A session a page move broke now goes red overnight instead of sitting stale on main. CLAUDE.md and docs/patterns/playtest/web-app.md name `ALL=1` and say console `no_errors` catches CSP violations (ui/run-every-playtest-session-regularly-a-make-target-for-all-sessions-and-a-nightly-run-so-stale-sessions-dont-sit-red-on-main).
- **Settled rules: owner acts, "a human starts the work", issue-reading lint checks.** docs/departments.md's Settled rules now say who may do an owner act (workers refused, the lead relays the owner's `--quote`, the viewer takes only the owner's browser, `fromOwnerBrowser`), that a human starts every agent with one exception (the read-only one-shot critic and the post-mortem at close, cited by their D-n), and which lint checks read issues. The Factory design prompt names the first two, so designs meet them instead of re-deriving them.
- **Small fixes from the retro sweep.** An in-progress issue whose section from entering its status (Implementation) still has an open box is no longer "on the owner · approval for …": the week report's blocked lines, dispatch's waiting-on-gate refusal, auto-start and the Ready flag treat the gate as the owner's only once those boxes are ticked. A bench run record carries `setup.sha`, the engine commit the issue-cli under test was built from (the resolved `--setup` ref, or the binary's own vcs.revision), which `issue-cli experiment run` reads for its baseline. `issue-cli create` on a workflow with no status before backlog says the workflow needs one instead of printing an empty `allowed:` list. A new test checks that commands taking no stdin input leave stdin unread (the reported stdin bug did not reproduce) (cli/small-engine-bugs-from-the-sweep-weekplan-counts-every-in-progress-issue-as-blocked-on-the-owner-bench-setup-has-no-sha-issue-cli-reads-stdin-creates-empty-allowed-list).
- **Workshop Board guide: page facts that bite.** docs/UI/workshop-board.md says Workshop pages have one theme (workshop.css has no theme switch), that a token in an inline `style="{{.}}"` renders as `ZgotmplZ`, that controls a script wires start disabled, that random ids stay out of text a playtest reads, and which browser tooling works on this Mac. New `TestWorkshopTemplateClassesHaveRules` fails when a Workshop template uses a `wb-*` class with no rule in static/workshop.css (eight dead classes dropped from templates); CLAUDE.md names workshop.css for Workshop pages (ui/workshop-board-guide-gaps-no-theme-switch-var-in-inline-styles-js-enabled-buttons-random-ids-workshop-css-vs-style-css-which-browser-works).
- **Closing a GitHub issue at done uses gh's own login, and publish refuses the upstream name.** When an issue with `repo:` and `number:` moves to done, the viewer used to run a token program from a fixed path in the home folder that exists on no other machine, so the close failed before gh ran. Now `gh issue close` uses gh's own login (`gh auth login`), or, when `github_token_command:` is set at the top of projects.yaml, that command's output as `GH_TOKEN` (no default). A failed close logs which step failed and says to sign gh in or set the key. `issue-cli release publish` (and its dry run) now also refuses a snapshot carrying the upstream author's name outside LICENSE.md, even with no private-names list. Fixtures, docs and this changelog no longer name other projects of upstream's; they use stand-in names. Note for upgrades: `report-bug`'s default engine repo changed with the module rename, so a projects.yaml that relied on the old repo to find the engine project needs `engine_project:` or a project with `repo: kacperwerema/dark-factory` (factory/upstream-leftovers-after-the-module-migration-the-github-close-runs-a-token-program-from-a-home-path-the-publish-leak-scan-upstream-project-names).
- **Installing from the public repo works, and builds name their version.** install.sh verifies the download with `shasum -a 256` when there is no `sha256sum` (a Mac without coreutils). go.mod retracts v0.55.0 and v0.56.0, which were tagged under the old module path and cannot build, so `go install …@latest` skips them. A build without the `-ldflags` version stamp, such as `go install github.com/kacperwerema/dark-factory/cmd/issue-cli@v0.60.0`, reports the module version Go recorded (`issue-cli version`, the viewer's build line) instead of `dev`.
- **One product name: Dark Factory.** `issue-cli`'s help and `process` overview, the owner sign-in dialog, the phone app's name, the stale-build refusal and CLAUDE.md say Dark Factory, and messages that named "the issue-viewer repository" say the dark-factory repository. The binaries keep their names, and so do the config and state folders (`ai-workflow-engine`), the `ISSUE_VIEWER_*` and `ISSUE_CLI_*` env vars and the release archive names: docs/getting-started.md lists them under Legacy names. Its Optional tools gain the shell notes (zsh, rtk, macOS BSD tools) that CLAUDE.md used to carry.
- **The public repo builds and tests each snapshot, and an outside PR is told to open an issue.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the gofmt check, `go vet` and `go test` (tmux from apt) on every push to main of the public repo; a private repo skips it. `PLAYTEST_CHROME=none` turns the playtest browser off, so the Chrome tests skip there. The privacy guard's failure on the public repo now says PRs are not merged here and asks for an issue, instead of pointing at a private repo an outsider can't see. It runs once the owner turns Actions on for the public repo.
- **The viewer lives in `cmd/issue-viewer` and `internal/viewer`.** No `.go` file is left in the repo root: the viewer's package (with its `templates/` and `static/`) is `internal/viewer`, and `cmd/issue-viewer` is its `main`, so `go install github.com/kacperwerema/dark-factory/cmd/issue-viewer@<version>` works. `make install` installs `issue-viewer` again (since the module rename it wrote a `dark-factory` binary). `make validate` checks `gofmt -l`.
- **The README is written for someone who finds the repo.** It uses one product name, Dark Factory, and says it ships two binaries, `issue-viewer` and `issue-cli`. It installs only from this repo: the curl line, `go install github.com/kacperwerema/dark-factory/cmd/issue-cli@latest`, or a clone. Contributing says the public repo gets one snapshot per release, issues are welcome and pull requests are not merged there. The Licence paragraph points at LICENSE.md for who grants a commercial license, and the section about another project is gone. The version line says 0.59.
- **Fewer loose ends at the repo root.** `sync-issues.sh` is now `scripts/sync-issues.sh` (docs/github-integration.md follows), three stray `.images/note-*.png` files are gone, `claude-mods/`, `playtest/` and `tools/` each have a short README saying what they are, and the root `workflow.yaml` opens with a header saying it is this repo's own workflow: start yours with `issue-cli project init` or `workflow.yaml.example`.
- **The module is `github.com/kacperwerema/dark-factory`.** go.mod and every import use it, and install.sh, the README install line, releases, the issues link and `issue-cli process changes` point at github.com/kacperwerema/dark-factory. `report-bug` finds the engine project by the repo `kacperwerema/dark-factory` (or `engine_project:`). The upstream author's name now appears only in LICENSE.md, which is unchanged; a new guard in `make validate` (`TestTrackedFilesNameUpstreamOnlyInLicense`, beside the leak scan) fails on it in any other tracked file and names the file and line. Fixtures and demo data use `example-org` placeholders.
- **A scenario board on demand, and before/after shots.** `make serve SCENARIO=<name> [BASE=main] [PORT=n]` stages a playtest scenario as ux-test does (own HOME, XDG dirs and tmux socket: the shared viewer.json is never written), leaves its board running on PORT when free (else a free port, said so), and prints the URLs, logs, shot and stop commands; `BASE=<ref>` builds that ref with `git archive` and serves the same scenario on a second board. `make serve-stop [SCENARIO=]` stops only processes whose command line names the board's projects.yaml and removes only marked folders. `make shot URL=/p/…` shoots the served board; `BASE=main` shoots both (OUT and OUT.base), `HTML=1` saves both pages' rendered HTML and prints the diff command. scenario.yaml `extra_config:` merges top-level keys into the staged projects.yaml (ui/a-scenario-board-on-demand-playtest-serve-stages-a-scenario-in-an-isolated-viewer-and-prints-its-url-and-make-shot-basemain-shoots-the-before).
- **Every tmux call in the tests names its own socket.** The test helpers (`isolateTmux` and `testTmux` in the repo root and `internal/agentrun`, the Crew page's, `internal/checks`' other server) and the playtest runner's `Scenario.Tmux` pass `-S <own socket>`, and every `kill-server` runs with `-S` and TMUX removed, including `lib.sh`'s private tmux, `scripts/security-check.sh` and `scripts/readme-shots.sh`. A guard test (`TestTmuxCallsInTestsNameTheirSocket`) fails on an `exec.Command("tmux", …)` in a `_test.go` without `-S` first, on a `"kill-server"` in any Go file outside a call that names `-S`, on a runner's `Run(ctx, "tmux", …)` without `-S` in any Go file, and on a shell line running kill-server without `env -u TMUX` and `tmux -S`. Every test binary also drops `$TMUX` before its first test: each package's `TestMain` calls `internal/tmuxguard` (TMUX and TMUX_PANE unset, TMUX_TMPDIR a private folder under /tmp, its server killed by `-S` at the end), so the code under test, scripts, built binaries and a child with a nil Env (the playtest `Scenario.Stop` shape that killed the shared server on 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01) never reach the developer's server. `TestEveryTestBinaryDropsTMUX` fails on a test package without it; `TestIsolatedTestsNeverReachTMUX` points `$TMUX` at a decoy server and checks a nil-Env tmux leaves it alone.
- **Decision expiry.** `issue-cli decisions --state expired` lists decisions past their `expires:` day; `decide --renew D-n --expires <date>` gives one a new last day and `decide --let-go D-n` closes out an expired one (`ended:` in its group); on a hard decision both are the owner's acts (worker refused, the lead relays `--quote`). An expired hard decision is one Needs you row with Renew / Let go (owner's browser only). Logged as `renewed` and `expired` events.
- **A branch that changes a hard decision's files must cite it.** New validate rule `decisions_respected` at `in progress → testing` and every edge into `shipping` in the `development` template (the `decision-ledger` catalog entry): when the work branch's own changes (three-dot diff from its merge-base with main, so a merge of main adds nothing) touch the `paths:` of an active hard decision, whatever its scope or `at:`, the issue must cite that ref in its body (an `n/a` line doesn't count) and have no open challenge of its own on it. The refusal names the decision, the files and the fixes; under YOLO it passes as a `yolo-hold:` entry naming the files. No recorded base: a ⚠ line says the audit didn't run. `issue-cli decisions --touched <slug>` prints the audit, and the fresh critic's brief lists the hard decisions the branch touches.
- **`make playtest-check` names four session pitfalls.** A target or pattern with `*` or `#` inside a segment (`status-*`) fails with "`*` and `#` match one whole segment" and the pattern to write; an action under `checks:` (`checks: [{open: /x}]`) fails with "move it to act:"; a target matching an `internal/testids` entry with a dash or capital fails here as it does in `make validate`, and an unknown target that isn't snake case says so; a YAML value that starts with a quote and goes on after it (`expect: "Saved" shows`) gets "quote the whole value" with its line instead of "did not find expected key". docs/patterns/playtest/README.md gets a Pitfalls list with the rules check can't see: checks run after act:, zero-size boxes, script-set test ids in a template, box ids from the heading, folder-relative slug pins, http not signed in, today's health snapshot (ui/playtest-session-pitfalls-the-runner-and-playtest-check-dont-flag-segments-quoted-expect-open-under-checks-zero-size-elements-testids).
- **The feature catalog is one file per entry, and its checks say the fix.** The 63 entries left in `workflow-features.yaml` moved to `cmd/issue-cli/templates/workflow-features/<id>.yaml`; the list file is gone, and the folder's README.md holds the field reference. Catalog order, which `workflow scan` records `features:` in, is by category, then id, so there is no list to keep in order; the bundled templates' records were rewritten in that order (nothing else in them changed). A guard failure now names its fix: a record out of order prints the expected `features:` block in order; a bare folder source (`internal/x/`) suggests `internal/x/**`; `@prompt` must stand alone; a new validator rule names the `rule_in:` to add and the folder. `issue-cli workflow catalog-check` runs the same checks at the branch head (an unknown category, sources that match no file, a template's record not what a scan records), and asks no stamp bump for a template whose `features:` record alone changed. New `issue-cli workflow scan --template <name>` rewrites a bundled template's record from the checkout's catalog (`--dry-run` prints it; outside a checkout it prints the bundled copy). A detect probe can name a map key (`types.docs`). Department update knows the old-order marketing and planning workflows as earlier texts (workflow/catalog-guard-tests-say-the-fix-features-record-order-template-stamps-category-order-bare-folder-sources-new-validator-rules).
- **A lead's rules and scope changes go into the issue body.** Every lead brief (project, custom `leads.project`, department and group leads, through `tracker.LeadRules`) now says: rules for a worker go in its issue before dispatch with `issue-cli append <slug> --section "Lead rules" --body "- ..."` (the section is made when missing), so the worker reads them at start and after a restart, not only in a peer message; a scope change mid-issue is an appended criterion box plus a D-n, and the message only points at them. The brief's boxes rule reads: owner boxes and other issues' boxes are untouched; your own Implementation/Testing/Shipping boxes are yours. docs/patterns/lead-session.md says the same (factory/a-leads-rules-and-scope-changes-go-into-the-issue-body-not-only-into-peer-messages).
- **Check-script library: `$SANDBOX` after `sandbox_project`, a second project, a git-repo sandbox, `is_worker`, and `make ported-checks`.** `sandbox_project` now sets `$SANDBOX` to the sandbox folder (an earlier `sandbox_dir`'s is kept); `sandbox_project --add <slug>` adds a second project to the same projects.yaml (`$SANDBOX_ADDED_PROJECT`, `$SANDBOX_ADDED_DIR`); `sandbox_git_repo [var] [name]` makes a repo with `.worktrees/` ignored, a local identity with no signing, hooks or auto maintenance, and a local bare remote with `main` pushed (`$SANDBOX_REPO`, `$SANDBOX_REMOTE`); `is_worker` asks `issue-cli lead whoami`, the worker guard's own test (`$AGENT_NAME` is often unset in a worker), and only reports: there is no owner wrapper that clears a worker's identity. `make ported-checks [LIB=<path>] [ONLY=<name>]` runs internal/checks/testdata/ported as machine runs; the three scripts are up to date with main (the exact-target switch command, a longer lead brief, the dispatch's waiting-on-gate refusal, proof-links now in main's workflow) and skip the transitions a worker's guard refuses (cli/guided-check-library-gaps-sandbox-after-sandbox-project-a-second-project-a-git-repo-sandbox-is-worker-an-owner-wrapper).
- **Challenge a decision through the lead.** `issue-cli decision challenge <ref> --why "…" [--propose "…"]` records that an agent thinks a decision is wrong: a `challenge:` comment on its issue and the decision's, a `challenged` event in the decisions log, a block on the lead's next issue-cli call and one Needs you row (To answer) with the decision, why, the proposal and the other issues on it. The owner keeps it (`decision resolve <ref> --keep`, the row's Keep it, or confirming it) or changes it (`decide --replaces <ref>`); either closes the challenge, and only the owner may: a worker is refused, a lead relays `--quote`, the viewer takes it from the owner's browser only. The new rule `decision_unchallenged` holds the challenger's own issue, and no other, until then; under YOLO it passes as a `yolo-hold:` entry, and `yolo queue` now lists decision holds by ref. The `decision-ledger` catalog entry is v0.60.1 (factory/decision-challenges-through-the-lead-decision-challenge-and-resolve-a-needs-you-row-the-decision-unchallenged-validator).
- **Adding a command: one checklist, and every help hint is checked.** docs/CLI/overview.md's "Adding a command" (was "Adding a new subcommand") lists every registry and guard test a new command or subcommand must satisfy before `make validate` finds them one at a time: `commandAccess` and `readSamples`, the parse calls and the flags-before-positional rule `TestEveryCommandRejectsUnknownFlags` checks, help without a project, help hints, telemetry, where code hooks in after `cmd.Run` in `runTraced` (`ctx.issue`), bootstrap mode, and tests on a project without `WorkflowFile`; a test fails when the section stops naming one or a name leaves the code. `TestEveryHelpHintNamesRealFlags` runs over every registered command's help, as `TestExperimentHintsNameRealFlags` did for experiment alone: each `issue-cli <cmd> [<sub>] … --flag` hint and each usage line starting with the command's name may name only flags that command (nested subcommands such as `bench task add` included) defines (cli/adding-a-cli-command-one-checklist-of-the-registries-it-must-join-found-before-make-validate).
- **The decision ledger, read and counted.** `issue-cli decisions log [--ref <slug>#D-n] [--issue <slug>] [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--json]` lists the audit log's events in time order (recorded, amended, confirmed, replaced, injected briefings, gate holds), leaving out writes that did not land; `--ref` also matches briefings that carried the decision. A real `transition` that `decisions_cited` or `citations_current` refuses, or YOLO passes, now appends one `held` event per rule and ref (rule, issue, from, to, passed); a dry run never does. The Stats page gets a Decisions card: in force by scope, hard and soft, provisional (no owner's words yet), open challenges, gate holds by decision and the briefings that carried the block in the page's range; a project with none says so and names `issue-cli decide`. A new `decisions` health family snapshots those counts (not a score component) and Stats draws "Decisions in force" from it (factory/decision-audit-log-and-stats-panel-decisions-log-the-decisions-card-and-the-decisions-health-family).
- **Judgement steps and step proof links come from the newest playtest run.** A step's `judgement_step` and `proves:` are read from the newest run of its session that has the step, not from any old run in `.playtest/runs`: a step whose `do:`/`expect:` came out is no longer the owner's box, and a step retargeted from AC2 to AC6 links AC6 only. A `-step` run of other steps leaves the rest as their newest run had them. `check`, `uncheck` and `check-remove` refusals on a box a judgement step made the owner's add `TPn is a judgement step (do:/expect: in <session>); drop them if the step is automated, then re-run make ux-test`, and the "add `proves:` to the step" way to prove a criterion ends with "then re-run make ux-test" (workflow/judgement-steps-and-proof-links-come-from-the-latest-playtest-run-per-session-not-from-any-old-run).
- **Six tests hold under load.** `expect_stand_in_ran` and `expect_stand_in_got` in the check-script library wait up to 30 s by default (was 5 and 10; they still return as soon as the stand-in shows), the playtest runner gives a scenario board a minute to answer with a 2 s timeout per request, and the git tests turn off git's detached auto maintenance, which could still be writing `.git/objects` while the test's temp dir was removed. TestAWorkflowChangeGetsAVerdictPerWorkflow's stand-in clock no longer advances on the budget watcher's ticks, so a slower worker can't show up as "wall +33%". Fixes the load flakes in TestLibAgentStandInStaysAndLogsInput, TestUXTestEndToEnd, TestPublishRefusals, TestChangelogUnionMerge, TestCommandEmbedder and TestAWorkflowChangeGetsAVerdictPerWorkflow.
- **Issue-scoped workflows keep `memory:`, `privacy:` and `decisions:`.** `WorkflowConfig.Clone`, which every issue-, type- and system-scoped config goes through, listed fields by hand and dropped `Memory`, `Privacy` and `Decisions`, plus the transition's runtime switches (`DeferOwnerProof`, `KeepOwnAssignee`, the red-round hooks, `DeferDecisionHolds`). So an issue's config ran with no embedder, no privacy choice and the default brief cap. It copies them all now. A reflection test fills every exported field and fails naming any field Clone leaves zero; a second does the same for a department file's merge with Product's project-wide blocks (workflow/workflowconfig-clone-copies-fields-by-hand-and-drops-memory-a-reflection-test-catches-a-missing-field).
- **A malformed `Catalog:` or `Proves:` line is refused at commit time.** The repository's new commit-msg hook (`make hooks`, which `make install` runs; `issue-cli workflow commit-msg --install`) reads every `Catalog:` and `Proves:` line with the parsers catalog-check and the proof check use, and refuses the commit with the fix: `n/a` without an id, a colon after the id, an id the catalog lacks, `<slug>#ACn`, a package path, a playtest step or `<session>/<step>`. Before, such a line was silently ignored or failed catalog-check minutes later at the end of `make validate`. docs/Workflow/proof.md now says playtest steps prove through `proves:` in their session, never through a trailer (workflow/catalog-and-proves-trailers-in-a-malformed-form-are-caught-at-commit-time-not-at-the-end-of-make-validate-or-never).
- **A refused `check --na` says so, and two more named forms.** Every refusal from `issue-cli check <slug> <id> --na` now exits non-zero and starts with `✗ not ticked:` and the reason, so it never reads as a tick. A free reason under three words says its count and the minimum (`"none" is too short: 1 word, a free reason needs at least 3 words`); a `<placeholder>` left in, such as `<project>`, is named. Shipping boxes have no N/A: the refusal (and check-remove's hint) says to tick them plainly when done. New forms: `cli flow covered by <TestName>` for a CLI-only change a go test walks, and `page state a scenario can't build (<what>), shown by <evidence>` for a page change a scenario board can't reach; lint `na-boxes` accepts both and the testing prompt lists them (cli/check-na-a-refused-reason-looks-ticked-short-reasons-dont-say-the-minimum-and-the-named-forms-miss-common-cases).
- **Designs cite the decisions in force; citations stay current.** Two new validate rules for the edges out of `in design`, in the `development` template and the `decision-ledger` catalog entry. `decisions_cited` refuses a design whose `## Design` doesn't name each hard decision in force for the issue (its ref, or a line `n/a <ref>: <why>`; a bare `n/a <ref>` refuses; soft decisions and the issue's own never hold it). `citations_current` refuses a body citing a replaced or expired decision and names the replacer. Each refusal prints the refs, their text and the commands that fix it; both take `since <date>`, so issues filed earlier pass. The Design checklist gets "Decisions in force cited". Under YOLO a hold passes and leaves a `yolo-hold:` comment in the review after the fact. `issue-cli health lint` now warns on a validate rule the build doesn't know (factory/decisions-cited-in-design-the-citation-step-and-the-decisions-cited-and-citations-current-validators).
- **A worker who moves its own issue to backlog keeps it.** The `in design → backlog` clear of the assignee now leaves it when the issue's own worker runs a forward transition, so its next `transition --to "in progress"` passes without a second `start` (the transition prints `Assignee kept`). The lead's or the viewer's move, and any move backwards, still clear it. `start`'s `Status:` line shows the status after its own auto-advance, and `transition --to <the current status>` prints `Status is already <status>: nothing changed.` with exit 0 instead of an error.
- **Decisions in force reach the agent.** `issue-cli start` and `show` print `== Decisions in force ==`, the worker briefing carries `## Decisions in force` next to `## Memory`, and the CEO and department lead briefs a "Decisions in force" item (the CEO project-scoped ones, a department lead project and its department's). Each line names the full ref, hard or soft, the scope, the text on one line and a short owner quote. Hard decisions are never folded; soft ones fill up to 12 lines (`decisions: brief_cap:`), the rest is "and N more: issue-cli decisions --in-force <slug>"; with none the block says so and names the command. The fresh critic's brief lists them by ref with the file that holds each, never the text. `decisions --lead ceo|<department>` lists a lead's set. `start`, a launched dispatch, a delivered reprompt and a lead spawn append one `injected` event to `<issues>/.decisions/log.jsonl` (briefing, issue, refs) when anything is in force; `show`, `decisions`, `dispatch-prompt` and `critique --brief` never write (factory/decisions-in-force-the-filtered-block-in-start-show-the-worker-lead-and-ceo-briefings-and-the-critics-brief).
- **Workers get a sanctioned way past an installed issue-cli older than main.** On the engine's own repository, when the installed build (`git describe` from `make install`, a soak's release candidate) is behind main, the worker briefing has a `## Your issue-cli build` section: build the branch's issue-cli in the worktree (`go build -o ./issue-cli ./cmd/issue-cli`, git-ignored) and use `./issue-cli` for the issue, and never make install, which replaces the build everyone on the machine runs. `issue-cli check-script <slug> --yes` runs the scripts with `$ISSUE_CLI` set to that branch build when it is there, or to `--cli <path>`; lib.sh's `sic` and `sandbox_project` use it. `issue-cli health lint`'s `schema` check now warns about a validate rule this build doesn't know, at its line, naming the rule (factory/workers-run-an-installed-issue-cli-older-than-main-the-briefing-names-no-sanctioned-way-to-get-a-build-that-knows-mains-rules).
- **Your phone gets a push when something new waits on you, and the viewer installs as an app.** The viewer sends an encrypted Web Push when a row appears on Needs you (a ready gate, a `question:`, a clarify round, local CI red, a playtest or checks to run, outputs, a due job, a post-mortem, a failing connector), when a live worker is stuck at a menu, and when a subscription hits its usage limit. Each event goes to each device once, and a tap opens the right page. Open **🔔 Notify my phone** on Needs you (or `/push`), sign in as the owner, and tap **Notify me here**; on iPhone add the page to the Home Screen first (Share → Add to Home Screen) and subscribe from that app. Per-project mute and quiet hours (one summary push when they end) are on the same page. The keys (VAPID) are made on your machine on first use and kept with the devices under the state dir's `push/` (0600); there is no account anywhere, and the push service is the browser's own. A push carries a title, the project, the kind and a link: no notes, file contents or paths. Only the viewer that holds `viewer.json` sends, so a sandbox viewer never pushes to your phone. RFC 8291 encryption and VAPID are written with Go's standard library, no new dependency.
- **catalog-check asks only the entries a change to a shared file belongs to.** A new case in `workflow_validate.go`'s rule switch is now asked about only for the entry whose `rule_in:` names that rule (it asked four or five gate entries before); a change above the switch still asks all of them. A change to a bundled template counts only for the entries whose `detect:` probes see the changed status or transition, so a stamp move by `catalog-bump` or another feature's block asks nothing more (the template still needs its stamp bump). A wording change to the built-in dispatch briefing (`dispatch_prompt.go`, string literals and comments only) asks nothing; a code change there still does. `playtest-sessions` now watches the runner's session keys and commands (`cmd/playtest/session.go`, `vocab.go`, `main.go`) instead of the whole runner, and `worktree` / `worktree-setup` watch `internal/agentrun/worktree.go` instead of the whole package. A branch behind main is checked against the merge base, as before, now pinned by a test (workflow/catalog-check-asks-for-catalog-na-on-every-entry-that-shares-a-source-file-not-only-the-entry-the-change-belongs-to).
- **One Crew page across every project, with the owner's actions, at phone width.** `/crew` lists every project's CEO, department leads and live workers with their state (working, waiting on you, idle, usage limit, and new: paused by mem-guard), memory and load at the top. Signed in as the owner, each row has its actions, each asking first: Start a lead (as `lead spawn`), Stop a worker (ends exactly its one tmux session; the worktree and branch stay, and the issue gets a `stopped:` comment), Nudge (one line, pasted as "Message from the owner (Crew page): …"), Tail (the pane's last 60 lines), and Pause / Resume for auto-start and the queue. A worker mem-guard paused shows as paused and can't be stopped or nudged. The routes are owner-only (`crew/*`, `queue/*`): the sign-in on loopback, the verified identity on a public name. Every action is logged with who and when in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/ai-workflow-engine/crew/actions.jsonl`, and the page lists the last five. Linked from the Map, All CEOs and each project's Crew page.
- **The owner can pause the lead's queue.** A paused queue holds `queue next` before anything else, even an item with its own quote: `hold: the queue is paused by the owner (Crew page) at Sat 21:40: resume it on the viewer's /crew page`. Only the viewer resumes it; issue-cli has no resume, so an agent can't lift the owner's pause.
