- **`issue-cli comments`, `decide --ref` and `replace --body-file`.** `issue-cli comments <slug>` prints an issue's comment trail alone, each as `#<id>` (the page's number) with day, author, kind and done, filtered by `--since`, `--author`, `--kind` and `--open`, and as JSON with `--json`. `decide --ref N-26` (repeatable) writes `(ref N-26)` on the D-n line, so a decision can cite a lead's provisional N-number; `show`, `show --json` (new `decisions` array) and `decisions` print it, and `--confirm` keeps it. `replace --body-file <path|->` reads the new section content from a file or stdin, as append does (cli/issue-cli-comments-decide-ref-and-replace-body-file).
- **Parking is not refused by an open red-rounds question.** With a check at the red-round cap (a `question: red rounds:` comment open), `issue-cli transition <slug> --to parked` refused with "… is red 4 of 4: it is a question for the owner now", though parking runs none of the held check. A transition to a side status (parked, or any optional status reached from every status) now skips the red-round cap: it neither refuses nor counts rounds. The forward transition stays held and still does not re-run the check. `issue-cli close --duplicate-of/--fixed-by` was never affected (cli/parking-an-issue-is-refused-by-an-open-red-round-question-on-a-forward-transition).
- **Decisions carry a scope and a strength.** A D-n line may end with an attribute group, `{scope: system:Workflow; hard; at: in progress; paths: cmd/**; expires: 2027-03-31}`: whom it reaches (`issue`, the default, `project`, `system:<S>`, `department:<key>`, `type:<t>`), whether it is the owner's hard rule, at which statuses it matters, the files a hard one guards and its last day. `decide --scope/--hard/--at/--paths/--expires` set it on a new decision; `decide --amend D-n` changes only the group, every other byte of the line stays, and a text change is refused with the `--replaces` command. Every change to a hard decision is the owner's: a worker is refused, a lead relays the owner's `--quote` (a `decision:` comment on the issue, waiting in `yolo queue` under YOLO), and the Decisions log's Confirm gets a Hard checkbox and a Make hard button. A decision replacing a hard one inherits hard, scope and paths. `decisions --in-force <slug> [--status S] [--json]` lists what reaches an issue (own, parent, cited, system, department, type, project), hard first. Every D-n write is logged in `<issues>/.decisions/log.jsonl` (pending, then done or failed; refs and groups only, no text or quote). The board's 596 existing D-n lines parse as before; a trailing `{from: …}` in old text stays text (factory/decision-records-scope-hard-at-paths-and-expires-on-the-d-n-line-decide-amend-the-in-force-query-and-the-audit-log).
- **The CEO chat on a phone: dictation, the camera, a switcher over every lead, and a box that stays above the keyboard.** At phone width the chat column follows the visible viewport, so the box and Send stay above the on-screen keyboard (the band and the keys hide while you type); one row of keys stays (Interrupt, ↑ ↓ ↵ 1 2 3) and **More** shows Send now, Show details, To background, the mode switch and Stop session. **🎤 Dictate** fills the box from the browser's own speech recognition (Chrome and Safari; Polish or English on the PL/EN chip, remembered per browser) and shows only where the browser has it; it never sends, and a result that arrives after Send is dropped. **📷** opens the camera on touch screens; a HEIC photo or an image over 10 MB is re-encoded to JPEG in the browser before the existing image route takes it (an undecodable one goes the file way). **Switch** opens a sheet with every project's CEO and department leads, each with its state (working, waits on you, idle, not running, or couldn't read), the CEO rows with the project's Needs-you count, read only when opened (`?part=switcher`, each pane under a 2 s deadline). /leads shows the same states and now counts Needs you the way the Needs you list does. An unsent draft survives switching chats. Playtest steps gain `stubs:` (`speech-recognition`, `no-speech-recognition`, `keyboard`) and the `tmux` pane check takes `not_contains` (docs/agent-dispatch.md#the-ceo-chat-on-a-phone, ui/ceo-chat-on-the-phone-voice-input-uploads-from-the-camera-roll-a-switcher-over-every-projects-and-departments-lead-chat).
- **The factory measures itself every night.** A bundled job, `builtin:nightly-improve` on the `Product` entry's `jobs:` (Product may now hold jobs, key `product/<id>`), runs `issue-cli bench nightly` on the owner's standing start: the benchmark on main's head, then at most one experiment the owner queued (`issue-cli experiment queue <slug> --quote …`), judged against that baseline and never adopted. Each night is a line in `<bench>/runs/<benchmark>/nights.jsonl` and a morning report: pass rate, median time and median tokens per task against the night before (better / same / worse, with `bench compare`'s thresholds) and the last 7 nights. Nothing runs while memory is 50% or more or the load is over the core count; the night is recorded as held. The Report gets a "Factory last night" card, status.md a `Last night:` line, and a pass rate under last week's median a Needs you row. Briefs may use `{{slug}}` (factory/nightly-self-improvement-loop-benchmark-on-main-one-queued-experiment-and-the-mornings-better-or-worse-numbers-on-the-report).
- **The red-round cap counts a repeated failure, not every red run.** Four designs reached the cap of 4 today on critic runs whose findings were each new and fewer (3, 2, 1, 1). A round now counts only when the same failure comes back: the same critic finding (path and text, case and punctuation folded, line left out), the same failing test, the same unproven criterion, or the same command output with numbers and shas masked. A round whose failures are all new starts the count over at 1; the same finding four times still raises the question. Any comment by the owner on the issue starts every count over. `issue-cli rounds` lists each round since the last green and why it counted ("same finding: … as round 2"), and the critic's red-round line says it too. A cap question on a check the transition no longer runs (the critic gate taken out of workflow.yaml) stays open but no longer holds the transition. Open questions stay open. `max_red_rounds` keeps its meaning and its default of 4; catalog entry `red-round-cap` is v0.60.0 (workflow/the-red-round-cap-counts-a-repeated-failure-not-every-red-run-an-owner-comment-starts-the-count-over).
- **On the public name, `/` is Needs you across every project, and each waiting row acts in one tap.** A request the identity gate verified for an owner (the Cloudflare Access token) lands on the list instead of the map, with a project switcher on top (each project's count, and Map at `/?map=1`); loopback keeps the map. On every Needs you page a gate's **Approve** stamps it in one tap (Approve selected keeps its confirm), a clarify row shows its questions with their options and costs as buttons, the recommended first (one tap answers it through the Station's route, the comment says "on Needs you"; more than 3 questions open the Station), a question row shows the question and a **Reply** box that posts your comment, an after-the-fact YOLO row has **Agree**, and every issue row links its station and the CEO chat. Every action posts to the route it used before, with the same owner checks. Works at 390px with no sideways scroll.

## v0.59.0 — 2026-10-31

- **A critique finding keeps its continuation lines.** A finding written as a lead-in line ending in `:` with bullets under it ("Some cases are not covered:" then three `- ` lines) was stored cut at the colon, and the bullets were lost from runs.jsonl, the `critique:` comment and the station. A finding ending in `:` now keeps the bullet lines right after it, up to the first line that is not a bullet. Only lines the parser used to ignore join: a bullet starting with a severity word, `Coverage:` or `Not read:` reads as before, so output that held the gate still holds it. They are indented under it in the comment, the CLI and the transition's refusal, and on their own lines on the station. Prose after a finding that does not end in `:`, or after a lead-in's bullets, is still ignored, and the brief tells the critic it may write bullets. Runs already recorded stay as they were (cli/critique-a-should-finding-whose-text-is-a-lead-in-line-ending-in-followed).
- **`make playtest-report APPLY=1` ticks the boxes in a worker's shell.** With no `PLAYTEST_PROJECT` it ran `issue-cli --project issue-viewer`, a project this machine doesn't have (the engine board is `dark-factory`), so it printed `tick (2): TP4 TP5` and ticked nothing. The report now takes the dispatched worker's `ISSUE_VIEWER_PROJECT`, else the configured project whose issues dir is this repo's, and prints it on a `project:` line. After APPLY it reads the checklist back and fails naming any box still unticked. `playtest check` keeps its old default (cli/make-playtest-report-issueslug-apply1-printed-tick-2-tp4-tp5-on-a).
- **One click from the company floor to a chat; the floor no longer moves.** One click on HQ's CEO robot ("Chat with the CEO →") opens the CEO chat, and every department tile, wing and sub-room has "Chat with the lead →"; the same link heads each room's inspector. The floor no longer pans or zooms: no minimap, no − / + / Fit and no 1–4 keys, since the whole floor always fits the screen.
- **The yolo-owner-checks playtest is green again, on the floor too.** Its step TP2 still looked for the owner box's text in the company's To review after YOLO list, which since the review after YOLO lists one row per issue with counts ("1 owner check shop/barcode-scan"). TP2 now checks that row on the floor's After YOLO tab and on `?classic=1`; the box text stays checked on the Station (factory/playtest-yolo-owner-checks-step-tp2-fails-on-main).
- **The company page is the floor: the whole company on one screen.** A fixed map that fits the view (Fit, zoom, keys 1–4, drag, pinch, a minimap) with an inspector rail holding Waiting on you, Room | After YOLO and the message box. Tiles never grow; what they drop is in the room's inspector. The old page is at `?classic=1`. Playtest sessions gain the `no_page_scroll` layout rule and the `inside` check, and `key:` takes `"1"`–`"4"`, `f`, `+`, `-` and `Control+`/`Meta+` combinations.
- **Department templates speak of your site(s), and department add says what to commit.** The marketing template said "client sites" everywhere (role, mission, goal, role.md, the weekly SEO brief, the seo-pass type); GTM said "clients". Both now fit a product company with one site as well as an agency. `department add` ends naming the files git doesn't track yet and the `git add … && git commit … --` for them; `--commit` commits exactly those. `issue-cli health lint` has an `untracked` check for department files git doesn't track. `department add` also says when an in-repo projects.yaml holds the uncommitted entry. `issue-cli department update <name>` brings an installed department to the new text (role.md, job briefs, role, mission, goal names, and a workflow file that differs only in wording) only where it still holds an earlier template's text byte for byte, keeping everything else (`--side` writes the template beside a changed file; lint names a copy left behind); the Updates page lists departments with new template text.
- **Evidence folders under issues/ no longer warn on every call, nor show as issues.** Issue loading skips folders whose name starts with `_` (as it already skipped `.` folders), so a README or report under `issues/_evidence/` no longer prints `warning: skipping …: missing frontmatter delimiter` ahead of every command's output, and evidence notes that have frontmatter no longer load as status-less issues. Images there still serve through `/files/`. A markdown file without frontmatter anywhere else still warns, once per file per process instead of once per board load.
- **The CEO chat takes any file the terminal takes: .zip, PDF, text and more next to images.** 📎 Attach, paste and drop now take any file. Images up to 10 MB still show as thumbnails. Anything else, including a bigger image, goes to the new owner-only `POST …/lead/file` (50 MB, 5 per message, empty files refused). It is streamed into `.agent-logs/<lead>/chat/files/<hash>/<name>` (0600, name cleaned to ASCII) and shows as a chip with type, name and size. Send adds one `[File: <absolute path>]` line per file for the lead to read. Your turn shows each file as a chip that downloads it (`GET …/lead/file/<hash>/<name>`, owner only, always an attachment with nosniff and a sandbox CSP, so an attached .html never runs on the viewer). The viewer never unpacks or opens a stored file, and no link leads out of the store (`os.Root`). Department chats use the same store. docs/API/security.md lists both routes (docs/agent-dispatch.md#lead-chat, ui/ceo-chat-takes-any-file-the-terminal-takes-zip-pdf-and-text-files-next-to-images).
- **The critic's first real use, fixed: multi-line refs, the usage-limit fallback, held runs count as red rounds, and an older build says it is behind.** A finding citing several lines (`issue.md:29,114`, `:14-16,144-147`) parses as one finding with every line kept. Before, 4 of 9 findings in a real run were marked malformed. When the critic stops on its CLI's usage limit, that subscription is marked resting (as for a worker) and the critic runs once more on the other installed agent, and the `critique:` comment says so. Each held run is one red round of its gate's `critique <from> → <to>` check, counted per run because the head doesn't move in design. Failed runs and runs missing only the Coverage line don't count. The fourth held run posts the owner's `question: red rounds:`, and `issue-cli critique` then refuses until it is resolved. A workflow rule the running build doesn't know now names that build and the fix, `git pull && make install` in the engine's checkout, instead of a bare `unknown validation rule` (docs/Workflow/critique.md, factory/critique-runner-fixes-from-its-first-real-use-pathlineline-refs-the-usage-limit-fallback-held-runs-count-as-red-rounds).
- **One clarify round per design: every question at once, with options, cost and the recommendation first.** The design step writes its questions once under `## Clarify`: each `### Q<n>.` has 2–4 options with `Cost:`, the recommendation first, and an `**Answer:** —` line. With nothing to ask, the section is one line, `Nothing to ask: <why>.` The owner answers them all in one form on the issue's Station (owner's browser only). Each answer becomes the answer line and a D-n decision in the owner's words. Needs you lists one Clarify row per issue. `issue-cli clarify <slug>` shows the round and its format problems; `--assume` marks open answers `assumed — <reason>`, and `--answer Qn=k --quote` records an answer the lead relays from chat. Under YOLO the gate out of design marks the open answers assumed, and the issue's entry in the review after YOLO lists them ("1 gate, 2 assumed answers"); one Agree covers them. The template's idea and in design prompts, the YOLO rule and the briefing say questions go only in the round, and a `question:` comment outside it is for a blocker only. Catalog entry `clarify-round` (docs/workflow.md#the-clarify-round, factory/one-clarify-round-per-design-every-question-at-once-with-options-cost-and-the-recommendation-first-assumed-answers-marked).
- **Status says when the installed build is behind main, and what main has that it lacks.** On the engine project, the Status page (a Behind main card at the top of the side column, `status.behind`) and status.md (`## Installed build behind main`) show `installed <describe> · main is N commits ahead` when the viewer's build (`git describe` from `make install`) is behind the project's main (`ci.branch` when set). They list the `## Unreleased` changelog entries the installed commit's changelog does not have and show `cd <repo> && make install`, then restart the viewer. Nothing runs it. Nothing shows for a `dev` build, a build git can't find, a build at main, or any other project.
- **A fresh critic reads the work before a gate, and its blocks findings hold it.** `issue-cli critique <slug>` starts a read-only agent that didn't write the work. It is the other installed CLI than the worker's when there is one, or `critique: agent:`. It gets paths only: the issue file, the branch's diff, the changed files and the settled rules, never the worker's summary or comments. It is told to assume the work is wrong, and it reports findings as `blocks` / `should` / `note`, each with path:line, plus `Coverage:` and `Not read:`. A malformed finding is reported and kept at its severity. The new `critique` validate rule holds a transition while the newest run from the current status has a blocks finding, failed, or has no Coverage line. A new run clears it, or the owner overrides with `--override --quote`; YOLO doesn't. mem-guard and the CPU load are checked before it starts. It runs headless and read-only (`claude -p` with Read/Grep/Glob only, `codex exec --sandbox read-only`) in the issue's worktree, so `spend` counts it for the issue. Runs go to `<issues>/.critique/runs.jsonl` plus a `critique:` comment. The station has a Critique block, `show` has a `Critique:` line, lint has a `critique` check, and the catalog has entry `critique-gate`. This project turns it on out of design and out of testing. **Install this build before a workflow file uses the rule:** an older issue-cli refuses it as unknown. See docs/Workflow/critique.md.
- **Post-mortem at close: each lesson goes to one place, only once the owner agrees.** With `postmortem:` in a workflow file, the transition into done starts a fresh read-only agent (`start: auto`) or prints its start command (`start: ask`, the default). The agent reads the run from paths only and saves a retro with Steps, Worked, Did not work, Decisions, Known and Proposed actions (`issue-cli postmortem write`). Each action must say what a fresh agent would do differently and name one place: memory, a markdown file, a skill, a workflow experiment, or none with the doc that already says it. The parser refuses anything else. The owner agrees or refuses all of them in one round on `/p/<project>/postmortem/<slug>`. Agreed actions become a memory, a one-file commit on main or an experiment proposal, and the retro links each outcome. A post-mortem waiting on you is a Needs you row. This repo turns it on at done (docs/workflow.md#post-mortem; factory/post-mortem-actions-each-lesson-to-one-place-only-if-a-fresh-agent-would-act-differently-applied-after-the-owner-agrees).
- **A check still red after four fix rounds becomes a question.** `max_red_rounds:` in workflow.yaml (4 when unset) caps how many rounds a gate's command, the proof check or a local CI command may stay red on an issue. Reds at one branch head are one round, and a green ends the streak. At the cap, `issue-cli transition` posts a `question: red rounds:` comment for the owner (recommendation first) and refuses without re-running anything until the owner resolves it; YOLO doesn't pass it. `issue-cli rounds <slug>` lists each red check as "red 3 of 4", and `--cannot-fix <check> --why "…"` asks at once. Red refusals show the count, `show`/`start` print a `Red rounds:` line, the briefing has a `## Red checks` section, and the Station's Tests panel lists red checks. Lint warns on a value below 1, and the catalog has entry `red-round-cap`.
- **Criteria a command can fail, and a `## Must not` the critic checks.** `issue-cli health lint` has a new `criteria` check: an open acceptance criterion that names no test (`TestName`, "tests", a fixture), no command (a span in backticks, `issue-cli`, "exits 1") and no playtest step ("playtest", "step", `(visual)`, a guided check script) is a warning, quoting a vague word it leans on ("works", "is clear", "looks good"), with a `check-edit` fix. Owner boxes (`(owner)`), ticked, N/A and template boxes are skipped. `issue-cli transition` prints the same warnings when an issue leaves design; they never refuse the move. The development template's idea → in design edge appends `## Must not` (plain bullets: what the change must not do, a line may name a test or a command); lint warns when an issue past design still has only its placeholder, and `tracker.MustNot` reads the lines for the fresh critic. Catalog entry `must-not-section` (docs/Workflow/proof.md#criteria-a-command-can-fail, factory/criteria-a-command-can-fail-lint-criteria-no-test-command-or-playtest-could-fail-and-a-must-not-list-the-critic-checks).
- **Release pick works on done issues, keeps the release changelog to what was picked, and patch releases cut and publish.** `issue-cli release pick <slug>` finds a done issue's commits on main by their `Refs:` trailer (its branch is deleted at close), oldest first. A conflict names the commit and files and leaves the branch as it was. A changelog conflict keeps the branch's `## Unreleased` plus the picked lines only, so main's unpicked entries no longer reach the release. After the pick, go build and go vet run in the release worktree and a failure says to pick the missing commit. `issue-cli release cut v0.N.1` tags a patch candidate on the existing `release/v0.N` with no milestone check. Waiting for release shows it with its own checks (CI on main, changelog on the branch, build, nothing picked since), and `release publish v0.N.1` accepts it, keeping a `## v0.N.1` heading already written on the branch.
- **catalog-check on a release branch reads only the commits since its last release tag.** On `release/vX.Y`, `issue-cli workflow catalog-check` (and so `make validate`) now starts from the newest `vX.Y.Z` or `vX.Y.Z-rc.N` tag reachable from HEAD instead of the merge-base with main. Before, a published commit with a bad `Catalog:` line (4da189e on release/v0.58) failed every later patch release, and it can't be reworded. Main and work branches are unchanged (factory/catalog-check-on-a-release-branch-reads-only-the-commits-since-its-last-release-tag).
- **Lint catches a playtest step that points at a data-testid no template carries.** Before, such a step failed only when its session ran in Chrome. `issue-cli health lint` has a new `testids` check. On the engine's repository (one that has `internal/testids`), it reads each `playtest/sessions/*.yaml` step's targets: the `target`/`pattern`/`within`/`a`/`b`/`to` fields, `visible: [..]`-style arguments, `targets:`, and `data-testid="…"` named inside a string such as an `http` check's `contains:`. Each id the list doesn't carry is a warning naming the session file, line and step id, with a `did you mean`. URLs, CLI calls and request bodies are left alone. The repository's own sessions pass it (`TestRepoSessionsCarryListedIDs`) (factory/lint-a-playtest-session-that-points-at-a-data-testid-no-template-carries).
- **A red newcomer walkthrough blocks shipping, and onboarding workers are told where the guarded steps go.** docs/departments.md's Settled rules name `TestNewcomerWalkthrough` (`newcomer_test.go`, run by `make validate`) as a blocker: a branch that turns it red doesn't ship. The worker briefing's Worktree section says the worker guard refuses `dispatch`, `department add` and `lead spawn` from a worker's shell even with `--config` on a sandbox, and to walk those steps in a Go test like that one (factory/the-newcomer-walkthrough-test-guards-main-factorys-settled-rules-call-it-a-blocker-and-onboarding-briefs-say-which-steps-the-worker-guard-keeps-in-a-go-test).
- **The lead's queue: an ordered plan the lead's tick starts while memory and CPU allow.** `issue-cli queue add|list|move|block|unblock|link|quote|drop|done|limits|next` keeps the project's plan in `<issues>/.queue/queue.json`: issue slugs or one-line ideas, `blocked on` items or slugs, and the owner's words on each. `queue next --start` starts the first startable item with auto-start's checks (mem-guard, the load under the core count, the worker cap, one start per gap machine-wide) and holds while local CI on main is red; an item starts only on its own quote or auto-start's stored words. It is marked `[>]`, and done with its merge SHA when the issue reaches its last status. Every list ends with "Out of work: think up more". The Report gets a Queue card (next 5, blocked, done today), `status.md` a `Queue:` line, `lead whoami` the tick command (docs/agent-dispatch.md#the-leads-queue).

- **Departments health says "not measured yet" instead of 0%.** A job that has never run counted as late, so a new department with only a job waiting for its first run read 0% on track and dragged the derived score (`departments 0`). A job with no run yet now counts as due, not late; a department with nothing to measure is left out of the on-track share, and with none measured the score leaves the `departments` component out. The Health table, the rooms' foot (`♥ not measured yet`), the Report and `/status.md` say what starts measuring it; the line counts `1 of 2 measured` (new row `departments.measured`; docs/workbench-files.md#the-departments-family, factory/departments-health-reads-0-when-a-department-has-no-goals-or-outputs-yet-say-not-measured-instead).
- **Shared memory answers "who knows what" with entities, still files only.** Write `#topic` and `@agent` (or `@person`) in a memory's text, or tag it (`--tag playtest,@lead-x`), and a `who` memory links the two: `issue-cli memory add "@lead-dark-factory knows the #playtest runner" --kind who`. `issue-cli memory search @lead-dark-factory` or `memory search '#playtest' chrome` keeps only the memories that carry them (quote a `#`: unquoted, the shell reads a comment). `issue-cli memory entities [--only topic|agent]` lists every entity you can see with its count, kinds and, for a topic, who knows it. `memory add` prints the entities it found, and a `who` memory without both gets a hint. The Memory page (`/p/<project>/memory`) has an Entities strip that filters by one (`?entity=`) and a Group: by entity view that puts each memory under its #topics and @names; `memory.json` adds `entities`. The index is folded from the memories on each read; nothing new is stored, and there is no graph database, server or model (factory/shared-memory-phase-4-entities-and-who-knows-what).
- **Review after YOLO: one entry per issue, on one page.** The after-the-fact queue counts issues, not gates: an issue with 3 gates passed and 2 owner boxes open is one entry ("3 gates, 2 owner checks"), in the header, the map, the sidebar, Needs you (`needs-you.json` `after_count`, `after_gates`, `after_checks`, `after_oldest`, `after_url`), `yolo.json` and `issue-cli yolo status|queue` (`--json` now lists entries). `/p/<project>/yolo/review` lists the entries oldest first, 15 issues a page, each with its evidence inline (up to two playtest shots, the tests line, the change). One Agree covers all of an issue's gates; each owner box is ticked or agreed without on its own; Back to sends the issue back once and settles its entry (`POST …/yolo/review`, owner-only like every YOLO write: agents and curl get 403). Needs you's After the fact shows the counts and the oldest age with Review all and at most 8 issues; the header's count, the company's list and `issue-cli yolo status|queue` link to the page (docs/Workflow/yolo.md#reviewing-the-queue-together).
- **Playtest: `S=` keeps another issue's shots where its Evidence tab reads them; `make shot FULL=1` reaches the Workshop floor.** `make ux-test S=<another issue's session>` from a worktree dropped the passing shots and wrote the run into the worktree, where that issue's Evidence tab never looks and prune clears it. A session asked for by name now keeps its shots and, when it is another issue's, its run goes to the project root's `.playtest/runs/` (the runner says so; `make playtest S=` reuses that run). `make shot FULL=1` on a page that only scrolls inside (the company floor and its roads) unrolls the biggest inner scroller before the full shot; `SCROLL=<selector>` names it. The worker briefing's shell facts and CLAUDE.md say rtk also hides `go test -v`'s `t.Log` lines: `rtk proxy go test -v …` (docs/patterns/playtest/web-app.md, ui/playtest-friction-ux-test-keeps-other-issues-shots-make-shot-full1-reaches-a-scrolling-floor).
- **Shared memory forms and tidies itself in the background, still without a model.** `issue-cli memory candidates <slug>` lists what an issue's trail could teach: its `progress:`, `question:`, `answer:`, `risk:` and `decision:` comments, its decisions and its retro, each with the closest memories (no transcript is read; nothing is written). `issue-cli memory consolidate` lists near-duplicate clusters, stale, expired and promotable memories; a merge is `memory add … --supersedes id,id`, which now takes several ids. The `memory-consolidate` job's brief is bundled: a department's `jobs:` entry names `brief: builtin:memory-consolidate` (lint and the viewer read it too); its standing start is off until the owner turns it on, and it proposes CLAUDE.md promotions as an issue, never an edit. Ranking decays: an old, unused memory ranks below a recent or used one (90-day half-life, uses and pins keep it strong); decay never deletes (docs/memory.md#consolidation-candidates-and-decay).
- Phone width: Needs you, the Station, the CEO chat, the shelf reader, the HQ Report and the page header keep every button and key at least 44px tall at 640px and below, and Needs you's row checkboxes toggle from a 44px square, so the owner can steer from a phone without zooming.
- **A branch's workflows are marked in the benchmark compare and kept per workflow in experiments.** `bench compare <a> <b> --branch <ref>` maps the files the ref changed since main to the project's workflows (a workflow's own file; a department's prompt or `departments/<key>/`; a bundled template to every workflow stamped with it; engine code to all) and marks each By workflow row changed or control, with a warning when a control turns net negative; `--json` adds `mark`, `changed_by`, `branch` and `not_mapped`. `experiment record` and `run` store the per-workflow verdicts, marked by the candidate's commit, in the experiment block (`workflows:`), the `experiment:` comment and `## Experiment result`; `experiment show` prints them. The overall verdict is unchanged and the owner still adopts (docs/benchmark.md#which-workflows-a-branch-changed, factory/evals-mark-the-workflows-a-branch-changes-and-keep-per-workflow-verdicts-in-experiments).
- Auto-start counts CPU load next to memory: a start waits while the 1-minute load average is at or above the core count (`issue-cli autostart on --max-load N` sets another limit), with the reason (`hold: load 16.0 on 14 cores (max 14)`). The Report's Auto-start card, `status.md`, `autostart status` and the dispatch: comment show the load and the cores. Reads `sysctl vm.loadavg` on macOS and `/proc/loadavg` on Linux; with no reading, load doesn't hold.
- **Shared memory can search by meaning with a local embedder.** `memory: embedder:` in workflow.yaml names an Ollama-style `/api/embed` URL on this machine or a command speaking the same JSON; `memory search`, start's `== Memories ==` and the reflection hint at done then rank by BM25 plus cosine, with vectors cached in `<issues>/.memory/vectors/`. Off by default. Before each embed request issue-cli asks `mem-guard used` and ranks by words alone at 70% or more, without mem-guard, or when the embedder fails; search says which (`Ranked by words + meaning (<model>).`). A URL off the machine is refused: a hosted embedder is the owner's call. `scripts/memory-embed-macos.swift` embeds with the model macOS ships (30–48 MB, measured in docs/memory.md). Lint check `memory`, catalog entry `memory-embedder` (docs/memory.md#semantic-search-local-embedder, factory/shared-memory-phase-2-local-embeddings-behind-the-embedder-seam).
- **Evals: `score: diff`, the content template's evals, and four more lint checks.** A `score: diff` eval grades the worker's diff against the task's base (new files too) with its rubric and assertions, the merged diff as `{{reference}}`; no diff is a fail. The development template gains a `docs` diff eval and the content template a `content` output eval (catalog `workflow-evals` v0.59.0, now proposed for content files too). `health lint --check evals` also warns about an output or diff eval nothing grades, an `assert:` type the scorer won't pass through (it needs a model, runs code, or promptfoo has no such type), two evals claiming one type, and types no eval covers (docs/benchmark.md#evals)
- `issue-cli bench run` gives each task's worker its own HOME in the sandbox (`<sandbox>/home`): only Claude Code's login is linked in (the macOS keychain folder, the credentials file, codex's auth.json) and a minimal `.claude.json` is written, so the owner's settings, hooks, plugins and history no longer reach a benchmark run; the usage is read from the transcripts there. `worker_home: owner` in the benchmark file keeps the old behaviour. A worker that finds no login ends as `error` naming docs/benchmark.md#the-workers-home, and `bench report` shows the run's worker home.

- `issue-cli bench candidates --department <key>` lists a department's done issues that could be output tasks: those with an output the owner approved and an eval for their type, each with the output and the eval that would score it (`--all` says why the rest are left out). Plain `bench candidates` points to it when a department's workflow declares evals, and `process workflow` now prints an `Evals:` line saying how the benchmark scores runs on that workflow (docs/benchmark.md#evals)
- **Routing between Claude and Codex: a `routing:` block picks the agent CLI per start and falls back while one subscription rests.** Rules on type, label, status, department or `lead: true` (first match wins, then `default:`) pick claude or codex for `dispatch`, ▶ (**Dispatch · codex**), auto-start, `lead spawn` and `job run`. `--agent` still wins. When a live pane shows the CLI's own limit message (`You've hit your 5-hour limit · resets 5pm (…)`, `You’ve hit your usage limit … try again at 6:30 PM.`), that subscription rests until the reset and new starts take the next agent in `fallback:`; auto-start and scheduled jobs hold when none is free. The stalled worker is left running and shown on the Crew page, which also names each card's agent and each subscription's state. `status.md` gets an `Agents:` line, and `issue-cli agents [status|rest|clear]` shows and sets rests. Spend splits by subscription (Codex from its own session logs), lint check `routing`, catalog entry `agent-routing`, and `bench run --worker` records the agent so runs can be compared. No API keys, accounts or logins; CCR isn't bundled (docs/agent-dispatch.md#routing-between-claude-and-codex, factory/route-work-between-claude-and-codex-subscriptions-ccr-pick-the-agent-per-task-fall-back-when-one-hits-its-limit).
- Bundled templates no longer tell agents to run commands with flags that don't exist: the marketing foundation's publish step says `issue-cli output publish <id>` (it said `--path`, which failed with "Valid flags: --url"), and the planning workflow and GTM weekly-plan job file new work with `create` then `append --body` (create has no `--body`). A test checks every `issue-cli` command in the bundled templates against the flags it parses. Projects that installed these files keep their copies: fix `workflows/marketing.yaml` and `workflows/gtm.yaml` by hand or with `issue-cli workflow upgrade`.
- **The company page renders in about half the time.** At 15 departments its warm render went from ~70 ms to ~49 ms (mean of 100), and it now passes its 150 ms test under a CPU and memory hog instead of going red under CI load. The page read every open issue's comments and workflow twice per render; it now reads them once and shares the result between the owner's queue and the rooms. Each room also asked tmux on its own whether its department lead was live; it now reads the session list the Crew page already uses. Roads and the page's HTML are unchanged.
- `issue-cli prove` on an issue with no `## Acceptance Criteria` heading now suggests `issue-cli append <slug> --section "Acceptance Criteria" --body "- [ ] ..."`, which creates the section. It used to suggest `replace`, which refuses a missing section.
- Security: pages now restrict scripts. Only the viewer's own scripts run, each inline block with a nonce per request, so injected markup can't run script. Buttons and fields no longer use inline `on…=` handlers (docs/API/security.md)
- Security: project pages can no longer send data to other hosts (their CSP keeps fetches, images and forms on the viewer), and the frame posts the status only while it still shows the page it loaded. A page at the project root serves only itself, and no page route serves dot-folders such as `.ci/` or `.worktrees/`
- Security: tmux targets are exact, so a dead `agent-foo` is never confused with a live `agent-foo-bar`. The `{{window}}` placeholder and the "Inside tmux" hint now read `'=agent-<slug>:'`
- Security: shelf files resolve symlinks, and error answers show the home folder as `~`

- **`prove --run` skips a test an older trailer names after it was renamed.** A `Proves:` trailer pins its commit, so when a later commit renamed or removed the test (an owner decision changed the design), --run said "3 of 12 named tests did not pass" and posted nothing, though every current test passed. Now a name that isn't found, with a newer trailer or `prove --test` link on each of its criteria naming a test that is, prints as `– <name> — superseded by <newer> (trailer on <sha>)` and is skipped; the tests: comment ends with `; N superseded: <names>`, and `prove` lists the old link as superseded. A missing name in the newest trailer still blocks. See docs/Workflow/proof.md.
- Security: owner decisions on loopback (approvals, Needs you, YOLO, auto-start, send-back, YOLO review, output review and comments, decision confirm, workflow station edits, owner checks, department add, health recommendations, shared memory edits and pins) now need the owner's sign-in. A browser signs in at `/owner` (the YOU circle) by typing a one-time code the viewer shows in a macOS dialog or on its own terminal (`-owner-code dialog|terminal|auto`); it gets an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict session cookie kept only in the viewer's memory, so a viewer restart signs out. Requests without it get 403, whatever browser headers they carry. issue-cli and agents keep every other route; an agent asks for an owner decision on its issue trail. The issue-approve mod's approve press now gets 403: approve in the browser. The playtest runner and `make security-check` sign their own sandbox boards in. See docs/API/security.md, "Owner sign-in".
- **Shared memory between agents: `issue-cli memory add|search|list|show|edit|forget|pin|unpin`.** It follows the patterns of memory libraries (mem0, Letta, Graphiti, LangMem) and leaves out their engines. An agent stores a fact with its kind, scope and source. `add` runs mem0's update step with the agent deciding: the same fact writes nothing, and a near-duplicate is refused with the similar memories, so the agent picks `--updates <id>`, `--supersedes <id>` (the old one stays in history, invalid) or `--new`. Scopes are project, department (the default for a department's agents), agent and machine; a private project can't write machine. Every memory keeps its history in an append-only log (`<issues>/.memory/events.jsonl`). Leads and the owner pin memories into every briefing; `start` prints the pins and the top 5 for the issue; done and retrospective ask what the next agent should know. Text that looks like a secret is refused and never printed. The owner filters, edits, pins and deletes (scrubbing the text) at `/p/<project>/memory`, linked from the HQ room. Plain files and BM25: no model, nothing leaves the machine; 41 MB RSS for start with 5,000 memories (docs/memory.md, factory/shared-memory-between-agents-mem0-or-similar-open-source).

## v0.58.1 — 2026-10-31

- **A fresh fork reaches a first worker without breaking.** Walking v0.58.0 as a newcomer (an empty home, no config, no rtk, mem-guard or i3) broke at the first dispatch: `project init` writes no `terminal:`, and unset meant i3 + alacritty everywhere, so a Mac got `✗ Open alacritty` and exit 1. An unset `terminal:` is now headless where `i3-msg` isn't on PATH, and the step says how to set one. `.agent-logs/` ignores itself in git like `.health/` and `.ci/`. `make demo PORT=8081` runs beside your own viewer, and `project init`'s git hint no longer promises worktrees the template doesn't turn on. docs/getting-started.md opens with one page from zero to a running factory (install, project, board, first issue, first worker, the CEO, a department, the optional tools and what stops without each, what to do when a step fails), and the README's Quickstart is those steps in short. `make validate` without Homebrew on PATH no longer fails: the guided-check library tests ran PATH's bash 3.2 (seven failed), and their pseudo-terminal helper hung until the ten-minute test timeout under macOS's system Python 3.9. They now use the bash 4 that `check-script` finds, or skip, and a pty loop that ends with the script. `TestNewcomerWalkthrough` re-walks it on every `make validate`: it builds both binaries and drives them in a clean env against a stand-in agent on a private tmux server.
- **A leak scan keeps private data out of the public tree.** A test in `make validate` and `issue-cli release publish` (the dry run included) refuse a tracked file with a user home path (`/Users/<name>/`, `/home/<name>/`, `C:\Users\<name>\`, `-Users-<name>-`), a Claude Code scratchpad path, or a name from the owner's private-names list kept outside the repo (`~/.config/ai-workflow-engine/private-names.txt`). Test inputs use placeholder users and projects; a fixture with a real home path and tests naming private projects were renamed (docs/workflow.md, The leak scan; factory/release-snapshot-leaks-a-fixture-with-a-user-path-and-brand-fixtures-naming-private-projects).

## v0.58.0 — 2026-10-31

- Security: the viewer now fails closed for any public request. A non-loopback Host, or any request that came through a proxy or tunnel, gets 403 unless `identity:` in projects.yaml is configured (Cloudflare Access team domain, AUD tag, owner emails) and the request's Access token verifies for an owner. **A LAN or VPN name under `allowed_hosts:` now needs `identity:` too.** See docs/API/security.md, which also has the go-public checklist and the route inventory
- Security: fixed several high and critical issues found in the pre-exposure review:
  - command injection through the agent name on dispatch routes;
  - writes outside the issues folder through issue create, which also no longer overwrites an existing issue;
  - frontmatter injection on create;
  - raw HTML and `javascript:` links in issue bodies;
  - an image upload that could be served as HTML;
  - shell quoting of paths and template values typed into shells;
  - git refs read from stats sidecars;
  - the `/page-raw/` CORS grant.

  Repros stay in the review issue
- Security: every answer carries nosniff, X-Frame-Options, Referrer-Policy and a base CSP (no framing by other sites). Request bodies are capped at 4 MiB, or 12 MiB for image uploads
- Security: `make security-check` attacks a throwaway loopback viewer as if it were public, then runs govulncheck. goldmark is upgraded to v1.7.17 (GO-2026-5320)

- **Outputs as documents: read a markdown output and comment on it.** A markdown output on the shelf opens in a reader (`/p/<project>/shelf/read/<id>`): the document set in the Workshop look with its contents, a Day / Night sheet, Raw file, and Approve / Reject. Select a passage, use 💬 on a heading, or write on the whole document: comments sit in the margin as threads with replies and Resolve, kept beside the file in `<issues>/.outputs/comments.jsonl`, and each lands on the output's issue as a `revise:` comment quoting the passage. `issue-cli output revise <id> --file` adds a version (back in review; the reader shows Changes since the last one and keeps each thread on its passage), `output comments <id>` lists the threads for the agent, `output reply` answers one. The shelf row shows `v2` and `💬 N open`. Only the owner's browser writes comments.
- **Anonymous usage sharing, on by default, and a collector to receive it.** Each install sends one names-only summary per day: command and flag names with counts, error classes, durations, bundled template names, workflow-feature ids, the number of projects, issues and departments, the version and OS. Never project names, slugs, paths, issue text or flag values. Nothing is sent while no collector URL is set, and the engine ships none yet. The first call says so once on stderr. Turn it off with `issue-cli telemetry share off` or `ISSUE_CLI_TELEMETRY=off`. `issue-cli telemetry sent` lists what was sent. `cmd/telemetry-collector` is a small receiver you can host yourself; it refuses anything that could carry free text. The viewer's `/telemetry` page draws its data (installs, versions, commands, features, workflows, error rates) once `telemetry_data:` in projects.yaml points at the collector's folder. What is sent, field by field: docs/CLI/telemetry.md, "Sharing usage" (factory/usage-telemetry-from-other-peoples-installs-opt-in-anonymous-and-a-view-for-the-owner).
- **HQ report: the company in a minute.** HQ's room on the company page links to a Report (`/p/<project>/hq/report`): one folded section per box (HQ, Product, each group and department in the company tree's order), whose headline says whether it is on track and what matters this week (goals moved, outputs to review, jobs due or missed, what is blocked and on whom, handoffs, spend against the budget). Opened, each section shows the detail behind it, a group's latest week report and the department lead's last `progress:` line. A department with nothing says so. It is derived from goals, the shelf, jobs, handoffs, open issues, spend and the newest health snapshot; no agent writes it. `/hq/report.md` and `issue-cli status --hq` give the same as text; the CEO's brief and `status.md` point at it (factory/hq-report-an-executive-summary-of-each-department-in-the-ceos-hq).
- **A group of departments has its own colour.** GTM's room on the company page, its building on the map, its crew column, shelf tab and connection lines are drawn in the group teal (`--group` in `static/workshop.css`) instead of the amber the GTM template wrote, which looked like HQ's yellow. Every group takes it, nested ones too, whatever `color:` projects.yaml sets; departments keep their own colour.
- **Evals per workflow: a workflow change gets a verdict for each workflow, with time and cost.** A workflow file declares `evals:`, saying per type how the factory benchmark scores a run: `tests` (hidden Go tests and checks, as before) or `output` (the run's output on the shelf, graded by promptfoo 0.124.1 through npx). Each output eval has `rubric:` lines graded by the benchmark's new `grader:` command, `assert:` items that need no model, and a `pass:` bar. The development and marketing templates come with evals (catalog entry `workflow-evals`), and lint check `evals` warns on a bad id, score, pass or type. A department's golden task comes only from an output the owner approved: `bench task add` copies the approved file as its reference and runs it on the department's own workflow file (`bench run --workflow <key>=<file>` replaces that file). Every task records wall time with scoring time, and tokens and cost (benchmark prices, then prices.yaml). `bench run` prints an estimate from the last run first. `bench compare` prints **By workflow (A → B)**: pass rate, score, wall, tokens and cost side by side, and net positive, net negative, no change or not comparable (+10% cost or +25% wall at the same quality is net negative). The worker and scorer get the sandbox's state and config dirs, and the scorer also gets its own HOME; the benchmark folder is hidden from both. Evals run only inside an owner-started `bench run`; nothing adopts by itself (factory/benchmarks-and-evals-for-every-agentic-workflow-is-a-workflow-change-a-net-positive-or-negative).
- **The company floor draws roads with work moving on them instead of typed lines.** One road per pair of rooms that pass work, routed like streets through the gaps between the rooms; roads that share a street merge. The open traffic moves along each road as small parcels in the sender's colour (● request, ▲ handoff, ■ output, ◆ dependency); with reduced motion they stand still and each road shows its counts. A red `!` sign marks traffic off the declared routes, and a declared road with no traffic is a thin dotted lane. Hovering or focusing a road names it under the floor, and clicking it opens the connections page. The data, the Connections list, `/connections`, `connections.json` and `issue-cli connections` are unchanged. Playtest sessions can set `session_checks: reduced_motion: true`.
- **`sandbox_dir` in guided check scripts gives a resolved path and isolates the state.** It gives a resolved path (no `//` from a TMPDIR ending in a slash, `/private/var` on macOS, as issue-cli and the viewer print it), and the first one points `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` into the sandbox, so a viewer or issue-cli a script starts never touches the shared viewer.json or projects.yaml. `sandbox_project` isolates `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` too; ticking the real issue keeps your own (cli/lib-sh-sandbox-paths-normalise-double-slashes-and-isolate-state-in-sandbox-dir-too).
- **Sibling workers agree a format through `issue-cli ask` / `answer`.** An `ask:` comment on the sibling's issue shows at the bottom of that worker's next issue-cli call (`== Sibling asks ==`, with the `issue-cli answer` line), and the `answer:` comes back the same way on the asker's issue, once. `show`'s Depends on line marks `· ask open` / `· agreed` and suggests an ask for an open dependency. `issue-cli status` lists open asks under `## Open asks between siblings`. Comments only, no new transport; an ask or answer never approves a gate.
- **`check` with acceptance criteria among other ids ticks the others.** Under the proof gate, `check <slug> D1 D2 AC3` refused the whole call, D1 and D2 included, in a line that was easy to miss. Now D1 and D2 are ticked and AC3 gets one `✗ Not ticked: AC3 is ticked by its proof, not by hand (issue-cli prove <slug> --list)` line; the exit is non-zero only when nothing was ticked. Unknown ids and owner boxes still refuse the whole call (cli/check-with-mixed-ids-ticks-nothing-quietly-and-a-tests-comment-cant-be-corrected).
- **`issue-cli comment edit <slug> <id|last> --text "..."` corrects your own latest comment.** Only a lead or worker session that signed it, and only its newest comment on the issue; the owner's comments, other agents' comments and approval:, dispatch:, yolo-review: and yolo-owner-check: comments are refused. The old text stays in the comment's `edits` list with the time it was replaced; `show` marks the comment `(edited)`.

- **Local CI keeps a red run's log past the next green.** A re-run of the same head wrote `.ci/runs/<sha>.log` again, so a flaky failure's log was gone by the time anyone looked. A red run's log is now copied to `.ci/red/` (the last 10, at most 30 days, and the newest first red after a green past the count); `ci status` prints `Last red log: <path>`, the Status card shows a Last red log line and the red boxes in Last runs link to their kept log (`/p/<project>/ci/log/<name>`). The storage health family counts `.ci/` as `storage.ci_mb`.
- **catalog-check stops asking for `Catalog: n/a` on test-only and fixture-only changes.** A `dir/**` source matched every file under the folder, so a branch that only edited `_test.go` files, `testdata/` or the playtest runner's scenario fixtures needed a trailer per entry (four on one branch, and an extra empty commit after a merge on another). `ExemptSources` in internal/workflowupgrade lists the paths catalog-check never counts: `**/*_test.go`, `**/testdata/**`, `playtest/scenarios/**` and `cmd/playtest/scenario.go` (scenario.yaml keys, read only by this repository's ux-test board). Session keys (cmd/playtest/session.go, vocab.go) and everything else still need a bump or a trailer, and the guard that every source matches a tracked file now also fails on a source that matches only exempt files (cli/catalog-check-asks-for-catalog-na-on-test-only-and-fixture-only-changes).
- A playtest session written in design keeps its step ids: when in progress appends `## Test Plan` and the issue's session already uses `TP1`, `TP2`, …, the section starts with one box per such step and its own boxes number after them. An existing Test Plan is never renumbered.
- `issue-cli prove --list` marks a playtest link stale when its step id is gone from the session file (renumbered or removed after the run); a stale link proves nothing.
- docs/patterns/playtest: how a step id maps to a box id (`## Reports` gives `R1`, not `RP1`).
- **A new catalog entry goes in a file of its own, and one command bumps an entry with its template stamps.** Parallel branches each appending an entry to `workflow-features.yaml` conflicted on adjacent lines. The catalog now also reads `cmd/issue-cli/templates/workflow-features/<id>.yaml`, one entry per file, placed after the last entry of its category; catalog-check, `workflow scan`, `upgrade` and the Updates tab see both forms, and the entries already in the list stay there. `issue-cli workflow catalog-bump <id>[,<id>] [--version vX.Y.Z] [--dry-run]` sets an entry's `version:` (default the next release) and raises every bundled template's stamp and `features:` record line with it, in one step; catalog-check's fix line and the template tests name it. A sandbox-repo test merges two sibling branches that each add an entry, a bool flag and a CHANGELOG line without a conflict, and shows the old shared-list way still conflicts (workflow/parallel-ships-conflict-on-the-same-shared-lists-claude-md-file-list-catalog-clilog-boolflags).
- **A new bool flag needs no second edit for the clilog.** The clilog's redaction kept free text out of records by knowing which flags take no value, from `boolFlags`, a list in internal/clilog kept by hand: every new `fs.Bool` needed a line there, two branches adding one conflicted on it, and a miss showed only in `make validate`. issue-cli now asks the FlagSets the call parsed (the global ones and the command's; for a call that failed before parsing, such as flags before the slug, the command's FlagSet introspected), so the list is gone. `tools/clilog-scrub` keeps a frozen list of the flags older logs can hold (workflow/parallel-ships-conflict-on-the-same-shared-lists-claude-md-file-list-catalog-clilog-boolflags).
- **`bench candidates --replay` replays each candidate before the owner picks it.** The filters looked sound but never ran anything: the benchmark-pack retro found tasks whose hidden tests needed main's merged-in code, tasks that didn't replay, and tests that already passed on base. `--replay [<slug>...]` replays each proposed candidate in a throwaway git worktree (temp dir, repo hooks off, removed and pruned after): base with the merged commit's hidden files must fail the hidden tests, base plus the branch's own commits (merges from main left out) must pass them. Each row gets `replay: solvable | needs later main | doesn't replay | tests pass on base | not checked — <why>`; `--json` adds `replay` and `replay_why`. One candidate at a time, `mem-guard check` before every `go test` (BLOCKED or 70% and up skips it as not checked). No model call, no benchmark file written; agents may run it.
- **The `(owner)` marker counts anywhere in a box.** Only a trailing `(owner)` made a box the owner's, so criteria written as `(owner) The owner picks …` or `Owner visual check … (after ship)` counted as the agent's: `criteria_proven` blocked testing → human-testing under YOLO and workers reworded them (five retros). A box is now the owner's when `(owner)` stands anywhere outside quotes or backticks, or when it starts with `Owner ` and names a check, read-through, review or decision. One rule (`tracker.IsOwnerMarked` behind `proof.OwnerBoxWhy`) for proof, the tick and check-edit refusals, the YOLO queue, the owner-checks page, lint's `na-boxes` and the benchmark's owner-steps risk; it only makes more boxes the owner's. New lint check `owner-marker` warns on an open criterion that reads like the owner's check without the marker, and the design prompt shows the form `- [ ] … (owner)` (cli/the-owner-marker-counts-anywhere-in-a-box-not-only-at-the-end).
- **A proposal becomes an experiment with one command, and the benchmark's token budget fits a task.** `issue-cli experiment init <slug>` refused an issue without the `experiment` label and told you to run `issue-cli update --labels`, a flag that doesn't exist. Now `init` adds the label itself (other labels and the status stay, so a proposal filed at idea works), and the other experiment commands name `init` when they refuse. A test checks that every issue-cli command and flag the experiment hints name exists. The benchmark's `tokens` count cache reads, about 15M a task, but the example budget was 6M: a run with no `tokens` set is now capped at 25M a task, `bench budget --tokens` under that gets a note, and docs/benchmark.md explains cache reads with a 10-task example of 250M. (factory/from-an-experiment-proposal-to-an-experiment-a-working-cli-path-and-a-realistic-budget)
- **A `report` type: research and report-only issues skip the build path.** A report-only issue walked the full feature path, with 13 boxes ticked N/A and an empty commit so `commits_reference_issue` passed. The `report` type in the `development` template walks idea → in progress (research and write) → human-testing (owner review) → done, with no new board column. The report goes on the outputs shelf (`issue-cli output add --issue <slug> --kind report --file <report.md>`), where the issue page lists it. A new `has_output: <kind>` rule gates the owner review, the Owner Review box is the owner's, and `done` needs only their approval (the YOLO queue under YOLO): no Shipping, no commit. Lint check `reports` warns on an issue past the gate whose report is gone. Catalog entry `report-type`; the lead brief says which type to file.
- **Read-only calls on another project leave it untouched.** Any `issue-cli --project <other>` call, even `workflow upgrade` (documented as read-only), appended a clilog line and a telemetry event to that repo's `.agent-logs/`, and to the issue assignee's clilog there. A read-only call by someone not working in the project (working directory outside its root and issues folder) now writes nothing there: its record goes to the caller's own project, or the machine log under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/issue-cli/`. Which calls are read-only is a table next to the command registry (`commandAccess` in cmd/issue-cli/readonly.go); a test makes every new command pick reads, writes or per subcommand, and runs every read call on a sandbox git repo and checks its status. A writing call on another project logs there as before; `help upgrade` says what it writes (cli/read-only-issue-cli-calls-on-another-project-still-write-its-agent-logs).
- **Agents can't tick owner boxes any more.** `issue-cli check` let an agent tick a Human Testing box, a box marked "(owner)" or a playtest judgement step (only `--na`, `uncheck` and `check-remove` refused), and `append`, `replace` and `update --body` could write `[x]` into one. Every path that ticks a box now asks one rule (`internal/proof/ownerbox.go`: `OwnerBoxWhy`, `OwnerTicks`) and refuses an agent (the worker guard's identity: a dispatched worker or lead, or a Claude Code session), with nothing written and the Owner checks page named: check by id, text, index or section, append, replace, update. `prove`'s ticking asks the same rule of the box as the file has it. `playtest report -apply` ticks machine steps and the owner's judgement steps in separate calls, so an agent's run still ticks the machine ones. The viewer's box toggle and body save answer 403 to a request that would tick an owner box unless it comes from the owner's browser. The owner, in their own shell or browser, ticks as before (cli/agents-can-tick-owner-boxes-through-issue-cli-check-and-prove-run).
- **A profile run no longer changes a tracked file.** `issue-viewer.test`, the 26.7 MB test binary `go test -cpuprofile` writes in the repo root, was committed in v0.57.0; it is untracked now, and `*.test` and `*.prof` are ignored, so `git status` stays clean after a profile run. It stays in the history (and in the v0.57.0 snapshot): purging it is the owner's call (cli/issue-viewer-test-a-26-mb-test-binary-is-tracked-in-git).
- **Three playtest sessions green again after their pages moved.** `updates` TP8, `workboard-markers` TP10 and `workflow-time` TP4 still looked for the classic pages' test ids after /updates, the issue list and /stats moved to the Workshop Board; each now checks the same things on the new page (the sessions were stale, the pages right). The Workshop Board guide's move checklist now says to run every session once (`make ux-test ISSUE=`) after a page move (ui/three-playtest-sessions-red-on-main-after-the-page-moves-updates-tp8-workboard-markers-tp10-workflow-time-tp4).
- **A transition after backlog names the missing assignee, not the approval.** Backlog clears the assignee, and `transition --to "in progress"` then said "not human-approved" under YOLO even though YOLO would pass the gate: the approval was held back because the assignee was missing, and the refusal blamed it. It now names what is still open (`no assignee — re-claim the issue with: issue-cli start <slug>`) and says YOLO passes the approval once that is done. With the approval stamped, the assignee refusal now points at `start` instead of `claim`. A transition that really lacks the approval still says "not human-approved" (cli/not-human-approved-when-the-real-gap-is-the-missing-assignee-after-backlog).
- **Groups nest: a group inside a group.** A group may name a parent group (GTM → Outbound → Sales), at most 3 groups above any department. Goals roll up every level into HQ, a group's spend and waiting count cover every department under it, a department lead reports to the nearest live lead up the chain, and a group lead's reach is its whole subtree. The company page draws a nested group's room inside its group's, the map its building inside, and Crew and /leads nest the same way; `lead list` indents each level. `department add <template> --parent <group>` takes a nested group while the result fits. Lint (`departments`) names a `parent:` loop and a chain deeper than 3, and treats that department as in no group; `via: <group>` may name any group above the asker. One-level projects look as before. The `nested-groups` playtest scenario is the board for it (factory/nested-groups-a-group-inside-a-group-with-goals-rolling-up-and-the-map-drawing-it).
- `issue-cli project init <dir>` makes a new project in one command: the folder with `issues/`, `docs/` and a stamped `workflow.yaml` from a bundled template (`--template`, default `development`), and its projects.yaml entry, printed, or added to the `--config <file>` you name (a new file, or spliced in as text keeping every other line). It never overwrites a file and never touches a config it wasn't given; a second run reports what is already there, `--dry-run` lists every change, `--json` gives the plan. Then it prints how to open the board (the company page's New department tile), start the CEO and create the first issue. Getting Started leads with it.
- **`experiment candidates` groups the workflow's own words.** Retros were grouped only by code-like terms, so themes told as `tests:` comments, `Proves:` or `Catalog:` trailers, or the CHANGELOG never made a pattern, though `tests:` alone comes back in 12 issues. A prefix inside a sentence (not a label like "Friction:" at its start) and an all-caps name that isn't a file name now group too, here and on the Retro sorting room's pattern line (factory/experiment-backlog-retro-themes-become-experiment-proposals-ready-for-a-baseline).
- **`bench candidates` leaves out tasks no Go test would judge, and names each candidate's risks.** A done issue whose test files hold no Go test function was proposed though the scorer runs only Go tests, so it would pass on its check alone; it is now left out with why. A proposed row gets a `risk:` line (tmux, browser, timing, broad when over 20 hidden tests, owner steps for an "(owner)" box, main merged in when base..merged holds files the branch never changed), and `--json` adds `system`, `tests` and `risks` (factory/benchmark-candidate-pack-a-ranked-list-of-30-golden-tasks-from-dark-factorys-done-issues-for-the-owner).
- **Workshop pages do what only their `?classic=1` pages did.** A sweep of every page with a classic version found 80 things only the classic page showed or did; the gap table and what happened to each row are in the issue. The station edits the title and fields in place (Status through the transition preview, Type asking for a status on its new path), deletes the issue, dispatches to Claude or Codex with the steps and gate banner, runs custom actions, shows the brief, what you're approving since the last gate, mockups, relations, an epic's children, and the whole body by section with clickable boxes and an Edit box (both hash-guarded). The crate store has its own New issue dialog (`?new=1&status=`) and By status / By version facets. The Report serves `?view=builtin` with a Project page / Report switch, lists what is in flight, the CEO's full plan and Recently released; Stats, the stats overview and Health carry their remaining rows (lines-of-code trend on Health, decided recommendations, every lint finding with its fix). Retros has a Bugs view (`?view=bugs`) with Open / Fixed / Won't fix; Decisions, Updates, Systems, Docs and Crew get their small rows. Every Workshop page now reloads within seconds when the project's issues change, held while a form or dialog is open. The classic pages stay; the rows left on them go to child issues or are dropped on purpose, each with a decision (ui/classic-page-gaps-move-what-still-lives-only-on-classic1-pages-into-the-workshop-board).
- **The company page and the map at 15 departments.** With a dozen departments in groups, every connection line ran full strength across every room, the map's lot ran off a phone screen, and the company page took about 200 ms to render. A floor with more than 3 departments and 8 or more lines now draws its lines faded, without badges, under a caption; hovering a room, tabbing to a button in it or tapping it lights that room's lines with their badges (Escape lets go), and a row of the Connections list lights its one line. The map lot's buildings narrow to fit the lot. Each department's workflow is parsed once per page and the rooms are built together, so the company page renders in about a third of the time. A 1–3 department company looks as before. The `company-scale` playtest scenario (15 departments, three groups) is the board for it.
- Owner checks: `/p/<project>/owner-checks?epic=<slug>` lists every box only the owner can tick under an epic and its children (playtest judgement steps, boxes a guided check left to the owner, Human Testing boxes, boxes marked "(owner)"), grouped by issue, each with its playtest shot and an Evidence tab link, and an epic picker (`epic=all` for every issue). Ticking there is the owner's only: `POST …/owner-checks/tick` refuses anything but the viewer's own page in a browser (403 for curl and agent sessions), ticks the box and adds an owner `owner-check:` comment. Needs you links it, and an agent refused an owner box by `uncheck`/`check-remove` is pointed at it.
- A box outside the acceptance criteria whose id is a playtest judgement step (the step id is the box id, as "TP7 … (owner's look)") is now the owner's, like a criterion linked to one.

- **Department spend and budgets.** Nothing said what a department's agents cost. Each room on the company page (HQ, Product, a department, a group) now shows its spend: tokens and an estimated cost by week for 4 weeks and its top 5 issues, read from Claude Code's transcripts on this machine (numbers only; nothing leaves the machine). Groups and HQ roll up. `issue-cli spend [<box>]` prints it, `department show` has a `Spend:` line. Costs use the prices in `~/.config/ai-workflow-engine/prices.yaml` (no prices are bundled; without one the cost is unknown). A `budget: {usd: 50, period: week}` on a department shows as "x of y" and never blocks anything; lint warns on a malformed one.
- **Department health in the daily snapshot.** A department's goals, outputs, jobs and handoffs showed only their present state, so nothing said whether a department was keeping up. A new `departments` health family measures, per department: goals moving (progress over 7 days), outputs reviewed within `health.limits.outputs_review_days` (default 7) and waiting, jobs on time and periods missed, open handoffs and the oldest one's age, and their mean as *on track*. The Health view gets a Departments table (`/health#departments`), each room's foot on the company page shows `♥ N% on track`, the Report's Health card and `/status.md` show `Departments: N% on track · M outputs waiting`, and Stats draws "Departments on track". The new `departments` score component counts only in a project with departments, so other projects keep their score. `health-tuning` catalog entry bumped (factory/department-health-family-goals-moving-outputs-reviewed-jobs-on-time).
- **A group's week: the lead of leads plans its departments' week and reports on the company page.** A group (GTM over Marketing) had goals, a shelf and jobs but nothing that made its lead plan, and nothing told the owner what moved in a week. `issue-cli department week <group>` reads, per department, the goals and how far each moved in the last 7 days, outputs waiting on review / reviewed / shipped, jobs, handoffs in and out, and what is blocked and on whom. `--write` writes the week report to `<issues>/.reports/<group>/<ISO week>.md`, kept with the work files. The group's room on the company page shows the latest report's counts and links `/p/<project>/reports/<group>/<week>`. The GTM template now installs a `planning` workflow (gather → plan → report → done) and a `weekly-plan` job: each Monday's run writes `## Week plan` and files its departments' work as issues at their first status. It never dispatches, approves or runs a job, and the run starts only by the owner's start or the job's standing start. A group may file work into its own departments without a declared route. The issues dir's dot folders are no longer read as issues. See docs/departments.md#the-week-plan-and-report (factory/the-lead-of-leads-at-work-the-gtm-lead-plans-its-departments-week-and-reports-on-the-company-page).
- `issue-cli handoff <to> --title … --why …`: a department asks another for work as an issue, with `requested_by:`, the route taken (`handoff_route:`) and the asker's issue (`handoff_for:`). A handoff via HQ or a group waits for `handoff forward` or `handoff decline --reason` by that lead. When it is done or declined, the asker's issue gets a `handoff:` comment. `issue-cli handoffs [--from|--to|--waiting]` lists them with their state. The rooms on the company page show Asked, Inbox and To forward, and the connection line and drill-in carry each handoff's state (factory/department-handoffs-one-department-asks-another-for-work-as-an-issue)

- **A sandbox viewer no longer takes over viewer.json.** A worker's viewer with a scratchpad config rewrote `~/.local/state/ai-workflow-engine/viewer.json`, and every issue-cli on the machine followed it: `--project dark-factory` failed with "not found in config, Available projects: rl" until the owner's viewer restarted. The viewer now leaves the file alone when its config is in a temp folder while the state dir isn't, or when another live viewer holds it for a different config, and says so on stderr; `-record-viewer` overrides. issue-cli skips a `viewer.json` whose pid is dead, and "project not found" names the config file and where it came from (`Config from: …`). The worker briefing says to give a hand-started sandbox viewer its own `XDG_STATE_HOME` (cli/a-sandbox-viewer-takes-over-the-machine-wide-viewer-json-and-every-issue-cli-follows-it).
- **Declared connections: who may ask whom and what feeds what, with routing.** The company page drew only what already flows between departments, so "engine requests go via the CEO" lived in prompts and nothing showed a planned line that sat idle or traffic nobody planned. `projects.yaml` now takes `connections: requests:` (`{from, to, via}`: `via: hq` or the asker's group forwards it) and `feeds:` (`{from, to, what}`). Once requests are declared, `issue-cli create` and `set-meta --key requested_by` refuse a department lead's or worker's request off the routes, naming the routes it has and the entry to add; the CEO and the owner route freely. `dispatch` by a department lead waits until the declared forwarder (the CEO, a group lead) has dispatched or approved the request. The company page, the connections page, `connections.json` and `issue-cli connections` mark each line declared, not declared (red `!`) or declared with no traffic (faint, `–`); a department lead's briefing lists its routes; lint's `departments` check warns on unknown boxes, self-routes, a via that forwards nothing, duplicates and a direct department → Product route. See docs/departments.md#declared-connections-and-routing (factory/declared-connections-who-may-ask-whom-and-what-feeds-what-with-routing-rules).
- **A Workshop Board guide page, and a test for wb-* class clashes.** The Workshop Board's rules and its component list lived in issue bodies; workers grepped templates for components, reused generic class names, and broke other branches' tests when a page moved behind `?classic=1` (20 retros). `docs/UI/workshop-board.md` has the rules, where things live, how to name a class, the classic-move checklist and a catalog of all 244 `wb/*` components; `TestWorkshopCatalogMatchesDefines` keeps the catalog in step with the templates. `TestWorkshopCSSClassesDefinedOnce` fails when a `wb-*` class has two rules in `static/workshop.css`. It found ten on main. One was a bug: since the system page's own `.wb-doc-title`, the docs page title was drawn at 12.5px on one line; that class is now `wb-sysdoc-title`. The outputs shelf's page wrapper is now `wb-shelf-page`, so the crate store keeps `wb-shelf`. The other eight were duplicate rules, folded into one rule each with the values they had together, so those pages look the same (checked pixel for pixel).
- **Shipping checks that a commit references the issue before the fast-forward.** The Shipping box asked for "a message referencing the issue" without saying how, and nothing checked it, so workers found out late, after `Proves:` trailers had pinned the shas a reword would break (6 retros). The box now names the forms: the merge commit's subject names the slug (`chore(merge): main into <slug>`), or one commit has a `Refs: <slug>` line. A new rule, `commits_reference_issue`, checks the branch's own commits at `shipping → done`, and the shipping guidance runs `issue-cli transition <slug> --to done --dry-run` before the fast-forward; a failure shows the commit to add. The shipping step's commit examples are conventional commits the commit hook accepts (`docs(changelog):`, `chore(checks):`, `chore(merge):`), and a test keeps every example in workflow.yaml that way.
- **Named N/A forms for testing boxes that don't fit, and a `docs` type.** A test-only fix, a CLI or tmux bug, a runner change and a docs issue all got the playtest, check-script and ux-test boxes, and workers wrote a throwaway check script or new N/A words each time (9 retros). `issue-cli check <slug> <id> --na "<reason>"` now ticks one box with its reason in a named form: `the repro is an automated test (<TestName>)`, `no page changes`, `runner-internal, covered by <TestName>`, `covered by playtest session <name>`, or a reason in a few words (`check --help` lists them). It refuses a reason out of form, and refuses an N/A on the owner's boxes (Human Testing, `(owner)`, owner-only criteria) for anyone; `check-edit` won't let an agent write an N/A into one or drop its `(owner)`. Lint check `na-boxes` warns on ticked ones out of form. The testing prompt lists the forms. New `docs` type for docs and screenshot issues: idea → in progress → shipping → done, no Test Plan. Catalog entries `testing-na-forms` and `docs-type` (workflow/test-plan-named-na-cases-and-a-docs-type-of-work).
- **`issue-cli close <slug> --duplicate-of <other>` / `--fixed-by <other>`, and the brief says who closes.** Leads asked workers to close duplicates or park an issue folded into theirs, which the brief forbade, so the worker commented and the lead did it later (8 retros). `close` parks the issue from any status in one step through the workflow's park transition (its required reason answered from the link), sets `duplicate_of:` / `fixed_by:`, comments on both issues and changes no checkbox. The lead and the human close any issue; a worker only duplicates of its own issue, when its lead asks; any other worker is refused with that rule. The worker brief states the same sentence (`tracker.CloseRule`) under the one-task rule, says a worker may file a child issue of its own when the lead asks, and a worker refused `transition <other> --to parked` is pointed at `close`. See docs/CLI/overview.md, `close`.
- **CHANGELOG entries no longer conflict on every merge.** Every shipping branch adds its line at the top of `## Unreleased`, so every merge of main conflicted there, often twice per issue (13 retros). `.gitattributes` now gives `cmd/issue-cli/CHANGELOG.md` git's built-in `merge=union`, so both sides' new lines are kept; nothing to configure. Union can't see headings: after a release renamed `## Unreleased`, git puts a branch's entry under the new version without a conflict. `TestChangelogBranchEntriesUnreleased` (in `make validate`) fails on a work branch when an entry it added sits below the first version heading, and the shipping guidance asks to check the entry's heading after merging main.
- **Playtest guide: Pitfalls.** Sessions kept failing on the same few things, each found only after a full ux-test run (12 retros). docs/patterns/playtest/web-app.md (and `issue-cli process playtest web`) gets a Pitfalls section with one line and one example each: check only stable values under `REPEAT=2`; `cli:` steps need `--project {project}`, because the scenario's issue-cli names the project `proj`; `PLAYTEST_PROJECT=` and `ISSUE=` for playtest-report on another board or branch; wait for the first health snapshot; wait for "Press Enter to close" in a tmux window started from a button; search the sessions' `contains:` checks after a rename, since go test doesn't run them; a step's shot is its last page; run the final `REPEAT=2` after the last merge of main. `playtest/sessions/example-tmux-window.yaml` is the tmux example. Docs only.
