- **`make shot URL=…` for one screenshot; `make ux-test S=` runs any session.** Workers had no reliable way to see one page on this Mac (14 retros): the Playwright and chrome-devtools MCPs look for /Applications/Google Chrome, and Chrome for Testing's `--screenshot` hangs on Workshop pages because they poll, so they wrote throwaway chromedp scripts. `make shot URL=<page> [W=390] [H=844] [FULL=1] [OUT=file.png]` runs the new `playtest shot`: the runner's browser (Chrome for Testing under ms-playwright when nothing else is found, no `PLAYTEST_CHROME` needed), the load event and the body, then the PNG, never waiting for network idle; default 1280x800 into `.playtest/shot.png`. On a work branch `S=<session>` no longer adds the branch's slug as `ISSUE`, so another issue's session runs (5 retros); with both given and no match, the error names the issue filter and the session's own issue. `tools/mockup-shots/shots.py`'s usage line says `uv run --with playwright`. See docs/patterns/playtest/web-app.md.
- **Checklist editing: `uncheck`, `check-remove`, stable ids, no starter criteria in a full section.** `issue-cli uncheck <slug> <id>...` unticks boxes. `issue-cli check-remove <slug> <id>...` removes them and leaves a hidden `<!-- removed AC6: … -->` line that keeps the slot, so every other box keeps its id: after AC6 goes, AC7 is still AC7, and its `Proves:` trailers still prove it. Both work by id, several at once, all-or-nothing. `check-remove` refuses a workflow box in a gated section (tick it or mark it n/a with `check-edit`). From an agent session both refuse the owner's boxes: Human Testing, `(owner)`, owner-only criteria, and guided-check owner boxes. The idea → in design move no longer adds "First / Second observable requirement" to an `## Acceptance criteria` section that already has boxes: `append_section` tops up an existing section only when a `section_checkboxes_checked` rule gates it. `checklist` names the proven criteria (`2/7 proven (AC1 AC3)`), and a trailer that names a playtest step id (`Proves: AC1 TP4`) gets a fix pointing at `proves: [AC1]` in the session. See docs/CLI/overview.md, `uncheck` and `check-remove`.
- **Guided checks: shared stand-ins, and a merged issue's script runs from history.** Check scripts kept re-writing the same helpers, and after the merge `check-script <slug>` found nothing, because shipping removes the script from main (16 retros). The library (`CHECKS_LIB_VERSION` 3) adds `stand_in_viewer` (a python http.server viewer with `viewer_url:` at the top of the sandbox config), `stand_in_claude_box <session> [--swallow-first-enter]` with `expect_box_submitted` / `expect_box_empty` (a Claude Code input box in a private tmux, for paste and nudge bugs), `sandbox_yolo_on` (the sandbox's own `.yolo/<project>.json`) and `seed_department <template>` (applies `department add --dry-run`'s plan to the sandbox without running the guarded command). When the issue's checkout has no script, `check-script <slug>` runs the newest copy a commit on main (or `work/<slug>`) removed, from the project folder, and says which commit; `--list` marks it, `--json` adds `from`. The lib docs say which `$SANDBOX_CONFIG` keys are top level and that `clean_shell` drops a worker's identity, so it must not carry an owner-only command; the ported example scripts call `cp`, not `/usr/bin/cp` (macOS has none). No worker guard changed: a sanctioned sandbox exception is a design waiting for the owner. No catalog bump: the library ships with issue-cli and no workflow line changed.
- **Worker briefing: `## Shell on this machine`.** Workers each lost a retry to the same shell facts (15 retros): zsh not word-splitting `$VAR` and failing on unquoted globs, rtk shortening `git log` and `go test -v` output that feeds a commit message or a `tests:` comment, BSD `sed -i ''`, `script` without `-c`, no `timeout`. The dispatch prompt now has a section after `## Your lead` with one line per fact that applies, detected from `$SHELL`, the OS and `rtk` on PATH; none applies, no section. CLAUDE.md lists the same under Validation.
- **`issue-cli prove <slug> --run` runs the trailers' tests and posts the `tests:` comment.** A `Proves: AC1 TestName` trailer read UNPROVEN ("no tests: report") until someone wrote a `tests:` comment by hand, and every later commit made it stale (12 retros). `prove --run` takes every test link of the issue, runs `go test -json -run '^(…)$'` on just their packages in the issue's worktree (after `mem-guard check` when installed), and posts `tests: prove --run at <sha>: …` only when every named test passed; a failing, skipped or unfound test prints its output and posts nothing. It refuses uncommitted code, a worktree without go.mod, or no worktree. `--json` adds a `run` object. Commits that touch only docs, Markdown, CHANGELOG, `playtest/sessions/`, `scripts/checks/`, `issues/`, `retros/` or `sessions/` no longer stale the report. The "no tests: report" / "stale" hints and the in progress prompt name `prove --run`; catalog `proof-links` v0.58.0.
- **YOLO: HT1 and the Manual repro box wait for the owner after the fact (fix).** "HT1 Human verification complete if required" and the bugfix path's Manual box stayed unticked at done with nothing saying whose they were: no gate checks them, so the owner-check queue never saw them. Now every box in `## Human Testing` and every box whose text ends in `(owner)` is the owner's, like the owner-check criteria: under YOLO a gate that passes from `testing` on (and the move to `done`) queues each one still unticked as "HT1: owner check, not done", in `yolo queue`, Needs you (now with the box's text), the sidebar and the Station. It leaves on the owner's tick, Agree, or Send back for fixes; the next gate queues it again while it is unticked. The bugfix path's box now reads "Repro steps re-run and the bug is gone … (owner)". `check-script --yes` still ticks nothing, but a passing run ends with "Left to the owner: TP4." in place of "Nothing ticked." and lists every box on the issue left to the owner, with why. With YOLO off nothing changes.
- **Departments: the settled rules written down once.** docs/departments.md has a "Settled rules" section (secrets as references, private projects, the one waiting-on-you count, box keys, projects.yaml writes, the two lint checks, pilots, channels), each with the decision it comes from, and the Factory overlay in workflow.yaml points Factory workers at it leaving idea and in design.
- **Dispatch records the base; `depends_on` shows at start and holds shipping.** The dispatch that cuts an issue's work branch writes the branch and commit it was cut from into the stats sidecar's Dispatch row (`base_branch`, `base_sha`), and `show` / `start` print `Base: main at 4d4d4e7 (cut by the dispatch on <date>)` (`show --json`: `base`). `start` lists each `depends_on` issue with its status and open branch. At the ship status (the last status before `done`, `shipping` on the development template) `start` and `transition` print a `== Shipping ==` block: the main checkout's path and checked-out branch, "stop and ask the lead" when that isn't the issue's base (or main), and `Ships after <slug> (<status>)` with a warning while it isn't done. `transition --to shipping --dry-run` warns per open dependency (`warnings` in `--json`) without making the move unready.
- **Gates YOLO never passes: spending money and publishing outside.** `owner_only: true` on a `require_human_approval` action makes that gate the owner's even in YOLO. With YOLO on, `transition` and `start` refuse it with "this gate is the owner's even in YOLO … Stop here and tell your lead", `--dry-run` and the Next hint say the same, and the issue waits in Needs you like any gate; your approval in the viewer, or the CEO's `approve --quote` with your words, passes it as before. With YOLO off nothing changes. Experiments' gates go through the same rule, and their note is now in the refusal itself. `yolo status` (`never_passes` in `--json`), `yolo on`, the turn-on dialog, the sidebar and hazard bands and `yolo.json` list the gates YOLO never passes: each `owner_only` gate in Product's and each department's workflow, the Human Testing approval, experiments, and `release publish` while releases are locked. The bundled `content` and `marketing` templates have it on `owner review → publish` (catalog entry `owner-only-gates`, offered by `workflow upgrade`). New `health lint` check `owner-only` warns on a path entering `publish`/`launch`/`send` without it, a department with a `sends: true` connector whose workflow has none, and `owner_only` on a non-approval action. See docs/Workflow/yolo.md, Gates YOLO never passes.
- **catalog-check reads `Catalog:` lines anywhere and rejects ones that mark nothing.** `issue-cli workflow catalog-check` reads `Catalog: n/a <id>[,<id>] <why>` in any paragraph of a branch commit's message (also one before `Co-Authored-By`), merge commits included, with the same reader as `Proves:`. A line that isn't an n/a, has no entry id after `n/a`, or names an entry the catalog lacks (`Catalog: n/a refactor only`) now fails the check, naming the commit and showing the form and the entries the branch touches. Files the branch has only through a merge of main (one in progress too) aren't asked about. A work branch with only uncommitted changes is checked instead of "skipped: not a work branch", so it runs from a worktree whatever `--project` says. The output starts with one line per entry and ends with a count. The engine's in progress guidance now asks for it during the work, not first at the end of `make validate`.
- **The test suite holds on a busy machine (fix).** Local CI went red on 2026-10-30 while other projects pushed memory to 66–77%, each time on a different test. There were two races. The dispatch tests' stand-in agent wrote its arguments in place, and sh line-buffers printf, so a reader could catch a multi-line prompt cut at its first line. Shutdown cancelled the hard context before naming what was still running, so a goroutine waiting only on that context could return first and drop off the "still running" list in the shutdown log. That one is a fix in the viewer itself. A sweep of every test that sleeps, times out or compares elapsed time also made the rest load-proof: polling loops capped at 1–2.5 s, a 50 ms health-family timeout and 500 ms delivery polls inside a 3 s budget now poll up to a generous deadline that only ends a hung test. `scripts/under-load.sh -- <command>` runs a command beside a CPU and memory hog that it starts and stops itself. It refuses to start when `mem-guard check` is blocked and stops at 70% memory.
- **YOLO: one rule instead of "clarify with the human".** With YOLO on, a worker was told to ask the human and stop for approval by the status guidance and the dispatch prompt, and to run the gate by the Next block. Now `start`, `show` and `transition` print one line, "YOLO rule: don't ask the owner and don't stop at gates…", in place of the guidance sentences that say to ask, clarify with or tell the human, or to stop and end the turn; the rest of the guidance prints as written. The rule says to record answers with `decide --provisional` and put what can't be answered in a `question:` comment, and names what still waits for the owner: experiments, product-policy rules, and anything that spends money or publishes outside the machine. The dispatch prompt under YOLO drops "stop at every gate" and its stop-for-approval steps for the same rule. With YOLO off nothing changes. New `decide --provisional` adds "provisional" to the decision's how (`chat, provisional`, `YOLO, provisional`), so the Decisions log lists it as waiting for your words and `--confirm` clears it. `transition --json` gains `yolo_rule`.
- **Connections between departments.** The company page lists what passes between HQ, Product and the departments, and draws each as a line between the rooms: a **request** (an issue with `requested_by:`, or one citing another department's decision; through HQ when the CEO recorded its approval or dispatch), an **output** (a shelf output another department's issue links to, or one recorded with `issue-cli output use <id> --by <department>`) and a **dependency** (`depends_on` / `blocked_on` across departments). Each line counts its open items and opens `/p/<project>/connections` (`connections.json`), filtered by from, to and kind; room feet show ⇄ in · out and the map's lot ⇄ N. `issue-cli create` fills `requested_by:` when a department lead, or a worker on another department's issue, files it (`--requested-by` sets it); `issue-cli connections` lists the same lines; `department show` counts them. Everything is derived from what is on disk: nothing is declared in config yet, and messages between sessions are not a source.
- **A department template installs once.** The company page's "+ New department" tile now lists only the templates the project hasn't added, and the optional name is gone: a template installs under its own name. With every template added (Marketing and GTM), the tile says "Every department template is added" and has no form. A Marketing you installed under another name still counts: its workflow file names the template. `department add marketing` on a project that has it, under a group or not, is refused with "Marketing is already a department of <project> (<projects.yaml>): a template installs once. To add it again, remove it first: issue-cli department remove marketing". Add in the viewer gives the same message with a 409 and writes nothing. `department add --name` is gone. The Updates page's "Departments you can add" uses the same rule.
- **Marketing starts with a brand foundation.** The bundled `marketing` workflow has two research-mode types ahead of the channels. In `foundation` (research → brand → icp → owner review → publish → done), the worker researches a topic you name and proposes the brand (traits, voice, do / don't) and the ICP (who, their problems, where they are, how to reach them), each with its sources. You settle them at the `publish` gate, and the approved text goes to `departments/<key>/brand.md` and `icp.md`, which the template's `role.md` cites. `market-size` does TAM / SAM / SOM the same way, into `market.md`, whenever you want it. Basic first: a `piece` or `seo-pass` brief can't move to draft until `brand.md` and `icp.md` exist. The refusal names the missing file and the `issue-cli create --type foundation` command, and `issue-cli health lint` warns ahead of time. Research prompts check `mem-guard` before a browser or bulk fetches. An installed Marketing gets it from `workflow upgrade --department marketing` (new catalog entry `brand-foundation`). See docs/departments.md, "Brand foundation and research mode".
- **New validator `file_exists`.** A transition can require a file under the project root to exist and not be empty, without a shell or `allow_shell`. `path:` and `hint:` take `{{department}}`, the department's key. Catalog entry `file-exists-gates`. `create --department` also takes a department's key (`growth-team`) as well as its name.
- **`create --type` starts any type at its first status.** A type whose first status sat far down the base list (the marketing channels' `brief`, after the foundation's statuses) was refused with an empty "allowed:" list.
- **Department groups: a lead of leads.** A department can sit under another with `parent: gtm` in projects.yaml, making that one a group, one level deep. The group's lead (`issue-cli lead spawn --department gtm`) reports to the CEO and dispatches and approves the group's issues and its departments', and nothing else. Its brief lists its departments and their leads. A department lead under a group reports to the group's lead while it runs, else to the CEO; a worker goes to its department's lead, then the group's, then the CEO. `lead whoami`, `lead list`, `show` / `start` and the worker's briefing name the chain. Department goals can roll up into a group goal (`rolls_up: pipeline`), which rolls into HQ. The company page draws a group as one room with its departments' rooms inside (its lead, goals and waiting count on it); the map, the Crew page and `/leads` nest them too. `department add gtm` installs the new GTM group template and Marketing under it, moving a Marketing you already have under it and keeping your files; `department add <template> --parent <group>` and the New department tile's "Under which group?" add a department under one. Lint flags a `parent:` that names no department, itself, HQ or Product, or a group under a group, and a `rolls_up:` that names no group or HQ goal.
- **YOLO: owner checks wait for you after the fact (fix).** A criterion only you can prove (it links a playtest judgement step, a guided check's machine run left its step to you as "needs your eyes" or skipped, or its text ends in `(owner)`) used to either stop YOLO at `testing → human-testing` or get ticked from a commit's `Proves:` trailer without anyone looking. Now no trailer, test or value ticks such a box; only your passing judgement step or your own tick does, and `prove --list` says so. Under YOLO the proof gate lets it through unticked, and the transition puts it in the after-the-fact queue by name, "AC6: owner check, not done", with its text and why: `issue-cli yolo queue`, the sidebar, the company's list, Needs you, and Passed in YOLO on the issue. It leaves the queue when you tick the box, Agree (`yolo review <slug> --agree --status AC6`) or send it back. Other boxes a guided check left to you are listed the same way. Experiments and YOLO off keep the old gate. See docs/Workflow/yolo.md, Owner checks.
- **Marketing channels are types of work, SEO first.** `department add marketing` now installs the new bundled `marketing` workflow: the `content` statuses plus a `measure` step after `publish`, with one type of work per channel. `piece` (the default: one post, page or email) walks brief → draft → owner review → publish → done as before. `seo-pass` (system SEO) walks on to `measure`, where the worker records the goal values and what the pass did, and it can't skip that step. PPC, social and cold outreach are commented-out slots in the template, so adding one is a template edit, not engine work. `department add` names the types in its next steps, and for an edited workflow file it kept, prints the command that writes the bundled one next to it. An installed Marketing keeps its file; docs/departments.md, "Channels as types", has the steps to move it over. New catalog entry `measure-after-publish`.
- **A recurring job can pick its runs' type.** `type:` on a `jobs:` entry creates each run as that type of work, at its first status. Marketing's weekly SEO pass sets `type: seo-pass`. A type the department's workflow doesn't define stops the run with the types it has, and `issue-cli health lint` warns about it.
- **`issue-cli process workflow --department <name>` works.** `process` rejected `--department` before the topic read it.
- **Add a department from the company page.** The "+ New department" tile is now a form over the bundled department templates (Marketing for now), with an optional name. **Preview** shows what `department add --dry-run` prints: each file written, already there or kept because you edited it, and the projects.yaml entry. Nothing is written until **Add**, which installs the department and brings you back to the company page with its room. The viewer re-reads the project from projects.yaml, so no restart is needed. A name the project already has is refused with the reason. Add only takes the owner's browser: a cross-origin request or one without browser headers (an agent's curl) gets 403. The Updates page's department cards have the same **Add…** button. Departments come only from the templates; there is no empty or custom choice.
- **Approval and send-back nudges are confirmed sent.** The viewer pasted them without bracketed paste, pressed Enter once after 200 ms and never looked again, so Claude Code sometimes took the Enter as part of the paste: the text sat typed in the worker's input box, the worker never continued, and the viewer said the notification was sent. They now go in the way the lead chat and Re-send prompt do (a named buffer, one bracketed paste, Enter), then the pane is read until the text is in the conversation. While it sits in the input box, Enter is pressed again, up to twice. If it never lands, the viewer shows the reason as the notification error. The same check fixes a false "Prompt delivered" for the lead chat and Re-send prompt: text still in the input box used to count as delivered.
- **check-script runs a merged issue's checks from main.** Once `work/<slug>` is merged into main and its worktree holds no uncommitted change, `check-script` (and ▶ Run checks) runs from the project folder instead of that worktree, whose code and scripts are older than main's, and says so. `--new` still writes into the worktree.
- **Check library: `expect '[ $? = 0 ]'` works, screenshots outlive the sandbox.** `expect` and `eventually` give their condition the exit code of the command before them (`wait_for_url …; expect '[ $? = 0 ]' …` always failed). New `keep <file>` copies a file into `.agent-logs/checks/<name>/files/`; `show` keeps the file first and opens it with `open` on macOS too.
- **Turning on a department, step by step.** docs/departments.md has the owner's steps to run a real department, from registering the project to the weekly standing start, with Marketing's SEO pass as the example. A guided check on the issue branch rehearsed them on a throwaway project with stand-in connectors. Fixes it found: `department add`'s next steps printed a `create` command that failed (it lacked `--title`) and told you to edit the job brief where it meant the role prompt. They now name the role prompt and each job's brief, and point at `issue-cli connectors <key>` for the connector config, since templates carry none. `show` and `start` no longer print the department twice for a department with a mission or goals. Marketing's weekly SEO pass brief now asks for a blog draft on the shelf and for the week's `goal set` numbers, so the template's Posts and Pages goals get values.

## v0.57.0 — 2026-10-30

- **`release cut` no longer pushes to a repo that may be public.** With `privacy.code: snapshots` but no public remote yet (`release setup --dev` has not run, Code shows Not switched), origin may still be the public repo. `release cut` used to push `release/vX.Y` there. Now it refuses before it creates a branch, tag or worktree, names origin, and prints `issue-cli release setup --dev <private dev repo url>`. After setup it pushes the branch to origin (the dev repo) as before. `release publish`, `--dry-run` included, already refused before the switch, and a test now covers it.
- `make ux-test` no longer waits out a step on a stalled screenshot. Headless Chrome for Testing sometimes never answers a capture, and the capture ran under the step's 90 s timeout, so a full run with `REPEAT=2` took about 50 minutes. Each capture now has 15 s and one retry; a capture that still fails is logged in the step's log as `no screenshot: screenshot failed after 2 tries: timed out after 15s`, and the step keeps the result of its checks. The overlay's fail shots and `make ux-shots` use the same bound.
- **Department connectors.** A department lists the outside tools its agents may use under `connectors:` in projects.yaml: an MCP server (`kind: mcp`) or a command (`kind: tool`). Secrets are referenced, never written: `env:<VAR>` or `keychain:<service>`. `issue-cli connector exec <dept>/<name>` runs one with its secrets in the child's environment only. It refuses another department's agents, Product's workers, the CEO and a plain shell; the owner's shell passes `--as-owner`. Dispatching a department worker or spawning its lead gives the agent the department's MCP servers (claude `--mcp-config`, codex `-c mcp_servers.…`), each pointing at `connector exec`, so no config file holds a secret. The briefing's `## Your department` and `start` / `show` list them. `issue-cli connector check` runs the read-only checks into `<issues>/.connectors/checks.jsonl`, and `issue-cli connectors` lists each secret as set or missing. The company page's room shows the connectors with **Check now**, a failing or missing one is a Needs you row, and `health lint` warns about bad config, including a raw value where a reference belongs. See docs/departments.md#connectors.
- The playtest test-id list is split into one file per page under `internal/testids/` (`sidebar.go`, `workboard.go`, `status.go`, ...), each appending to `IDs`, so branches that add ids to different pages no longer conflict on one file. The ids and `TestTemplateTestIDs` are unchanged.
- **The factory benchmark.** `issue-cli bench` measures how well the factory works on the same work: done issues with a known good result become golden tasks, a worker solves each again in a sandbox from the commit it started at, and two runs are compared. `bench candidates` lists done issues that make good tasks (a recorded branch, test files in its commits, up to 600 lines). `bench task add <benchmark> <slug>` finds the base and merged commits, the hidden tests and the issue as it stood at its start, and refuses a task whose base or merged commit fails its check. `bench run` takes one task at a time after `mem-guard check`, in a clone with no remote and a scratch tracker with YOLO on, and scores it by the hidden tests from the merged commit and the task's check (a judge command can add a 0-5 score). Tokens and cost come from Claude Code's result or transcripts, never guessed. The run stops at the task's wall cap and at the run's token, cost or wall budget; `--resume` continues it. `--setup <ref>` builds issue-cli from an engine commit, `--workflow <file>` swaps the workflow, and `bench compare` exits 1 when the second run regresses. The benchmark lives in your config folder (`~/.config/ai-workflow-engine/bench/`): only you change it (agent sessions are refused), and holdout tasks only ever show as passed n/m. The Report gets a Benchmark card, Stats a pass-rate trend, and Health a `bench` family. See docs/benchmark.md.
- **The experiment loop.** `issue-cli experiment` measures a change to prompts, workflow.yaml or a tool before it is adopted. `candidates` lists what the retros' Workflow findings keep asking for. `init <slug>` writes the hypothesis and target on an issue labelled `experiment`. `run <slug>` checks the branch (it must contain main and touch no protected path, such as the benchmark or the verdict rule), runs the benchmark on main (or reuses a finished run of the same commit) and on the branch, and records a verdict (promising, rejected or broken) with the numbers as an `experiment:` comment. `--dry-run` shows the plan without running anything. `rollback <slug>` prepares a `revert/<slug>` branch and never touches main. YOLO passes no gate of an experiment issue: the owner adopts. The Updates card shows an experiment's verdict when a catalog entry names it. `run` needs `issue-cli bench` (factory/factory-benchmark-and-evals); until then `record` judges two existing runs. See docs/workflow-stats.md#experiments.
- `check-script` finds scripts for a slug with a space (a system such as `Level Design` gives `level design/…`). The `# issue:` header was read up to the first space, so `--list`, `--yes` and ▶ Run checks found nothing; it now runs to the first space after the slug's slash. When the workflow has no worktrees and there is no `work/<slug>` branch (a slug with a space can't have one), check-script uses the worktree you run it from, so `--new` writes there and its scripts are found.
- **Recurring jobs per department.** A department lists `jobs:` in projects.yaml (`every: day|week|month`, `on:`, `at:`, `brief:`). Each run is an issue in the department (`job:`, `run:`), created from the brief at its workflow's first status and dispatched like any worker, so its gates, timeline, token estimate and shelf outputs are the run's record. `issue-cli job due` tells the lead's check-in what is due and the one command to run; `job run <job> --quote` starts a run on your words; `job run <job> --scheduled` starts it unattended only on the job's standing start (`job auto on <job>|--all --quote`, off by default), inside auto-start's mem-guard check, worker cap and machine-wide gap. Without one, a due job waits in Needs you (Due jobs, ▶ Start run). `issue-cli jobs` lists them with the last run and its outputs; `department show`, the department rooms on the company page, `lead whoami`, the lead briefs and `status.md` show them; `health lint --check departments` checks them. Every due, hold and start goes to `<issues>/.jobs/runs.jsonl`. Department templates carry jobs too: `department add marketing` installs a weekly SEO pass with its brief, standing start off. The tool still runs nothing on a timer.
- **Add a department from a template.** `issue-cli department add marketing` installs the bundled Marketing department (SEO and content): `workflows/marketing.yaml` from the `content` template, a role prompt at `departments/marketing/role.md` and a `departments:` entry with its colour, role, mission, `SEO` system and two weekly goals in the project's projects.yaml. The edit keeps every other line of projects.yaml as it was (comments included), and a file you already have is never overwritten: it is reported as kept. `--name <Name>` installs it under another name, `--dry-run` prints the plan. `issue-cli department list` shows HQ, Product (the development template) and the templates, installed or not; `department remove <name>` takes the entry out again (its files stay) and refuses while the department has open issues. Workers can't run add or remove. The Updates page gains **Departments you can add**, with the command for each template the project doesn't have; the sidebar count is unchanged. `lead spawn` on a project without departments, `workflow init --out` and `department show <unknown>` point at `department list`. See docs/departments.md#templates.
- The hall is the board. `/p/<project>/board` and `/p/<project>/workboard` redirect to `/hall`; the classic board (drag-and-drop) and the workboard stay at `?classic=1`, under the sidebar's More, and their filters keep `classic=1`. The sidebar's Board opens the hall. Canvas and Graph stay classic pages, unchanged and not linked from the Workshop Board (`factory/retire-board-canvas-graph`, provisional).
- **Hall stats on the Workshop Board.** `/p/<project>/stats` now opens in the board's look, with Stats in the hall tabs (Line · Report · Health · Stats …): Now gauges, trends from the health snapshots, where the time goes (with drill-downs to the issues), token cost, CLI usage over 30 days and the time tables folded at the bottom. The numbers are the same as before. Lines of code and test ratio move to a card linking to Health, which owns code shape, and Big files links there too. A block without data says what's missing and gives the command that fills it, e.g. `issue-cli --project <slug> health backfill`. `/stats` across projects is one card per project. The old pages stay at `?classic=1`, and their own links keep you there. Range and group-by are links; the last ones are remembered per project and shared by both pages. The agents canvas, and the header of every hall page, no longer parse each done issue's workflow: on an 800-issue project that took 1.4 s per request. See docs/workflow-stats.md, "Stats page".
- **Workflow updates across projects is a Workshop Board page.** `/updates` now has the Workshop Board header (Map › Workflow updates, with what waits on you in every project), a tally (projects, stamped and behind, never scanned, to decide) and one card per project in its map colour: template stamp, state, features to decide, record, newest proposal and **Open its updates**. A project on the built-in or a custom workflow says it has no catalog to compare. The old table stays at `?classic=1`. `/leads` is now called **All CEOs**, as on the map, and it and `/needs-you` are built from named `wb/*` components (`wb/ceo-grid`, `wb/ny-*`) with no visible change.
- **A system's page is a Workshop.** `/p/<project>/systems/<System>` and its family pages are now Workshop Board pages under the hall's Systems tab, the page the Systems index's workshop names open. They have a Systems › System › Family crumb, the summary (or the hint naming `workbench.systems.<System>`), open / done / gates-for-you figures and the umbrella chip. The main column has the workshop floor (every issue as a crate with the index's colours, plus a status bar), the issues as crate cards, families or epics with progress, and the latest decisions as decision-log rows. The side has the system's own code map (its `paths:` files, or a Draft from its issues), the design-doc sections and their state, guard metrics, knobs and balance notes, each hidden when it has no data. An unknown system or family is a 404 on the Systems page with a note. The classic page stays at `?classic=1`.
- **Agents is the Crew page on the Workshop Board.** `/p/<project>/agents` is now the Crew: You → the CEO → a column per department (Product first) with its lead and its live workers, each a card with its state in words (working, idle since the last issue-cli call, waiting on you at a gate, asked a question), its issue, status and epic; then the stopped jobs (with `issue-cli dispatch <slug>` to copy) and sessions not bound to an issue. `?agent=<session>` opens the panel: Open in a terminal, the attach (or, with `terminal: none`, switch) command, the issue with a link to approve its gate at the station, the branch's diffstat and the last comments; a CEO or department lead that is not running gets its Start button. The header's working / idle / waiting-on-an-agent counts link to it on every project page, and the Company HQ room has a Crew link. The radial canvas stays at `?classic=1`. A draft from the design system: the owner picks the final look.
- **The issue list is the crate store.** `/p/<project>/` is now a Workshop Board page, the hall's new **Issues** tab: every issue as a crate on shelves by status in workflow order (done and parked fold to the 10 most recently changed), with its system, type, priority, labels, worker robot and the hall's markers (waiting on you, CI, YOLO). The old filters all work, as quick pills (All, Open, Waiting on you, Agent at work), removable filter pills and side facets that count what the other filters leave. New issue opens the create form. The classic list stays at `?classic=1`. On phones the Workshop Board header no longer sticks to the top. See docs/UI/overview.md#crate-store.
- **`prove` counts only the issue's own trailers, finds `D-8: …` tests and takes `--shot` without a runner.** Commits another issue's branch held first no longer count: a branch started on another issue's unmerged branch used to list that issue's `Proves:` trailers under its own criteria, and after the merge the walk of main picked up every issue fast-forwarded meanwhile. A commit now belongs to the issue whose recorded branch head reached it first, so a branch that only fast-forwarded to main, with no commits of its own, lists none. A `Proves:` line counts in any paragraph of the message, also one before `Co-Authored-By` (git's trailers read only the last paragraph). A test name holding a colon (an `it('D-8: …')` title) is found instead of "not found". `prove --shot` is refused only when the project has a playtest runner behind the `playtest-sessions` feature (`playtest_command:`, `playtest/sessions/*.yaml` or `.playtest/runs/`); the feature alone, as the template installs it, no longer blocks it. See docs/Workflow/proof.md.
- **`depends_on` counts as waiting on an agent.** On the Workshop Board a worker whose issue lists, in `depends_on`, an issue with a live worker now shows as waiting on an agent (`<slug> → <dep>`), and the header's N waiting on an agent counts it; before, only `blocked_on` did.
- **`start` passes the YOLO gate.** While YOLO is on, `issue-cli start` on a backlog issue (or any handoff status) passes the approval gate as `transition` does: it writes the `approval: "YOLO"` marker, claims and advances to `in progress` in one step, and prints the `YOLO: gate … passed` line. It used to refuse with "human approval for \"in progress\" is missing" although `transition --dry-run` said the gate passes, and the fallback `transition` then failed `has_assignee` because in design → backlog clears the assignee.
- **Guided check scripts find the issue's worktree, a newer bash, and gate human-testing.** `check-script` (`--new`, `--list`, `--yes`, the owner's run) and the viewer's ▶ Run checks and checks-due count now use the git worktree checked out on `work/<slug>` when the workflow declares no `worktree:`, under any folder name; `--new` no longer writes into the main checkout. Scripts run with a bash 4 or newer: bash on PATH, else `/opt/homebrew/bin/bash`, `/usr/local/bin/bash` or `/opt/local/bin/bash`, first on the script's PATH too; with only macOS's bash 3.2 the run stops with exit 2 and says `brew install bash`. When the workflow uses check scripts, `transition --to human-testing` (and `--dry-run`) refuses until each of the issue's scripts has a newest machine run (`check-script <slug> --yes`) with no failed step.
- **What a feature unlocks lives in the catalog.** A `workflow-features.yaml` entry can carry `unlocks:` (`text`, a project-relative `url`): the pages the board shows locked or empty without it. The hall's Updates page reads them instead of a list in Go; the six rows (local-ci, status-changelog, balance-board, metrics-and-knobs, system-pages, privacy) moved over unchanged, so the page looks the same. `make validate` now fails on an unlocks row without text, with a url the viewer doesn't serve, or on any catalog key the engine doesn't know (a misspelled `unlock:` used to vanish). Changing only `unlocks:` needs no version bump from `workflow catalog-check`. See docs/Workflow/overview.md.
- **Systems get code paths, and the code map stops being a draft.** `workbench.systems.<name>.paths:` lists repo globs of a system's code (`**` any directories, `dir/` everything under it, relative to the repository root). The Systems page code map reads ownership from them: a workshop's CODE row lists its own files (the ones its issues name first, then hotspots), and the map loses its **Draft** mark once every system on the page has paths. Until then its note names the systems still read from their issues, and an "Unlock: a confirmed code map" card asks the CEO to draft the globs. `issue-cli health lint` has a new `paths` check: a glob that matches no file, or leaves the repository, is a warning with its workflow.yaml line. This repo's workflow.yaml now has paths for UI, CLI, Workflow, API and a new Factory entry. The `system-pages` catalog entry documents the key (v0.57.0).
- **Comments say who wrote them, and the Radio carries workers and leads.** A comment now stores `author`: issue-cli signs it with the session (`agent-<slug>`, `lead-<project>`, a department lead) or a worktree worker's assignee, and says so (`✓ Comment added to <slug> by <author>`); from a plain shell it signs nothing. The viewer signs yours `owner`. Older comments are left as they are and read their author from " — recorded by <name>". The company page Radio lists `progress:` and `question:` comments from the last week below the CEO's lines, newest first, with author, issue link, kind and age; other comments stay off it. The classic issue page shows each comment's author and the Station's question card says who asked.
- **Confirm a decision yourself.** On the Decisions log, Confirm on an orange box now records your words (optional; "Confirmed." without them) on that same D-n: its "(provisional…)" mark goes and its how ends "confirmed by owner <date>". It no longer needs the CEO running, and the ref and its citations stay valid (the old advice, `decide --replaces`, made citing issues stale). Provisional decisions that carry a relayed quote now get the box too. The endpoint is `POST /p/<project>/decisions/<slug>/<D-n>/confirm {text?}`; agents relaying your confirmation use `issue-cli decide <slug> --confirm D-n --quote "…"`. `issue-cli decisions` marks rows that need your words. Question it still goes to the CEO.
- **Send back for fixes moves the issue back.** On the Station, Send back for fixes now moves the issue back one status on its path (human-testing → testing; a tweak at testing → in progress) with your words as a `rejected:` comment, clears the stamp and tells the worker in its session; before, it only left a comment. The endpoint is `POST /p/<project>/issue/<slug>/send-back {text, to?}`. The YOLO review's Back now works on workflows that declare no backward edge (it answered 409 on the default workflow).
- **A lead per department.** `issue-cli lead spawn --department <name>` (or ▶ Start lead in the department's room, or the agents canvas) starts `lead-<project>-<dept>`, reporting to the CEO, with a brief from the department's mission, workflow, role prompt and the CEO's rules. It may dispatch and approve only its own department's issues. A department issue's worker reports to it while it is live (else to the CEO, covering) and its briefing gains `## Your department` with the role prompt inlined. `show` / `start` print `Lead: … · CEO: …`; `lead whoami`, `lead list` and `lead message department:<name>` know department leads; the canvas, the company rooms and `/leads` show them, and `/p/<project>/lead?dept=<name>` is a department lead's chat.
- **The map's lots show the project's goal.** The bar under each lot is now HQ's first goal from projects.yaml (else Product's or a department's), with value / target, instead of the top open epic. Projects without goals keep the epic. The New project tile lists the workflow templates issue-cli really bundles (development, content, review, writing) and gives `issue-cli workflow init --template <name>`; it used to name templates that do not exist.
- **The project lead is called the CEO.** The viewer's classic pages (sidebar and agents canvas "Start the CEO", the issue page's CEO box, Every CEO, the status page and status.md) and issue-cli's help and output say CEO; `issue-cli ceo` is an alias of `issue-cli lead`, and `/p/<project>/ceo` and `/ceos` redirect to `/lead` and `/leads`. The tmux name `lead-<project>`, the `lead` command, `/lead` URLs, JSON keys and the `lead_terminal` / `lead_window_session` keys stay. `--how` now defaults to "CEO chat"; the Decisions page reads old "lead chat" records the same.
- **A health review recorded as the minute turns keeps its score.** `issue-cli health review record` used to date the opinion with a second clock reading. If that reading fell in the next minute, the review snapshot skipped the opinion as newer than itself, and `opinion.score` was missing. It now uses one reading for both. Tests that timed pages or waited on tmux panes now hold on a loaded machine: the stats page is timed against an uncached request in the same run, and the private-tmux check waits for the pane.
- **`issue-cli release publish <version> --dry-run` shows the public commit before it exists.** It runs every check a real publish runs and prints the parent, the full message and a diff stat of what the one public commit would change. It commits, tags and pushes nothing, and lists a YOLO window or an unmet Waiting for release check instead of refusing.
- **A release snapshot's message carries its changelog.** The public commit's subject is still `vX.Y.Z`; its body is now the release's changelog section, so the public log reads as release notes. `release setup` no longer says the old public history must be cleaned: the first snapshot goes on top of it.

- **Auto-start: your standing start, one worker at a time while memory is low.** `issue-cli autostart on --quote "<your words>"` turns it on for one project; it's off for every project until then. At each check-in the lead runs `issue-cli autostart next`, which says `start <slug>` (the first ready unclaimed backlog issue in `next` order) or `hold:` with the reason: paused, no mem-guard or `mem-guard check` refusing, memory at or above `--below` (50%), `--max-workers` (2) live, another project started one less than `--gap` (5) minutes ago, or nothing ready. `issue-cli dispatch <slug> --autostart` re-checks under a lock every project on the machine shares, starts the worker and quotes your stored words with the memory reading in its `dispatch:` comment. `pause`, `resume` and `off` stop it. The lead brief and `lead whoami` carry the rule; the Report shows an Auto-start card with your words, the verdict and Pause / Turn off; the sidebar and `status.md` show it while it's on. docs/agent-dispatch.md's "No unattended runs" now names auto-start as its one opt-in exception. See docs/agent-dispatch.md, Auto-start.

## v0.56.0 — 2026-10-30

- **Edit a station from the Product hall.** A station's page (`/p/<project>/hall/station/<status>`) has **Edit**: change the status's description and prompt, add, move or remove the actions on the transitions into and out of it, and switch your approval gate on each way in on or off. **Preview** shows the diff of workflow.yaml and its checks and writes nothing. Only the edited entries are rewritten, so comments and every other line stay as they were. Unknown action types or rules, approvals for missing statuses and new schema problems are refused. **Confirm** asks for your reason and commits the file on main (`workflow(workflow.yaml): <status>: <what changed>`, your reason quoted). It refuses a file that changed since you opened the form, a checkout not on main and a file with uncommitted changes. With YOLO on, the preview says when the YOLO gate list changes. Overlays and `*` edges are listed with their lines for hand edits. See docs/UI/overview.md, Editing a station.
- **The README shows the Workshop Board.** It now opens with why the engine exists, then how (one step at a time, your gates, just-in-time guidance, files, a factory that runs itself), then what: the map, the company with departments and the outputs shelf, the hall and its station editor, the station, the CEO chat, Needs you, the Report, YOLO mode and shipping on one `main` with local CI. Every picture is new, from the demo in the light theme, and the classic-page screenshots are gone. `make demo` is now Acme Shop: HQ with business goals, a Marketing department on its own `content` workflow, an outputs shelf and gates waiting on you. `make readme-shots` retakes the pictures (`playtest shots` over `docs/images/shots.yaml`, a staged demo with a CEO and workers on a private tmux server). docs/getting-started.md has a Find your way around section for the board.
- **Live agents show with a relative `workdir:`.** `workdir: "."` (the README's Quickstart) or `"demo"` (`make demo`) no longer hides every running lead and worker from the map, the hall and the CEO chat's worker list: a session's path is compared with the absolute project root.
- **The outputs shelf's selected tab shows its label.** The current department and state tabs were ink on ink.

## v0.55.0 — 2026-10-30

- **The CEO chat reads the lead's transcript: whole messages, question cards, answered questions drop off.** The conversation now comes from the lead's Claude Code transcript (`~/.claude/projects/<cwd>/<session>.jsonl`, found by the lead's `-n` name), not the terminal. A reply is one bubble with its markdown rendered (lists, code, tables) instead of one bubble per wrapped line. Tool calls fold into a line that opens to each call's description, and answered AskUserQuestion calls stay in the history as grey cards with what was chosen. An open AskUserQuestion is a card above the message box, read from the lead's screen: pick options or type Other, then Submit (`POST …/lead/answer` presses the widget's keys, and nothing when the screen has moved on), then Submit answers on the review tab. *CEO's questions* and `lead.json` (`questions`, new `open_questions`) list only open questions: the widget on screen and the `?` sentences of the CEO's last message until you reply. Without a transcript the chat reads the pane as before and says so.
- **Needs you: every waiting item in one place, with Approve all.** Every "N waiting on you" count now opens a list of what waits on you: `/p/<project>/needs-you`, or `/needs-you` across projects. That covers the sidebar's Status number, the Workshop header, the Report's card, the company panel and the map. The list shows red CI, gates to approve, playtests and check scripts to run, open questions, outputs to review and unsent bug reports, each with its own button (Approve, Run checks, Play, Answer, Open shelf). Tick the ready gates, or **Select all ready**, then press **Approve selected (N)**. One confirm lists each issue as from → to, and one click stamps them all, with the same record the station writes and your optional words saved on each issue. Nothing moves and no agent is messaged. A gate that stopped being ready in the meantime is not stamped, and its row says why. The counts now agree everywhere: the company header and the map now also count outputs to review and unsent bug reports, as the sidebar did. `issue-cli` approval hints and the `dispatch` waiting-on-gate refusal add the list's link. `/p/<project>/needs-you.json` gives the lead the same rows.
- **▶ Run playtest from the page you are on.** Where your playtest is due (the station's playtest step, the Issue tab's Needs-you band, the Evidence tab's Playtest card), ▶ Run playtest opens tmux window `playtest-<slug>` in the checkout holding the session and runs it; on a Mac it finds Playwright's Chrome for Testing for you. The page says it is running, then lists each step you judged and offers **Tick passed boxes**, which runs the report in the same window; nothing is ticked without that click. The commands are `playtest_command:` and `playtest_report:` in workflow.yaml (default `make playtest ISSUE={slug}` and `make playtest-report ISSUE={slug} APPLY=1`); the button only shows with `playtest: owner`. `make ux-test` / `make playtest` also find Playwright's Chrome when none is installed. See docs/patterns/playtest/README.md, Run it from the page.

- **The Product hall opens each part of the line.** Click a station's head (Done and Parking too), a gate or the CI scanner: `/p/<project>/hall/station/<status>` lists every issue there, the status prompt, the ways in and out with each action in words, and the types and systems that change it; `/hall/gate/<status>` shows what the gate stands on and who waits at it; `/hall/ci` shows the CI state, commands, failures and what landed since green. `issue-cli process transitions` ends with a line pointing at these pages.
- **Images in the CEO chat.** Paste, drop or attach (📎) a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP in the CEO's message box (10 MB each, 5 per message). The viewer keeps it in `.agent-logs/lead-<project>/chat/images/` and the message ends with one `[Image: <absolute path>]` line per image, which the CEO reads. Thumbnails show in the box (× removes one) and in your turn of the conversation. Playtest sessions gain an `upload` action that picks a file in a file input. See docs/agent-dispatch.md.
- **Playtests can be the agents' alone.** A top-level `playtest:` key in workflow.yaml says who runs playtest sessions: `owner` (the default, as before), `agents` or `off`. With `agents` or `off` an issue in human-testing no longer shows "Playtest <slug>" in Waiting on you, a playtest step on the issue page or a playtest marker on its card; agents still write sessions and run `make ux-test`. The engine's own workflow.yaml sets `playtest: agents`, and its human-testing prompt no longer asks the owner to run `make playtest`. `wf.PlaytestForOwner()` answers it in code. Catalog entry `playtest-owner-switch`. See docs/patterns/playtest/README.md.
- **YOLO mode: gates pass without you for a window, and you review them after the fact.** `issue-cli yolo on --until 08:00|4h|off --quote "<your words>"` (the lead, from your words in lead chat; never a worker) opens a window. Until it ends, `issue-cli transition` lets a `require_human_approval` gate on a forward step through once nothing else is missing, and writes `approval: "YOLO" (yolo · <agent> · from <status>, <date>) → <status>` on the issue. Backwards steps, optional statuses such as `parked`, Human Testing's own approval and `release publish` (unless `--allow-releases`) still wait for you. CI, tests, proofs, review, retros and permission prompts stay on. CI red for more than 30 minutes turns YOLO off by itself. `start`/`show` print a `YOLO:` line, the gated next step says to run it, `--dry-run` drops the approval YOLO would pass, and `decide` without `--quote` is tagged `YOLO`. Every project page's sidebar shows the hazard line while it is on (Extend / Turn off now) and "N after the fact" with **Agree** / **Back to <status>** until you have reviewed each pass; `issue-cli yolo status|extend|off|queue|review`. On the Workshop Board the **YOLO** switch sits in the company header and opens the turn-on dialog (gates, what still guards, how long, valves, your words); while it is on, a yellow-black hazard band runs under the header on every project screen with Extend / Turn off now, the map draws the lot in stripes with "YOLO until 08:00" and "N after the fact" in place of "!", the hall raises its gates and marks the crates that passed, the issue page shows **Passed in YOLO** with Agree / Back to <status>, and YOLO decisions carry the tag in the Decisions log. State lives in `.yolo/` in the main checkout. See docs/Workflow/yolo.md.

- **Outputs shelf.** Each department has a shelf of what it made: posts, designs, pages, reports, invoices. `issue-cli output add --title "…" --kind page --url <link>` (or `--file`, copied onto the shelf, or `--path`, a file in the repo) records one in `<issues>/.outputs/outputs.jsonl`, under the issue's department (`--issue`), else `--department`, else Product, with `--job` for work no issue made. An output starts in review: the owner approves or rejects it on the shelf page (`/p/<project>/shelf`) or through the lead (`issue-cli output review <id> approve --quote "…"`; workers can't), and `issue-cli output publish <id> --url` works only once it is approved. The company page shows "📦 N to review · M made this week" on each room and the Product line, and HQ the total and the newest five. The issue's station lists what it made, and departments with outputs in review are counted in Waiting on you (company panel, Status, `status.md`, sidebar). `issue-cli outputs` lists the shelf. `start`/`show` print an Outputs line, and a transition into owner review, publish or done hints at `output add` when the issue made nothing. The content template's Draft and Published boxes point at the shelf. `issue-cli health lint` (`departments`) warns about bad log lines, gone files, outputs of a removed department and outputs waiting for review for more than 14 days. See docs/departments.md.
- **Slow e2e tier green after the Workshop Board (fix).** On macOS the agents canvas, card footers and agent panel showed live agents as stopped when the project root was a path through a symlink (`/var` is `/private/var`): tmux reports the resolved folder and the project filter dropped the session. The classic Health page's File as issue, Dismiss and Run review now come back to the classic page (`?classic=1`) instead of the Workshop one. On a phone the hall's view tabs wrap instead of making the page scroll sideways. The playtest sessions written for the classic pages open them at `?classic=1`, and the steps that read the board's first daily snapshot wait for it. See docs/patterns/playtest/web-app.md, Gotchas.
- **Department role and goals.** In projects.yaml, `company:` gives HQ a mission, a role prompt file and business goals, and each department (and an optional `Product` entry) takes `mission:`, `prompt:` and `goals:`. A goal has an id, target, unit, period (week, month, quarter), direction and source: `manual`, recorded with `issue-cli goal set <dept>/<id> <value>` in `<issues>/.goals/values.jsonl`, or `file:<path>#<metric id>` from a JSON file in the metric run format. Department goals with `rolls_up:` hang under an HQ goal, and an HQ goal with no value of its own shows their mean progress. The company page header shows HQ's goals instead of the open epics, and HQ, the rooms and the Product line show mission and goals. `issue-cli goals` prints the roll-up, `issue-cli department show <name>` the mission, role prompt and goals, and `start`/`show` print a Department line. `issue-cli health lint` has a new `departments` check. See docs/departments.md.
- **Each department can run on its own workflow file.** A department in `projects.yaml` can set `workflow: workflows/<name>.yaml` (relative to the project root). Its issues then walk that file: transitions, gates, prompts, `show`, `start`, `next`, the dispatch prompt, the issue page and the station all read the issue's own file. Product keeps `workflow.yaml`, and a department without `workflow:`, or a project without departments, works as before. Project-wide keys (`workbench`, `ci`, `health`, `privacy`, `leads`, `project_actions`, `allow_shell`) always come from Product's file. A missing or broken department file stops only that department's issues, with an error naming the file, and its room in the viewer shows an error chip. The Board gets a **Workflow** switch (`?department=`), and a department room draws its own line. Moving an issue to a department whose file lacks its status is refused with that file's statuses listed; `set-meta … --status <s>` moves it. New: the `content` template (brief → draft → owner review → publish → done, with your `publish` approval before anything goes out), `workflow init --out <path>`, `--department` on `create`, `list`, `process workflow|transitions` and `workflow upgrade|scan`, and a `department` check in `health lint`. See docs/workflow.md, Department workflows.
- **Workshop Board: safe to merge (fix).** Five blockers from the lead's review, plus one owner request. The station's **Stamp it**, target radios and words box are never locked: while a playtest, guided checks or a question are open, a note says "Steps 1–N are still open: you can stamp ahead." The hall's message box no longer prefills `approved: <slug>` (empty when the page picks the issue, `<slug>: ` when you do), so a stray Enter approves nothing. Lead chat's Ctrl+C guard is checked and recorded under the lead's lock: a second press within 5 s gets 429 also while a message is delivering or when two presses race, a failed press doesn't start the window, and a send no longer holds the lock through the 20 s delivery check. The Report (`/status`) carries Waiting for release (milestone readiness with issue links, Unreleased, Not pushed, the release candidate's cut, pick or publish step) and the Local CI card. The classic issue page's Issue tab links to `?classic=1` and stays classic. See docs/UI/overview.md and docs/agent-dispatch.md.
- **`make validate` passes on macOS (fix).** 28 tests failed on a Mac and passed on Linux: tmux sockets under the deep macOS temp dir passed the ~104-byte socket path limit (tests now keep `TMUX_TMPDIR` under `/tmp`), the guided-check library tests called `script -c`, which BSD `script` lacks (they drive a pty through Python elsewhere), and tests compared printed paths with `/var/…` where the CLI prints `/private/var/…`. Test-only: no change to what the viewer or `issue-cli` do.
- **The CEO chat: talk to the project lead from the board.** `/p/<project>/lead` (L1 · HQ on the Workshop Board; the company page's HQ desk, the map's *Talk to the CEO* and the sidebar's Lead chat link it) reads the lead's tmux pane as a conversation: your messages and the CEO's answers as turns, its tool calls folded into one line ("used 3 tools: Bash ×2, Read"), a note when older turns are only in tmux, and **Terminal** for the raw pane. The CEO's spinner shows as a working line with **Interrupt (Esc)**; a permission menu is a *CEO waits on your choice* card whose buttons press 1–3; the session mode (auto, plan, accept edits) is a chip, and **Mode · switch** presses shift+tab. The message box sends on Enter (Shift+Enter adds a line) as one bracketed paste plus Enter. The session controls are **Send now · interrupt** (ctrl+x ctrl+s), **Show details** (ctrl+o), **To background** (ctrl+b ctrl+b), **Stop session** (Ctrl+C, after a confirm; a second one within 5 s is refused, since two close Claude Code) and ↑ ↓ ⏎ 1 2 3. The side panel shows what waits on you in the project, the CEO's latest questions (**Answer in chat** starts your reply and sends nothing), the project's live workers and the `tmux attach` line. With no lead running, the page says so and offers **Start the CEO**. It polls every 1.5 s, replaces only the parts that changed, and keeps your typed text and scroll position. **Every CEO** (`/leads`) shows each project's lead as a card: live or not, its waiting count, *Talk to the CEO*. `lead.json` adds `turns`, `older`, `working`, `prompt` and `mode`. What you send is you typing in the lead's chat; the page sends nothing by itself. The writes are `POST …/lead/send` (JSON, 20 KB at most, control characters dropped) and `POST …/lead/key` (an allowlist of keys), behind the usual origin check, and the tmux target always comes from the project. See docs/agent-dispatch.md, Lead chat.

## v0.54.1 — 2026-10-24

- **The release Action writes its notes again (fix).** v0.54.0's run failed after tagging (exit 141): the step piped `git show` of the CHANGELOG into an `awk` that stops at the next heading, so `git show` died of SIGPIPE under `pipefail` and no release or binaries were made. It now reads the file first.

## v0.54.0 — 2026-10-24

- **The privacy badges know when a repo is private.** The viewer's health watcher asks GitHub, at most once an hour per repo and never on a page view, whether the code repo (origin) is publicly visible, and caches the answer in its git folder. Work files tracked in a private repo now read as a green **Private repo** (info, not the red "Work in code repo"), and so does Code when privacy isn't set up; the Health line adds what to do before making the repo public (tracker init, release setup, a one-time history clean). Not on GitHub, or not asked yet: the badges are as before.
- **An epic's page shows its children, and the Epics card shows where an epic continues.** On an epic, the first tab is named **Epic** with its closed/total children (amber, green when all are closed) and lists the children by version, open ones first. In a version's panel of the Status Epics card, an epic with children still open in other versions says so (**+8 → 0.55**).
- **Epics get their own short path.** A `type: epic` issue goes idea → in progress → done (or parked) instead of the full feature path: the lead moves it to in progress when its first child starts, and it closes on your approval once every child is done or parked. New validator `children_closed` (optional `values:` for the closing statuses) checks the children; it is what the Epics card's **close?** shows.
- **Status shows which open issues are epics and which epics you can close.** In Waiting for release, an open epic carries an **epic** chip; the milestone card says how many epics are still open (they count as open issues); in the Epics card an epic whose children are all done, in every version, shows **close?** (else its own status), the Version select and the Versions card list only versions not released yet (the card's count sums those).
- **Releases on the public repo are signed.** `issue-cli release publish` signs the snapshot commit with your `user.signingkey` (GPG or SSH), so GitHub shows it Verified; a key that fails to sign stops the publish before anything goes public. `privacy check` and the Status Code badge turn amber (**Release unsigned**) while the public tip has no signature, with the fix. The publish line says whether it signed.
- **The feature catalog says what is so (fix).** An audit of Kestrel's installed features found the engine saying things that weren't true:
  - **Wording:** `system-pages` is now "System page details". Every issue `system:` has a page anyway, and `workbench.systems` only adds summaries, design docs and families. `metrics-and-knobs` and `status-changelog` likewise say what shows without their key (Branches in Metrics; CHANGELOG.md, else git tags, on Status).
  - **`workflow upgrade`** no longer calls a current stamp `up_to_date` while the record isn't. It reports `undecided` (proposals wait on you) or `stale_record` (the file has features the record doesn't list; `workflow scan` records them), with `undecided` in `--json`. `process workflow`, `health lint` and the Updates tab ("not recorded") flag a stale record whatever the stamp; undecided proposals still show only on Updates and in `upgrade`.
  - **Status page:** it no longer suggests `workbench.changelog` when the record declines `status-changelog`.
  - **Health:** a glob that matches no file (`e2e_sessions`, default included, or `test_globs`) is now a family warning, printed by `issue-cli health` and listed on the Health view, instead of a silent zero.
  - **`workflow scan`** prints each category heading once (proof-links moved into gates).
- **Private work files and private development.** `privacy:` in workflow.yaml records the owner's choice of where issues, sessions, retros, bugs and health snapshots live; `issue-cli init` asks on a terminal and `workflow upgrade` proposes the new `privacy` entry. With `work_files: tracker` they live in a private tracker repo whose git folder sits outside the checkout (docs/workflow.md, Private work and code).
  - `issue-cli tracker init --remote <url>` creates or adopts it, ignores the work paths and pushes the first backup; `issue-cli tracker sync` commits, pulls and pushes. The viewer syncs every 10 minutes, prompting no session.
  - A pre-push guard refuses a push that adds work files to the code repo; `issue-cli privacy check` shows the state and the fixes.
  - **Status** shows a Work badge after the title (green private, amber no backup or failing, red work in the code repo, grey public by choice) and its line on the Health card; the projects list and the `/stats` overview show the marker.
  - **Private development, public releases.** With `code: snapshots` the dev history stays in a private dev repo (origin) and the public repo gets ONE commit per release, the release branch's tree with the message `vX.Y.Z`. `issue-cli release setup --dev <url>` switches an existing repo; the pre-push guard refuses dev commits and tags to the public remote. With `code: public` it refuses commit messages that quote a decision.
  - **Releases are cut on a branch** (docs/roadmap.md, Cutting a release): `issue-cli release cut v0.N.0` creates `release/v0.N` and its worktree and tags the rc there; fixes land on main and `release pick <slug>` cherry-picks them; `release publish v0.N.0` publishes from the branch. Waiting for release watches the branch: picks after rc.K need the next one, and **Every done fix is on release/v0.N** lists what to pick.
  - The Release Marker Action skips private repos and takes the release notes from the CHANGELOG's version section. A new Privacy Guard Action fails a PR that touches work files, and with `code: snapshots` any PR on the public repo.
  - Status gains the **Code** badge next to Work: green private, amber not switched or guard off, red dev history on the public branch, grey public by choice.
  - Catalog entries can carry `setup:` steps, each the owner's or the agent's. The Updates card lists them, `workflow upgrade` prints them, and the install agent walks them, leaving the owner's steps to the owner.
- **Ticking a box on the issue page saves that box (fix).** A click used to save "the nth `[ ]`" in the raw markdown, which also counted `[ ]` in inline code, code blocks and prose, so on an issue that quotes the checkbox syntax every box below the quote ticked or unticked the box above it, gate boxes included. Each rendered box now carries its line in the body, a click posts `{line, checked, base_body_hash}` to the new `POST /issue/<slug>/checkbox`, and the server flips only that task-list box: 409 when the file changed since the page loaded or the box is already in that state, 400 for a line that holds no box. Check boxes you clicked on issues that quote `[ ]` above their boxes.
- **Done keeps a game project's playtest runs (fix, critical).** At → done (and in `issue-cli playtest prune`) the archive kept only runs of mode `auto` or `judge`, so a game project's `ux-test` / `playtest` runs were all deleted with the worktree and its Evidence tab went empty for good. One mode reading (`ux-test` → auto, `playtest` → judge) now serves the archive and the Timeline/Evidence tabs. An own run goes only when a newer run of its session and mode is kept; the run a kept `-cached` run takes its shots from, an own run of another mode and a run it can't classify are kept. The line now says `archived N of M runs, deleted K`, with a ⚠ when none of the issue's runs was archived. The 30-day shrink also reduces a game's shots beside `results.yaml`. Runs already lost are not recovered.
- **A game's playtest runs show on the Evidence and Timeline tabs.** Kestrel's runner writes its own results.yaml (`started:` with no timezone, `ux-test` / `playtest` modes, `screenshots:` named `<step>@<W>x<H>.png` beside results.yaml, `kind`/`subject`/`passed` checks, `verdict:`). The viewer dropped every such run on its date, so the tabs said no playtest had run. It now reads both schemas when a run is loaded, and lists a run it still can't read at the top of both tabs instead of skipping it. A step's shot comes from the newest run that has the file (a `-cached` replay is passed over). When no step proves a criterion, the Shots card shows each session's latest ux-test shots. docs/patterns/playtest/README.md lists the fields of both schemas the viewer reads.
- **Workers don't message their lead unless you say so.** A worker's briefing no longer invites it to message the lead or other agents. It messages only when you, in its own chat, give it permission or ask it to. Questions go to you in its chat or as `question:` comments, and progress stays on the issue trail. The lead's briefing matches: it doesn't ask workers to report in. Running workers keep their old briefing until re-briefed (`issue-cli dispatch <slug> --reprompt`).
- **A lead writes its handoff note before it exits (issue-approve mod 0.6.0).** In a project lead's session (`ISSUE_VIEWER_LEAD_SCOPE` set, no issue slug), the first `/exit` doesn't exit: the mod says so and starts a turn asking the lead to write its handoff note (what is live, what waits on whom, decisions, `## Next`) and back up the private tracker. The next `/exit` leaves. Workers and other sessions are untouched. Ctrl+C, Ctrl+D or a killed window can't be caught; the mod records the missed handoff, and the project's next lead session gets a line in its first prompt to write it before other work. Running sessions need `/reload-plugins`.
- **Every issue gets a `created:` date.** `issue-cli create`, `report-bug`, CI's red issue and the viewer's New issue form write `created: "<today>"`: the date the `criteria_proven` gate reads, which counted an undated issue as new. A one-off backfill dated the 1,442 undated issues on this machine's 13 projects, each by the earliest date recorded for it (stats sidecar, comments, decisions, first git commit, file mtime as the fallback). `set-meta <slug> --key created --value YYYY-MM-DD` dates an issue that has none and never changes an existing date. The Evidence tab's label now follows the gate's rule exactly.
- **`issue-cli health lint` checks workflow.yaml and the board.** Six checks, every finding a warning with its fix:
  - `schema`: keys the parser silently drops, with "did you mean"; deprecated keys.
  - `template`: a stamp behind the bundled template.
  - `placeholders`: `{{name}}` placeholders a field never fills.
  - `sections`: checkbox and test-plan gates on sections no template writes.
  - `undated`: issues without `created:`.
  - `workflow`: the warnings `process workflow` printed.

  The new `lint` health family counts them, a `lint` component (weight 10, 0 → 20 warnings) joins the derived score, the Health view lists them live in a Lint section, and the Status page's Health card shows the count.
- Fix: `set-meta` on a new field no longer leaves a blank line before the closing `---`.

- **Releases go through a release candidate first.** With `workbench.release_candidates: true` (issue-viewer sets it), a ready milestone is cut as a local `v0.N.0-rc.1` tag, installed and run on this machine's projects. It is published only on the owner's ok on that soak (docs/roadmap.md, Cutting a release).
  - **Waiting for release** shows the candidate in silver under its title (`v0.54.0-rc.1 · release candidate · soaking 2d`), with its details folded to one summary line: when it was cut, and the commits merged after it. Each of those needs the next rc, bugfixes included (the `soaked` check). It never calls the release ready by itself: it says `rc.K soaking` and to publish only on the owner's ok. On the engine project it also shows the viewer and issue-cli builds running (flagged when they aren't the candidate), which projects ran the candidate (their usage logs), and the open report-bug issues filed from it.
  - **Version stamp:** `make build` / `make install` stamp both binaries with `git describe`, so `issue-cli version` stops saying `dev`. A candidate build says it is one and how to report a problem.
  - **report-bug from a candidate** puts the bug in the release's milestone (`Added to milestone 0.54 …`): a bug found in the soak waits in this release, not the next.
  - Usage-log events carry the build as `ver`. Tags with a pre-release suffix no longer count as a release when there is no changelog. The lead brief says never to publish without the owner's ok on a soaked candidate.
- **A human starts the work; agents then run on their own.** A disclaimer in Agent Dispatch (docs/agent-dispatch.md, "A human starts the work"): no scheduler, timer or auto-dispatch, and unattended runs belong on `claude -p` or the Agent SDK with your own API key, not a Claude subscription.
  - **`issue-cli dispatch` needs `--quote "<the human's words>"`**, from the lead and from your own shell. Without it nothing starts (exit 2). Each start or re-prompt leaves `dispatch: "<words>" (<how>, <date>)` on the issue. One slug per call. The board's ▶ is unchanged. `issue-cli health review --spawn` needs `--quote` the same way.
  - **The lead recalls decisions, never invents them.** Every lead brief now says: answer workers only from recorded decisions and cite them; ask the human when none covers it and tell the worker to wait; dispatch only the issues the human names in the lead's chat; tick gates only with the human's quote.
  - **`issue-cli lead message` never pastes into a Claude session.** It pastes only into Codex sessions; for a Claude session it prints the `SendMessage` call to make.
  - **Local CI no longer pastes `[from ci]` into the lead.** Red and green still reach the Status page, `status.md`, the `ci-red` markers and the `ci:` comments; the lead reads `issue-cli ci status` when you ask.
  - **A worker stays on its issue.** In a worker session, `start`, `claim` and `transition` on another slug fail ("you work on <own-slug> only; ask your lead"), and the worker prompt says to stop when the issue is done or at a gate, never picking the next one. `start` and `claim` default the assignee to the worker's session name (`agent-<slug>`), its `SendMessage` address.
- **`make ux-test` is green on main again.** The frontmatter-escapes and local-ci sessions predated the retitle that keeps the slug and the Needs you tile; they now follow the pinned slug and open the tile before checking it. Run another issue's session from a worktree with `make ux-test ISSUE= S=<session>`.
- **The issue page's Evidence tab says whether an issue is proven.** It replaces the "coming" card, and its label carries the count on every tab: green "8 / 8 proven", amber when short, grey "13 ticked · 0 linked" for an issue the proof gate doesn't cover. Issues with no `created:` are dated by their first transition, so older done issues read grey.
  - **Shots:** the latest playtest run's screenshot of each step a criterion links to, tagged with what it proves. The widest screen shows first, with chips for the others; a failed screen shows its failing check. Shots are shown in place from the run folders; nothing is copied into the issue.
  - **Criteria → proof:** the same report as `issue-cli prove --list` and the gate, with each link's source, its check and the fix for a failing one. Unproven rows are amber, and stray trailers are listed.
  - **Verdict, Change, Playtest per step, Watch, Stale text:** the merge, the `tests:` comment against the last code commit, and time and waits as on the Timeline. Then commits and files by area with lines in tests; each step's machine result next to your verdict (judge run or `playtest:` / `approval:` words); `risk:` comments; and Open questions / pending / TBD lines left in the body, each with the D-n or Clarifications bullet that likely answered it.
  - `issue-cli show` and `issue-cli prove --list` print the Evidence link.
- **Docs-only commits no longer make test links stale.** A commit whose files are all under `docs/`, Markdown or a CHANGELOG doesn't count as a change for the `tests:` check, so the documentation commit no longer turns every test link stale on a done issue (`prove --list`, the `criteria_proven` gate, the Evidence tab). `prove` also looks every test name up in one `git grep`, so a long list is fast.
- **Playtest shots stop piling up.** `.playtest/runs` held 1.2 GB across this repo's worktrees.
  - `make ux-test` keeps screenshots only for the run's own issue's sessions (from `-issue`, else the `work/<slug>` branch), plus failing screens and judge runs; `screenshot:` paths are relative to the run folder.
  - At → done, issue-cli keeps the latest auto and judge run of each of the issue's own sessions, moves them to `<root>/.playtest/runs/` (removing the worktree no longer loses the evidence) and deletes the rest. It prints one `playtest:` line and never fails the transition.
  - **`issue-cli playtest prune [<slug> | --all] [--dry-run]`** applies the same rules to existing worktrees (here: 1,240 MB → 46 MB).
  - The viewer's daily health pass shrinks the shots of issues done 30 or more days ago to small JPEGs (`health.shrink_shots_after_days` in workflow.yaml, 0 turns it off); the Evidence tab marks them "reduced".
- **Disk use on the Stats page.** A new `storage` health family records how many MB the project takes (worktrees, playtest shots, `.git`, `.agent-logs`, attachments, total), shown as a Disk use card with a bar per area and the total's trend.
- **▶ Playtest opens a tmux window you can see, never a hidden child of the agent (issue-approve mod 0.5.0).** The mod used to run `make playtest` under the agent's own process; in Kestrel that started Godot instances the owner could neither see nor stop. Now ▶ Playtest runs the project's `scripts/playtest-window <slug>` when the checkout has one (Kestrel), else opens `make playtest ISSUE=<slug>` in a new detached window `playtest-<slug tail>` of the agent's tmux session, in its worktree; the window waits for Enter when the playtest ends.
